Sixty-fifth Year SPECIAL No. 21 December 13, 2024

Sixty-fifth Year	SPECIAL No. 21	December 13, 2024

OFFICIAL GAZETTE

OF THE REPUBLIC OF MALI

LAW NO. 2024-027 OF DECEMBER 13, 2024 ESTABLISHING THE PENAL CODE

GENERAL SECRETARIAT OF THE GOVERNMENT

LAW NO. 2024-027 OF DECEMBER 13, 2024 ESTABLISHING THE PENAL CODE

The National Transitional Council deliberated and adopted, in its session of October 31, 2024,

The President of the Transition, Head of State, promulgates the law whose content follows:

BOOK I

GENERAL PROVISIONS TITLE I: CRIMINAL LAW

Chapter 1: General principles

Article 111-1

Criminal offenses are classified, according to their severity, into crimes, délits and contraventions.

Article 111-2

The law determines crimes and délits and sets the penalties applicable to their perpetrators.

Regulations determine contraventions and set, within the limits and according to the distinctions established by law, the penalties applicable to offenders.

Article 111-3

No one may be punished for a crime or for a délit whose elements are not defined by law, or for a contravention whose elements are not defined by law or regulation.

No one may be punished with a penalty that is not provided for by law, if the offense is a crime or a délit, or by regulation, if the offense is a contravention.

Article 111-4: Criminal law is strictly interpreted

Article 111-5

Criminal courts have jurisdiction to interpret administrative acts, whether regulatory or individual, and to assess their legality when the solution to the criminal proceedings before them depends on such examination.

Chapter 2: Application of criminal law in time

Article 112-1

Only acts constituting an offense on the date they were committed are punishable. Only penalties legally applicable on the same date may be imposed.

However, new provisions apply to offenses committed before their entry into force and which have not resulted in a conviction that has become res judicata when they are less severe than the former provisions.

Article 112-2

The following apply immediately to the punishment of offenses committed before their entry into force:

1°) laws on jurisdiction and judicial organization, as long as a judgment on the merits has not been rendered at first instance;

2°) laws setting the procedures for prosecution and the forms of procedure;

3°) laws relating to the regime for execution and application of penalties; however, these laws, when they would result in making more severe the penalties imposed by the conviction decision, are only applicable to convictions pronounced for acts committed after their entry into force;

4°) when prescription has not been acquired, laws relating to the prescription of public prosecution and the prescription of penalties.

Article 112-3

Laws relating to the nature and grounds for opening remedies as well as the time limits within which they must be exercised and the capacity of persons admitted to appeal apply to appeals filed against decisions pronounced after their entry into force. Appeals are subject to the formal rules in force on the day they are exercised.

Article 112-4

The immediate application of the new law has no effect on the validity of acts performed in accordance with the former law.

However, the penalty ceases to be executed when it was imposed for an act which, by virtue of a law subsequent to the judgment, no longer has the character of a criminal offense.

Chapter 3: Application of criminal law in space

Article 113-1

For the application of this chapter, the territory of the Republic includes the airspace connected to it.

Section 1

Offenses committed or deemed committed on the territory of the Republic

Article 113-2

Malian criminal law is applicable to offenses committed on the territory of the Republic.

An offense is deemed committed on the territory of the Republic when one of its constituent acts took place on that territory.

Article 113-3

Any crime or any délit committed by means of an electronic communication network, when it is attempted or committed to the detriment of a natural person residing on the territory of the Republic or a legal person whose headquarters is located on the territory of the Republic, is deemed committed on the territory of the Republic.

Article 113-4

Malian criminal law is applicable to offenses committed on board vessels flying the Malian flag, or against such vessels or persons on board, wherever they may be. It alone is applicable to offenses committed on board vessels of the national navy, or against such vessels or persons on board, wherever they may be.

Article 113-5

Malian criminal law is applicable to offenses committed on board aircraft registered in Mali, or against such aircraft or persons on board, wherever they may be. It alone is applicable to offenses committed on board Malian military aircraft, or against such aircraft or persons on board, wherever they may be.

Article 113-6

Malian criminal law is applicable to anyone who has been guilty on the territory of the Republic, as an accomplice, of a crime or a délit committed abroad if the crime or délit is punished both by Malian law and by foreign law and if it has been established by a final decision of the foreign court.

Section 2: Offenses committed outside the territory of the Republic

Article 113-7

Any Malian who, outside the territory of Mali, commits an act qualified as a crime punished by Malian law, may be prosecuted and tried by Malian courts.

Any Malian who, outside the territory of Mali, commits an act qualified as a délit by Malian law, may be prosecuted and tried by Malian courts if the act is punished by the legislation of the country where it was committed.

The same applies if the accused, the defendant or the accused person acquired Malian nationality only after the commission of the crime or délit.

However, whether it is a crime or a délit, no prosecution takes place if the accused, the defendant or the accused person proves that he has been definitively tried abroad, and in case of conviction, that he has served or his sentence has been prescribed or he has obtained pardon.

In case of a délit committed against a Malian or foreign private individual, prosecution may only be initiated at the request of the Public Prosecutor; it must be preceded by a complaint from the injured party or an official denunciation to the Malian authority by the authority of the country where the délit was committed.

Article 113-8

Any foreigner who, outside the territory of Mali, commits either as perpetrator or as accomplice, a crime against the security of the State or counterfeiting of the State seal, national currencies in circulation, national papers, banknotes authorized by law, may be prosecuted and tried according to the provisions of Malian laws, if he is arrested in Mali or if the Government obtains his extradition.

Article 113-9

Malian criminal law is applicable to crimes and offences committed on board or against aircraft not registered in Mali or against persons on board:

1°) when the perpetrator or the victim is of Malian nationality; 2°) when the aircraft lands in Mali after the crime or offence;
3°) when the aircraft has been leased without crew to a person who has the principal place of business or, failing that, permanent residence in the territory of the Republic.

Article 113-10

Malian criminal law applies to crimes and offences classified as acts of terrorism and punished by articles 251-1 to 251-7 of this Code, committed abroad by a Malian or by a person habitually residing in Malian territory.

Article 113-11

Malian criminal law applies and national courts have jurisdiction over offences provided for in articles 257-1 to 257-11 of this Code, relating to the fight against the financing of terrorism, and in articles 431-1 to 431-11 of this Code, relating to money laundering, committed by any natural or legal person, regardless of their nationality or the location of their headquarters, even outside the national territory, provided that the place of commission is located in one of the Member States.

They may also have jurisdiction over the same offences committed in a third State, provided that an international convention gives them jurisdiction.

Article 113-12

The provisions of this chapter are supplemented by those of articles 814 to 820 of the Code of Criminal Procedure.

TITRE II : DE LA RESPONSABILITE PENALE

Chapitre 1 : Des dispositions générales

Article 121-1 : No one is criminally liable except for their own act

Article 121-2

Legal persons, with the exception of the State, are criminally liable for offences committed, on their behalf, by their organs or representatives.

However, local authorities and their groupings are criminally liable only for offences committed in the exercise of activities that may be the subject of public service delegation agreements.

The criminal liability of legal persons does not exclude that of natural persons who are perpetrators or accomplices of the same acts.

Article 121-3 : There is no crime or offence without intent to commit it

However, when the law so provides, there is an offence in the case of deliberate endangerment of another person.

There is also an offence, when the law so provides, in the case of recklessness, negligence or breach of an obligation of care or safety provided for by law or regulation, if it is established that the perpetrator of the acts did not exercise normal diligence taking into account, where applicable, the nature of their duties or functions, their competence and the power and means at their disposal.

In the case provided for in the preceding paragraph, natural persons who did not directly cause the damage, but who created or contributed to creating the situation that allowed the damage to occur or who did not take measures to avoid it, are criminally liable if it is established that they either manifestly and deliberately violated a particular obligation of care or safety provided for by law or regulation, or committed a gross fault that exposed others to a risk of particular gravity that they could not have been unaware of.

There is no contravention in the case of force majeure.

Article 121-4

The perpetrator of the offence is the person who: 1°) commits the incriminated acts;
2°) attempts to commit a crime or, in cases provided for by law, an offence.

Any natural person who personally and principally accomplishes the constituent elements of an offence by commission or omission or who is at the origin of such acts is a perpetrator or co-perpetrator.

Any legal person with a civil, commercial, industrial or financial purpose in whose name and in whose interest acts of execution or abstention constituting an offence have been accomplished by the deliberate will of its organs or its representative in the exercise of their functions is also a perpetrator or co-perpetrator.

Article 121-5

An attempt is constituted when, manifested by a commencement of execution, it was suspended or failed to have its effect only by reason of circumstances independent of the will of its perpetrator.

Article 121-6 : Are punished as accomplices to an act classified as a crime or offence

1°) those who, by gift, promise, threat, order, abuse of authority or power, machination or culpable artifice, advice, injunction, provoke this action or give instructions, indications, information, to commit it;

2°) those who provide weapons, instruments, or any other means that served the action, knowing that they were to be used for it;

3°) those who, with knowledge, aid or assist the perpetrator or perpetrators of the action in the acts that prepared or facilitated it or in those that consummated it, without prejudice to the penalties that are specifically provided by this Code against the perpetrators of conspiracies or attacks against the security of the State, even in the case where the crime that was the object of the conspirators or instigators was not committed;

4°) those who knowingly suppress or attempt to suppress evidence of the action, or who, with knowledge, by any means whatsoever, help the perpetrators or accomplices of the crime or offence to evade the action of justice.

5°) The perpetrators of acts of complicity are punished with the same penalties as the perpetrators of the crime or offence of which they have made themselves accomplices.

The provisions of paragraph 5 of this article are not applicable to direct line ascendants and descendants of the perpetrators or accomplices of the action, to their brothers, to their sisters, to their spouses, to their guardians and to their wards.

Article 121-7

Also accomplices to a crime or an offence are those who, without risk to themselves and to their relatives, having witnessed it, refrain from intervening to prevent its perpetration or who, having had knowledge of it, refrain from reporting the perpetrators or accomplices.

Chapitre 2 : Des causes d'irresponsabilité ou d'atténuation de la responsabilité

Article 122-1

A person who was suffering, at the time of the acts, from a psychic or neuropsychic disorder that abolished their discernment or control of their acts is not criminally liable.

A person who was suffering, at the time of the acts, from a psychic or neuropsychic disorder that impaired their discernment or hindered the control of their acts remains punishable. However, the court takes this circumstance into account when it determines the penalty and sets its regime.

Article 122-2

A person who acted under the influence of a force or constraint to which they could not resist is not criminally liable.

Article 122-3

A person who performs an act prescribed or authorized by legislative or regulatory provisions is not criminally liable.
Subject to the provisions of Title 1 of Book III relating to crimes against humanity, a person who performs an act ordered by legitimate authority is not criminally liable, unless that act is manifestly unlawful.

Article 122-4

A person is not criminally liable who, faced with an unjustified attack against themselves or others, performs, at the same time, an act commanded by the necessity of self-defence of themselves or others, unless there is a disproportion between the means of defence employed and the seriousness of the attack.

A person is not criminally liable who, in order to interrupt the commission of a crime or an offence against property, performs an act of defence, other than voluntary homicide, when that act is strictly necessary for the purpose pursued provided that the means employed are proportionate to the seriousness of the offence.

Article 122-5

A person is presumed to have acted in a state of self-defence who performs the act:

1°) to repel, at night, entry by breaking and entering, violence or trickery into an inhabited place;

2°) to defend themselves against the perpetrators of thefts or looting carried out with violence.

Article 122-6

A person is not criminally liable who, faced with a present or imminent danger that threatens themselves, others or property, performs an act necessary for the protection of the person or property, unless there is a disproportion between the means employed and the seriousness of the threat.

Article 122-7

The age of criminal majority is set at eighteen years. Minors capable of discernment are criminally liable for crimes, offences or contraventions of which they have been found guilty, under conditions laid down by the Code of Criminal Procedure and all other legislative or regulatory provisions determining the measures of protection, assistance, supervision and education to which they may be subject.

These provisions also determine the educational measures that may be imposed on minors from ten to under eighteen years of age as well as the penalties to which minors from thirteen to under eighteen years of age may be sentenced, taking into account the mitigation of liability from which they benefit by reason of their age.

TITRE III : DES PEINES, DES MESURES DE SURETE ET DES MESURES EDUCATIVES

Article 130-1

In order to ensure the protection of society, to prevent the commission of new offences and to restore social balance, with respect for the interests of the victim, the penalty has the following functions:

1°) to punish the perpetrator of the offence;

2°) to promote their reform, integration or reintegration.

Article 130-2

The judge must impose only penalties that are provided for and strictly and evidently necessary.

Chapitre 1 : De la nature des peines

Section 1

Des peines applicables aux personnes physiques Sous-section 1: Des peines criminelles

Article 131-1 : Les peines criminelles encourues par les personnes physiques sont

1°) la mort ;

2°) la réclusion criminelle à perpétuité ;

3°) la réclusion criminelle de trente ans au plus ; 4°) la réclusion criminelle de vingt ans au plus ; 5°) la réclusion criminelle de quinze ans au plus ; 6°) la réclusion criminelle de dix ans au plus.

Article 131-2

La peine de réclusion criminelle n'est pas exclusive d'une peine d'amende et d'une ou de plusieurs des peines complémentaires prévues à l'article 131-6.

Sous-section 2 : Des peines correctionnelles

Article 131-3 : Les peines applicables aux délits sont

1°) l'emprisonnement pour une durée n'excédant pas dix ans ; 2°) l'emprisonnement pour une durée n'excédant pas sept ans ; 3°) l'emprisonnement pour une durée n'excédant pas cinq ans ; 4°) l'emprisonnement pour une durée n'excédant pas trois ans ; 5°) l'emprisonnement pour une durée n'excédant pas deux ans ; 6°) l'emprisonnement pour une durée n'excédant pas un an ; 7°) l'emprisonnement pour une durée n'excédant pas six mois ; 8°) l'emprisonnement pour une durée n'excédant pas trois mois;

9°) la peine de travail d'intérêt général ; 10°) l'amende ;
11°) les peines complémentaires prévues à l'article 131-6 ci- dessous.

La peine d'un jour d'emprisonnement est de vingt-quatre heures. Celle d'un mois est de trente jours. Celle d'un an est de douze mois.

Article 131-4

La peine de travail d'intérêt général est une peine alternative à l'emprisonnement.

Elle a pour but de promouvoir les meilleures conditions de réhabilitation, de réinsertion sociale et d'amendement du condamné.

Article 131-5

[Décret n°036 du 31 janvier 2007] La peine de travail d'intérêt général consiste à faire exécuter par le condamné qui y consent un travail non rémunéré au profit d'une Collectivité publique, d'un service public ou d'une association reconnue d'utilité publique.

Elle est prononcée à titre de peine principale et ne peut être cumulée avec une peine d'emprisonnement.

Elle n'est applicable qu'aux délits pour lesquels le maximum de la peine encourue n'excède pas deux ans.

Elle ne peut être inférieure à 40 heures ni supérieure à 240 heures pour le condamné majeur.

Elle ne peut être inférieure à 20 heures ni supérieure à 180 heures pour le condamné mineur de seize à dix- huit ans non révolus.

La non-exécution, même partielle de la peine de travail d'intérêt général entraîne, sur décision du juge de l'application des peines, l'application en tout ou partie de la peine d'emprisonnement prononcée au moment de la condamnation.

Sous-section 3 : Des peines complémentaires encourues pour certains crimes ou délits

Article 131-6

Lorsque la loi le prévoit, un crime ou un délit peut être sanctionné d'une ou de plusieurs peines complémentaires qui, frappant les personnes physiques, emportent interdiction, déchéance, incapacité ou retrait d'un droit, injonction de soins ou obligation de faire, immobilisation ou confiscation d'un objet, confiscation d'un animal, fermeture d'un établissement ou affichage de la décision prononcée ou diffusion de celle-ci soit par la presse écrite, soit par tout moyen de communication au public par voie électronique.

Article 131-7

Lorsqu'un délit est puni d'une ou de plusieurs des peines complémentaires mentionnées à l'article 131-6 ci- dessus, la juridiction peut ne prononcer que la peine complémentaire ou l'une ou plusieurs des peines complémentaires encourues à titre de peine principale.

La juridiction peut alors fixer la durée maximum de l'emprisonnement ou le montant maximum de l'amende dont le juge de l'application des peines peut ordonner la mise à exécution en tout ou partie en cas de violation par le condamné des obligations ou interdictions résultant des peines prononcées en application des dispositions du présent article.

Le Président de la juridiction en avertit le condamné après le prononcé de la décision.

L'emprisonnement ou l'amende que fixe la juridiction ne peut excéder les peines encourues pour le délit pour lequel la condamnation est prononcée.

Sous-section 4 : Des peines contraventionnelles

Article 131-8

La peine contraventionnelle encourue par les personnes physiques est l'amende n'excédant pas 100 000 francs sous réserve des textes spécifiques.

La confiscation peut être appliquée comme peine complémentaire.

Subsection 5: Content and Methods of Application of Certain Penalties

Article 131-9

The complementary penalty of confiscation is incurred in the cases provided for by law or regulation. It is also incurred for crimes and for offenses punishable by a term of imprisonment exceeding one year, with the exception of press offenses.

Confiscation applies to all property, movable or immovable, whatever its nature, divided or undivided, having been used to commit the offense or which were intended to commit it, and of which the convicted person is the owner or, subject to the rights of the owner in good faith, over which he has free disposal.

It also applies to all property which is the instrument or object or the direct or indirect proceeds of the offense, with the exception of property capable of restitution to the victim. If the proceeds of the offense have been mixed with funds of lawful origin for the acquisition of one or more properties, confiscation may apply to such property only up to the estimated value of such proceeds.

Confiscation may furthermore apply to any movable or immovable property defined by the law or regulation which punishes the offense.

If it is a crime or an offense punishable by at least five years' imprisonment and having procured a direct or indirect profit, confiscation also applies to movable or immovable property, whatever its nature, divided or undivided, belonging to the convicted person or, subject to the rights of the owner in good faith, over which he has free disposal, when neither the convicted person nor the owner, given the opportunity to explain the property whose confiscation is contemplated, have been able to justify its origin.

When the law which punishes the crime or offense so provides, confiscation may also apply to all or part of the property belonging to the convicted person or, subject to the rights of the owner in good faith, over which he has free disposal, whatever its nature, movable or immovable, divided or undivided. Confiscation is mandatory for objects classified as dangerous or harmful by law or regulation, or whose possession is unlawful, whether or not such property is the property of the convicted person.

The complementary penalty of confiscation applies under the same conditions to all incorporeal rights, whatever their nature, divided or undivided.

Confiscation may be ordered in value. Confiscation in value may be executed on all property, whatever its nature, belonging to the convicted person or, subject to the rights of the owner in good faith, over which he has free disposal.

The confiscated thing is, except for special provision providing for its destruction or attribution, devolved to the State, but it remains encumbered, up to its value, with real rights lawfully constituted in favor of third parties.

When the confiscated thing is a vehicle which has not been seized or impounded during the proceedings, the convicted person must, upon the injunction made to him by the Public Prosecutor, surrender this vehicle to the service or organization responsible for its destruction or disposal.

Confiscation in the absence of criminal conviction is ordered at the request of the Public Prosecutor, subject to the rights of the owner in good faith, when the perpetrator of the offense cannot be prosecuted due to death, flight or absence or in other appropriate cases such as when the property to be confiscated is perishable, when it has been abandoned by the owner, when its preservation would cost more than the property itself, when its preservation is dangerous for public health or safety and when it will lose value over time.

In these cases, the Public Prosecutor demonstrates that the property constitutes the proceeds or instrument of an offense or that the criminal conduct is established by applying the criterion of the highest probability, which means that the assets may be obtained even when the evidence is not sufficient to obtain a criminal conviction.

Article 131-10: The prohibition of civic, civil and family rights applies to

1°) the right to vote; 2°) eligibility;
3°) the right to exercise a judicial function or to be an expert before a court, to represent or assist a party before the courts;

4°) the right to testify in court other than to make simple statements;

5°) the right to be a guardian or curator; this prohibition does not exclude the right, after the conforming opinion of the guardianship judge, the family council having been heard, to be guardian or curator of one's own children.

The prohibition of civic, civil and family rights may not exceed a duration of ten years in case of conviction for a crime and a duration of five years in case of conviction for an offense.
The court may pronounce the prohibition of all or part of these rights.
The prohibition of the right to vote or ineligibility pronounced pursuant to this article entails prohibition or incapacity to exercise a public function.

Article 131-11

The penalty of prohibition of residence entails a prohibition to appear in certain places determined by the court. It includes, in addition, measures of supervision and assistance.

The list of prohibited places as well as the measures of supervision and assistance may be modified by the sentence enforcement judge, under the conditions set by the Code of Criminal Procedure.

The prohibition of residence may not exceed a duration of ten years in case of conviction for a crime and a duration of five years in case of conviction for an offense.

Article 131-12

When the prohibition of residence accompanies a custodial sentence without suspension, it applies from the commencement of that sentence and its execution continues, for the duration fixed by the conviction decision, from the day the deprivation of liberty ended.
Any detention occurring during the prohibition of residence is credited against the duration thereof.

Section 2

Penalties Applicable to Legal Persons Subsection 1: Criminal and Correctional Penalties

Article 131-13

The criminal or correctional penalties incurred by legal persons are:

1°) the fine;

2°) in the cases provided for by law, the penalties listed in Article 131-15 below.

Article 131-14

The maximum rate of the fine applicable to legal persons is equal to five times that provided for natural persons by the law which punishes the offense.

When it is a crime for which no fine is provided against natural persons, the fine incurred by legal persons is 100,000,000 francs.

Article 131-15

Where the law so provides against a legal person, a felony or misdemeanor may be punished by one or more of the following penalties:

1°) dissolution, where the legal person was created or, in the case of a felony or misdemeanor punishable in respect of natural persons by a term of imprisonment of three years or more, diverted from its purpose to commit the incriminated acts;

2°) prohibition, permanently or for a period of five years at most, from directly or indirectly engaging in one or more professional or social activities;

3°) placement for a period of five years at most under judicial supervision;

4°) permanent closure or closure for a period of five years at most of the establishments or of one or more of the establishments of the enterprise that were used to commit the incriminated acts;

5°) exclusion from public contracts permanently or for a period of five years at most;

6°) prohibition, permanently or for a period of five years at most, from making a public offer of financial securities or from having its financial securities admitted to trading on a regulated market;

7°) prohibition, for a period of five years at most, from issuing cheques other than those that allow the withdrawal of funds by the drawer from the drawee or those that are certified or from using payment cards;

8°) the penalty of confiscation, under the conditions and according to the procedures provided for in Article 131-9 of this Code;

9°) posting of the decision rendered or dissemination thereof either by the written press or by any means of communication to the public by electronic means;

10°) confiscation of the animal that was used to commit the offence or against which the offence was committed;

11°) prohibition, permanently or for a period of five years at most, from keeping an animal;

12°) prohibition, for a period of five years at most, from receiving any public aid granted by the State, local authorities, their establishments or their groupings as well as any financial aid paid by a private person entrusted with a public service mission.

The additional penalty of confiscation is also incurred automatically for felonies and for misdemeanors punishable by a term of imprisonment exceeding one year, with the exception of press offences.

The penalties defined in 1° and 3° above are not applicable to legal persons under public law whose criminal liability is capable of being engaged. They are also not applicable to political parties or groupings or to trade unions. The penalty defined in 1° is not applicable to staff representative institutions.

Sous-section 2 : Des peines contraventionnelles

Article 131-16 : Les peines contraventionnelles encourues par les personnes morales sont

1°) the fine;

2°) the penalties depriving or restricting rights provided for in Article 131-18 below.

Article 131-17

The maximum rate of the fine applicable to legal persons is equal to five times that provided for natural persons by the regulation that punishes the offence.

Article 131-18

The penalty of a fine may be replaced by one or more of the following penalties depriving or restricting rights:

1°) prohibition, for a period of one year at most, from issuing cheques other than those that allow the withdrawal of funds by the drawer from the drawee or those that are certified or from using payment cards;

2°) confiscation of the thing that was used or was intended to commit the offence or of the thing that is the product thereof.

Sous-section 3 : Du contenu et des modalités d'application de certaines peines

Article 131-19

The decision ordering the dissolution of the legal person includes the referral thereof to the competent Court to proceed with its liquidation.

Article 131-20

The decision to place the legal person under judicial supervision includes the appointment of a judicial representative whose mission the court specifies. This mission may only relate to the activity in the exercise or on the occasion of the exercise of which the offence was committed. At least every six months, the judicial representative reports to the sentence enforcement judge on the performance of his mission.

In view of this report, the sentence enforcement judge may refer the matter to the court that ordered the placement under judicial supervision. The latter may then either impose a new penalty or release the legal person from the placement measure.

Article 131-21

The prohibition from making a public offer of financial securities or from having its financial securities admitted to trading on a regulated market entails prohibition, for the placement of securities of any kind whatsoever, from having recourse both to credit institutions, financial institutions or brokerage firms and to any methods whatsoever of advertising.

Chapitre 2 : Du régime des peines

Article 132-1

Where the law or regulation punishes an offence, the regime of penalties that may be imposed is subject, except where otherwise provided by law, to the rules of this chapter.

Any penalty imposed by the court must be individualized.

Within the limits set by law, the court determines the nature, quantum and regime of the penalties imposed according to the circumstances of the offence and the personality of its perpetrator as well as his material, family and social situation, in accordance with the purposes and functions of the penalty set out in Article 130-1 of this Code.

Section 1 : Des Dispositions générales

Sous-section 1 : Des peines applicables en cas de concours d'infractions

Article 132-2

There is a concurrence of offences where an offence is committed by a person before that person has been definitively convicted for another offence.

Article 132-3

Where, in the course of the same proceedings, the person prosecuted is found guilty of several concurrent offences, and where several penalties of the same nature are incurred, only one penalty of that nature may be imposed within the limit of the highest statutory maximum.

Each penalty imposed is deemed common to the concurrent offences within the limit of the statutory maximum applicable to each of them.

Article 132-4

Where, in the course of separate proceedings, the person prosecuted has been found guilty of several concurrent offences, the penalties imposed are served cumulatively within the limit of the highest statutory maximum. However, total or partial merger of penalties of the same nature may be ordered either by the last court called upon to rule or under the conditions provided for by the Code of Criminal Procedure.

Article 132-5

For the application of Articles 132-3 and 132-4 above, custodial sentences are of the same nature and any custodial sentence is merged with a life sentence.

Account is taken, where appropriate, of recidivism.

Where life imprisonment, incurred for one or more of the concurrent offences, has not been imposed, the statutory maximum is set at thirty years' imprisonment.

The benefit of the suspended sentence attached in whole or in part to one of the penalties imposed for concurrent offences does not preclude the execution of penalties of the same nature not accompanied by a suspended sentence.

Article 132-6

When a sentence has been the subject of a pardon or remission, the sentence resulting from the measure or decision is taken into account for the application of merger.

Remission occurring after merger applies to the sentence resulting from the merger.

The duration of the sentence reduction is credited against that of the sentence to be served, where applicable, after merger.

Article 132-7

Notwithstanding the preceding provisions, fines for contraventions accumulate among themselves and with those incurred or imposed for crimes or offenses in concurrence.

Sous-section 2

Penalties applicable in case of recidivism Paragraphe 1: General provisions

Article 132-8

The offenses of theft, extortion, fraudulent dispossession, blackmail, fraud, stellionate, usury, breach of trust, misuse of corporate assets, insider trading and complicity in these offenses are considered, with regard to recidivism, as a single offense.

Article 132-9

The offenses of sexual violence and complicity provided for by articles 325-3, 325-5, 325-7, 327-2, and 327-3 of this Code are considered, with regard to recidivism, as a single offense.

Article 132-10

The offenses of involuntary homicide or involuntary injury to the person committed while driving a motorized land vehicle are considered, with regard to recidivism, as a single offense.

The offenses and complicity in offenses provided for such as hit and run, driving under the influence of alcohol, driving without a license, failure to comply with driving license regulations, are considered, with regard to recidivism, as a single offense.

Article 132-11

The acts of trafficking in persons, procuring, slavery and similar practices, pledging, hostage-taking, kidnapping, smuggling of migrants and child trafficking and complicity in these acts are considered, with regard to recidivism, as a single offense.

Article 132-12

The offenses of intentional violence against persons as well as any offense committed with the aggravating circumstance of violence are considered, with regard to recidivism, as a single offense.

Article 132-13

The offenses of vagrancy, begging and incitement to begging are considered with regard to recidivism as a single offense.

Article 132-14

Third-class police contraventions are considered with regard to recidivism as a single offense.

In this case, the fine incurred may be increased to double the maximum provided by law.

Article 132-15

Legal recidivism is the commission of any offense that results in an aggravation of the sentence under the conditions specified in articles 132-16 to 132-21 below.

The state of legal recidivism may be raised on the court's own motion even when it is not mentioned in the prosecution document, provided that during the hearing, the person prosecuted has been informed of it and has been given the opportunity to be assisted by a lawyer and to present observations.

Paragraphe 2 : Natural persons

Article 132-16

When a natural person, already definitively convicted of a crime or of an offense punishable by ten years' imprisonment under the law, commits a crime, the maximum sentence incurred may, for temporary sentences, be increased up to double.

Article 132-17

When a natural person, already definitively convicted of a crime, commits, within five years from the expiration or prescription of the previous sentence, an offense punishable by imprisonment, the maximum imprisonment and fine sentences incurred is doubled.

Article 132-18

When a natural person, already definitively convicted of an offense, commits, within five years from the expiration or prescription of the previous sentence, either the same offense or an offense assimilated to it under the rules of recidivism, the maximum imprisonment and fine sentences incurred is doubled.

Paragraphe 3 : Legal persons

Article 132-19

When a legal person, already definitively convicted of a crime or of an offense punishable by imprisonment with respect to natural persons, engages its criminal liability through a crime, the maximum rate of the fine applicable to legal persons is doubled. In this case, the legal person also incurs the penalties mentioned in article 131-15 above, subject to the provisions of the last paragraph of that article.

Article 132-20

When a legal person, already definitively convicted of a crime or of an offense punishable by imprisonment with respect to natural persons, engages its criminal liability through an offense punishable by the same sentence, within ten years from the expiration or prescription of the previous sentence, the maximum rate of the fine applicable to legal persons is doubled.

In the cases provided for in the preceding paragraph, the legal person also incurs the penalties mentioned in article 131-15 above, subject to the provisions of the last paragraph of that article.

Article 132-21

When a legal person, already definitively convicted of an offense, engages its criminal liability, within five years from the expiration or prescription of the previous sentence, either through the same offense or through an offense assimilated to it under the rules of recidivism, the maximum rate of the fine applicable to legal persons is doubled.

Sous-section 3 : Penalties applicable in case of repetition of offenses

Article 132-22

There is repetition of criminal offenses when a person has already been definitively convicted of a crime or an offense and commits a new offense that does not meet the conditions of legal recidivism.
The sentences imposed for the offense committed in repetition accumulate without limitation of quantum and without possibility of merger with the sentences definitively imposed in the previous conviction.

Sous-section 4 : Pronouncement of sentences

Article 132-23

No sentence may be applied if the court has not expressly imposed it.

The court may impose only one of the sentences incurred for the offense before it.

When an offense is punishable by a sentence of imprisonment, the court may impose a sentence of imprisonment for a duration less than that which is incurred.

When an offense is punishable by a fine, the court may impose a fine of an amount less than that which is incurred.

Article 132-24

The sentence, like the decision on guilt, must be reasoned. This reasoning takes into consideration the seriousness of the facts of the case, the personality of their perpetrator as well as his material, family and social situation.

Section 2 : Methods of personalizing sentences

Article 132-25

Sentences may be personalized according to the methods provided for in this section.

Sous-section 1

Semi-liberty, external placement and placement under electronic monitoring

Paragraphe 1 : De la semi-liberté et du placement à l'extérieur

Article 132-26

Where the trial court pronounces a sentence equal to or less than two years' imprisonment, or, for a person in a state of legal recidivism, a sentence equal to or less than one year, it may decide that this sentence shall be executed in whole or in part under the regime of semi-liberty with regard to the convicted person who provides evidence:

1°) either of the exercise of a professional activity, even temporary, of participation in an internship or of his or her regular attendance at teaching, vocational training or job seeking;

2°) either of his or her essential participation in the life of his or her family; 3°) either of the necessity of undergoing medical treatment;
4°) either of the existence of serious efforts at social rehabilitation resulting from his or her sustained involvement in any other specific project of integration or reintegration likely to prevent the risks of recidivism.

These provisions are also applicable in the case of pronouncement of an imprisonment partially accompanied by suspension or suspension with probation, where the firm part of the sentence is less than or equal to two years, or, if the person is in a state of legal recidivism, less than or equal to one year.

In the cases provided for in the preceding paragraphs, the court may also decide that the sentence of imprisonment shall be executed under the regime of external placement.

Article 132-27

The convicted person admitted to the benefit of semi-liberty is required to return to the penitentiary establishment according to the procedures determined by the sentence enforcement judge based on the time necessary for the activity, teaching, vocational training, job seeking, internship, participation in family life, treatment or integration or reintegration project for which he or she was admitted to the regime of semi-liberty. He or she is required to remain in the establishment on days when, for any reason whatsoever, his or her external obligations are interrupted.

The convicted person admitted to the benefit of external placement is required, under the supervision of the administration, to carry out activities outside the penitentiary establishment.

The trial court may also subject the convicted person admitted to the benefit of semi-liberty or external placement to the measures provided for by Articles 132-35 to 132-39 below relating to suspension with probation.

Paragraphe 2 : Du placement sous surveillance électronique

Article 132-28

Where the trial court pronounces a sentence equal to or less than two years' imprisonment or, for a person in a state of legal recidivism, a sentence equal to or less than one year, it may decide that the sentence shall be executed in whole or in part under the regime of placement under electronic monitoring with regard to the convicted person who provides evidence:

1°) either of the exercise of a professional activity, even temporary, of participation in an internship or of his or her regular attendance at teaching, vocational training or job seeking;

2°) either of his or her essential participation in the life of his or her family;

3°) either of the necessity of undergoing medical treatment;

4°) either of the existence of serious efforts at social rehabilitation resulting from his or her sustained involvement in any other specific project of integration or reintegration likely to prevent the risks of recidivism.

These provisions are also applicable in the case of pronouncement of an imprisonment partially accompanied by suspension or suspension with probation, where the firm part of the sentence is less than or equal to two years, or, if the person is in a state of legal recidivism, less than or equal to one year.

The decision of placement under electronic monitoring may only be taken with the consent of the convicted person previously informed that he or she may request to be assisted by the lawyer of his or her choice, where applicable, the one appointed ex officio by the Bar President at his or her request, before giving his or her consent.

If it concerns an unemancipated minor, this decision may only be taken with the consent of the holders of the exercise of parental authority.

Article 132-29

Placement under electronic monitoring entails, for the convicted person, a prohibition on being absent from his or her home or any other place designated by the sentence enforcement judge outside the periods fixed by the latter. The periods and places are fixed taking into account: the exercise of a professional activity by the convicted person; the fact that he or she is attending teaching or training, carrying out an internship or occupying temporary employment with a view to his or her social integration; his or her participation in family life; the prescription of medical treatment.

Placement under electronic monitoring also entails for the convicted person the obligation to respond to summonses from any public authority designated by the sentence enforcement judge.

Article 132-30

The trial court may also subject the convicted person admitted to the benefit of placement under electronic monitoring to the measures provided for by Articles 132-38, 132-39 and 132-40 below relating to suspension with probation.

Sous-section 2 : Du sursis simple

Article 132-31

In the case of a conviction to imprisonment or to a fine, the courts and tribunals may, if the accused or the defendant has not undergone, during the five years preceding the facts, a conviction to imprisonment for a crime or offence, order by giving reasons for their decision, that execution of all or part of the sentence shall be suspended.

If during the period of five years, from the pronouncement of the judgment or the decision, the convicted person has not incurred any conviction, the latter is considered as null and void.

After the pronouncement of the conviction accompanied by suspension, the president of the court warns the convicted person, when he or she is present, of the consequences that would result from a conviction for a new offence committed within the period of five years. He or she also informs him or her of the possibility of having his or her conviction declared null and void.

Article 132-32

The court may, by special decision, revoke totally or partially, for a duration or an amount that it determines, the suspension previously granted, whatever the sentence it accompanies, when it pronounces a new conviction to a sentence of criminal imprisonment or to a sentence of imprisonment without suspension.

Article 132-33

In the case of revocation of simple suspension ordered by the court, the first sentence is executed without it being able to merge with the second.

Article 132-34

Where the benefit of simple suspension has been granted only for part of the sentence, the conviction is deemed null and void in all its elements if the total revocation of the suspension has not been pronounced under the conditions provided for in Article 132-32 above, the fine or the part of the fine not accompanied by suspension remaining due.

Subsection 3: Suspended sentence with probation

Paragraph 1: Conditions for granting suspended sentence with probation

Article 132-35

The court that pronounces a term of imprisonment may, under the conditions provided hereinafter, order that its execution be suspended, with the convicted natural person being placed under the probation regime.

Suspended sentence with probation is applicable to sentences of imprisonment pronounced for common law crimes or offences.

In this case, the Court may declare the execution of the sentence provisionally.

It sets the probation period which may not be less than three years nor more than five years, as well as the probationary measures assigned to the convicted person.

After pronouncing the imprisonment with suspended sentence with probation, the president of the court notifies the convicted person, when present, of the obligations to be respected during the suspended sentence with probation and warns of the consequences that would result from a conviction for a new offence committed during the probation period or a breach of the control measures and particular obligations imposed upon them. The president informs them of the possibility of having their conviction declared null and void if they observe satisfactory conduct.

Article 132-36

When a conviction is accompanied by suspended sentence with probation, the convicted person is placed under the supervision of the sentence enforcement judge or, failing that, the public prosecutor in the jurisdiction where they have their habitual residence or, if they have no residence in Mali, under the supervision of the sentence enforcement judge or, failing that, the public prosecutor of the court that pronounced the conviction.

If the acts necessary for the execution of the probationary measures must be carried out outside the limits of their jurisdiction, the sentence enforcement judge or, failing that, the public prosecutor instructs their territorially competent counterpart to oversee them.

Paragraph 2: Probation regime

Article 132-37

During the probation period, the convicted person must comply with the control measures provided for in Article 132-38 below and with those particular obligations provided for in Article 132-39 below that are specifically imposed upon them. In addition, the convicted person may benefit from assistance measures intended to promote their social rehabilitation.

These measures and particular obligations cease to apply and the probation period is suspended during the time the convicted person is incarcerated.

Article 132-38

The control measures to which the convicted person must submit are as follows:

1°) respond to summonses from the sentence enforcement judge or the designated social worker;

2°) receive visits from the social worker and provide them with information or documents of a nature to allow verification of their means of subsistence and the execution of their obligations;

3°) notify the social worker of changes in employment;

4°) notify the social worker of changes in residence or any travel exceeding fifteen days and report their return;

5°) obtain prior authorization from the sentence enforcement judge for any change in employment or residence, when such change is of a nature to hinder the execution of their obligations;

6°) inform the sentence enforcement judge in advance of any travel abroad.

Article 132-39

The sentencing court or the sentence enforcement judge may specifically impose upon the convicted person compliance with one or more of the following obligations:

1°) engage in a professional activity or pursue education or vocational training;

2°) establish their residence in a specified place;

3°) submit to medical examination, treatment or care measures, even under hospitalization. These measures may consist of a therapeutic injunction when it appears that the convicted person uses narcotics or habitually and excessively consumes alcoholic beverages;

4°) prove that they contribute to family expenses or regularly pay maintenance for which they are liable;

5°) make reparation in whole or in part, according to their contributory capacity, for the damage caused by the offence, even in the absence of a decision on the civil action;

6°) prove that they pay, according to their contributory capacity, the sums owed to the Public Treasury following the conviction;

7°) refrain from driving certain vehicles determined by the licence categories provided for in the Road Code;

8°) not engage in the activity in the exercise of or on the occasion of the exercise of which the offence was committed or not engage in an activity involving habitual contact with minors;

9°) refrain from appearing in any place, any category of places or any specifically designated area;

10°) not place bets, particularly with mutual betting organizations, and not take part in games of money and chance;

11°) not frequent drinking establishments;

12°) not associate with certain convicted persons, particularly the perpetrators or accomplices of the offence;

13°) refrain from entering into contact with certain persons, including the victim, or certain categories of persons, and particularly minors, except, where applicable, those designated by the court;

14°) not possess or carry a weapon;

15°) in case of an offence committed either against their spouse, or against their children or those of their spouse, reside outside the home or residence of the couple and, where applicable, refrain from appearing in this home or residence or in the immediate vicinity thereof, as well as, if necessary, undergo health, social or psychological care; these provisions are also applicable when the offence is committed by the former spouse of the victim, the home concerned being then that of the victim. For the application of this paragraph, the victim's opinion is obtained, as soon as possible and by all means, on the advisability of requiring the convicted person to reside outside the couple's dwelling. Except in special circumstances, this measure is taken when acts of violence likely to be repeated are involved and the victim requests it. The court may specify the terms for covering the costs relating to this accommodation;

16°) obtain prior authorization from the sentence enforcement judge for any travel abroad.

Paragraph 3: Revocation of suspended sentence with probation

Article 132-40

Suspended sentence with probation may be revoked by the trial court under the conditions provided for in Article 132-41 below.

It may also be revoked by the sentence enforcement judge when the convicted person has not complied with the control measures and particular obligations imposed upon them. Any breach of these measures and obligations committed after the probation has become enforceable may justify revocation of the suspension. If this revocation is ordered while the conviction had not yet acquired a final character, it becomes null and void in the event that this conviction is subsequently reversed or annulled.

Article 132-41

If the convicted person commits, during the probationary period, a common law crime or offence followed by a conviction to a custodial sentence without suspension, the trial court may, after obtaining the opinion of the sentence enforcement judge, order the revocation in whole or in part of the suspension or suspensions previously granted. This revocation may not be ordered for offences committed before the conviction accompanied by the suspension has acquired a final character.

Article 132-42

The decision ordering partial revocation of the suspension does not end the probation regime and does not attach to the conviction the effects of a conviction without suspension.

Article 132-43

When the court orders the revocation of the suspension in whole or in part, it may, by special and reasoned decision, enforceable provisionally, have the convicted person incarcerated.

Section 3

On the definition of certain circumstances resulting in the aggravation of penalties

Article 132-44

Premeditation consists in the design formed before the action, to make an attempt on the person of an individual, or even on that of whoever will be found or encountered even though this design may be dependent on some circumstance or some condition.

Article 132-45

Lying in wait consists in waiting for a longer or shorter time in one or various places, for an individual, either to put him to death, or to exercise on him acts of violence provided for by Article 321-4 of this Code.

Article 132-46

Breaking and entering consists in the forcing, degradation or destruction of any closing device or any kind of enclosure. Assimilated to breaking and entering is the use of false keys, keys unduly obtained or any instrument that can be fraudulently used to operate a closing device without forcing or degrading it.

Article 132-47

Scaling is the act of entering any place, either over an element of enclosure, or through any opening not intended to serve as an entrance.

Article 132-48: A weapon is any object designed to kill or injure

Any other object likely to present a danger to persons is assimilated to a weapon when it is used to kill, injure or threaten or when it is intended, by the person carrying it, to kill, injure or threaten.

Assimilated to a weapon is any object which, presenting with the weapon defined in the first paragraph a resemblance likely to create confusion, is used to threaten to kill or injure or is intended, by the person carrying it, to threaten to kill or injure.

The use of an animal to kill, injure or threaten is assimilated to the use of a weapon. In case of conviction of the owner of the animal or if the owner is unknown, the Court may decide to hand over the animal to an animal protection organization recognized as being of public utility or declared, which may freely dispose of it.

Pocket knives and scissors, simple canes are deemed weapons only insofar as they have been used.

Chapter 3: On the execution of penalties

Article 133-1: Every person sentenced to death shall be shot

The woman sentenced to death who is recognized as pregnant shall not undergo her sentence until after her delivery.

The woman who is breastfeeding shall not be executed until after the weaning of the child.

Article 133-2

Persons sentenced to imprisonment may be employed in works of public utility with the exception of those aged sixty years completed at the time of judgment; women are employed in works appropriate to their sex.

Article 133-3

The duration of any custodial sentence counts from the day when the convicted person is detained by virtue of the conviction that has become irrevocable which pronounces the sentence.

In case of provisional detention, this is entirely deducted from the duration of the sentence pronounced by the judgment of conviction.

Article 133-4

All individuals convicted for the same crime or for the same offence are held jointly and severally liable for fines, restitutions, damages and costs.

BOOK II: CRIMES AND OFFENCES AGAINST THE NATION, THE STATE AND PUBLIC PEACE

TITLE I: HIGH TREASON

Single Chapter: High treason

Article 211-1

There is high treason when the President of the Republic violates his oath provided for in Article 55 of the Constitution.

Article 211-2

The sanction for high treason and its implementation are carried out in accordance with the provisions of Article 73 of the Constitution.

TITLE II: CRIMES AND OFFENCES AGAINST THE EXTERNAL SECURITY OF THE STATE

Chapter 1: Treason

Article 221-1

Is guilty of treason and punished by death: 1°) any Malian who bears arms against Mali;
2°) any Malian who maintains intelligence with a foreign power with a view to engaging it to undertake hostilities against Mali or provides it with the means, either by facilitating the penetration of foreign forces on Malian territory, or by undermining morale or shaking the loyalty of the land or air forces, or in any other manner;

3°) any Malian who delivers to a foreign power or to its agents, Malian troops, portion of the national territory, cities, fortresses, works, posts, stores, arsenals, equipment, munitions, vessels or aircraft, belonging to Mali or placed under its guard;

4°) any Malian who, in time of war, incites military personnel to enter the service of a foreign power, facilitates the means for them or makes enlistments for a power at war against Mali;

5°) any Malian who, in time of war, maintains intelligence with a foreign power or with its agents, with a view to favouring the enterprises of this power against Mali.

Article 221-2: Is guilty of treason and punished by death

1°) any Malian who delivers to a foreign power or to its agents, in whatever form and by whatever means, a secret of national defence, or who secures, by whatever means, possession of a secret of this nature, with a view to delivering it to a foreign power or to its agents;

2°) any Malian who destroys or voluntarily damages a ship, an aircraft, equipment, a supply, a construction or an installation capable of being used for national defence, or knowingly commits, either before or after their completion, defects likely to prevent them from functioning or to cause an accident;

3°) any Malian who knowingly participates in an enterprise of demoralization of the army having as its object to harm national defence.

However, in time of peace, is punished by ten years' imprisonment, any Malian or foreigner who is guilty of:

a) voluntary defect in the manufacture of war material when this defect is not likely to cause an accident;

b) voluntary damage or destruction of equipment or supplies intended for national defence or used for it;

c) obstruction of the circulation of this equipment;

d) participation with knowledge of the facts in an enterprise of demoralization of the army, having as its object to harm national defence.

Also punished by twenty years' imprisonment is voluntary participation in an action committed in a gang and by open force, having as its object and result one of the crimes provided for in paragraphs a, b, c of this article, as well as the preparation of said action.

Chapter 2: Espionage

Article 222-1

Any foreigner who commits one of the acts referred to in Articles 221-1 paragraphs 2, 3, 4 above and 221-2 paragraphs 1°, 2° and 3° above is guilty of espionage and punished by death.

The provocation to commit or the offer to commit one of the crimes referred to in Articles 221-1 and 221-2 above and in this article is punished as the crime itself.

Article 222-2

The following are deemed national defense secrets for the application of this Code:
1°) information of a military, diplomatic, economic or industrial nature which, by its nature, must be known only to persons qualified to hold it, and must, in the interest of national defense, be kept secret from any other person;
2°) objects, materials, writings, drawings, plans, maps, photographs or other reproductions, and all other documents whatsoever which, by their nature, must be known only to persons qualified to handle or hold them, and must be kept secret from any other person, which may lead to the discovery of information belonging to one of the categories referred to in the preceding paragraph;

3°) military information of any nature, not made public by the Government and not included in the above enumerations, the publication, dissemination, disclosure or reproduction of which is prohibited by a law or by a decree;

4°) information relating to measures taken to discover and arrest the perpetrators and accomplices of crimes or offenses against the security of the State.

Chapter 3: Other offenses against the external security of the State

Article 223-1

Any Malian or any foreigner is guilty of an offense against the external security of the State and punished by the penalties set forth in Article 223-5 below:

1°) who, by hostile acts not approved by the Government, exposes Mali to a declaration of war;

2°) who, by acts not approved by the Government, exposes Malians to suffer reprisals;

3°) who, in time of peace, enlists soldiers on behalf of a foreign power, in Malian territory;

4°) who, in time of war, knowingly maintains, without authorization from the Government, correspondence or relations with the subjects or agents of an enemy power;

5°) who, in time of war, in defiance of enacted prohibitions, directly or through an intermediary, engages in acts of commerce with the subjects or agents of an enemy power.

Article 223-2

Any Malian or any foreigner is guilty of an offense against the external security of the State and punished by the penalties set forth in Article 223-5 below:

1°) who undertakes, by any means whatsoever, to undermine the integrity of Malian territory;
2°) who maintains with the agents of a foreign power intelligence having as its object or having as its effect to harm the military or diplomatic situation of Mali.

Article 223-3

Any Malian or foreigner is guilty of an offense against the external security of the State and punished by the penalties set forth in Article 223-5 below:

1°) who, for a purpose other than delivering it to a foreign power or its agents, or secures, being without qualification by any means whatsoever, possession of a national defense secret, or knowingly and without qualification holds an object or document deemed a national defense secret, or which may lead to the discovery of such a secret, or brings said secret, in whatever form and by whatever means, to the knowledge of the public or of a qualified person;

2°) who, through imprudence, negligence or non-observance of regulations, allows to be destroyed, removed or taken away, in whole or in part, and even momentarily, objects, materials, documents or information entrusted to him, and knowledge of which may lead to the discovery of a national defense secret, or allows knowledge, copy or reproduction thereof to be taken, even in part;

3°) who, without prior authorization from the competent authority, delivers or communicates to a person acting on behalf of a power or a foreign enterprise, either an invention concerning national defense, or information, studies or manufacturing processes relating to an invention of this kind, or to an industrial application concerning national defense.

Article 223-4

Also guilty of an offense against the external security of the State, and punished by the same penalties, without prejudice if applicable, to the penalties imposed for the attempt of the crimes provided for in Articles 221-1 and 221-2 of this Code, any Malian or any foreigner:

1°) who enters, in disguise or under a false name, or by concealing his quality, or his nationality, into a fortress, a work, post or arsenal, into the works, camps, bivouacs or cantonments of an army, into a warship, or a merchant vessel employed for national defense, into a military establishment of any nature, or into an establishment or shipyard working for national defense;

2°) who, even without disguising himself or without concealing his name, his quality or his nationality, organizes in a covert manner any means of correspondence or remote transmission likely to harm national defense;

3°) who flies over Malian territory by means of a foreign aircraft without being authorized to do so by a diplomatic convention or permission from the Malian authority;

4°) who, in a prohibited zone fixed by the military authority, executes without the authorization thereof drawings, photographs, surveys or topographical operations inside or around military and maritime places, works, posts or establishments;

5°) who stays, in defiance of an enacted regulatory prohibition, within a determined radius around fortified works or military and maritime establishments.

Article 223-5

If they are committed in time of war, offenses against the external security of the State are punished by imprisonment for twenty years.

If they are committed in time of peace, they are punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 1,600,000 francs.

However, the imprisonment may be increased to ten years and the fine to 3,600,000 francs with regard to the offenses referred to in Articles 223-1 paragraph 1, 223-2 paragraph 1 and 223-3 above.

In time of war, all other acts, knowingly accomplished, of a nature to harm national defense, are punished, if they are not already punished by another text, by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 1,600,000 francs.

In all cases, the guilty parties may in addition be deprived for twenty years of the rights mentioned in Article 131-6 of this Code. They may also be subject to a residence ban of twenty years.

The attempt of the offense is punished as the offense itself.

The offense committed abroad is punished as the offense committed in Malian territory.

Article 223-6

The confiscation of the object of the crime or offense and of the objects and instruments used to commit it is automatic, without there being any need to investigate whether or not they belong to the convicted persons.

The remuneration received by the guilty party, or the amount of its value when the remuneration could not be seized, are declared acquired by the Public Treasury by the judgment.

For the application of the penalty and the regime of provisional detention, crimes and offenses against the external security of the State are considered as common law crimes and offenses.

Article 223-7

In addition to the persons designated in Article 121-6 of this Code, any Malian or any foreigner is punished as an accomplice or as a receiver:

1°) who, knowing the intentions of the perpetrators of crimes and offences against the external security of the State, provides them with subsidies, means of existence, lodging, place of retreat or meeting;

2°) who knowingly carries the correspondence of the perpetrators of crimes or offences against the security of the State or who facilitates for them in any manner whatsoever, the search for, concealment, transport or transmission of the object of the crime or offence;

3°) who knowingly conceals the objects and instruments having served or intended to serve to commit the crime or offence or the objects, materials or documents obtained by the crime or offence;

4°) who knowingly destroys, removes, conceals, hides or alters a public or private document of a nature to facilitate the investigation of the crime or offence, the discovery of evidence or the punishment of its perpetrators.

Article 223-8

Unless there are express provisions to the contrary, the penalties imposed for crimes or offences against the external security of the State are applied to those of these offences which are committed in time of peace, as well as to those which are committed in time of war.

The Government may, by decree taken in the Council of Ministers, extend either for time of war or for time of peace, all or part of the provisions relating to crimes and offences against powers allied or friendly to Mali.

TITRE III : DES CRIMES ET DELITS CONTRE LA SURETE INTERIEURE DE L'ETAT

Chapitre 1 : Des attentats et complots contre le Gouvernement

Article 231-1

An attack the purpose of which is either to overthrow the lawful Government by force or to change the republican form of the State, or to incite citizens or inhabitants to arm themselves against the authority, is punished by the death penalty or by imprisonment for life or for a term.

The attempt is punished as the crime.

Article 231-2

There is a conspiracy as soon as the resolution to act is concerted and agreed upon between two or more persons.

A conspiracy having as its purpose the crimes mentioned in the preceding article, if it is followed by an act committed or begun to prepare its execution, is punished by imprisonment for twenty years.

If the conspiracy is not followed by any act committed or begun to prepare its execution, the penalty is that of imprisonment for ten years.

If there has been a proposal made and not agreed to act, to form a conspiracy to achieve the crimes mentioned in the preceding article, the person who made such a proposal is punished by imprisonment for five years, a fine of 500,000 francs and a residence ban for ten years.

Chapitre 2

Des crimes portant atteinte à la sécurité intérieure de l'Etat ou à l'intégrité du territoire par la guerre civile, l'emploi illégal de la force armée, la dévastation et le pillage public

Article 232-1

An attack the purpose of which is either to provoke the secession of a part of the territory of the Republic, or to incite civil war, by arming or pushing citizens or inhabitants to arm themselves against each other, or to bring devastation, massacre and pillage in one or more regions, cities, communes and villages of the Republic, is punished by the death penalty or by imprisonment for life.

A conspiracy having as its purpose one of the crimes provided for in this article and the proposal to form this conspiracy, are punished by the penalties set forth in Article 223-5 above according to the distinctions established therein.

Article 232-2 : Sont punis de mort

1°) those who raise or cause to be raised armed troops, engage or enlist soldiers or provide or procure arms or ammunition for them without order or authorization from the lawful authority;

2°) those who, without right or legitimate reason, take command of a troop and any other public force, of a garrison or of a camp of this garrison, of an administrative center, of a locality;

3°) those who retain, against the order of the Government, a command of the public forces, the commanders of said forces who keep their troops assembled after the discharge or separation has been ordered.

Article 232-3

Any person who, being able to dispose of the public force, has requisitioned or ordered, caused to be requisitioned or ordered the action or use thereof against the orders of the Government, is punished by imprisonment for a term. If this requisition or this order has been followed by effect, the guilty party is punished by death.

Article 232-4

Those who, participating in an insurrectionary movement, are found carrying arms and ammunition, occupy or attempt to occupy public buildings or private properties; erect barricades, oppose by violence and threats the convocation or assembly of the public force; provoke or facilitate the gathering of insurgents by flags, rallying signs or any other means; break or attempt to break telegraph or telephone lines; intercept or attempt to intercept communications between the depositaries of the public force; seize by violence or threat arms and ammunition, by pillaging shops, posts, stores, arsenals or other public establishments or by disarming agents of the public force, are punished by imprisonment for life.

Those individuals who have made use of their arms are punished by the death penalty.

Article 232-5 : Est puni de mort

1°) any individual who sets fire to or destroys by explosive device buildings, stores, arsenals or other properties belonging to the State;

2°) whoever, either to invade domains or properties of the State, cities, posts, stores, arsenals, or to pillage and share public funds, public or national properties or those of a generality of citizens, or to make attack or resistance against the public force acting against the perpetrators of these crimes, places himself at the head of armed bands or exercises therein any command function whatsoever;

3°) the same penalty is applied to those who direct the association, raise or cause to be raised, organize or cause to be organized bands or knowingly and voluntarily provide or procure for them subsidies, arms, ammunition and instruments of crime, or send substances, or who have, in any other manner, practiced intelligence with the leaders of the bands.

Article 232-6

In the case where one or more of the crimes mentioned in Articles 231-1 and 232-1 of this Code are executed or simply attempted by a band, the death penalty is applied, without distinction of rank, to all individuals forming part of the band and who are seized at the place of the seditious assembly.

Anyone who directs the sedition or exercises any command whatsoever in the band is punished by the same penalties, although not seized at the place.

Article 232-7

Except in the case where the seditious assembly has as its object or result one or more of the crimes set forth in Articles 231-1 and 232-1 of this Code, individuals forming part of the aforementioned bands, without exercising any command therein, and who have been seized at the places, are punished by imprisonment for twenty years.

Article 232-8

Those who, knowing the purpose and character of said bands, provide them without constraint with lodgings, place of retreat or meeting, are punished by imprisonment for twenty years.

Article 232-9

No penalty is imposed for the act of sedition against those who, having been part of these bands, without exercising any command and without holding any position or function, withdraw at the first warning from the civil or military authorities, or even those who are seized outside the places of seditious assembly, without offering resistance and without weapons.

They are punished, in these cases, only for the particular crimes and offences that they personally committed; nevertheless, they may be subject to a residence ban of ten years.

Article 232-10

All objects designed to kill or injure, within the meaning of Article 132-48 of this Code, are considered as weapons.

Article 232-11

Those who, before any execution or attempt of these conspiracies or these crimes and before any proceedings have commenced, were the first to give the Government, the administrative authorities or the judicial police authorities, knowledge of these conspiracies and crimes and of their authors or accomplices, or who, even since the commencement of proceedings, facilitated the arrest of said authors or accomplices, are exempted from the penalties imposed against the perpetrators of conspiracies or other crimes against the security of the State.

The guilty parties who have given such information or facilitated such arrests may, nevertheless, be subject to a residence ban of five years.

TITLE IV: CRIMES AND OFFENCES AGAINST THE PUBLIC INTEREST

Chapter 1

Crimes and offences against the public interest committed by natural persons holding public authority or entrusted with a public service mission or vested with an elective mandate

Section 1: Attack on the Constitution

Article 241-1

Any conduct violating the Constitution and its principles constitutes an attack on the Constitution.

An attack on the Constitution is punished by thirty years' imprisonment and a fine of 10,000,000 francs.

Article 241-2

Any public official, public agent or administrative employee, within the meaning of Article 241-14 below, who requisitions or orders, causes to be requisitioned or ordered, the action or use of public force against the execution of an order, a judicial warrant, any order emanating from the lawful authority, is punished by ten years' imprisonment. If this requisition or this order is carried out, the penalty is fifteen years' imprisonment.
