The penalties stated do not cease to be applicable to officials or employees who have acted on the orders of their superiors except insofar as this order was given by them for matters within their jurisdiction and on which hierarchical obedience was owed to them; in this case, the penalties set out above are applied only to the superiors who first gave this order.

If as a result of said orders or requisitions, other crimes punishable by penalties stronger than those expressed in this article occur, these stronger penalties are applied to the officials, public agents or employees guilty of having given said orders or made said requisitions.

Article 241-3

When a public official within the meaning of Article 241-14 of this Code orders or does any arbitrary act or act violating either individual liberty, or the civic rights of one or more citizens, or the Constitution, he is deprived of his civic rights.

If however, he proves that he acted on the orders of his superiors for matters within the jurisdiction of the latter, on which hierarchical obedience was owed to them, he is exempted from the penalty, which, in this case, is applied only to the superiors who gave the order.

Article 241-4

If it is a minister who orders or does the acts or one of the acts mentioned in Articles 241-2 and 241-3 above, if he refuses or neglects to have these acts repaired, he is punished by five years' imprisonment and a fine of 1,800,000 francs.

Article 241-5

If a minister accused of having ordered or authorized the act contrary to the Constitution claims that his signature was obtained by deception, he is required, while causing the act to cease, to denounce the person he declares to be the author of the deception; otherwise he is prosecuted personally and liable to the penalties provided for in the preceding article.

Article 241-6

The damages that may be awarded in respect of the attacks referred to in Article 241-3 above are claimed, either in the prosecution or by civil action and are determined, having regard to the persons, the circumstances and the harm suffered, without in any case, and whatever the injured individual, said damages being able to be below 5,000 francs, for each day of illegal and arbitrary detention and for each individual.

Article 241-7

If the arbitrary act in violation of the Constitution is done pursuant to a false signature of the name of the minister or a public official, the authors of the forgery and those who knowingly made use of it are punished by the penalty of fixed-term imprisonment, the maximum of which is always applied in this case.

Article 241-8

Public officials responsible for administrative or judicial police, who refuse or neglect to comply with a lawful complaint seeking to establish illegal and arbitrary detentions, either in houses intended for the custody of detainees, or anywhere else and who do not prove that they reported them to the superior authority, are punished by two years' imprisonment and a fine of 240,000 francs.

Article 241-9

The managers and supervisors of penitentiary establishments who receive a prisoner without a warrant or judgment or without an extradition detention order, those who detain him or have refused to present him to the judicial police officer or to the bearer of his orders without justifying the prohibition of the public prosecutor or the judge; those who refuse to exhibit their registers to the judicial police officer, are considered guilty of arbitrary detention and punished by the penalties provided for in the preceding article.

Article 241-10

Any crime or offence committed by a public official in the exercise of his functions is a malfeasance.

Article 241-11

Any act of malfeasance is punished by ten years' imprisonment when the law does not provide for a lesser or greater penalty.

Article 241-12

All Judicial Police Officers, all Prosecutors General or Advocates General, all Public Prosecutors, all Deputy Prosecutors, all Judges who provoke, give or sign a judgment, an order or a warrant tending to the personal prosecution or the indictment, either of a minister or of a member of Parliament without the formalities or authorizations prescribed by law or who do not suspend the detention or prosecution at the request of Parliament, or who, outside cases of flagrante delicto, without the same formalities and authorizations, give or sign the order or warrant to seize or arrest one or more ministers or members of Parliament, are guilty of malfeasance and punished by twenty years' imprisonment.

Article 241-13

Prosecutors General or Advocates General, Public Prosecutors, Deputy Prosecutors, Judges or Judicial Police Officers who detain or cause to be detained an individual outside the places determined by the Government or by the public administration, or who bring a citizen before a criminal court without his having been previously lawfully indicted, are also punished by ten years' imprisonment.

Section 2: Coalition of public officials against the Constitution and the laws

Article 241-14

For the purposes of this Code, all natural persons who are depositaries of public authority, entrusted with a public service mission, vested with an elective mandate, exercising functions as representatives, administrators or agents of the State or of a public authority, of a public establishment or of a State company, of a company with majority public financial participation, of a private law legal entity acting on behalf of the State or of a public law legal entity benefiting from its financial support or guarantee, as well as all other persons acting on behalf of one of the aforementioned persons, are deemed to be public officials or agents.

All citizens who, under any designation and to any extent, are vested with a mandate, even temporary, remunerated or gratuitous, the execution of which is linked to a public interest, and who in this capacity contribute to the service of the State, public administrations, municipalities or administrative groupings, are assimilated to public officials and agents.

Furthermore, persons chosen by private individuals or delegated by the courts in the capacity of expert, arbitrator or interpreter are assimilated to public officials and agents.

Article 241-15

All depositaries of any part of authority, by delegation or correspondence between them, who concert measures contrary to the Constitution and the laws, are punished by twenty years' imprisonment. In addition, deprivation of civil rights and of any public employment may be pronounced for ten years.

Article 241-16

If by one of the above means, measures are concerted against the execution of the laws or against the orders of the Government, the penalty is five years' imprisonment and optionally a residence ban of ten years.
If this concert takes place between the civil authorities and the military or security forces or their leaders, those who are the authors or instigators thereof are punished by ten years' imprisonment and a residence ban of twenty years.
In the cases referred to in this article, deprivation of civil rights and of any public employment for twenty years is, in addition, pronounced.

Article 241-17

In the case where this concert has had as its object or result a plot threatening the internal security of the State, the guilty parties are punished by life imprisonment or the death penalty.

Article 241-18

Public officials who, with the intention of opposing the laws or governmental action, by deliberation, decide to give resignations individually or collectively the object or effect of which would be to prevent or suspend either the administration of justice or the performance of any public service, are guilty of malfeasance and punished by ten years' imprisonment.

Article 241-19

The foregoing provisions do not in any way prejudice the right to strike and the freedom to join cooperative organizations or trade union organizations of their choice for the defence of their professional interests.

Section 3: Encroachment by administrative and judicial authorities

Article 241-20

Judges, Prosecutors General or Prosecutors of the Republic or their Deputies, Judicial Police Officers who either irregularly arrest or suspend the execution of one or more laws, or prohibit the execution of regular orders emanating from the administration, are punished by a fine of 200,000 francs.

Ministers, mayors and other administrators who either irregularly arrest or suspend the execution of one or more laws, or illegally interfere in the cognizance of private rights and interests within the jurisdiction of the courts, are punished by the same penalties.

Section 4: Illegally prolonged exercise of public authority

Article 241-21

Any public official revoked, dismissed, suspended or legally prohibited who, after having had official knowledge thereof, continues the exercise of his functions or who, vested with elective or temporary functions, exercises them after having been replaced or when his functions have ended, is punished by two years' imprisonment and a fine of 500,000 francs. He is prohibited from exercising any function or public employment for ten years.

Section 5: Refusal of a service legally due and denial of justice

Article 241-22

Any commander of internal security forces, any administrator or administrative authority legally served with a requisition from the civil authority, who refuses his services or refrains from causing the forces under his orders to act, is punished by dismissal and five years' imprisonment.

Article 241-23

Any judge who, under any pretext whatsoever, even the silence or obscurity of the law, denies rendering the justice he owes to the parties, after having been requested to do so, and who persists in his denial, after warning or injunction from his hierarchical superiors, is punished by a fine of 240,000 francs, and prohibition from exercising public functions for five years.

Article 241-24

Witnesses or assessors who allege an excuse recognized as inaccurate, are punished, in addition to the fines pronounced for non-appearance, by two months' imprisonment.

Chapter 2

Crimes and offences against the public interest committed by private individuals

Section 1: Crimes and offences of a racial, regionalist or religious nature

Article 242-1

Any statement, any act likely to establish or give rise to racial or ethnic discrimination, any statement, any act aimed at provoking or maintaining regionalist propaganda, any dissemination of news tending to undermine the unity of the nation or the credit of the State, any manifestation contrary to freedom of conscience and freedom of worship likely to set citizens against one another, is punished by five years' imprisonment and a residence ban of ten years.

Section 2: Crimes and offences relating to the exercise of civic rights

Article 242-2

When, by unlawful assembly, acts of violence or threats, one or more citizens are prevented from exercising their civic rights, each of the guilty parties is punished by two years' imprisonment and deprived of his civic rights for ten years.

Article 242-3

If this act is committed pursuant to a concerted plan to be executed either throughout the Republic, or in one or more administrative districts, the penalty is ten years' imprisonment and a residence ban of twenty years.

Article 242-4

Any citizen member of a polling station, any scrutineer who, during operations, falsifies or attempts to falsify, removes or attempts to remove, adds or attempts to add ballots, writes or attempts to write on the ballots of illiterate voters names other than those declared to them, misleads or attempts to mislead as to the meaning of the colours of the ballots, prevents or attempts to prevent a citizen from exercising his right to vote, is punished by two years' imprisonment and deprivation of the right to vote and to be eligible for ten years.

All other persons guilty of the acts set out above are punished by six months' imprisonment, deprivation of the right to vote and to be eligible for ten years.

Article 242-5

Anyone who, by gifts or donations in money or in kind, by promises of donations, favours, public or private employment or other advantages, influences or attempts to influence the vote of one or more electors, either directly or through an intermediary, anyone who, by the same means, determines or attempts to determine one or more electors to abstain, is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 1,000,000 francs.
May also be pronounced the forfeiture of civil rights and of all public functions or employment for ten years.

Article 242-6

Any person who knowingly publishes or disseminates false, misleading or fictitious data or information or who spreads disinformation in the written press, broadcasting or on a computer system, with the intention that the data or information will be considered or treated as authentic, with or without financial gain, commits an offence, punished by imprisonment of five years or a fine of 5,000,000 francs.
False information or fake news means news, allegations or imputations that are false, inaccurate or misleading with the aim of manipulating or deceiving the public or of a nature to weaken the State and institutions, to spread disorder, war or to incite persons to violence, to advocate hatred, ethnic, separatist or regionalist discrimination, to affect the reputation of others or to alter the sincerity of the ballot, disseminated in a deliberate, artificial or automated and massive manner in the media, on the internet and social networks.

Section 3

Serious disturbances to public order Paragraph 1: Unlawful demonstrations

Article 242-7

A demonstration is lawful when the organisers have made a declaration thereof to the competent administrative authority under the conditions provided for by the laws and regulations in force relating to freedom of assembly and demonstration on the public highway.

Article 242-8: For the purposes of this Code, a demonstration is unlawful when

1°) the organisers have not taken the precaution of making a declaration thereof to the competent administrative authority under the conditions provided for by law;

2°) the demonstration has been prohibited by the competent administrative authority after the declaration has been made to it;

3°) the organisers have made an incomplete or inaccurate declaration of a nature to mislead as to the object or conditions of the planned demonstration.

Article 242-9

The organisers of an unlawful demonstration as defined in Article 242-7 above are punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 1,000,000 francs.

The penalty is imprisonment of one year and a fine of 600,000 francs for persons who have participated therein voluntarily.

Article 242-10

When as a result of an unlawful demonstration or one prohibited by the administrative police authority, acts of vandalism have been committed:

1°) the organisers of that demonstration who have not given the order to disperse as soon as they become aware of such violence or assault, destruction or damage, are punished by imprisonment of four years and a fine of 3,000,000 francs;

2°) persons who continue to participate actively in that demonstration, after the commencement and with knowledge of the violence, assault, destruction or damage, are punished by imprisonment of three years and a fine of 1,000,000 francs;

3°) are punished by imprisonment of five years persons authorised or not to demonstrate on the public highway and who have voluntarily destroyed all or part thereof.

Article 242-11

Persons found guilty of one of the offences referred to in this paragraph are held jointly and severally liable for civil reparations [Law No. 06-029 of 29 June 2006, as amended, relating to the protection of the public highway].

Paragraph 2: Unlawful assemblies

Article 242-12

The formation of armed unlawful assemblies on the public highway as well as unarmed unlawful assemblies which are of a nature to disturb public tranquillity are prohibited.

An unlawful assembly is deemed to be armed when several of the individuals composing it are carrying visible or concealed weapons or when a single one of these individuals, carrying visible weapons, is not immediately expelled from the unlawful assembly by those very persons who form part of it.

Article 242-13

All persons who form unlawful assemblies in public places or on the public highway are required to disperse at the summons of the authorities responsible for maintaining order.

If the unlawful assembly does not disperse, the summons is repeated twice.

If the three summonses have remained without effect or even in the case where after a first summons or a second, it is not possible to make the second or the third, force may be used.

Article 242-14: The public force may be used without summons

1°) if there is imminence of serious or fatal injuries or if violence or assault is exercised against the forces of order and/or against a third person;

2°) if the forces of order cannot otherwise defend with less violent means the ground they occupy or the persons and posts in their charge.

Article 242-15

Anyone who, having formed part of an armed unlawful assembly which disperses at the customary summonses, is punished by imprisonment of one year and a fine of 600,000 francs. If the unlawful assembly is formed at night, the penalty of imprisonment is three years and the fine 1,000,000 francs.

Nevertheless, no penalty for unlawful assembly is pronounced against those who, having formed part thereof without being personally armed, withdraw at the first summons.

Article 242-16

Is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 2,000,000 francs anyone who forms part of an armed unlawful assembly which disperses only after use of the public force.

The penalty of imprisonment is ten years and the fine 4,000,000 francs, if the armed unlawful assembly has been dispersed only after the public force has made use of its weapons.

Article 242-17

The aggravation of penalties provided for in Article 242-15 above is applicable to unarmed individuals forming part of an armed unlawful assembly, in the case of concealed weapons, only when they have had knowledge of the presence in the unlawful assembly of several persons carrying those weapons.
Those who have not had such knowledge incur the penalties provided for in Article 242-15 above.

Article 242-18

In the cases provided for in Article 242-15 above, the court seised may also pronounce the prohibition from exercising civil rights for a period which may not exceed five years.

Article 242-19

Is punished as the crime or the offence according to the distinctions established in the preceding articles, any provocation followed by effect, to an armed or unarmed unlawful assembly by speeches publicly uttered or by writings or printed matter posted or distributed.

Printers, engravers, lithographers, bill-posters and distributors are punished as accomplices, when they act knowingly.

Provocation not followed by effect is punished by imprisonment of six months.

Article 242-20

Prosecutions brought for crimes and offences of unlawful assembly do not preclude the prosecution of crimes and offences which are committed during those unlawful assemblies.

Article 242-21

In all cases, the confiscation of weapons and ammunition must be pronounced.

Paragraph 3: Acts of vandalism

Article 242-22

An act of vandalism is the act of damaging, destroying, degrading or deteriorating intentionally property belonging to another during demonstrations on public roads.

Vandalism is punishable by three years' imprisonment and a fine of 2,000,000 francs.

Article 242-23

The offence of act of vandalism applies: 1°) to acts of vandalism as provided for in the following article;
2°) to the organizers of unlawful demonstrations on public roads, during which acts of vandalism have been committed;

3°) to the perpetrators and accomplices of acts of vandalism;

4°) to persons who enter a demonstration, even a lawful one, with the intent to commit or cause other participants to commit acts of vandalism.

Article 242-24

The following acts committed during demonstrations on public roads constitute acts of vandalism: destruction or damage caused to property, movable or immovable, private or public, destruction of registers, minutes or acts of public authority.

Paragraph 4: Obstruction of public roads

Article 242-25

Obstruction of public roads by unlawful assembly, barricades, lighting or maintaining fire with the intent to hinder or prevent the free movement of persons or to sow panic among the population, is punishable by three years' imprisonment and a fine of 200,000 francs.

If the acts provided for in the preceding paragraph are committed by armed assembly, in a gathering or in a gang and by open force, the penalty of imprisonment may be increased to five years and the fine to 400,000 francs.

Paragraph 5

Violation of general building and urban planning regulations

Article 242-26

The project owner for the preparation and execution of a construction requiring authorization must appoint a project manager and a contractor. The project owner must provide to the authorities responsible for building control, the necessary documents in accordance with the regulations in force.

For constructions, installations and equipment not requiring authorization, the appointment of a project manager is not mandatory. For works carried out oneself or with the help of third parties, the appointment of a contractor is not necessary, if technicians provide their assistance. Demolition works requiring authorization must be carried out by a specialist.

If the project owner appoints persons who, for their tasks, do not have the expertise and experience necessary to accomplish these tasks, the authority responsible for building control and regulation may require that these persons be replaced by qualified persons. The authority responsible for building control and regulation may stop the works until there are the required skills for the execution of the works.

The authority responsible for building control may require for specific works that a reputable contractor be appointed. If the project ownership changes holder, the new project owner must immediately inform the authority responsible for building control in writing of this change.

The project owner bears the following costs: 1°) the taking of samples and their testing;
2°) the services of experts or expert offices; 3°) the surveying by a surveyor - expert.

Article 242-27

Anyone wishing to undertake or establish a construction for residential use or not, must, beforehand, obtain a building permit.

This obligation applies to public services of the State, regions, circles and communes as well as to private persons.

The building permit is required for works to be carried out on existing constructions, when they have the effect of changing their purpose, modifying their external appearance or their volume or creating additional levels.

Article 242-28

[Law No. 2017-038 of 14 July 2017 amending Law No. 01-077 of 18 July 2001 establishing the general rules of construction]:

Shall be punished by imprisonment of six months and a fine of 500,000 francs, anyone who:

1°) erects a construction despite a work stoppage order;

2°) wilfully refuses to present or deposit the required documents at the construction site;

3°) violates the provisions concerning the servicing of land, the rules of location and ground coverage and the height rules;

4°) whether architect, engineer, contractor or property developer, participates in the execution of unauthorized works;

5°) erases or removes markings made on constructions by agents of the technical services responsible for control;

6°) narrows the accesses as well as the vehicular areas by constructions, does not keep them continuously clear or parks equipment there, in violation of the general rules of construction;

7°) in violation of the specific rules applicable to the construction and operation of assembly establishments, established by decree taken in the Council of Ministers:

a)	parks or deposits objects on the emergency exits or on the movement areas for firefighting equipment;

b)	does not keep the emergency exits clear or does not light them during darkness at the time of operation of the assembly establishment;

c)	closes the doors or keeps them fixed;

d)	keeps on the stage, extensions or other performance areas, decorations, furniture, accessories, costumes and similar objects;

e)	uses on the stage decorations and equipment made of flammable materials;

f)	uses on the forestages and platforms other than non-combustible decorations and scenic elements or fixes on the fixing elements furniture and lamps made of flammable materials;

g)	is not present or represented during the operation of the establishment;

h)	authorizes the operation of stages or platforms without the technical staff being present;

i)	authorizes the operation of an installation without fire safety monitoring being ensured;

j)	does not take into account the remarks and suggestions of the fire safety monitoring;

k)	changes the order established in the seating plan or occupies seats not provided for in the plan;

l)	keeps in the assembly hall more film reels than authorized;

8°) in violation of the specific rules applicable to the construction and operation of high-rise buildings, established by decree taken in the Council of Ministers:

a)	does not keep the emergency exits clear;

b)	fixes the doors in the open state without smoke-reactive devices;

c)	does not keep in service permanently the safety lighting;

9°) in violation of the specific rules applicable to the construction and operation of educational establishments, established by decree taken in the Council of Ministers:

a)	keeps in the rescue and evacuation routes combustible products and materials such as books, papers and school equipment;

b)	does not keep in specially appropriate places waste, such as packaging material, old papers, etc...;

c)	fixes the fire doors;

d)	does not display on the ground floor in a clearly visible place the plans containing all safety information;

e)	does not keep clear the spaces intended for firefighters;

10°) in violation of the specific rules applicable to the construction and operation of catering and accommodation establishments, established by decree taken in the Council of Ministers:

a)	parks vehicles or deposits objects on the emergency exits outside the building as well as the parking areas and movement areas for firefighters;

b)	does not keep clear or does not light the emergency exits inside the building in case of darkness during the operating time of the establishment;

c)	fixes the smoke-tight and automatically closing doors as well as the doors of fire resistance class P 30 and P 90, as well as those equipped with smoke-reactive fixing devices;

d)	does not keep in service during the presence of customers, the safety lighting in catering establishments and permanently in accommodation establishments;

e)	uses materials not having the properties defined for decorations;

f)	does not remove flammable waste from dining rooms;

g)	does not place in the indicated location the identification sign for emergency exits in accommodation establishments;

11°) In violation of the specific rules applicable to the construction and operation of commercial establishments, established by decree taken in the Council of Ministers:

a)	parks equipment or deposits, stores or suspends objects on the emergency exits as well as the movement areas for firefighting equipment;

b)	closes the doors leading to the emergency exits so that it is not easy to open them;

c)	closes the openings of doors, gates or passages during operating hours;

d)	does not light the emergency exits and signs;

e)	deposits articles and sales stands on the stairs or stair landings;

f)	uses inside sales rooms, display cases or exhibition rooms decoration materials that are not difficult to ignite, or places in the main corridors or in the main stairwells decorations.

Article 242-29

[Law No. 01-077 of 18 July 2001 establishing the general rules of construction]:

Is punished by imprisonment of one year and a fine of 1,000,000 francs, anyone who:

1°) uses non-regulated construction materials, products or components, in violation of the rules for the use of construction materials, products and components;

2°) uses construction techniques provided for by law without the required authorisations and approvals;

3°) does not engage a company for the execution of a construction requiring a company, in violation of paragraph 1 of Article 242-26 above;

4°) carries out himself or with the help of acquaintances demolition works requiring authorisation in violation of paragraph 2 of Article 242-26 above;
In case of recidivism, the maximum penalty is applied;

5°) undertakes or establishes a construction without a building permit in violation of Article 242-27 above.

In the event that the construction does not conform to the vocation of the land, in addition to the penalties cited above, the construction is entirely demolished;

6°) in violation of the specific rules applicable to the construction and operation of assembly establishments, established by decree taken in the Council of Ministers:

a)	smokes, uses open fire or keeps flammable liquids in the specified places;

b)	smokes, uses open fire, in particular matches and lighters or uses cooking appliances in the projection booth for Celluloid films;

c)	does not suspend the operation of the assembly establishment in case of failure of an installation, equipment or device necessary for safety;

7°) in violation of the specific rules applicable to the construction and operation of hospital establishments, established by decree taken in the Council of Ministers:

a)	does not carry out inspections or does not carry them out on time;

b)	does not repair the defects noted by experts during inspections;

8°) in violation of the specific rules applicable to the construction and operation of educational establishments, established by decree taken in the Council of Ministers:

a)	does not carry out inspections or does not carry them out on time;

b)	does not repair the defects noted by experts during inspections;

c)	uses open fire in inappropriate rooms and without permanent supervision;

d)	does not maintain and regularly inspect technical installations and equipment;

e)	does not keep fire extinguishers ready in rooms with a high risk of fire.

9°) in violation of the specific rules applicable to the construction and operation of catering and accommodation establishments, established by decree taken in the Council of Ministers:

a)	does not have the prescribed inspections carried out or does not have them carried out on time;

b)	does not repair the defects noted;

10°) in violation of the specific rules applicable to the construction and operation of commercial establishments, established by decree taken in the Council of Ministers:

c)	smokes or uses an open fire;

d)	uses electric radiant cookers;

e)	carries out welding work or similar fire work without supervision of the fire safety service.

Article 242-30

Is punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 2,000,000 francs, any person who, despite sufficient knowledge, makes false declarations or submits false documents to obtain or prevent the execution of an administrative act issued in accordance with this paragraph.

In case of recidivism, the maximum penalty is always applied.

The Court may, in addition, order the total or partial demolition of the constructions.

Article 242-31

[Law No. 02-016 of 03 June 2002, establishing the general rules of urban planning]:

Any person who carries out or undertakes, has carried out or has undertaken constructions or installations in a subdivided zone without prior authorisation from the administration or in violation of the legislative and regulatory provisions in force, is punished by a fine of 2,000,000 francs.

This fine is calculated according to the state of progress of the works.

Architects, contractors or any other person having contributed to the execution of said constructions or installations, are punished by the same penalties.

Article 242-32

[Law No. 02-016 of 03 June 2002, establishing the general rules of urban planning]:

Any person who divides a property for family use in a subdivided zone without prior authorisation from the administration, is punished by a fine of 50,000 francs per parcel resulting from the division.

Any person who divides a property other than family property in a subdivided zone without prior authorisation from the Administration is punished by a fine of 1,000,000 francs per parcel resulting from the division;

Article 242-33

[Law No. 02-016 of 03 June 2002, establishing the general rules of Urban Planning]:

Without prejudice to the sanctions provided for by other legislative and regulatory texts, any person who subdivides a property in a subdivided zone without having obtained prior administrative authorisation, is punished by imprisonment of six months and a fine of 2,000,000 francs per parcel resulting from the subdivision.

The Court may, upon request of the Administration, order for all the offences referred to in this chapter, the demolition of constructions erected in violation of the applicable provisions and the restoration of the premises at the expense of the offender.

Section 4

Acts of obstruction to Justice Paragraph 1: Obstruction of access to Justice

Article 242-34

Any threat or any other act of intimidation towards anyone, committed with a view to inducing the victim of a crime or an offence not to file a complaint or to retract, is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 1,000,000 francs.

Article 242-35

The act for anyone having knowledge of a crime of which it is still possible to prevent or limit the effects, or whose perpetrators are likely to commit new crimes which could be prevented, of not informing the judicial or administrative authorities is punished by the penalties provided for in the preceding article.

Are exempted from the preceding provisions, except with regard to terrorist acts and crimes committed against minors:

1°) relatives in direct line and their spouses, as well as brothers and sisters and their spouses, of the perpetrator or accomplice of the crime;

2°) the spouse of the perpetrator or accomplice of the crime, or the person who notoriously lives in a marital situation with him.
Also exempted from the provisions of the first paragraph are persons bound to secrecy under the conditions provided for by Article 322-2 of this Code.

Paragraph 2: Obstruction of the proper functioning of justice

Article 242-36

Is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 1,000,000 francs anyone who:
1°) resorts to physical force, threats or intimidation or promises to offer or grant an undue advantage to obtain false testimony or prevent testimony or the presentation of evidence in a proceeding;
2°) resorts to physical force, threats or intimidation to prevent an officer of justice or an officer of the detection and law enforcement services from exercising the duties of his office.

Article 242-37

Is punished by the same penalties the act, with a view to obstructing the manifestation of truth:
1°) of modifying the state of the scene of a crime or offence either by altering, falsifying or erasing traces or evidence, or by adding, moving or removing any objects;

2°) of destroying, removing, concealing or altering a public or private document or an object likely to facilitate the discovery of a crime or offence, the search for evidence or the conviction of the guilty.

When the acts provided for in this article are committed by a person who, by virtue of their functions, is called upon to contribute to the manifestation of truth, the penalty is increased to seven years' imprisonment and a fine of 2,000,000 francs.

Paragraphe 3 : Du faux témoignage-du refus de témoigner et de la subornation de témoin

Article 242-38

Whoever, in any manner whatsoever, is guilty of perjury without retracting before the close of proceedings, is punished by five years' imprisonment and a fine of 300,000 francs. If the perjury is committed in criminal matters, the penalty is twenty years' imprisonment, a fine of 1,000,000 francs and a residence ban of twenty years.

The person guilty of subornation of a witness is liable to the same penalties as the perpetrator of perjury.

Article 242-39

The refusal to answer questions by a witness or by an expert, either during the investigation or at the hearing, is punished by two years' imprisonment and a fine of 500,000 francs.

Section 5 : De l'opposition à l'autorité légitime

Article 242-40

Are punished by three months' imprisonment and a fine of 120,000 francs:

1°) those who oppose by acts, words, gestures, or any manoeuvres whatsoever the exercise of the lawful authority of an agent holding public authority or of any citizen charged with a ministry of public service and, thereby, undermine or attempt to undermine public order or obstruct or attempt to obstruct the proper functioning of administrative or judicial services, as well as any incitement to such opposition;

2°) those who, without lawful excuse, have not responded to lawful summonses from administrative or judicial authorities;

3°) those who without lawful excuse, refuse to respond to requests for information from the National Financial Intelligence Processing Unit in the context of the analysis, enrichment and exploitation of any information likely to establish the origin or destination of sums or the nature of transactions that have been the subject of declarations or information received;

4°) those who, by wilful abstention undermine or attempt to undermine public order or obstruct or attempt to obstruct the proper functioning of administrative or judicial services.

Wilful abstention, under the terms of this article, must reveal in the person who is its author a will of characterized indiscipline.

When the offence defined above is committed by several persons acting in concert, the penalties provided for may be doubled.

Article 242-41

In case of recidivism, a term of imprisonment is mandatorily imposed and the Judges may, in addition, pronounce a residence ban for a period of five years.

There is recidivism when a first judgment for an identical offence has been rendered against the offender in the preceding twelve months.

Section 6 : Des atteintes à la liberté de travail

Article 242-42

Is punished by three months' imprisonment and a fine of 200,000 francs, whoever, by means of violence, assault, threats or fraudulent manoeuvres, either undermines freedom of hiring and work, or provokes or maintains an individual or collective cessation of work, or disrupts the normal course of school or university work.

The attempt is punished as the offence itself.

Article 242-43

The same penalty is applied to whoever, abusing their functions or authority, compels an individual to work for their account or for the account of another.

Section 7

De la résistance, de la désobéissance et autres manquements envers l'autorité publique

Paragraphe 1 : De la rébellion

Article 242-44

Any attack, any resistance with violence, assault or threats against public Officers or ministerial officers, officials, agents or employees of public authority, acting for the execution of laws, regulations or orders of public authority, is qualified as rebellion.

If the rebellion is committed by more than two persons equipped with weapons, instruments or projectiles visible or concealed, the offenders are punished by twenty years' imprisonment, and a residence ban of twenty years; if it takes place without weapons, the penalty is five years' imprisonment. The penalty of a residence ban of five years may, in addition, be pronounced.

If the rebellion is committed by fewer than three persons, equipped with weapons, instruments or projectiles visible or concealed, it is punished by two years' imprisonment, and if it takes place without weapons, by six months' imprisonment.

Article 242-45

In case of rebellion with a gang or gathering, Article 232-9 of this Code is applicable to rebels without function or employment in the gang, who withdrew at the first warning from public authority, or even since, if they were seized only outside the place of rebellion, and without new resistance and without weapons.

Article 242-46

Any gathering of individuals for the commission of a crime or offence is deemed an armed gathering when more than two persons carry visible weapons.

Article 242-47

Persons who are found equipped with concealed weapons and who are part of a troop or gathering not deemed armed are individually punished as if they were part of an armed troop or gathering.

Article 242-48

The perpetrators of crimes and offences committed during or on the occasion of a rebellion, are punished by the penalties pronounced for each of these crimes, if they are more severe than those for rebellion.

Article 242-49

In all cases where a simple penalty of imprisonment is pronounced for an act of rebellion, the offenders may be sentenced, in addition, to a fine of 240,000 francs.

Article 242-50

Are considered and punished as gatherings of rebellion, those which have been formed, with or without weapons and accompanied by violence or threats against the administrative authority, the public force or the agents who represent them:

1°) by persons working in workshops or factories;

2°) by individuals admitted to State hospital establishments;

3°) by detainees.

Article 242-51

The penalty applied for rebellion by detainees is served under the following conditions:

For those who are sentenced to a penalty other than the death penalty or life imprisonment, immediately after the expiration of their sentence.

And for the others, immediately after the final judgment or decision or the act that ends their detention.

Article 242-52

The leaders of a rebellion and those who provoked it may be sentenced to ten years' residence ban.

Paragraphe 2

De la destruction et de la profanation du drapeau national et des autres attributs de l'Etat

Article 242-53

Any person who, in order to express their discontent or opinion, publicly soils, tramples, tears or burns the national flag is punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 400,000 francs.

Any person who, for the same reasons and in the same circumstances, desecrates the national flag is punished by the same penalties.

Any use and any desecration of other attributes of the State are punished by the penalties provided for in the first paragraph.

Paragraphe 3

Insults and violence against holders of authority or public force

Article 242-54

Anyone who, either by speeches, cries or threats uttered in public meetings or places, or by writings, printed matter, sold or distributed, offered for sale or displayed in public meetings or places, insults the person of the Head of State is punished by imprisonment of one year and a fine of 600,000 francs.

The same provisions are applicable with regard to foreign Heads of State visiting Mali.

When one or more magistrates of the administrative or judicial order, when one or more assessors receive in the exercise of their functions or on the occasion of that exercise, any insult by word, writing or drawing not made public tending in these various cases to harm their honour or their integrity, the person who addressed that insult to them is punished by imprisonment of one year.

If the insult by word takes place at the hearing or within the precincts of a court or tribunal, the imprisonment is two years.

The insult made by gesture or by threat or by sending any objects with the same intention and targeting a magistrate or an assessor in the exercise of their functions, is punished by six months' imprisonment; if the insult takes place at the hearing of a court or tribunal, it is punished by imprisonment of two years.

Article 242-55

The insult made by words, gestures or threats, writings or drawings not made public or by sending any objects with the same intention and targeting any ministerial officer or agent holding public force, in the exercise of their functions is punished by imprisonment of three months and a fine of 240,000 francs.

Article 242-56

The insult mentioned in the preceding article, when it is directed against a commander of the public force, is punished by imprisonment of three months and a fine of 600,000 francs.

Article 242-57

Any individual who, without a weapon and without resulting injury, engages in violence or assault against a magistrate in the exercise of their functions or on the occasion of that exercise, or commits any other violence or assault against them in the same circumstances, is punished by imprisonment of five years. The maximum of this penalty is always pronounced if the assault or violence takes place at the hearing or within the precincts of a court or tribunal.

In either of the cases referred to, the offender may in addition be sentenced to stay away for ten years from the place where the magistrate sits and within a radius of fifty kilometres. This provision is enforceable from the date of the day when the convicted person serves their sentence. If the convicted person violates this order before the expiry of the fixed time, they are punished by imprisonment of three months and a residence ban of five years.

Article 242-58

The violence or assault provided for in Article 242-57 above directed against a ministerial officer, an agent of the public force, compliance agents and officials of the National Financial Information Processing Unit in the context of the fight against money laundering, terrorist financing and the proliferation of weapons of mass destruction, if they took place in the exercise of their functions are punished by imprisonment of three years and a fine of 600,000 francs.

Article 242-59

If the violence and assault exercised against the officials and agents designated in Articles 242-57 and 242-58 above cause an incapacity for work exceeding twenty days, the penalty is ten years' imprisonment. If death ensues, the offender is punished by life imprisonment.

In the case where such violence and assault have not caused bloodshed, injury or illness, the offenders are punished by imprisonment of twenty years if the blows are struck with premeditation and ambush.

Article 242-60

If the blows are struck, or the injuries inflicted, on officials or agents designated in Articles 242-57 and 242-58 above in the exercise or on the occasion of the exercise of their functions with intent to kill, the offender is punished by the death penalty.

Paragraphe 4 : Mutiny

Article 242-61

Any operation launched by a group of military personnel, prisoners, crew personnel of a vehicle, aircraft or military vessel following a revolt or disputes manifesting themselves by a descent into premises, offices, establishments, vehicles, aircraft or vessels with a view to capturing the leaders, the authorities on whom they depend in order to exercise acts of violence or assault, torture, summary executions while seizing key points of the city, military garrisons, gendarmerie, post offices and stations, whatever its reduced duration or not, its limited scope or not and which may be used for political purposes or to free themselves from subjugation, oppression or for any other cause, is punished by imprisonment of five years.

Incitement to mutiny is punished by the same penalty.

If it is followed by a panic movement of soldiers facing a possible reaction of reprisals from Officers or leaders facing nervous and disorderly soldiers, the penalty is imprisonment of seven years.

Article 242-62

Detainees or persons interned in an establishment by decision of the authority who have rioted with the intention of attacking, by common accord, an official of the establishment or any other person responsible for supervising them, of compelling, by violence or the threat of violence, an official of the establishment or any other person responsible for supervising them to do an act or to refrain from doing so or to escape by using violence, are punished by imprisonment of three years.

Those among them who have committed violence against persons or property are punished by imprisonment of five years.

Section 8 : Detainee and escape of detainee

Article 242-63 : For the purposes of this Code, any person is considered a detainee

1°) who is placed in police custody;

2°) who is pending or in the process of being presented to the judicial authority at the end of police custody or in execution of a warrant to bring or arrest warrant;

3°) who has been notified of a committal warrant or an arrest warrant continuing to produce effect;

4°) who is serving a custodial sentence or who has been arrested to serve that sentence;

5°) who is placed under extradition detention;

6°) who is the subject of an order of arrest; 7°) who is placed under a community service regime; 8°) who is placed under electronic monitoring.

Article 242-64

Any detainee who escapes or attempts to escape from the place where they are detained, from a health or hospital establishment where they are transferred, or during a work detail, is punished by imprisonment of one year.

Article 242-65 : Also constitutes an escape punishable by the same penalties the act by

1°) a detainee placed in a health or hospital establishment, of evading the surveillance to which he is subject;

2°) any convicted person, of evading the control to which he is subject when he has been the subject of a decision of placement outside a penitentiary establishment or when he benefits either from the semi-liberty regime, or from a leave permit;

3°) any convicted person, of failing to return to the penitentiary establishment at the end of a measure of suspension or splitting of imprisonment, of placement outside, of execution of community service work; of semi-liberty or of leave permit.

Article 242-66

Any person assigned to the custody or escort of a detainee, guilty of having let him escape through negligence, is punished:

a)	if the escapees or one of them were charged or convicted for a crime, by three years' imprisonment;

b)	if the escapees or one of them were charged for an offence, by one year's imprisonment.

Those who, without being charged with the custody or escort of the detainee, procure, facilitate or attempt to procure or facilitate his escape, are punished as follows:

c)	if the detainee who escaped is in the case provided for by paragraph a) of this article, by two years' imprisonment;

d)	if the detainee who escaped is in the case provided for by paragraph b) of this article, by six months' imprisonment.

Article 242-67

Detainees who escape or who attempt to escape by prison breaking or by violence are, by that fact alone, punished by imprisonment of two years. They shall serve this sentence immediately after the expiry of that which they incur for the crime or offence by reason of which they were detained, or immediately after the judgment or decision which acquitted or discharged them, absolved them of the said crime or offence, all without prejudice to the convictions they may have incurred for offences committed on the occasion of such violence.

Article 242-68

The penalties referred to in Article 242-66 above cease when the escapees are recaptured.

Section 9 : Des bris de scellés et de la dégradation de monuments

Article 242-69

Whoever intentionally breaks or removes seals, notices, by means of which the administrative or judicial authorities have prohibited access to premises or the removal of objects, is punished by imprisonment of three years.

If it is the custodian of the seals who breaks them, he is punished by imprisonment of five years. If he is convicted of simple negligence, the penalty is imprisonment of six months.

In the cases provided for in the first two paragraphs of this article, a fine of 200,000 francs is imposed on the offender.

Article 242-70

Any theft committed by means of breaking seals is considered as theft committed by means of breaking and entering.

Article 242-71

For the removal, destruction, taking away of documents of criminal proceedings or other papers, registers, acts or effects contained in archives, registry offices or public depositories or entrusted to a public depositary in that capacity, the penalties are against negligent court clerks, notaries and other depositaries, imprisonment of one year and a fine of 500,000 francs.

Whoever has voluntarily destroyed, demolished, mutilated, or damaged monuments, statues and other immovable property intended for public utility or decoration, is punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 400,000 FCFA.

Article 242-72

Whoever is guilty of removal, misappropriation, taking away, alteration or destruction mentioned in the preceding article, is punished by imprisonment of ten years and a residence ban of five years.

If the offence is the work of a depositary himself, he is punished by imprisonment of twenty years and residence ban of twenty years.

If the removal, misappropriation, taking away, alteration, destruction referred to in the first paragraph of this article are committed with violence against persons or property, the penalty is, against any person, imprisonment of twenty years and residence ban of twenty years.

Section 10 : De l'usurpation de titre ou de fonction

Article 242-73

Whoever, without title interferes in public civil or military functions, or performs an act of one of these functions, is punished by imprisonment of five years, without prejudice to other convictions incurred on the occasion of the offence.

Any person who publicly wears a costume, uniform or decoration to which he is not entitled, is punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 50,000 francs.

Is punished by the same penalties whoever, without fulfilling the required conditions, uses or claims a title attached to a legally regulated profession, an official diploma or a quality whose conditions of attribution have been fixed by the public authority.

Is punished by a fine of 600,000 francs whoever, without right, and with a view to attributing to himself an honorary distinction, publicly adorns himself with a title, or changes, alters or modifies the name assigned to him by civil status records.

In all cases provided for in this article, the Court may order the full or partial publication of the judgment in the newspapers it designates, at the expense of the convicted person.

Section 11

Des atteintes au crédit de l'Etat et du refus de payer les impositions, contributions et taxes assimilées

Paragraphe 1 : Des atteintes au crédit de l'Etat

Article 242-74

Are punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 240,000 francs:

1°) those who, by any ways and means whatsoever, knowingly spread among the public false news or mendacious allegations likely to shake directly or indirectly its confidence in the credit of the State, territorial collectivities, public establishments, all organizations in which these collectivities and public establishments have a participation;

2°) those who, by any ways and means whatsoever, incite the public to withdraw funds from public funds or from establishments obliged by law to make their payments into the funds;

3°) those who, by the same means and with the aim of provoking panic, incite the public to sell annuity securities or other public securities, or dissuade them from purchasing or subscribing to them, whether or not these incitements are followed by effect.

In all cases, the judgment is published in two newspapers designated by the Court and at the expense of the convicted person.

Paragraphe 2 : Du refus de payer les impositions, contributions et taxes assimilées

Article 242-75

Are punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 2,400,000 francs those who, by violence, acts of force, threats or concerted maneuvers, organize or attempt to organize the collective refusal to pay taxes, contributions and similar charges.

Article 242-76

Are punished by imprisonment of six months and a fine of 400,000 francs those who collectively refuse payment of taxes, contributions and similar charges.

Article 242-77

Individual refusal to pay taxes, contributions and similar charges, if not justified by a certificate of relief or discharge, is punished by imprisonment of six months and a fine of 120,000 francs.

Article 242-78

In case of recidivism within five years, the penalties provided for in Articles 242-75, 242-76, and 242-77 above are doubled.

Article 242-79

In the cases provided for in Articles 242-75, 242-76 and 242-77 above, proceedings may only be initiated by the Public Prosecutor upon complaint of the minister responsible for Finance or, where applicable, at the request of the legal representatives of the organizations concerned.

Article 242-80

In the case provided for in Article 242-79 above, proceedings may be initiated upon complaint by the officer responsible for collection.

However, and except where expressly provided by the finance law, no criminal proceedings may take place before the expiration of a period of three months after the date on which the tax rolls are made enforceable.

The provisions of Articles 242-77, 242-78 and 242-79 above do not preclude the procedure of fiscal seizure and sale pursued normally by the Treasury against recalcitrant taxpayers.

In all cases, payment of taxes, contributions and similar taxes stops the proceedings or the execution of the sentence.

Section 12

On the obligation for citizens to provide their assistance in the event of public calamity

Article 242-81

In the event of fire, bush fires, cyclone, earthquake, invasion of locusts, grasshoppers, millet-eaters, or other harmful animals and generally in the event of calamities or public threat, endangering the life and property of all or a fraction of the citizens, any person present at the scene, called for help or requisitioned by the administrative authorities, is required to provide assistance to the public authorities to combat this scourge.

Those who, without valid reason, refuse or neglect to provide the assistance to which they are required, are punished by imprisonment of three months and a fine of 300,000 francs. In the event of a repeat offence, the prison sentence is mandatory and the fine is doubled.

Those who, without valid reason, refuse to respond to the requisition of which they are the subject or, responding to it, refuse without valid reason or neglect to do the work or service required, are punished by imprisonment of six months and a fine of 500,000 francs. In the event of a repeat offence, the prison sentence is mandatory and the fine is doubled; in addition, deprivation of all or part of civil rights is imposed for a period of three years.

Section 13: Crimes and offences against public peace

Paragraphe 1: Criminal association - harbouring of criminals

Article 242-82

Any association formed, whatever its duration and the number of its members, any agreement, for the purpose of preparing or committing an attack against persons or property, constitutes a crime against public peace.

Whoever, with knowledge, joins an association formed or participates in an agreement established for the purpose specified in the paragraph above, is punished by imprisonment of ten years and a residence ban of ten years.

Those who are guilty of the crime mentioned in this article are exempted from punishment if, before any proceedings against a named person, they reveal to the constituted authorities the agreement established or the existence of the association.

Article 242-83

Is punished by imprisonment of ten years, whoever knowingly and voluntarily favours the perpetrators of the crimes provided for in Article 242-82 above by providing them with instruments of the crime, means of correspondence, shelter, accommodation or meeting place.

The guilty party may, in addition, be subject to the residence ban provided for in the preceding article.
However, the provisions contained in the last paragraph of Article 242-82 above are applicable to the guilty party of the acts provided for by this article.

Article 242-84

Those who, knowing the criminal conduct of criminals engaging in brigandage or violence against the security of the State, public peace, persons, provide them with accommodation, shelter, place of retreat or meeting, are punished as accomplices.

Those who, outside the cases provided for above, knowingly harbour a criminal or an individual sought by justice or who remove or attempt to remove the criminal from arrest or searches, or help him to hide or take flight, are punished by imprisonment of three years and a fine of 500,000 francs, all without prejudice to other convictions.

The persons referred to in Article 121-6 in fine of this Code are exempted from the foregoing provisions.

Paragraphe 2

Recruitment and use of children by armed forces and groups

Article 242-85

Recruitment and use of children means the conscription or compulsory, forced or voluntary incorporation of children into national armed forces of a State or an armed group of any nature whatsoever, or the act of using them, whatever the function exercised therein. This may include, in particular but not exclusively, children, girls or boys, used as combatants, cooks, porters, messengers, spies or for sexual purposes.

The term does not only designate a child who participates or has participated directly in hostilities.

Is punished by the penalties provided for in Article 242-83 above, the act of recruiting, in a forced or voluntary manner, children under eighteen years of age into security forces or armed groups, or using them in security forces or armed groups, whatever the function they exercise therein.

Paragraphe 3: Violence in stadiums or on the occasion of shows

Article 242-86

Are punished by imprisonment of six months and a fine of 500,000 francs those who bring into a sports enclosure or into a performance hall, weapons, flares, fireworks, or any other object likely to constitute a weapon.

Article 242-87

Are punished by imprisonment of six months and a fine of 200,000 francs:

1°) those who bring alcoholic beverages into a sports enclosure or into a performance hall;

2°) those who, on the occasion of a sports competition or a cultural performance, incite spectators to hatred or violence, towards the referee, a sports judge, a player, an artist or any other person or group of persons;

3°) those who bring, carry or display in a sports enclosure or in a performance hall, insignia, signs or symbols of a racist or xenophobic nature;

4°) those who deliberately disturb the course of the competition or cultural performance, or harm the safety of persons or property;

5°) those who throw projectiles or any other object manifestly presenting a danger to the safety of persons and property.

Paragraphe 4: Vagrancy

Article 242-88

Work is a duty for every Malian and for every person residing on the national territory.

Vagrants or persons of no fixed abode are those who have neither a fixed domicile, nor means of subsistence and who do not habitually exercise any trade or profession.

Article 242-89

Vagrants or persons of no fixed abode who are legally declared as such are, for this fact alone, punished by imprisonment of six months. They may in addition, in the event of a repeat offence, be subject to a residence ban for five years.

Article 242-90

Individuals not originating from the Republic of Mali declared vagrants may be conducted by order of the Government outside the Republic.

Vagrants born in Mali may, even after a judgment that has become final, be claimed by resolution of the Council of the commune or village where they were born or guaranteed by a solvent citizen.

If the Court accepts the claim or approves the guarantee, the individuals thus claimed or guaranteed are by order of the Government returned or conducted to the commune or village that claims them or to such other locality that is assigned to them as residence at the request of the guarantor.

Paragraphe 5 : Begging-Incitement to begging

Article 242-91

Any able-bodied and adult person who is found begging on the public highway is punished by imprisonment of six months.

Persons with disabilities who, during their stay in hospital or charitable institutions, are found begging in public places are punished by the same penalty.

In all circumstances, incitement to begging is prohibited.

Any person convicted of incitement to begging is liable to the penalty set out in the first paragraph above.

However, if the person incited to begging is a minor child, the offender is punished by imprisonment of one year.
Any beggar, even with a disability, who uses threats or insults or has entered without permission and against the will of the owner or occupants of the house, into a dwelling, into an enclosure dependent thereon, is punished by imprisonment of six months.

Paragraphe 6 : Common provisions relating to vagrants and beggars

Article 242-92

Any vagrant or beggar who is apprehended disguised in any manner whatsoever and equipped with instruments suitable either to commit thefts or other offences, or to provide him with the means to enter houses, is punished by imprisonment of five years.
Any vagrant or beggar who exercises or attempts to exercise any act of violence whatsoever against persons, is punished by imprisonment of five years.

Vagrants and beggars who are sentenced to the penalties set out in this article are prohibited from residing for ten years.

Paragraphe 7 : Games of chance

Article 242-93

Lotteries or all other games of chance offering the hope of a substantial gain for a relatively small stake are prohibited on the territory of the Republic, except by authorization by law which sets the conditions thereof.

Article 242-94

Those who illegally install on the public highway machines dispensing money or consumption tokens and in general all machines whose operation is based on skill or chance and which are intended to provide a gain or consumption in return for a stake are punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 500,000 francs.

Those who, unlawfully, keep a house of games of chance where the public is admitted, either freely or upon presentation of interested parties or affiliates are punished by the same penalties. In case of infringement, the owners of the premises, administrators, directors, agents or employees of the establishment are prosecuted.

The offenders may furthermore, from the day they have served their sentence, be deprived of civic and civil rights for ten years.

In all cases, all funds and effects which are found exposed to the game or to the lottery or raffle, the furniture, instruments, utensils, machines used or intended to be used for games or lotteries or raffles, the furniture and movable effects with which the premises are furnished or decorated are confiscated.

Article 242-95

Those who engage in all kinds of games of chance not authorized by law, on the territory of the Republic are punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 200,000 francs.

In case of recidivism, the penalty may be increased fivefold.

Article 242-96

Individuals domiciled or not who habitually derive their subsistence only from practicing or facilitating the exercise of illicit games are punished by imprisonment of three years and five years' prohibition from residing.

Paragraphe 8 : Simulation of an offence

Article 242-97

Anyone who reports to the public authorities an offence which he knows did not exist or who fabricates false evidence relating to a real or imaginary offence, is punished by imprisonment of three years and a fine of 100,000 francs.

Paragraphe 9 : Offences relating to prohibition from residing

Article 242-98

The convicted person who contravenes the prohibition from residing or who leaves the residence assigned to him pursuant to the provisions in force, is punished by imprisonment of five years.

Section 14 : Provocation within associations

Article 242-99

If by speeches, exhortations, invocations or prayers, in whatever language or by reading, posting, publication or distribution of any writings whatsoever, any provocation to crimes or offences is made in the assemblies of an association, the penalty is imprisonment of three years and a fine of 200,000 francs against the leaders, directors and administrators of that association and against the authors of the provocation, without prejudice, with regard to the latter, to the more severe penalties provided by law for the offences which were committed in the circumstance.

Chapitre 3 : Economic crimes and offences and offences against public property

Section 1

Offences against currency, marks, titles or other fiduciary securities issued by public authority and forgery

Sous-section 1

Counterfeiting of currency Paragraphe 1: General provisions

Article 243-1

The provisions of this section are intended to punish counterfeiting of currency and other offences against monetary signs. They apply to offences committed:

1°) on the national territory;

2°) on the territory of other member States of the West African Monetary Union - WAMU;

3°) abroad, outside the member States of WAMU, according to the distinctions and conditions provided by the legislation in force.

Article 243-2: For the purposes of this Code, the following terms mean

1°) competent authorities: bodies which, by virtue of a legal or regulatory provision, are empowered to perform or order the acts or measures provided for by the uniform law relating to counterfeiting;

2°) BCEAO or Central Bank: the Central Bank of West African States;

3°) counterfeiting: the manufacture of a monetary sign imitating a monetary sign issued by the BCEAO or any other authorized foreign issuing institution;

4°) foreigner: any person who lives in the Union without having the nationality of one of the member States of WAMU;

5°) falsification: the alteration of a monetary sign with a view to modifying its substance or weight;

6°) counterfeit currency, counterfeit banknotes or counterfeit coins: banknotes and coins which have the appearance of banknotes or coins issued by the BCEAO or any other authorized foreign issuing body or, although intended to be put into circulation, have not yet been issued by the institutions authorized for this purpose;

7°) counterfeiting:

a)	all fraudulent acts, counterfeiting and falsification of manufacture or alteration of monetary signs issued by the BCEAO or any other authorized foreign issuing institution, for this purpose, whatever the means employed to produce the result;

b)	the putting into circulation of counterfeit currency with full knowledge;

c)	the act of holding, importing, exporting, transporting, receiving or obtaining counterfeit currency, with the aim of putting it into circulation with full knowledge;

d)	the fraudulent act of manufacturing, holding, receiving or obtaining instruments, objects, computer programs or any other process intended, by their nature, for the manufacture of counterfeit currency, the alteration of currencies or the manufacture of security elements for monetary signs;

8°) FCFA: Franc of the African Financial Community;

9°) putting counterfeit currency into circulation: the issue of counterfeit currency, regardless of the number of banknotes or coins circulated;

10°) reproduction of monetary signs: creation of any tangible or intangible image which bears a resemblance to a banknote or the image of a coin, whatever the size of the image, the materials, instruments and techniques used to produce it and regardless of whether the patterns, letters and symbols appearing on the monetary sign have been modified or not;

11°) monetary signs: banknotes or coins having or having had legal tender;

12°) WAMU or Union: West African Monetary Union.

Paragraph 2: Offences and applicable penalties

Article 243-3

The counterfeiting or falsification of banknotes or coins having legal tender in the national territory of a member State of WAMU or abroad is punished by twenty years' imprisonment and a fine equal to ten times the value of said signs without being less than 20,000,000 francs.

The counterfeiting or falsification of banknotes or coins which, although intended to be put into circulation, have not yet been issued by the institutions authorized for this purpose, is punished by the same penalties.
If the offender benefits from mitigating circumstances, the penalty may not be less than five years' imprisonment and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.
Suspension of sentence may not be granted.

Article 243-4

The custodial sentence provided for in the first two paragraphs of the preceding article is accompanied by a safety period of seven years.
During the safety period, the convicted person may not benefit from the provisions relating to the method of adjustment of sentences, in particular those concerning the suspension or splitting of the sentence, external placement, leave permits, semi-liberty and conditional release.

Article 243-5

The counterfeiting or falsification of banknotes or coins having had legal tender in the national territory or abroad, is punished by five years' imprisonment and a fine equal to ten times the value of said signs, without being less than 2,000,000 francs.

Article 243-6

The manufacture of banknotes and coins carried out using authorized installations or equipment intended for this purpose, when it is carried out in violation of the conditions set by the institutions authorized to issue these monetary signs and without their agreement, is punished by the penalties provided for in Article 243-3 above.

Article 243-7

The putting into circulation, use, display, distribution, importation, exportation, transport, receipt, possession, with full knowledge, of monetary signs having legal tender that are counterfeited or falsified, is punished by seven years' imprisonment and a fine equal to ten times the value of said signs without being less than 5,000,000 francs.

When they are committed by an organized gang, the offences provided for in the preceding paragraph are punished by the penalties provided for in Article 243-3 above.

The putting into circulation, use, display, distribution, importation, exportation, transport, receipt, possession, of monetary signs having had legal tender that are counterfeited or falsified, with full knowledge, is punished by three years' imprisonment and a fine equal to ten times the value of said signs, without being less than 1,000,000 francs.

When they are committed by an organized gang, the offences provided for in paragraph 3 of this article are punished by the penalties provided for in Article 243-5 above.

Article 243-8

Those who, having received monetary signs believing them to be good and who, after having discovered their defects, knowingly keep them and refrain from handing them over to the BCEAO or to the competent authorities, are punished by a fine equal to ten times their value, without the amount of the fine being less than 250,000 francs.

Credit institutions, decentralized financial systems, authorized manual currency exchangers and postal financial services which, having received counterfeited or falsified monetary signs during transactions with their customers, have not retained them, against receipt, for the purpose of handing them over to the BCEAO or to the competent authorities, are punished by a fine equal to ten times their value, without the amount being less than 2,000,000 francs.

Article 243-9

The putting back into circulation, after having discovered the defects, of counterfeited or falsified banknotes which were believed to be good at the time of receipt, is punished by five years' imprisonment and a fine equal to ten times the value of said signs without being less than 500,000 francs.

Article 243-10

The manufacture, offer, receipt, importation, exportation, or possession, without authorization, of marks, materials, apparatus, instruments, computer programs, or any other element specially intended for the manufacture or protection against counterfeiting or falsification of banknotes or coins is punished by seven years' imprisonment and a fine of 10,000,000 francs.

Article 243-11

The manufacture, possession, circulation, use, display, distribution, importation or exportation of unauthorized monetary signs, intended to replace coins or banknotes having legal tender in the national territory or abroad, is punished by imprisonment of seven years and a fine of 10,000,000 francs.

The same penalties apply to the possession, circulation, use, display, distribution, importation or exportation of banknotes and coins which, although intended to be put into circulation, have not yet been issued by the institutions authorized for this purpose and do not yet have legal tender in the national territory or abroad.

Article 243-12

The manufacture, possession, circulation, use, display, distribution, importation or exportation of printed matter, forms or tokens intended to be accepted as a means of payment, is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 10,000,000 francs.

Article 243-13

Any person who commits the following acts is punished by imprisonment of one year and a fine of 1,000,000 francs:
reproduces, totally or partially, by any means whatsoever, monetary signs having legal tender in the national territory or abroad, without the prior authorization of the BCEAO or, in the case of foreign monetary signs, of the authority that issued them;
displays, distributes, imports or exports reproductions of monetary signs, including by means of newspapers, books or prospectuses without the prior authorization of the Central Bank or, in the case of foreign monetary signs, of the authority that issued them;
uses banknotes having legal tender in the national territory or abroad, as a medium for any advertising.

Article 243-14

The deliberate deterioration, defacement or overloading of a monetary sign is punished by imprisonment of six months and a fine of 1,000,000 francs, when it has the effect of rendering it unfit for use as a means of payment.

Article 243-15

The refusal to accept currency having legal tender in a Member State of the Union according to the value for which it has legal tender is punished by a fine of 600,000 francs.

Article 243-16

The collection of a commission in exchange for the delivery of monetary signs issued by the BCEAO against other monetary signs of its issue, is punished by imprisonment of three years and a fine of 3,000,000 francs.

Article 243-17

Regardless of the classification of the offense, the counterfeited or falsified monetary signs and others referred to in articles 243-3 to 243-14 above as well as the metals, papers and other materials found in the possession of the offenders and intended for the commission of similar offenses are confiscated.

The instruments used to commit the offense are also confiscated, except when they were used without the knowledge of their owner.

Article 243-18

The competent court shall mandatorily impose on natural persons found guilty of the offenses provided for in articles 243-3 to 243-12 above, the following additional penalties:

1°) the prohibition from engaging in an activity in the banking and financial sector for a period not exceeding twenty years;

2°) the prohibition of residence, permanently, or for a period not exceeding twenty years for foreigners.
