It may, in addition, impose on them the deprivation of civil rights for a period not exceeding twenty years.

Article 243-19

Legal persons other than the State are criminally liable for the offenses defined herein, when they are committed on their behalf by their organs or representatives.

The legal person found criminally liable is, without prejudice to the penalties incurred by the natural persons who are co-perpetrators or accomplices of the same acts, punished by a fine equal to five times the amount provided for natural persons.

The competent court shall also impose the following additional penalties:

1°) the dissolution of the legal person, when it was created or diverted from its corporate purpose to commit the offenses referred to in articles 243-3 to 243-12 above;

2°) the permanent closure of the enterprise or for a period between one year and five years.

Article 243-20: The attempt of the offenses referred to in this section is punishable

Article 243-21

In case of recidivism, the penalties provided for in this section are doubled.

Convictions pronounced by the criminal courts of a Member State of UMOA for the offenses provided for in this section, are taken into account for recidivism in all other Member States.

Article 243-22

Any person who, having participated in the offenses provided for in articles 243-3 to 243-12 above, has disclosed them to the competent authorities or revealed the perpetrators before any prosecution, is exempted from penalties. However, such person may be prohibited from residence if he or she has the status of a foreigner.

Any person who, having participated in the offenses referred to in the preceding paragraph, has, after the initiation of prosecution, enabled the arrest of the other participants, may be exempted from penalties, totally or partially. However, such person may be prohibited from residence if he or she has the status of a foreigner.

Article 243-23

When it pronounces a conviction pursuant to the provisions of this section, the competent court may order the posting or dissemination of all or part of the decision or of a press release informing the public of the reasons for and the operative part of the decision.

It shall determine, where applicable, the extracts of the decision and the terms of the press release that must be posted or disseminated.

The posting or dissemination of the decision or press release may include the identity of the victim only with the consent of the victim or his or her legal representative or successors in title.

The posting shall be carried out in the places and for the duration indicated by the court. Unless otherwise decided by the court, the posting may not exceed two months. In case of removal, concealment, or tearing of the posted notices, posting shall be carried out again at the expense of the person found guilty of these acts.

The dissemination of the decision is done by one or more press publications or by one or more electronic communication services available to the public. The publications or electronic communication services available to the public responsible for this dissemination are designated by the court. They may not oppose this dissemination.

The posting and dissemination may be ordered cumulatively.

The posting of the decision pronounced or the dissemination thereof is at the expense of the convicted person. The posting or dissemination costs recovered from the latter may not, however, exceed the maximum fine incurred.

Paragraphe 3: Applicable procedure

Article 243-24

By way of derogation from the provisions of the Code of Criminal Procedure, the correctional courts have jurisdiction to hear the crimes provided for in this chapter.

The procedure followed is that applicable in correctional matters.

Article 243-25

By way of derogation from the provisions of the Code of Criminal Procedure, for the offenses provided for in this section, the public prosecution is subject to limitation according to the following distinctions:
by twenty years for crimes;
by ten years for offenses.

Article 243-26

When they are seized with cases relating to counterfeiting or discover, during their investigations, counterfeit or falsified monetary signs, the competent authorities are required to transmit to the Central Bank, for analysis and identification, at least one specimen of each type of banknotes or coins suspected to be false.

The provisions of the preceding paragraph are not applicable when there exists only one specimen of a type of banknotes or coins suspected to be false, as long as it is necessary for the establishment of the truth.

Article 243-27

Counterfeit or falsified monetary signs as well as materials and instruments intended to be used for their manufacture, confiscated pursuant to Article 243-17 above, are handed over to the Central Bank for their possible destruction, subject to the requirements of the administration of justice.

Article 243-28

When the Central Bank recognizes as counterfeit or falsified monetary signs that are handed over to it, it is authorized to retain them and possibly to destroy them, subject to the requirements of the administration of justice.

Sous-section 2

Counterfeiting of State seals, public or private instruments, punches, stamps and marks

Article 243-29

Is punished by thirty years' imprisonment and a fine of 10,000,000 francs, whoever counterfeits a seal of the State or makes use of a counterfeit seal.

Article 243-30

Is punished by twenty years' imprisonment and a fine of 5,000,000 francs, whoever counterfeits or falsifies either one or more national stamps, or one or more punches of the State used to mark gold or silver materials, or who makes use of falsified or counterfeit stamps or punches.

Article 243-31

Is punished by ten years' imprisonment and a fine of 3,000,000 francs, whoever, having improperly obtained genuine stamps or punches of the State designated in the preceding article, makes an application or use thereof prejudicial to the rights and interests of the State, its subdivisions and third parties.

Article 243-32

Is punished by six months' imprisonment and a fine of 600,000 francs, whoever:
manufactures the seals, stamps, marks, cachets of the State or of any authority without the written order of the accredited representatives of the State or of that authority;
manufactures, holds, distributes, purchases or sells stamps, seals, marks or cachets likely to be confused with those of the State or of any authority whatsoever, even foreign.

Article 243-33

Is punished by five years' imprisonment and a fine of 1,500,000 francs, whoever manufactures, sells, hawks, distributes or uses printed matter which, by its format, its color, its text, its typographical layout or any other characteristic, presents with letterhead papers or official printed matter in use in institutions, public administrations and the various courts, a resemblance likely to cause confusion in the mind of the public.

Article 243-34

Is punished by five years' imprisonment and a fine of 1,500,000 francs, whoever:

1°) counterfeits marks intended to be affixed in the name of the Government or of a public service on various kinds of foodstuffs or goods, or who makes use of these false marks;

2°) counterfeits the seal, stamp or mark of any authority whatsoever or makes use of the counterfeit seal, stamp or mark;

3°) counterfeits or falsifies postage stamps, franking impressions or reply coupons issued by the Malian postal administration, mobile fiscal stamps, stamped papers or forms, stamps, impressions, reply coupons, stamped papers or forms that are counterfeit or falsified.

In addition, deprivation of civil and professional rights and prohibition of residence may be pronounced for a period which may not exceed five years.

Article 243-35

Is punished by three years' imprisonment and a fine of 1,000,000 francs, whoever, having improperly obtained genuine seals, marks or printed matter provided for in the preceding article, makes or attempts to make a fraudulent application or use thereof.

In addition, deprivation of civil and/or professional rights and/or prohibition of residence may be pronounced for a period which may not exceed five years.

Article 243-36

Is punished by one year's imprisonment and a fine of 600,000 francs, whoever:

1°) knowingly makes use of postage stamps, mobile stamps or stamped papers or forms having already been used, or who, by any means, alters stamps with the aim of removing them from cancellation and thus permitting their subsequent reuse;

2°) overprints by printing, perforation or any other means Malian postage stamps or other postal fiduciary values, expired or not, or who sells, hawks, offers, distributes, exports postage stamps thus overprinted;

3°) counterfeits, imitates or alters stamps, vignettes, franking impressions or reply coupons issued by the postal service of a foreign country, sells, hawks or distributes said stamps, vignettes, franking impressions or reply coupons or knowingly makes use thereof.

Article 243-37

Is punished by two years' imprisonment and a fine of 1,500,000 francs, whoever is guilty of manufacturing, introducing into Mali, selling or distributing any objects, tokens, printed matter or forms, obtained by any process whatsoever and which, by their appearance, would present with annuity certificates, shares, bonds, interest shares, dividend or interest coupons relating thereto and generally with fiduciary securities issued by companies or private enterprises, a resemblance likely to facilitate the acceptance of said objects, tokens, printed matter or forms in place of the imitated securities.

In addition, deprivation of civil and professional rights and prohibition of residence may be pronounced for a period which may not exceed five years.

Article 243-38

Is punished by ten years' imprisonment and a fine of 10,000,000 francs, whoever counterfeits, falsifies or alters certificates, bonds or obligations issued by the Public Treasury with its stamp or its mark, or interest coupons relating to these certificates, bonds or obligations.

Article 243-39

Is punished by ten years' imprisonment and a fine of 10,000,000 francs, whoever, in any manner whatsoever, knowingly participates in the issue, distribution, sale or introduction into Mali of the certificates, bonds or obligations designated in the preceding article.

Article 243-40

Is punished by ten years' imprisonment and a fine of 2,000,000 francs, whoever manufactures, acquires, holds or transfers knowingly products or equipment intended for the commission of the offenses punished above, unless the act constitutes a more serious offense.

Article 243-41

Benefits from an absolute excuse one of the perpetrators of the crimes mentioned in Articles 243-39 and 243-40 above who, before the consummation of these crimes and before any prosecution, gives notice thereof to the authorities and reveals the identity of the perpetrators or who, even after prosecution has commenced, facilitates the arrest of the other perpetrators.

Article 243-42

In the case of the offenses referred to in Articles 243-38, 243-39 and 243-40 above, the trial court pronounces the confiscation of products and equipment.

It may in addition order the closure of the establishment for a period which may not exceed five years.

Section 2: Forgery in writing

Article 243-43

Forgery constitutes a crime when it is any alteration of the truth likely to cause harm to others and committed in a writing, with criminal intent:

1°) either by distorting the substance or circumstances of an act;

2°) either by writing therein agreements other than those drawn up or dictated by the parties;

3°) either by recording as true facts that are false or as acknowledged facts that were not;

4°) either by fabrication of all or part of a document;

5°) either by counterfeiting or alteration of writings or signatures; 6°) either by false signature;
7°) either by substitution of persons;

8°) either by addition or alteration of clauses, declarations or facts that any act was intended to receive or record.

Article 243-44

Any public official, within the meaning of Article 241-14 of this Code, who commits forgery in the exercise of their functions, is punished by twenty years' imprisonment and twenty years' prohibition from residence.

Forgery committed by any other person is punished by ten years' imprisonment and ten years' prohibition from residence.

When the certain or potential harm is assessable in money and less than 50,000 francs, the penalty is, regardless of the perpetrator, five years' imprisonment. Prohibition from residence may in addition be imposed for five years.

Article 243-45

Those who knowingly make use of forged documents are punished by the penalty incurred by the author of the forgery.

Article 243-46

Any person who assumes a false name in a passport, work permit or any other document issued by the Malian administrative authority or assists as a witness in having said documents issued under a false name is punished by two years' imprisonment.

The same penalty applies to any individual who makes use of the documents referred to above under a name other than their own.

Article 243-47

In the cases referred to in Article 243-46 above, a fine is always imposed on the convicted person, the maximum of which is double the amounts unduly demanded or received.

Section 3

Corruption and related offences Subsection 1: Corruption

Paragraph 1: Corruption of public officials and agents

Article 243-48

Is punished by ten years' imprisonment and a fine double the value of the promises accepted or things received or requested, without said fine being less than 100,000 francs, anyone who solicits or accepts offers or promises, solicits or receives gifts or presents to:

1°) being a public official of the administrative or judicial order, being a member of the military or equivalent, being an assessor of a court of judgment, agent or employee of a public administration or an administration placed under the control of public authority, citizen charged with a ministry of public service, being invested with an elective mandate, do or refrain from doing an act of their functions or employment, just or not but not subject to salary;

2°) being an arbitrator or expert appointed either by the Court or by the parties, render a decision or give an opinion favourable or unfavourable to a party;

3°) being a doctor, dental surgeon or midwife, falsely certify or conceal the existence of diseases or infirmities or a state of pregnancy or provide false information on the origin of a disease or infirmity or the cause of a death.
The custodial sentence incurred by the author or accomplice of the offence provided for in paragraph 1 of this article is reduced by two thirds if, having notified the administrative or judicial authority, they have made it possible to stop the offence or to identify, where applicable, the other authors or accomplices.
The confiscation of the proceeds or instrument or object of corruption is imposed as an additional penalty.

Paragraph 2

Corruption of foreign public agents and officials of public international organizations

Article 243-49: For the purposes of this Code, the following are understood as

1°) foreign public agent, any person who holds a legislative, executive, administrative or judicial mandate in a foreign country, whether appointed or elected; and any person who exercises a public function for a foreign country, including for a public body or public enterprise;
2°) official of a public international organization, an international official or person authorized by such an organization to act on its behalf.

Article 243-50

Is punished by ten years' imprisonment and a fine equal to triple the value of the promises accepted or things received or requested, without said fine being less than 5,000,000 francs:
1°) any foreign public agent or official of a public international organization who, directly or indirectly solicits or accepts offers or promises or receives gifts or presents or other undue advantages, for themselves or for another person or entity, so that they perform or refrain from performing an act in the exercise of their functions or employment, with a view to granting, obtaining, causing to be obtained, retaining or causing to be retained a contract or other undue advantage, in connection with an economic or commercial transaction;

2°) anyone who promises, offers or grants to a foreign public agent or to an official of a public international organization, directly or indirectly, promises, gifts or presents or other undue advantages, for themselves or for another person or entity, so that they perform or refrain from performing an act in the exercise of their functions or employment, with a view to obtaining, causing to be obtained, retaining or causing to be retained a contract or other undue advantage, in connection with an economic or commercial transaction.

Article 243-51

Is punished by ten years' imprisonment and a fine of 5,000,000 francs:

1°) anyone who promises, offers or grants to a foreign public agent or to an official of a public international organization, directly or indirectly, gifts or presents or any other undue advantage, for themselves or for another person or entity, so that they perform or refrain from performing an act in the exercise of their official functions;

2°) any foreign public agent or official of a public international organization, who solicits or accepts directly or indirectly, for themselves or for another person or entity, promises, offers, gifts or presents or any other undue advantage, so that they perform or refrain from performing an act within their official functions;

3°) any foreign public agent or official of a public international organization, who solicits or accepts remuneration in cash or in kind, for themselves or for a third party, in payment for an act of their function already performed).

The confiscation of the proceeds of corruption is imposed as an additional penalty.

A ten-year prohibition from residence may be imposed on the foreign public agent and the official of the public international organization.

Paragraph 3: Corruption in the private sector

Sub-paragraph 1: Incrimination of corruption in the private sector

Article 243-52

Is punishable by five years' imprisonment and a fine corresponding to twice the value of the proceeds of corruption, provided that the amount of the fine may not be less than 2,000,000 francs:

1°) the act by any individual, in the context of economic, financial or commercial activities, of promising, offering or granting, directly or indirectly, an undue advantage to any person who directs a private sector entity or works for such an entity, in any capacity whatsoever, for herself or himself or for another person, so that, in violation of her or his duties, she or he performs or refrains from performing an act;

2°) the act by any person who directs a private sector entity or works for such an entity, in any capacity whatsoever, of soliciting or accepting, directly or indirectly, in the context of economic, financial or commercial activities, an undue advantage for herself or himself or for another person, in order to perform or refrain from performing an act in violation of her or his duties.

The custodial sentence incurred by the perpetrator or accomplice of the offence provided for in paragraph 1 of this article is reduced by two thirds if, having notified the administrative or judicial authority, he or she has enabled the offence to be stopped or, where applicable, the other perpetrators or accomplices to be identified.

The confiscation of the proceeds or the instrument or object of corruption is imposed as an additional penalty.

It is prohibited to allow tax deductibility of expenses that constitute bribes, the payment of which is one of the constituent elements of the offences established in accordance with articles 243-48 and 243-49 to 243-51 above and, where applicable, other expenses incurred for purposes of corruption.

Article 243-53

Is punishable by five years' imprisonment and a fine of 10,000,000 francs, any director of a commercial company, a financial institution, a cooperative, any agent of an association, a private enterprise, a mutual society or any foundation, who makes use of the property or credit of the said company, institution, cooperative, association, private enterprise or foundation in a manner which he or she knows to be contrary to the interest of that company, institution, cooperative, association, private enterprise or foundation, for personal purposes, material or moral, or to favour a third party or another legal person in which he or she has a direct or indirect interest.

Sub-paragraph 2: Misappropriation of property in the private sector

Article 243-54

Is punishable by five years' imprisonment and a fine of 5,000,000 francs, any person who directs a private sector entity or works for such an entity in any capacity whatsoever and who, intentionally, in the context of economic, financial or commercial activities, misappropriates any property or any private funds or securities or any other thing of value which have been entrusted to him or her by reason of his or her functions.

Sous-section 2

Offences assimilated to corruption Paragraph 1: Offences against public property

Article 243-55

The following property has the character of public property: property belonging to the following institutions and bodies:

1°) the State and public authorities; 2°) State Companies and Enterprises; 3°) Public Establishments;
4°) cooperative bodies, unions, associations or federations of such bodies;

5°) Associations recognised as being of Public Utility;

6°) bodies of an industrial or commercial nature in which the State or other public authorities hold a fraction of the share capital.

The property referred to in the preceding paragraph includes:

1°) monies, funds, coins, fiduciary securities and generally all securities having an estimated value in money, which have entered the coffers or which are collected to be paid into the coffers of the State, public authorities or the bodies referred to in the first paragraph above;

2°) active securities representing such monies;

3°) payment instruments and securities, transferable securities; 4°) documents containing or effecting obligation or discharge;
5°) movable property, materials, equipment, weapons, ammunition, goods, foodstuffs or any objects whatsoever;

6°) immovable property titles.

Article 243-56

Any official, civil or military within the meaning of article 241-14 of this Code, any agent or employee of the State, public authorities or the bodies referred to in the preceding article, who commits an offence against public property by one of the following means: fraudulent misappropriation, embezzlement or breach of trust, fraud, forgery and use of forgery, other malfeasance, is punished by the penalties provided for in this article.

Are considered as accomplices, the heads of State companies and enterprises, officials and agents of the State or enterprises, persons responsible for control who, by failing in the duties of their offices, facilitate or cover up offences against public property.

In all the cases cited in the preceding paragraphs:

1°) when the amount of the damage is less than ten million francs, the penalty is five years' imprisonment and a fine of 5,000,000 francs;

2°) when the amount of the damage is equal to ten million but less than twenty million francs, the penalty is ten years' imprisonment and a fine of 10,000,000 francs;

3°) when the amount of the damage is equal to or greater than twenty million francs, but less than fifty million francs, the penalty is ten years' imprisonment and a fine of 10,000,000 francs;

4°) when the amount of the damage exceeds fifty million francs, the penalty is life imprisonment and a fine of 20,000,000 francs.

A prohibition on residence of twenty years may be imposed in the cases provided for in 2° and 3° above.

Paragraphe 2

Unlawful and abusive use and retention of public property by a public agent

Article 243-57

Is punishable by five years' imprisonment and a fine of 1,500,000 francs any public agent within the meaning of article 241-14 of this Code who knowingly and unlawfully retains for his or her own benefit or for the benefit of another person or entity, any property, any public funds or securities, or any thing of value which have been entrusted to him or her either by virtue of a contract or by reason of his or her functions, or who makes unlawful and abusive use of public property.

Paragraphe 3 : Unjustified advantages and corruption in public procurement

Article 243-58

Is punishable by ten years' imprisonment and a fine of 10,000,000 francs:

1°) any official or public agent within the meaning of article 241-14 of this Code who enters into, endorses or reviews a contract, an agreement, a public order or an amendment in violation of the legislative and regulatory provisions in force with a view to procuring an unjustified advantage for another person;

2°) any merchant, industrialist, artist or artisan, private sector contractor or in general, any natural or legal person who enters into, even on an occasional basis, a contract or a public order with the State, Territorial Authorities, public law establishments or bodies and State companies by taking advantage of the authority or influence of the agents of the aforementioned bodies to increase the prices they normally and habitually charge or to modify, to their advantage, the quality of goods or services or the delivery or supply deadlines.

Article 243-59

Is punishable by the penalties provided for in article 243-58 above, anyone who compels or attempts to compel by force or threats, corrupts or attempts to corrupt by promise, offer, gift or present, a public agent, whether or not the attempt has been successful.

Article 243-60

Is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine equivalent to three times the amount or value of the benefit received or to be received, any public official or public agent within the meaning of Article 241-14 of this Code who, on the occasion of the preparation, negotiation, conclusion or execution of a public order, contract or amendment concluded in the name of the State or Territorial Authorities, public establishments of the State or State-owned companies, receives or attempts to receive, directly or indirectly, for his own benefit or for the benefit of a third party, remuneration or a benefit of any nature whatsoever from a private contractor.

Article 243-61

Is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine equivalent to twice the promises accepted, undue benefits, things received or requested of the value of a public order and a prohibition from bidding on public contracts for two years, any natural or legal person contracting with the State or Territorial Authorities, public establishments of the State or State-owned companies who grants or proposes remuneration or any benefit by himself or through an intermediary to a public agent with a view to obtaining a public order.

Article 243-62

Is punished by imprisonment of three years and a fine equivalent to twice the promises accepted, undue benefits, things received or requested, of the value of the public order, any public official or public agent within the meaning of Article 241-14 of this Code who abusively resorts to the direct agreement procedure in a public order concluded in the name of the State or Territorial Authorities, public establishments of the State or State-owned companies.

Paragraphe 4 : De la surfacturation

Article 243-63

Is punished by imprisonment of five years, confiscation of the amount received as a rebate and a fine equivalent to three times the value received without this fine being less than 2,000,000 francs, without prejudice to the sanctions provided for by the regulations on public procurement, any public agent who proceeds or causes to proceed with the invoicing, for an amount higher than its actual cost, of a good or service to be acquired by an entity of the national or local public administration.

Paragraphe 5 : De la concussion

Article 243-64

Public officials or public agents within the meaning of Article 241-14 of this Code, their clerks or agents who, with fraudulent intent, order the collection of and demand or receive what they know is not due for duties, taxes, contributions, revenues, salaries or wages are punished by:

1°) imprisonment of five years if the total of the sums unduly demanded or received or whose collection is ordered is less than 50,000 francs;

2°) imprisonment of ten years, if the total of the sums unduly demanded or received or whose collection is ordered is equal to or greater than 50,000 francs.

The attempt of this offence is punished as the offence itself.

Paragraphe 6 : Des exonérations et franchises illégales

Article 243-65

Is punished by imprisonment of ten years and a fine increased to ten times the value of the property or right compromised, any public agent who, for whatever reason, in an illegal manner, grants exemptions or franchises from taxes, duties, fines, bonds and other rights or gives free of charge or sells at a low price, public property in violation of laws and regulations.

Paragraphe 7

De l'ingérence des personnes physiques dépositaires de l'autorité publique ou chargées d'une mission de service public ou investies d'un mandat électif dans les affaires du commerce incompatible avec leur qualité

Sous paragraphe 1: Du conflit d'intérêts et de la prise illégale d'intérêt

Article 243-66

Is punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 2,000,000 francs, any public agent within the meaning of Article 241-14 of this Code whose private interests coincide with the public interest and are likely to influence the normal exercise of his functions, and who does not inform his hierarchical superior thereof.

Article 243-67

Any public official, under the terms of Article 241-14 of this Code who, either openly, or by simulated acts, or by interposition of persons, takes or receives any interest whatsoever in acts, adjudications, enterprises or administrations of which he has or had at the time of the act, in whole or in part the administration or supervision, or in a matter of which he was responsible for ordering payment or making the settlement, is punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine which cannot exceed one quarter of the restitutions and compensation.

Any public official responsible by reason of his very function, for the supervision or direct control of a private enterprise and who, either on leave or availability status, or after admission to retirement, or in a position of dismissal or revocation and for a period of five years from the cessation of the function, except in the case of hereditary devolution with regard to capital, takes or receives a participation through work, advice or capital in the concessions, enterprises or administrations which were directly subject to his supervision or control, is punished by the same penalty of imprisonment and a fine of 500,000 francs.

The managers of a concession, enterprise, administration, considered as accomplices, are subject to the same penalties.

The guilty parties may, in addition, be declared incapable of exercising a public function for five years.

Article 243-68

The act for a natural person holding public authority within the meaning of Article 241-14 of this Code, of taking, receiving or keeping directly or indirectly, any interest whatsoever in an enterprise or operation of which he has, at the time of the act, in whole or in part, responsibility for ensuring the supervision, administration, settlement or payment is punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine which cannot exceed one quarter of the restitutions and compensation.

Sous paragraphe 2: Du népotisme et du favoritisme

Article 243-69

Is punished by imprisonment of three years and prohibition from exercising a function in the national public administration for a period of five years, any political personality, any public agent, any member of the defence and security forces who uses his function or uses his influence, to procure directly or indirectly any undue material benefit, or employment, to a member of his family in the direct line or in the collateral line up to the third degree.

Article 243-70

Any natural person holding public authority within the meaning of Article 241-14 of this Code, who procures or attempts to procure for another an unjustified benefit by an act contrary to the rules of the Public Procurement Code, intended to guarantee freedom of access and equality of candidates in public contracts, is punished by imprisonment of eighteen months and a fine of 100,000,000 francs.

Without prejudice to disciplinary proceedings, the perpetrator may in addition be prohibited from exercising the functions he held at the time of the award of the contracts for a period equal to or less than three years.

The court seized may order the publication at the expense of the convicted person of all or an extract of its decision in a legal notices newspaper.

Article 243-71

Offences relating to the methods of awarding public contracts and the legal threshold for awarding public contracts are punishable by five years' imprisonment and a fine of 5,000,000 francs, without prejudice to compensation for damage suffered by the contracting authority.

Article 243-72 : Splitting of expenditure is an offence

The persons designated in Article 243-70 above, perpetrators or accomplices of splitting of expenditure, are punishable by five years' imprisonment and a fine of 3,000,000 francs.

Article 243-73

The persons designated in Article 243-70 above who, in the absence of any derogation, award public contracts to contractors, suppliers or service providers excluded from the award of public contracts in accordance with the laws and regulations in force, are punishable by the penalties provided for in the preceding article.

Article 243-74

Public officials, State agents and any other person responsible for monitoring the execution of a public contract who, through negligence or fraud, voluntarily refrain from sending periodic reports to the contracting authority on compliance with the performance schedule and on any failures by the contract holder, if this has resulted in damage to the contracting authority equal to fifty per cent of the initial contract amount, are punishable by three years' imprisonment and a fine of 2,000,000 francs.

However, if the damage is less than fifty per cent, they are punishable by a fine at least equal to the amount of the damage.

Article 243-75

The perpetrators or accomplices of deliberate inaccuracies committed in the justifications of eligibility for public contracts are punishable in accordance with Articles 243-43, 243-44 and 243-45 of this Code.

Article 243-76

All other types of fraud, not specified in Articles 243-69, 243-70, 243-71, 243-72, 243-73 and 243-74 of this Code, intended to procure or attempt to procure for another person an unjustified advantage by an act contrary to the rules of the Public Procurement Code aimed at guaranteeing freedom of access and equality of candidates in public contracts, are punishable by the penalties provided for in Article 243-70 above.

Article 243-77

The following are punishable in accordance with the provisions of this Code punishing corruption:
1°) tenderers who have used promises, offers or gifts for the purpose of acquiring a public contract;
2°) persons responsible for the preparation of public contracts and members of the tender opening and evaluation committee who have solicited or accepted offers or promises, solicited or received gifts or presents for the purpose of undermining freedom of access and equality of candidates in public contracts.

Paragraphe 8 : Du trafic d'influence et de l'abus de fonction

Article 243-78

Any person who solicits or accepts offers or promises, solicits or receives gifts or presents in order to obtain or attempt to obtain decorations, medals, distinctions, rewards, positions, functions or employment or any favours granted by the public authority, contracts, undertakings or other benefits resulting from contracts concluded with the public authority or an administration placed under the control of the public authority, or, in general, a favourable decision from such an authority or administration and thus abuses real or supposed influence, is punishable by the penalties provided for in Article 243-48 of this Code.

Article 243-79

Anyone who, in order to obtain either the performance or obtaining of an act, or one of the advantages or favours provided for in the preceding articles, uses violence or threats, promises, offers, gifts or presents, or yields to solicitations tending to corruption, even if he does not take the initiative, is, whether or not the corruption has produced its effect, punishable by the penalties laid down by Article 243-48 of this Code against the corrupted person.

Article 243-80

The act, for a public agent, of intentionally abusing his functions or position, by performing or refraining from performing, in the exercise of his functions, an act in violation of laws and regulations in order to obtain an undue advantage for himself or for another person or entity, is punishable by ten years' imprisonment and a fine of 10,000,000 francs.

Article 243-81

Where the corruption or influence peddling relates to a criminal act carrying a heavier penalty than that laid down in Article 243-48 of this Code, that heavier penalty shall be applied to the offender.

The corruptor is never granted restitution of the things delivered by him, nor of their value; these are confiscated.

The custodial sentence incurred by the perpetrator or accomplice of the offence provided for in paragraph 1 of this article is reduced by two thirds if, having notified the administrative or judicial authority, he has made it possible to bring the offence to an end or to identify, where appropriate, the other perpetrators or accomplices.

Paragraphe 9 : De l'utilisation d'informations non connues du public

Article 243-82

Any agent of the public or private sector who exploits, in anticipation, knowingly, information not known to the public of a nature to break equality of opportunity or which would influence the course of any economic activity and of which he became aware by virtue of his situation or position, is punishable by five years' imprisonment and a fine of 20,000,000 francs which may be up to ten times the value of the profit made.

The trial court orders ex officio the confiscation of the property and income that are the subject of the offence.

Paragraphe 10 : Du commerce incompatible

Article 243-83

Any public agent engaging in commercial or lucrative activities other than the marketing of his non-industrial, literary, scientific and artistic agro-pastoral productions is punishable by ten years' imprisonment and a fine of 500,000 francs.

In addition, confiscation of the means of such trade or lucrative activity is ordered.

Paragraphe 11 : De la simulation illicite

Article 243-84

Any person in the private sector who agrees to represent a public agent, acquires property or engages in commercial or lucrative activities on his behalf pursuant to a written or verbal nominee agreement, is punishable by five years' imprisonment and a fine of 1,500,000 francs.
The public agent, party to such nominee agreement, is punishable by the same penalties, notwithstanding the administrative and disciplinary sanctions to which he may be subject.
In addition, the trial court orders ex officio the confiscation of the property and income that are the subject of the offence.

Paragraphe 13 : Du défaut ou de la fausse déclaration d'intérêt ou de patrimoine

Article 243-85

Any public agent legally subject to a declaration of interest or assets who, three months after a written formal notice from the institution responsible for combating illicit enrichment, knowingly fails to make a declaration of his assets or makes an incomplete, inaccurate or false declaration or knowingly makes false observations or deliberately violates the obligations imposed on him by law, is punishable by five years' imprisonment and a fine of 2,000,000 francs.

Paragraphe 14: Disclosure of information

Article 243-86

Any agent of the authority holding declarations of interest and assets or of the registry offices found guilty of having disclosed, except in cases provided for by law, in any manner whatsoever, totally or partially, declarations of interest or assets or observations received, shall be punished by imprisonment of three years and a fine of 1,000,000 francs.

Paragraphe 15: Offence of acceptance or offer of undue gifts

Article 243-87

Any public agent who accepts from a person or grants to a person a gift or any undue advantage likely to influence the handling of a procedure or transaction in progress related to his functions or having a connection with such handling or such transaction shall be punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 2,000,000 francs.

The donor shall be punished by the same penalties.

Sous-section 3: Receiving the proceeds of corruption and related offences

Article 243-88

Any person who, knowingly, receives, conceals or continuously retains in whole or in part, property knowing that said property comes from one of the offences provided for in this section shall be punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.

Sous-section 4: Laundering the proceeds of corruption and related offences

Article 243-89

The following constitute laundering of the proceeds of corruption and related offences:

a) the conversion, transfer or assignment of ownership knowing that such ownership is the proceeds of acts of corruption or related offences with a view to hiding or disguising the illicit origin of the ownership or assisting any person involved in the perpetration of the offence to escape the legal consequences of his action;

b) the concealment or disguising of the true nature, source, location, disposition, movement or ownership or rights concerning ownership which is the proceeds of acts of corruption or related offences;

c) the acquisition, possession or use of ownership knowing, at the time of its receipt, that such ownership is the fruit of acts of corruption or related offences;

d) whether or not such operations have a transnational character.

The laundering of the proceeds of the offences referred to in this section shall be punished in accordance with the provisions of the uniform law on combating money laundering and the financing of terrorism.

Section 4

Protection of witnesses, experts, whistleblowers and victims

Article 243-90

No one may be excluded from a recruitment procedure or from access to an internship or training period in a company, no employee may be sanctioned, dismissed or subjected to discriminatory measures, direct or indirect, particularly regarding remuneration, training, reclassification, assignment, qualification, classification, professional promotion, transfer or contract renewal for having reported or testified, either to his employer or to judicial or administrative authorities, about facts qualified as an offence within the meaning of this chapter of which he became aware in the exercise or on the occasion of the exercise of his functions.

No civil servant or public agent, civil or military, may be sanctioned or subjected to discriminatory measures, direct or indirect, for having reported or testified, either to the hierarchical authority or to judicial or administrative authorities, about facts qualified as an offence within the meaning of this chapter of which he became aware in the exercise or on the occasion of the exercise of his functions.

Article 243-91

Any person who resorts to physical or moral violence, revenge, intimidation or threat in any form whatsoever and in any manner whatsoever, against the person of witnesses, experts, whistleblowers or victims or their relatives or other persons close to them shall be punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 2,000,000 francs.

Any person who reveals the identity or address of a witness having benefited from the protection provisions provided for in this chapter shall be punished by the same penalties, without prejudice to other repressive provisions of the Code of Criminal Procedure regarding the protection of persons referred to in the above paragraph.

Article 243-92: The protection period begins

1°) for the person who reported the crime or offence on the date of receipt of the report by the competent authority;

2°) for the person associated with the investigation and his counsel, on the date on which they were associated with the investigation.

The duration of protection is set at three years from the date of closure of the investigation report or of a final judicial conviction.

Article 243-93

The protection granted to the person who reports a crime or offence shall be lifted on the date of closure of the investigation report if it contains sufficient elements to conclude that the person who reported the crime or offence acted knowing full well that such report was not sincere.

Article 243-94

The protection granted to the person associated with the investigation shall be lifted on the date of closure of the investigation report if it contains sufficient elements to conclude that the associated person deliberately provided dishonest information, not in conformity with reality and manifestly incomplete to the investigators.

Article 243-95

If during the protection period, measures referred to in Article 243-90 above are taken against the protected person, once he presents factual elements that allow the presumption that he reported or testified in good faith about facts constituting an offence or a crime, the burden of proof that no measure or threat of measure is or has been taken or formulated lies with the authority presumed to have taken such measure or threat of measure.

Article 243-96

On the basis of the final conviction, the employer shall reinstate within three months following such decision the person who was subjected to a measure in violation of Article 243-90 above.

After this period, the labour court or the administrative court seized shall order the reinstatement of the person who was subjected to non-renewal of contract, dismissal or revocation or any other measure affecting the conditions or circumstances of his work.

This decision is not subject to appeal.

Article 243-97

The whistleblower is a natural person who reveals or reports, in a disinterested manner and in good faith, a crime or offence, a threat or serious harm to the general interest, of which he has personally become aware.

Facts, information or documents, whatever their form or medium, covered by national defence secrecy, medical secrecy or the secrecy of relations between a lawyer and his client are excluded from the alert regime defined by this article.

TITRE V: TERRORISM

Chapitre 1: Acts of terrorism

[Loi n°08-025 du 23 juillet 2008 portant répression du terrorisme au Mali]

Article 251-1 : Constitute acts of terrorism

1°) the act of hijacking an aircraft, a ship or a vehicle by violence, threat of violence or any other form of intimidation; seizing an aircraft, a ship or a fixed platform in order to exercise control over it;

2°) the act of compromising the security of an aircraft, the services of an airport, a railway station or a river wharf, the navigation of a ship, by knowingly communicating false information;

3°) the act of committing an act of violence:
against one or more persons on board an aircraft in flight, a ship, a fixed platform or a vehicle, if such act is likely to compromise the security of that aircraft, the navigation of the ship or the platform or the vehicle;
using a device, a substance or a weapon, in an airport, an aerodrome, a railway station or a river wharf, an act of violence which causes or is likely to cause serious violence or death, if such act, in the case of an airport, is likely to compromise its security.

4°) the act of destroying an aircraft in service, a ship, a fixed platform, a vehicle, air or maritime navigation facilities or services, including the facilities of an airport serving international civil aviation;

5°) the act of damaging:
an aircraft in service, a ship, its cargo or a fixed platform, a vehicle in such a way as to compromise its security;
the facilities of an airport serving international aviation, an aircraft parked at the airport.

6°) the act of disrupting the services of the airport, using a device, a substance or a weapon; if such act is likely to compromise security at that airport;

7°) the act of interrupting, disrupting air or maritime navigation services, disrupting their operation, if any of these acts is likely to compromise the security of an aircraft in flight or the navigation of a ship;

8°) the act of attempting to sabotage a train, or a watercraft, using an explosive or substance capable of destroying said train or watercraft, causing damage to it that renders it unfit for circulation or navigation;

9°) the act of placing or causing to be placed on an aircraft in service, a ship or a fixed platform, a vehicle, by any means whatsoever, a device or substances capable of destroying the aircraft, the ship, the vehicle or the fixed platform, or causing damage to it likely to compromise its security;

10°) the act of threatening to commit any of the offences provided for in this chapter if such threat is likely to compromise the security of an aircraft, a ship or a fixed platform, a vehicle with the aim of compelling a natural or legal person to perform or refrain from performing any act.

Article 251-2 : Also constitute acts of terrorism

1°) the act of seizing a person or detaining them and threatening to kill them, injure them or continue to detain them in order to compel a State, an international organization, intergovernmental organization, a natural or legal person or a group of persons to perform any act or refrain from doing so as an explicit or implicit condition for the release of the person;

2°) the act of seizing under the same conditions and for the same purposes a civilian or any other person who does not participate directly in hostilities in a situation of armed conflict;

3°) the act of recruiting and using children under eighteen years of age by groups involved in acts of terrorism, regardless of the function that the children perform therein;

4°) the act of committing murder, kidnapping or another attack against the person or liberty of a person enjoying international protection;

5°) the act of committing, by resorting to violence, against the official premises, private residence or means of transport of a person enjoying international protection an attack likely to endanger their person or liberty;

6°) the act of threatening to commit such an attack;

7°) the act of delivering, placing, exploding or detonating an explosive device or any other lethal device, in or against a public place, a Government facility or other public facility, a public transport system or infrastructure:
with the intent to cause death or serious bodily harm;
with the intent to cause massive destruction to the places and property referred to above, when such destruction causes or is likely to cause considerable economic losses.

Article 251-3 : Also constitute acts of terrorism

1°) when, either by their nature or their context, they are intended to intimidate a population or to compel a Government or an international organization to perform or abstain from performing any act:
the act of using against or on board a ship or a fixed platform, or of discharging from a ship or a fixed platform, explosives, radioactive materials or biological, chemical or nuclear weapons, in a manner that causes or is likely to cause death or serious bodily or material damage;
the act of discharging, from a ship or a fixed platform, hydrocarbons, liquefied natural gas, or other harmful or potentially dangerous substances, which are not covered by the preceding paragraph, in quantities or concentrations that cause or are likely to cause serious bodily or material damage;
the act of using a ship in a manner that causes death or serious bodily or material damage;
the act of threatening to commit any of the offences referred to above;

2°) the act of transporting on board a ship:
explosives or radioactive materials, knowing that they are intended to cause or to threaten to cause death, serious bodily or material damage, such threat being accompanied or not by a condition, in order to intimidate a population or to compel a Government or an international organization to perform or abstain from performing any act;
any biological, chemical or nuclear weapon, knowing that it is such a weapon;
source materials or special fissionable materials, equipment or materials specially designed or prepared for the processing, use or production of special fissionable materials, knowing that such materials, products or equipment are intended for a nuclear explosive activity or any other nuclear activity not subject to safeguards under a comprehensive safeguards agreement of the International Atomic Energy Agency;
equipment, materials or software or related technologies that contribute significantly to the design, manufacture or delivery of a biological, chemical or nuclear weapon;
any person knowing that such person has committed an act that constitutes an offence covered by this chapter, with the intention of helping them to evade criminal prosecution;

3°) the concealment, transfer, alteration, disposal or dispersal of nuclear materials, without the required authorization, resulting in or likely to result in death or serious injury to others or substantial damage to property or the environment;

4°) the possession of nuclear materials, without the required authorization, resulting in or likely to result in the death of a person or causing them serious bodily harm or with the intention of causing substantial damage to property or the environment;

5°) the use of radioactive materials or devices in any manner whatsoever or the use or damaging of a nuclear facility so as to release or risk releasing radioactive materials:
with the intention of causing the death of a person or causing them serious bodily harm;
with the intention of causing substantial damage to property or the environment;
with the intention of compelling a natural or legal person, an international organization or a Government to perform or not perform an act.

6°) simple theft or aggravated theft of nuclear materials;

7°) the diversion or any other unlawful appropriation of nuclear materials;

8°) the transport, dispatch or movement of nuclear materials to or from a State without the required authorization;

9°) the act directed against a nuclear facility, or disrupting its operation by which the perpetrator intentionally causes or knows that they may cause death or serious injury to others or substantial damage to property or the environment as a result of exposure to radiation or the release of radioactive substances unless such act is undertaken in accordance with the law of the State on whose territory the nuclear facility is located;

10°) the act of demanding:
nuclear materials for the purpose of causing death or serious injury to others or substantial damage to property or the environment or of committing the offence described in point 251-3.3 of this article;
the commission of one of the offences described in points 251-3.6 and 251-3.9 of this article for the purpose of compelling a natural or legal person, an international organization or a State to do or abstain from doing an act;
the commission in circumstances that make the threat credible, of one of the offences covered by this Code.

Article 251-4 : Also constitute acts of terrorism

1°) the act of participating in the commission of one of the offences provided for in articles 251-1, 251-3 and 257-1 of this Code;

2°) the act of organizing, instigating or facilitating the commission of one of the said offences by one or more other persons knowing their intention.

Article 251-5

Also constitute acts of terrorism, when they are intentionally in connection with an individual or collective enterprise aimed at seriously disturbing public order through intimidation or terror:

1°) computer offences as defined by this Code;

2°) harbouring of criminals, forgery, the offence relating to combat groups or dissolved movements as defined by this Code and ratified conventions;

3°) the acquisition, possession, transport or illegal carrying of explosive substances or devices manufactured using such substances;

4°) the possession, carrying and transport of weapons and ammunition of war or defence defined by law;

5°) the act of introducing, spreading on the ground, in the atmosphere, the subsoil or waters, a substance likely to endanger the health of humans or animals or the natural environment.

Article 251-6 : Terrorist offences of a criminal nature are not subject to a statute of limitations

Article 251-7

The offences provided for by this chapter when they are of a criminal nature are punishable by life imprisonment.

However, when the act of terrorism has resulted in the death of one or more persons, it is punished by the death penalty.

In all cases, a fine of 10,000,000 francs is imposed.

In addition, a residence ban of ten years may be imposed for nationals and a temporary or permanent ban from the national territory for foreigners.

Nevertheless, the person who has attempted to commit an act of terrorism is exempted from punishment if, having notified the administrative or judicial authority, they have made it possible to prevent the commission of the offence and to identify, where applicable, their co-perpetrators.

However, a residence ban may be imposed on the said person if they are a national or a ban from the territory if they are a foreigner.

Article 251-8

Subject to the provisions provided for by the Code of Criminal Procedure, children associated with a group involved in acts of terrorism, being victims of recruitment and/or use by this group, are not prosecuted for the sole fact of their association with the group, nor for acts that they are driven to commit during their association. These children are transferred without delay to the civil child protection authorities for their social reintegration.

Chapter 2: Preparatory acts

Article 252-1

Is punished by imprisonment of ten years and a fine of 10,000,000 francs, whoever provides or facilitates the provision of weapons as defined in Article 132-48 of this Code, knowing that they may be used for the commission of one of the offences provided for in this title.

Article 252-2

Is punished by the penalties provided for in Article 252-1 above, whoever recruits, maintains or provides training to another person to commit or participate in the commission of one of the offences provided for in this title or asks them to commit or participate in the commission of one of these offences or to join an association or group, with the intention or knowing that the purpose of this recruitment or request is to participate in the commission of one or more offences provided for in this title.

Article 252-3: Is punished by the penalties provided for in Article 252-1 above

1°) the national who travels or attempts to travel to a State other than their State of residence or of which they are a national, or any person who leaves or attempts to leave the national territory to travel to a State other than their State of residence or nationality, with the intent to commit, organize or prepare acts of terrorism, to participate therein or to provide or receive training in terrorism;

2°) any person who deliberately provides or collects, by any means whatsoever, directly or indirectly, funds which they intend to use or which they know will be used to finance the travel of persons who travel to a State other than their State of residence or of which they are nationals, with the intent to commit, organize or prepare acts of terrorism, or to participate therein or to provide or receive training in terrorism;

3°) any person who, on the national territory, deliberately organizes the travel of persons who travel to a State other than their State of residence of which they are nationals, with the intent to commit, organize or prepare acts of terrorism, or to participate therein or to provide or receive training in terrorism, or participation in other activities that facilitate these acts, including recruitment.

Article 252-4

Is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs, the act for a person of being unable to justify resources corresponding to their lifestyle, while being in regular contact with one or more persons engaging in one or more of the terrorist acts defined in this title.

Article 252-5

Constitutes an act of terrorism and punished by a penalty of imprisonment of ten years and a fine of 10,000,000 francs, the act of training or being trained in the handling of weapons or any form of combat, in the manufacture or use of explosive, incendiary, nuclear, radiological, biological or chemical substances or in the piloting of aircraft for terrorist purposes.

Article 252-6

Constitutes an act of terrorism and punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs, the act of habitually consulting, for terrorist purposes, one or more communication services of acts of terrorism.

Chapter 3

Criminal association with a view to perpetrating acts of terrorism

Article 253-1

The act of participating in a group formed or an agreement established with a view to preparing one of the terrorist acts characterized by one or more material facts is punished by imprisonment of ten years.

Article 253-2

The act of directing or organizing the group or agreement is punished by imprisonment of thirty years.

Chapter 4: Harbouring of terrorists

Article 254-1

Those who, knowing the actions of individuals committing terrorist acts or any other provided for in this title, habitually provide them with lodging, place of retreat or meeting, are punished by imprisonment of ten years.

Article 254-2

Those who knowingly harbour a person whom they know is sought by justice, for acts provided for in this title or who remove them or attempt to remove them from arrest or searches, or help them to hide or take flight, even if they are members of their families, are punished by imprisonment of ten years.

Chapter 5: Apology for terrorism

Article 255-1

Any person who, by any means, calls for or incites the commission of terrorist offences, incites ethnic, racial or religious fanaticism or uses a name, term, symbol, public expressions of support for acts of terrorism and/or terrorist groups, disseminates hate speech or promotes ideologies favourable to terrorism, reinforces ethnic and religious tensions likely to provide a basis for terrorism, or uses any other sign for the purpose of making an apology for an organization qualified as terrorist, is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 1,000,000 francs.

The penalties are increased to imprisonment of seven years and a fine of 2,000,000 francs when the acts were committed using an online public communication service.

When the acts are committed through the written or audiovisual press or online public communication, the particular provisions of the laws governing these matters are applicable with regard to the determination of the persons responsible.

Chapter 6: Recruitment of terrorists

Article 256-1

Whoever recruits a person to commit or participate in the commission of one or more of the offences provided for in this title, with the intention or knowing that the purpose of this recruitment or request is to participate in the commission of one or more offences provided for in this title, is punished by imprisonment of ten years and a fine of 20,000,000 francs.

Chapter 7: Financing of terrorism

[Law No. 2016/08 of 17 March 2016 establishing uniform law on the fight against money laundering and the financing of terrorism].

Section 1

Criminalization of the financing of terrorism Article 257-1: Financing of terrorism means:
1°) any act committed by a natural or legal person who, by any means whatsoever, directly or indirectly, has deliberately provided or collected property, funds and other financial resources with the intention of using them or knowing that they will be used, in whole or in part, for the commission of:

a)	one or more terrorist acts;

b)	one or more terrorist acts by a terrorist organization;

c)	one or more terrorist acts by a terrorist or a group of terrorists;

2°) the act by any person on Malian territory of collecting, providing, managing and making available funds, securities, financial assets, economic resources, financial services or other related services or any property whatsoever with the intention of using them or knowing that such funds, securities, financial assets, economic resources, financial services or other related services or any property whatsoever are intended to finance the travel of individuals to a third country other than their country of residence or of which they hold nationality for the purpose of perpetrating, planning, preparing or participating in terrorist acts, or to administer or receive terrorist training or simply to provide for needs related to terrorism.

The attempt to commit an offense of financing of terrorism or the act of aiding, inciting or assisting someone to commit it, or the act of facilitating its execution, also constitutes an offense of financing of terrorism.

The offense is committed whether or not the act referred to in this article occurs, or whether or not the property has been used to commit such act.

The offense is also committed by any natural or legal person who participates as an accomplice, organizes or incites others to commit the aforementioned acts.
Knowledge or intent, as elements of the aforementioned activities, may be inferred from objective factual circumstances.

Section 2

Failure or delay in reporting suspicious transactions related to the financing of terrorism

Article 257-2

When factual circumstances lead to the inference that sums of money or transactions involving sums of money may originate from a money laundering offense as defined in Article 7 of the uniform law on combating money laundering and the financing of terrorism, the suspicious transaction report provided for in Article 79 of the same law must be made immediately.

The same applies to attempted suspicious transactions.

Article 257-3

Persons, managers and agents of natural or legal persons subject to reporting obligations in the area of combating money laundering and the financing of terrorism who have intentionally failed to make the suspicious transaction report provided for in Article 79 of said law, including in the context of attempted suspicious transactions, or made a late report, when the circumstances led to the inference that the sums of money could originate from a money laundering offense as defined in Article 7 of said law, are punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of not less than 1,500,000 francs.

Persons, managers and agents of natural or legal persons referred to in Articles 5 and 6 of the law on combating money laundering and the financing of terrorism who have unintentionally:

1°) failed to make the suspicious transaction report provided for in Article 79 of the law on combating money laundering and the financing of terrorism, including in the context of attempted suspicious transactions, or made a late report;

2°) violated the provisions of Articles 16, 18 to 40 and 79 of the law on combating money laundering and the financing of terrorism.

are also punished by a fine of 750,000 francs.

Section 3: Refusal of any justification

Article 257-4

No consideration of a political, philosophical, ideological, racial, ethnic, religious nature or any other motive may be taken into account to justify the commission of the offense referred to in Article 257-1 of this Code.

Section 4: Criminal sanctions incurred by natural persons

Article 257-5

Natural persons guilty of an offense of financing of terrorism are punished by a term of imprisonment of ten years and a fine equal to at least five times the value of the property or funds that were the subject of the financing of terrorism operations.

The attempt to finance terrorism is punished by the same penalties.

Section 5: Aggravating circumstances

Article 257-6

The penalties provided for in Article 257-3 of this Code are doubled:

1° when the offense of financing of terrorism is committed habitually or by using the facilities provided by the exercise of a professional activity;

2°) when the perpetrator of the offense is in a state of recidivism. In this case, convictions pronounced abroad are taken into account to establish recidivism;

3°) when the offense of financing of terrorism is committed by an organized gang.

When the crime or offense from which the property or sums of money that were the subject of the offense of financing of terrorism originates is punished by a custodial sentence of a duration greater than that of the imprisonment incurred under Article 257-3 above, the financing of terrorism is punished by the penalties attached to the related offense of which its perpetrator had knowledge and, if this offense is accompanied by aggravating circumstances, by the penalties attached only to the circumstances of which he had knowledge.

Article 257-7

The following persons and directors or agents of natural or legal persons referred to in Articles 5 and 6 of the Uniform Act on the fight against money laundering and the financing of terrorism are punished by imprisonment of four years and a fine of 3,000,000 francs, when they have intentionally:

1°) made disclosures to the owner of the sums or to the perpetrator of the acts referred to in Article 8 of the said Act, concerning the declaration they are required to make or the action taken thereon, or failed to make the suspicious transaction report provided for in Article 79 of the said Act, including in the context of attempted suspicious transactions, or made a late declaration;

2°) destroyed or removed documents or records relating to the operations and transactions referred to in Articles 32, 33, 35 and 37 to 40 of the said Act;

3°) carried out or attempted to carry out under a false identity one of the operations referred to in the provisions of Articles 18 and 21, 26 to 34, 36, 38 to 40 and 50 to 58 of the said Act;

4°) informed, by any means, the person or persons subject to the investigation conducted for acts of financing of terrorism of which they became aware by reason of their profession or functions;

5°) made false declarations or communications when carrying out one of the operations referred to in the provisions of Articles 24 to 39 of the said Act;

6°) communicated information or documents to persons other than the judicial authorities, State agents responsible for the detection and prosecution of offences related to the financing of terrorism acting under a judicial mandate, supervisory authorities and the National Financial Intelligence Processing Unit (CENTIF);

7°) contravened the obligations to implement administrative freezing measures.

The following persons and directors or agents of natural or legal persons referred to in Articles 5 and 6 of the said Act are punished by a fine of 1,500,000 francs, when they have unintentionally:

1°) failed to make the suspicious transaction report provided for in Article 79 of the said Act;

2°) contravened the due diligence and suspicious transaction reporting obligations imposed on them by the provisions of this Title.

Section 6

Optional additional criminal sanctions incurred by natural persons

Article 257-8

Natural persons guilty of the offences defined in Articles 257-1 and 257-5 of this Title may also incur the following additional penalties:

1°) permanent ban from the national territory or for a period of seven years, pronounced against any convicted foreigner;

2°) residence ban, for a period of seven years, in certain administrative districts;

3°) prohibition from leaving the national territory and withdrawal of passport, for a period of five years;

4°) deprivation of civil and political rights, for a period of five years;

5°) prohibition from driving motorized land, sea and air vehicles and withdrawal of permits or licenses, for a period of ten years;

6°) permanent or ten-year prohibition from exercising the profession or activity in connection with which the offence was committed and prohibition from exercising a public function;

7°) prohibition from issuing cheques other than those which allow the withdrawal of funds by the drawer from the drawee or those which are certified, and prohibition from using payment cards for a period of five to ten years;

8°) prohibition from possessing or carrying a weapon subject to authorization for a period of ten years;

9°) confiscation of all or part of the convicted person's assets of lawful origin;

10°) confiscation of the property or thing which was used or intended to commit the offence or the thing which is the product thereof, with the exception of objects capable of restitution.

Article 257-9

No sanction pronounced for an offence of financing of terrorism may be accompanied by a suspended sentence.

Section 7: Criminal sanctions incurred by legal persons

Article 257-10

Legal persons other than the State, on whose account or for whose benefit an offence of financing of terrorism or one of the related offences has been committed by one of their organs or representatives, are punished by a fine at a rate equal to five times that incurred by natural persons, without prejudice to the conviction of the latter as perpetrators or accomplices of the same acts.

Legal persons other than the State may, in addition, be sentenced to one or more of the following penalties:

1°) exclusion from public contracts, permanently or for a period of up to ten years;

2°) confiscation of the property which was used or intended to commit the offence or the property which is the product thereof;

3°) placement under judicial supervision, for a period of up to five years;

4°) prohibition, permanently or for a period of up to ten years, from exercising directly or indirectly one or more professional or social activities in connection with which the offence was committed;

5°) permanent or up to ten-year closure of the establishments or one of the establishments of the enterprise which was used to commit the incriminated acts;

6°) dissolution, when they were created to commit the incriminated acts;

7°) posting of the decision pronounced or dissemination thereof, by the written press or by any audiovisual means of communication, at the expense of the convicted legal person.

The sanctions provided for in points 3, 4, 5, 6 and 7 of the second paragraph of this Article are not applicable to financial institutions subject to a supervisory authority with disciplinary powers.

The competent supervisory authority, referred to by the public prosecutor of any prosecution initiated against a financial institution, may take appropriate sanctions, in accordance with the specific legislative and regulatory texts in force.

Section 8: Grounds for exemption from criminal sanctions

Article 257-11

Any person guilty, on the one hand, of participation in an association or agreement with a view to committing one of the offences provided for in this Chapter and, on the other hand, of aiding, inciting or advising a natural or legal person with a view to executing them or facilitating their execution, is exempted from criminal sanctions if, having disclosed the existence of this agreement, association, aid or advice to the judicial authority, they thereby enable, on the one hand, the identification of the other persons involved and, on the other hand, the prevention of the commission of the offences of money laundering and financing of terrorism.

Section 9: Grounds for mitigation of criminal sanctions

Article 257-12

The penalties incurred by any person, perpetrator or accomplice of one of the offences provided for in this Chapter who, before any prosecution, enables or facilitates the identification of the other guilty parties or, after the initiation of prosecution, enables or facilitates the arrest of the latter, are reduced by half. In addition, the said person is exempted from the fine and, where applicable, from accessory measures and optional additional penalties.

In matters of financing of terrorism, when the penalty incurred is life imprisonment, it is reduced to twenty years.

Section 10

Mandatory confiscation of funds and other financial resources related to the financing of terrorism

Article 257-13

In all cases of conviction for the offense of financing of terrorism or attempt, the courts shall order the confiscation for the benefit of the Public Treasury, of funds and other financial resources related to the offense as well as any movable or immovable property intended for or having served in the commission of said offense.

The State may allocate the funds and other financial resources as well as the property referred to in the first paragraph above, to a fund for combating organized crime or to the compensation of victims of the offenses provided for in Article 8 of the uniform law relating to the fight against money laundering and the financing of terrorism or their successors in title.

The decision ordering a confiscation shall identify and locate the funds, property and other financial resources concerned.

When the funds, property and other financial resources to be confiscated cannot be produced, their confiscation may be ordered by value.

Any person who claims to have a right over property or funds that have been the subject of a confiscation may, to be restored to their rights, seize the court that rendered the confiscation decision within a period of six months, from the notification of the decision.

The confiscation of illicit proceeds and property shall be pronounced even in the absence of a criminal conviction by reason of the extinction of the public prosecution or any other cause blocking the exercise of the public prosecution.

Section 11

Implementation of targeted financial sanctions in matters of financing of terrorism and financing of the proliferation of weapons of mass destruction

Article 257-14

It is strictly prohibited for persons subject to obligations in matters of combating money laundering and the financing of terrorism and any person or entity located in Mali, to make available directly or indirectly, funds and other property, economic resources or financial services and other related services, directly or indirectly, wholly or jointly, for the benefit of designated persons or entities; and persons and entities acting on behalf of or on instructions from persons or entities designated pursuant to United Nations Security Council Resolutions relating to terrorism, its financing or the financing of proliferation, except contrary authorization, in accordance with said Resolutions.
