It is also strictly prohibited for the persons referred to in paragraph 1 above, to provide or continue to provide services to designated persons or entities, to persons and entities acting on behalf of or on instructions from designated persons or entities, or to use them for their benefit.

It is also prohibited for persons and entities to carry out or participate, intentionally, in operations having the purpose or effect of circumventing, directly or indirectly, the provisions of this article.

Article 257-15

Obligated persons and all persons or entities who receive an order from a client to execute on their behalf a transaction for the benefit of a person, body or entity subject to a freezing measure, shall suspend the execution of this order and inform, without delay, the competent authority.

The funds or financial instruments relating to this order shall be frozen, unless the competent authority authorizes the transaction.

Article 257-16

Persons and directors or agents of obligated natural or legal persons who act intentionally in violation of the prohibitions and suspension obligations referred to in Articles 257-14 and 257-15 above shall be punished by imprisonment of four years and a fine of 3,000,000 francs.

When they act unintentionally, the penalty is a fine of 1,500,000 francs.

LIVRE III

CRIMES AND OFFENSES AGAINST PERSONS TITLE I: INTERNATIONAL CRIMES
[Rome Statute of 17 July 1998]

Chapitre 1 : Du génocide

Article 311-1

The crime of genocide means any of the following acts committed with intent to destroy, in whole or in part, a national, ethnical, racial or religious group, as such:

a)	killing members of the group;

b)	causing serious bodily or mental harm to members of the group;

c)	deliberately inflicting on the group conditions of life calculated to bring about its physical destruction in whole or in part;

d)	imposing measures intended to prevent births within the group;

e)	forcibly transferring children of the group to another group.

Chapter 2: Crimes against humanity

Article 312-1

A crime against humanity means any of the following acts when committed as part of a widespread or systematic attack directed against any civilian population, with knowledge of the attack:

a)	murder;

b)	extermination;

c)	enslavement;

d)	deportation or forcible transfer of population;

e)	imprisonment or other severe deprivation of physical liberty in violation of fundamental rules of international law;

f)	torture;

g)	rape, sexual slavery, enforced prostitution, forced pregnancy, enforced sterilization, or any other form of sexual violence of comparable gravity;

h)	persecution against any identifiable group or collectivity on political, racial, national, ethnic, cultural, religious, gender as defined in paragraph 3, or other grounds that are universally recognized as impermissible under international law, in connection with any act referred to in this paragraph;

i)	enforced disappearance of persons;

j)	the crime of apartheid;

k)	other inhumane acts of a similar character intentionally causing great suffering, or serious injury to body or to mental or physical health.

2.	For the purpose of paragraph 1:

a)	"attack directed against any civilian population" means a course of conduct involving the multiple commission of acts referred to in paragraph 1 against any civilian population, pursuant to or in furtherance of a State or organizational policy to commit such attack;

b)	"extermination" includes the intentional infliction of conditions of life, inter alia the deprivation of access to food and medicine, calculated to bring about the destruction of part of a population;

c)	"enslavement" means the exercise of any or all of the powers attaching to the right of ownership over a person and includes the exercise of such power in the course of trafficking in persons, in particular women and children;

d)	"deportation or forcible transfer of population" means forced displacement of the persons concerned by expulsion or other coercive acts from the area in which they are lawfully present, without grounds permitted under international law;

e)	"torture" means the intentional infliction of severe pain or suffering, whether physical or mental,#### Chapter 2: Crimes against humanity

c)	"enslavement" means the exercise of any or all of the powers attaching to the right of ownership over a person and includes the exercise of such power in the course of trafficking in persons, in particular women and children;

d)	"deportation or forcible transfer of population" means forced displacement of the persons concerned by expulsion or other coercive acts from the area in which they are lawfully present, without grounds permitted under international law;

e)	"torture" means the intent#### Chapter 2: Crimes against humanity

a)	murder;

b)	extermination;

c)	enslavement;

d)	deportation or forcible transfer of population;

e)	imprisonment or other severe deprivation of physical liberty in violation of fundamental rules of international law;

f)	torture;

h)	persecution against any identifiable group or collectivity on political, racial, national, ethnic, cultural, religious, gender as defined in paragraph 3, or other grounds that are universally recognized as impermissible under international law, in connection with any act referred to in this paragraph;

i)	enforced disappearance of persons;

j)	the crime of apartheid;

k)	other inhumane acts of a similar character intentionally causing great suffering, or serious injury to body or to mental or physical health.

Chapter 3: War crimes

Articles 313-1: "War crimes" means:

a) the following offences:

j) wilful killing;

ii) torture or inhuman treatment, including biological experiments;

iii) wilfully causing great suffering, or serious injury to body or health;

iv) extensive destruction and appropriation of property, not justified by military necessity and carried out unlawfully and wantonly;

v) compelling a prisoner of war or other protected person to serve in the forces of a hostile Power;

vi) wilfully depriving a prisoner of war or other protected person of the rights of fair and regular trial;

vii) unlawful deportation or transfer or unlawful confinement;

viii) taking of hostages.

b) other serious violations of the laws and customs applicable to international armed conflict, within the established framework of international law, namely, any of the following acts:

i) intentionally directing attacks against the civilian population as such or against individual civilians not taking direct part in hostilities;

ii) intentionally directing attacks against civilian objects, that is to say, objects which are not military objectives;

iii) intentionally directing attacks against personnel, installations, material, units or vehicles involved in a humanitarian assistance or peacekeeping mission in accordance with the Charter of the United Nations, as long as they are entitled to the protection given to civilians or civilian objects under the international law of armed conflict;

iv) intentionally launching an attack in the knowledge that such attack will cause incidental loss of life or injury to civilians or damage to civilian objects or widespread, long-term and severe damage to the natural environment which would be clearly excessive in relation to the concrete and direct overall military advantage anticipated;

v) attacking or bombarding, by whatever means, towns, villages, dw#### Chapter 3: War crimes

i) intentionally directing attacks against the civilian population as such or against individual civilians not taking direct part in hostilities;

ii) intentionally directing attacks against civilian objects, that is to say, objects which are not military objectives;

iii) intentionally directing attacks against personnel, installations, material, units or vehicles involved in a humanitarian assistance or peacekeeping mission in accordance with the Charter of the United Nations, as long as they are entitled to the protection given to civilians or civilian objects under the international law of armed conflict;

iv) intentionally launching an attack in the knowledge that such attack will cause incidental loss of life or injury to civilians or damage to civilian objects or widespread, long-term and severe damage to the natural environment which would be clearly excessive in relation to the concrete and direct overall military advantage anticipated;

v) attacking or bombarding, by whatever means, towns, villages, dwellings or buildings which are un#### Chapter 3: War crimes

Chapter 4: Common Provisions

Articles 314-1: All crimes provided for in this title are punishable by death. They are not subject to any statute of limitations and cannot be the subject of amnesty or pardon. Likewise, any sentence pronounced in punishment of these crimes is not subject to any statute of limitations and cannot be the subject of any amnesty or pardon.

Articles 314-2: By way of derogation from the provisions of Title II of Book 1 of this Code, a person is criminally responsible and may be punished for a crime provided for in this title if:

a) he or she commits such a crime, whether individually, jointly with another person or through another person, whether or not that other person is criminally responsible;

b) he or she orders, solicits or encourages the commission of such a crime, where there is commission or attempted commission of that crime;

c) for the purpose of facilitating the commission of such a crime, he or she aids, abets or otherwise assists in the commission or attempted commission of that crime, including by providing the means for its commission;

d) he or she in any other way contributes to the commission or attempted commission of such a crime by a group of persons acting with a common purpose. Such contribution shall be intentional and shall either:

i) be made with the aim of furthering the criminal activity or criminal purpose of the group, where such activity or purpose involves the commission of a crime within the Rome Statute; or

ii) be made in the knowledge of the intention of the group to commit the crime;

e) in respect of the crime of genocide, he or she directly and publicly incites others to commit it;

f) he or she attempts to commit such a crime by acts which, by their substantial character, constitute a commencement of execution but the crime is not completed due to circumstances independent of his or her will.

However, a person who abandons the effort to commit the crime or otherwise prevents its completion cannot be punished for the attempt if he or she has completely and voluntarily renounced the criminal purpose.

Article 314-3

A military commander or a person effectively acting as a military commander is criminally responsible for crimes provided for in this title committed by forces under his or her effective command and control, or effective authority and control, as the case may be, where he or she failed to exercise control properly over such forces in cases where:

a) that military commander or person either knew or, owing to the circumstances at the time, should have known that the forces were committing or about to commit such crimes; and

b) that military commander or person failed to take all necessary and reasonable measures within his or her power to prevent or repress their commission or to submit the matter to the competent authorities for investigation and prosecution.

Article 314-4

With respect to superior and subordinate relationships not described in the preceding article, a superior is criminally responsible for crimes committed by subordinates under his or her effective authority and control, where he or she failed to exercise control properly over such subordinates in cases where:

a) the superior either knew that the subordinates were committing or about to commit such crimes or consciously disregarded information which clearly indicated as much;

b) the crimes concerned activities that were within the effective responsibility and control of the superior; and

c) the superior failed to take all necessary and reasonable measures within his or her power to prevent or repress their commission or to submit the matter to the competent authorities for investigation and prosecution.

Articles 314-5: The fact that a crime provided for in this title was committed pursuant to an order of a Government or of a superior, whether military or civilian, shall not relieve the person who committed it of criminal responsibility, unless:

a) the person was under a legal obligation to obey orders of the Government or the superior in question;

b) the person did not know that the order was unlawful; and

c) the order was not manifestly unlawful.

For the purposes of this article, orders to commit genocide or a crime against humanity or a war crime are manifestly unlawful.

Articles 314-6: The prohibition on committing in any manner whatsoever a crime provided for in this title applies to all equally, without any distinction based on official capacity.

In particular, official capacity as Head of State or Government, member of a Government or parliament, elected representative or government official shall in no case exempt a person from criminal responsibility for a crime of genocide, crime against humanity or war crime, nor shall it constitute in itself a ground for reduction of sentence, notwithstanding any other law or any amnesty provision.

Chapter 5: Mercenarism

Articles 315-1: Shall be punished by imprisonment of ten years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs:

1°) any person, specially recruited to fight in an armed conflict and who is neither a national of a State party to the said armed conflict, nor a member of the armed forces of that State, nor sent on mission by a State other than one of those party to the conflict as a member of the armed forces of the said State, who takes or attempts to take a direct part in the hostilities for the purpose of obtaining personal advantage;

2°) any person, specially recruited to take part in a concerted act of violence aimed at overthrowing the institutions or undermining the territorial integrity of a State and who is neither a national of the State against which such act is directed, nor a member of the armed forces of the said State, nor sent on mission by a State, who takes or attempts to take part in such an act for the purpose of obtaining personal advantage.

Articles 315-2: Shall be punished by imprisonment of twenty years and a fine of 10,000,000 francs, any person who directs or organizes a group whose purpose is the recruitment, employment, remuneration, equipment or military training of a person defined in the preceding article.

TITLE II: OFFENCES AGAINST THE HUMAN PERSON AND GENDER

Chapter 1: Offences against the physical or mental integrity of the person

Section 1: Homicides

Article 321-1: Homicide committed voluntarily is classified as murder

Any murder committed with premeditation or lying in wait within the meaning of articles 132-44 and 132-45 of this Code is classified as assassination.

The murder of legitimate, natural or adoptive father and mother or any other legitimate ascendant is classified as parricide.

Infanticide is the murder or assassination of a newborn child.

Feminicide is the murder or assassination of a woman by reason of her status as a woman.

Any attempt on the life of a person by means of substances that can cause death more or less promptly is classified as poisoning; in whatever manner such substances were used or administered and whatever the consequences.

Article 321-2

Any person guilty of assassination, parricide or poisoning is punished by death.

However, the mother, principal perpetrator or accomplice in the assassination or murder of her newborn child is punished by life imprisonment or imprisonment of twenty years, but this provision may not apply to her co-perpetrators or accomplices.

In all cases, the mother who reoffends is sentenced to death.

All wrongdoers, whatever their designation, who, in carrying out their crime, employ torture or commit acts of barbarity are punished as guilty of assassination.

Article 321-3

Murder carries the death penalty when it has preceded, accompanied or followed another crime or offence.

Murder also carries the death penalty when its purpose is to facilitate the escape or ensure the impunity of the perpetrators or accomplices of that crime or offence.

Similarly, murder carries the death penalty when it is committed for ritual or speculative purposes or when it is committed by immolation by fire.

In addition, a definitive prohibition from exercising the professional activity on the occasion of or in the exercise of which the offence was committed is pronounced against the person guilty of murder or assassination.

In all other cases, the person guilty of murder is punished by life imprisonment. A residence ban of twenty years may also be pronounced.

Article 321-4

Blows, injuries and intentional violence, exercised without intention to cause death, but having nevertheless caused it, are punished by twenty years' imprisonment and a residence ban of twenty years.

In case of premeditation or ambush, the penalty is life imprisonment.

Article 321-5

Involuntary homicide committed or caused by clumsiness, negligence, inattention or failure to observe regulations, is punished by five years' imprisonment and a fine of 500,000 francs.

Section 2

Violation of tomb and sepulchre - removal and trafficking of organs or human bones

Article 321-6

Any individual who is guilty of violation of tomb or sepulchre or desecration of corpse, even buried, is punished by five years' imprisonment and a fine of 200,000 francs.

Article 321-7

When the violation of tomb, sepulchre or desecration of corpse has been followed by mutilation or removal of organ, the penalty is five years' imprisonment and a fine of 1,000,000 francs.

Article 321-8

Whoever engages in trafficking of human bones is punished by two years' imprisonment and a fine of 500,000 francs.

If this trafficking concerns a human organ, the penalty is ten years' imprisonment without prejudice to more severe penalties in case of homicide.

Section 3

Blows and injuries, violence, torture Paragraph 1: Intentional blows and injuries

Article 321-9

Any individual who, intentionally, strikes blows or causes injuries or commits any other violence or assault, if these kinds of violence have resulted in illness or incapacity for personal work for more than twenty days, is punished by five years' imprisonment and a fine of 500,000 francs.

If there has been premeditation or ambush, the penalty is ten years' imprisonment.

When the violence, injuries or blows are followed by mutilation, amputation, deprivation of the use of a limb or a sense, blindness, loss of an eye or other infirmities or illnesses, loss of a foetus, the penalty is ten years' imprisonment.

The same penalty is applicable if the violence, intentional blows and injuries, on a person reduced to slavery, result in incapacity of more than twenty days.

If there has been premeditation or ambush, the penalty is twenty years' imprisonment.

When the blows, injuries or violence mentioned in this section were inflicted by the offender on the occasion of or in the exercise of his profession, a suspension of up to ten years from the exercise of that profession is pronounced in addition.

In the cases provided for in paragraphs 2, 3, 4 and 5, a residence ban of ten years may be pronounced.

Article 321-10

When the injuries, blows, violence or assaults have not caused any illness or incapacity for personal work of the kind mentioned in the preceding article, the offender is punished by two years' imprisonment and a fine of 100,000 francs.

If there has been premeditation or ambush, the imprisonment is five years and the fine 150,000 francs.

Intentional violence, blows and injuries, on a person reduced to slavery, having resulted in incapacity of less than twenty days, are punished by five years' imprisonment and a fine of 200,000 francs.

A residence ban of ten years may in addition be pronounced.

Paragraph 2: Violence and intentional blows and injuries by reason of gender

Article 321-11

Whoever, by reason of gender, has intentionally struck blows not having caused injuries to another person is punished by two years' imprisonment and a fine of 150,000 francs.

Whoever, by reason of gender, has intentionally struck blows having caused injuries to another person is punished by five years' imprisonment and a fine of 1,000,000 francs.

In case of injury, the penalty is ten years' imprisonment and a fine of 1,000,000 francs, if the victim is married civilly or before a minister of religion to the perpetrator, or if the perpetrator is an intimate partner of the victim, his or her ascendant or a close relative.

The penalty is ten years' imprisonment when the violence, injuries or blows by reason of gender have been followed by mutilation, amputation, deprivation of the use of a limb or a sense, blindness, loss of an eye or other infirmities or illnesses.

In the cases specified in paragraphs 3 and 4 above, if there has been premeditation or ambush, the penalty is ten years' imprisonment.

In case of injury, the penalty is ten years' imprisonment and a fine of 1,000,000 francs, if the victim is in a state of particular vulnerability by reason of gender, age, illness, physical or mental disability, or state of pregnancy.

If there has been premeditation or ambush, the penalty is fifteen years' imprisonment.

The penalty is twenty years' imprisonment when the blows and injuries have resulted in negative consequences on the pregnancy, on the health or life of the foetus.

In the cases provided for in paragraphs 3, 6 and 8 above, a residence ban of ten years may be pronounced.

Paragraph 3: Hazing

Sub-paragraph 1: Incrimination

Article 321-12

Apart from cases of violence, threats or sexual assault, the act by a person of causing another, against his or her will or not, to undergo or commit humiliating or degrading acts or to consume alcohol or any other intoxicating substance excessively, during events or gatherings linked to school, sports and socio-educational environments is punished by the penalties provided for in the first paragraph of Article 321-11 above.

Article 321-13

The offence defined in Article 321-12 above is punished by ten years' imprisonment when it is committed on a person whose particular vulnerability, due to age, illness, infirmity, physical or mental deficiency or state of pregnancy, is apparent or known to its perpetrator.

Sub-paragraph 2: Criminal liability of legal persons

Article 321-14

Legal persons declared criminally liable, under the conditions provided for by Article 121-2 of this Code, for the offence defined in this paragraph incur, in addition to the fine according to the procedures provided for by Article 131-14 of this Code, the penalties provided for by Article 131-15 of this Code.

The prohibition mentioned in paragraph 2° of Article 131-15 of this Code relates to the activity in the exercise or on the occasion of the exercise of which the offence was committed.

Paragraphe 4 : Torture

Article 321-15

The term "torture" means any act by which severe pain or suffering, whether physical or mental, is intentionally inflicted on a person for such purposes as obtaining from him or a third person information or a confession, punishing him for an act he or a third person has committed or is suspected of having committed, or intimidating or coercing him or a third person, or for any reason based on discrimination of any kind, when such pain or suffering is inflicted by or at the instigation of or with the consent or acquiescence of a public official or other person acting in an official capacity. This term does not extend to pain or suffering arising only from, inherent in or incidental to lawful sanctions.

Any act of torture is punished by imprisonment of five years.

If the violence has resulted in mutilation, amputation, deprivation of the use of a limb or a sense, blindness, loss of an eye or other infirmity or illness, the penalty is imprisonment of ten years.

If death has resulted, the death penalty is applicable.

Torture committed on a person reduced to slavery is punished by imprisonment of ten years.

If the violence on a person reduced to slavery has resulted in mutilation, amputation, deprivation of the use of a limb or a sense, blindness, loss of an eye, or other infirmity or illness, the penalty is imprisonment of twenty years.

When torture on a person reduced to slavery has been committed on a minor under fifteen years of age, on a pregnant woman, or if it has resulted in the death of the victim, the provisions of paragraph 4 are applicable.

Article 321-16

The order of a superior or a public authority cannot be invoked to justify torture.

Article 321-17

No exceptional circumstances whatsoever, whether a state of armed conflict or threat of armed conflict, internal political instability or any other state of emergency, may be invoked to justify torture.

Paragraphe 5 : Involuntary injuries

Article 321-18

Anyone who, through clumsiness, imprudence, inattention, negligence or failure to observe regulations, has involuntarily inflicted blows, caused injuries, or occasioned illnesses to another, is punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 300,000 francs.
When the fault, even the clumsiness, imprudence, inattention, negligence or failure to observe regulations of a health care worker has caused the death of a patient, caused involuntary injuries or damage of a severity resulting in dependency, the perpetrator, co-perpetrator and accomplice of the offence are punished by the penalties provided for in Articles 321-5 and in the first paragraph above.

Paragraphe 6 : Abortion

Article 321-19

Any abortion is prohibited which consists in the use of means or substances with a view to provoking the premature expulsion of the foetus, whatever the moment of the pregnancy at which this expulsion is performed for any reason other than the safeguarding of the life of the woman with the exception of incest, rape and the state of health of the mother.

Article 321-20

Except for abortions performed for therapeutic reasons or endangerment of the physical or psychological health of the mother or her life or that of the foetus, abortion voluntarily attempted or obtained in any manner whatsoever, whether by the woman or by a third party, even with her consent, is punished by imprisonment of five years, a fine of 1,000,000 francs and a residence ban of ten years.

An aggravating circumstance is the act of imposing on a woman or girl to have an abortion by fraud, coercion or violence.

Anyone who is guilty of an abortion in the aforementioned aggravating circumstance is punished by imprisonment of ten years; if death ensues, the penalty is imprisonment of twenty years.

Article 321-21

Doctors, midwives, dental surgeons, pharmacists, as well as students or employees in pharmacy, herbalists, bandage makers, dealers in surgical instruments, nurses, masseurs, who have indicated, facilitated or practised the means of provoking abortion as defined in the preceding article, are sentenced to the penalties provided for in Article 321-20 above.

Suspension for at least five years or temporary or permanent prohibition from practising their profession may, in addition, be pronounced against the guilty parties.

Anyone who contravenes the prohibition from practising his profession pronounced pursuant to the preceding paragraph is punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 1,200,000 francs.

Paragraphe 7 : Treatment of ordeals and other practices harmful to health

Sous-paragraphe 1: Administration of substances harmful to health

Article 321-22

Anyone who, without criminal intent, voluntarily administers to a person substances or engages in practices or manoeuvres on him, even with his consent, which have caused or could have caused an illness or incapacity for personal work equal to twenty-one days, is punished by imprisonment of three years, a fine of 2,000,000 francs and a residence ban of five years.

If it results in an illness or total incapacity for personal work exceeding twenty-one days, the penalty of imprisonment is five years and the fine 5,000,000 francs.

If it results in an illness appearing incurable or permanent incapacity, or the loss of use of an organ, the penalty is imprisonment of ten years. A residence ban of ten years may be pronounced.

If death ensues, the penalty is imprisonment of twenty years and a residence ban of ten years.
In all the cases provided for in paragraphs 2, 3 and 4 above, the court seized may in addition pronounce the prohibition from exercising family rights, the residence ban and the professional prohibition for a period which may not exceed five years.

Article 321-23

When the acts specified in Article 321-22 above are committed by an ascendant, descendant, spouse or successor of the victim or a person having authority over him or having custody of him, the penalties are increased to:

1°) five years' imprisonment in the case provided for in paragraph 1; 2°) ten years' imprisonment in the case provided for in paragraph 2; 3°) twenty years' imprisonment in the case provided for in paragraph 3;
4°) thirty years' imprisonment in the case provided for in paragraph 4.

The court seized may in addition pronounce the prohibition from exercising family rights for a period between five years and ten years.

Sous-paragraphe 2: Voluntary transmission of HIV

[Law No. 06-028 of 29 June 2006 establishing the rules relating to the prevention, care and control of HIV/AIDS]

I.	General provisions

1°) Test results

Article 321-24

Any person having a presence of HIV or anti-HIV antibodies in his body during the screening test is seropositive.

Article 321-25

Any screening test result is confidential and may only be given to the following persons:

1°) the person who has undergone the test;

2°) either parent of a minor child who has been tested;

3°) the guardian in the case of incapable persons or orphans who have undergone the test;

4°) the judicial authority having legally required the test.

2°) Disclosure to spouses and sexual partners

Article 321-26

Any person living with HIV is required to disclose their HIV serostatus to their spouse or sexual partner as soon as possible. This period may not exceed six full weeks from the date on which they learned of their HIV serostatus.

In the event that the person whose serostatus has just become known does not voluntarily comply with the disclosure obligation provided for in the first paragraph of this article within the prescribed period, the doctor or any other paramedical personnel of the health establishment, after having informed them, may make the disclosure to the spouse or sexual partner, without violating the provisions relating to confidentiality, provided for by the laws in force.

3°) Disclosure to minors and protected adults

Article 321-27

The minor is informed of their serostatus and of the acts and examinations necessary for their state of health, according to their age and their faculties of understanding. The doctor or any paramedical personnel shall ensure in particular that the disclosure is made and that the means implemented are adapted to any difficulties in communication and understanding of the minor.

The protected adult benefits from appropriate information. The members of their family are informed and the doctor or any paramedical personnel shall ensure in particular that the disclosure to the family is made and that the means implemented are adapted to any difficulties in communication and understanding of the family members.

The persons referred to in the preceding paragraph are bound by professional secrecy.

However, for legitimate and exceptional reasons, a seropositive minor or protected adult may be left in ignorance of their serostatus for as long as the doctor or paramedical personnel deem it necessary and this situation does not create a risk for the minor, the protected adult or for others.

4°) Discriminatory acts

Article 321-28

Any discrimination, in whatever form, against a person whose HIV seropositivity is real or supposed, particularly in matters of job application, hiring, promotion and retirement is prohibited.

Article 321-29

In addition, any discrimination is prohibited: 1°) in school and university establishments;
2°) in health establishments;

3°) relating to the right to travel and housing;

4°) relating to conditions of access to public and elective functions; 5°) relating to conditions of access to credit and insurance services.
II.	Penalties incurred by infected persons and practitioners

Article 321-30

Is punished by a fine of 1,000,000 francs, anyone who, having knowledge of their serostatus, refrains from informing their spouse or sexual partner.

Article 321-31

Is punished by imprisonment of ten years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs, anyone who, having knowledge of their seropositive status, refrains from informing their spouse or sexual partner and contaminates them.
If death results from this contamination, the perpetrator is punished by imprisonment of thirty years.
If the contamination results from rape, the perpetrator is punished by imprisonment of thirty years.

Article 321-32

Anyone who, knowingly, transmits or attempts to transmit, by any means whatsoever, HIV to any other person, is punished by imprisonment of three years and a fine of 20,000,000 francs.

If this results in contamination of at least two persons, the penalty is life imprisonment.

Article 321-33

Is punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 300,000 francs anyone who, through clumsiness, imprudence, inattention, negligence or non-observance of regulations, including directives established in this matter, involuntarily infects another person with HIV in the course of practicing their profession.

Article 321-34

Is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 4,000,000 francs, any person who practices traditional medicine in matters of HIV/AIDS without prior approval.

Article 321-35

Is punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 500,000 francs, any person guilty of violation of the confidentiality rules provided for in articles 321-26 and 321-27 of this Code.

Article 321-36

Is punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 2,000,000 francs, any person guilty of one of the discriminatory acts provided for in articles 321-28 and 321-29 of this Code.

III.	Penalties incurred by legal persons

Article 321-37

If the perpetrator is a legal person, the fines provided for in articles 321-35 and 321-36 above are increased fivefold.

The Court may, in addition, order the suspension or revocation of the perpetrator's authorization to practice or the withdrawal of the establishment authorization when the responsibility of a health establishment is engaged.

Sub-paragraph 3: Violence against health care

Article 321-38: Without prejudice to the provisions of article 313.1

b) ix) c) iv, of this Code, is punished by imprisonment of ten years and a fine of 1,000,000 francs, anyone who in time of peace, armed conflict, or internal troubles and tensions exercises violence against health personnel, patients, medical structures, medical transport means.

Article 321-39

By violence against health personnel is meant the act of killing, injuring, abducting, harassing, threatening, intimidating and robbing persons because they are performing their medical tasks.

Article 321-40

By violence against patients is meant the act of killing, injuring, harassing or intimidating patients or persons attempting to have access to health care; blocking or delaying access to health care; deliberately failing in the duty to assist or refusing to provide assistance; practicing discrimination in access to health care or in the quality of care; and interrupting medical care.

Article 321-41

By violence against medical structures is meant bombardments, artillery and light weapons fire, looting, break-ins, encirclement maneuvers and any other act forcibly hindering the proper functioning of medical structures.

Article 321-42

By violence against medical transport means is meant the act of attacking and stealing medical vehicles, or hindering their movements.

Article 321-43

Health personnel includes: persons with professional training in health care, including doctors, nursing personnel, paramedical personnel, physiotherapists and pharmacists; persons who work in hospitals, dispensaries and first aid posts, ambulance drivers, hospital administrators or personnel who work in the community in a professional capacity; personnel and volunteers of the International Red Cross and Red Crescent Movement contributing to the provision of health care; medical personnel of the armed forces; personnel of international and non-governmental organizations active in the field of health; first responders.

Article 321-44

The wounded and sick are all civilian or military persons who need medical assistance and who refrain from any act of hostility. Also included in this category are pregnant or parturient women, newborns and the infirm.

Article 321-45

Medical structures are hospitals, laboratories, dispensaries, first aid posts, blood transfusion centres, community health centres, reference health centres and faith-based centres as well as medical equipment depots and pharmacies of these establishments.

Article 321-46

Medical transport means are ambulances, medical ships or aircraft, whether civilian or military, and vehicles transporting the wounded and sick, health personnel and medical equipment or material. This term covers all vehicles used for medical purposes, even if they are not exclusively assigned to medical transport and under the control of a competent authority of a party to a conflict, including private cars used to transport the wounded and sick to a health structure, vehicles transporting medicines and medical equipment and vehicles transporting medical personnel to their place of work.

Sub-paragraph 4: Illicit supply of medical products

Article 321-47

Whoever engages in the supply or offer of supply, including brokerage, trafficking, including storage, importation or exportation of counterfeit medical products, active substances, excipients, elements, materials and accessories is punished by imprisonment of ten years and a fine of 50,000,000 Francs.

Article 321-48

Natural or legal persons guilty of the offence of supply, offer of supply, including brokerage, trafficking, storage, importation, exportation of counterfeit medical products, active substances, excipients, elements, materials and accessories also incur the additional penalty of prohibition from engaging in commercial activity, brokerage or trading in the medical field for a period not exceeding five years.

Sub-paragraph 5: Illegal practice of health professions

Article 321-49: Illegally practices the health professions

1.	any person who habitually takes part or by continued direction, even in the presence of a practitioner, in the practice of medicine, pharmacy, dentistry, midwifery, nursing by consultation, personal act or any other processes, whatever they may be, without holding a diploma or other equivalent title required for the practice of the profession concerned or without fulfilling the other conditions set by the laws establishing the relevant professional Orders;

2.	any person who, even holding a regular title, goes beyond the powers conferred upon them by law, that is to say who exceeds the limits of their competences and reserved acts not falling within their profession;

3.	any practitioner who practices their profession during the period of a temporary or permanent prohibition from practicing it and without fulfilling the conditions or satisfying the obligations provided for by the directives of the West African Economic and Monetary Union (WAEMU) relating to free movement;

4.	any person who practices the health professions without satisfying the legal conditions for practice:
who makes a diagnosis or treats an illness or performs a professional medical, paramedical or pharmacy act, habitually or by continued direction, without having a diploma and authorization to practice it;
who exceeds their competences and performs acts reserved to the doctor, pharmacist, dental surgeon, midwife and nurse, notably beauty professionals, herbalists and others in the context of so-called parallel medicines;	who falsely claims to be a doctor: usurpers of titles
; and	who engages in untimely and misleading advertising to attract or increase clientele.

Article 321-50

The Orders of doctors, pharmacists, dental surgeons, midwives and nurses, interested associations and unions may bring matters before the courts by way of direct summons with regard to the illegal practice of their professions but may also constitute themselves as civil parties in any prosecution initiated by the Public Prosecutor.

Article 321-51

The illegal practice of the professions of doctors, pharmacists, dental surgeons, midwives and nurses is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.
In all cases, the confiscation of equipment or any other products or objects having enabled the illegal practice is ordered. Medicines or products seized and confiscated may be destroyed by the competent services.

Section 4: Justifications, excuses

Article 321-52

If the murder and violence of the kind defined by Article 321-4, and paragraphs 2, 3 and 5 of Article 321-9 of this Code were provoked by serious violence against persons, the penalty is that of the first paragraph of said Article 321-9.

Article 321-53

The crimes mentioned in the preceding article are excusable and punished by the penalties provided for in the first paragraph of Article 321-9 of this Code, if they were committed in repelling, during the day, intrusion into a dwelling or its outbuildings, notably by scaling or breaking through walls, fences or entrances.

Article 321-54: Parricide is never excusable

Article 321-55

A person who commits homicide, inflicts injuries, strikes blows or exercises violence is not criminally liable when these were commanded by present necessity or legitimate defence of oneself or others.

Article 321-56

Included in the case of present necessity of defence are the following two cases:

1°) if the homicide was committed, if the injuries were inflicted and the blows were struck or if the violence was exercised in repelling, during the night, intrusion into a dwelling or its outbuildings, notably by scaling or breaking through fences, walls or enclosures.

2°) if the act took place in defending oneself against the perpetrators of thefts or pillaging carried out with violence.

Section 5: Threats, blackmail

Article 321-57

Whoever, by words, writings, gestures or conventional signs, threatens another with an attack against their person which would be punishable by the death penalty or imprisonment, is punished by imprisonment of three years.

If the threats are made with an order or under condition, or by reason of gender, the penalty is imprisonment of five years and a fine of 500,000 francs.

Whoever, by words, writings, gestures or conventional signs, threatens another with blows, injuries, violence or voluntary assault other than those provided for in paragraphs 2, 3, 5 and 6 of Article 321-9 of this Code, if the threat is made with an order or under condition, is punished by imprisonment of three months.

Article 321-58: Constitute aggravating circumstances the fact

1°) for the victim to be in a state of particular vulnerability by reason of their age, an illness, a physical or psychological disability or their state of pregnancy;

2°) for the perpetrator to be an ascendant, a close relative, a spouse, an intimate partner or a person having authority over the victim;

3°) for the perpetrator to abuse the authority conferred upon them by their functions;

4°) that the threat is uttered by several persons;

5°) that the threat is uttered with use or threat of a weapon.

Whoever is guilty of threat with one of the aforementioned aggravating circumstances is punished by imprisonment of seven years.

This excerpt sits under: Soixante-cinquième Année SPECIAL N°21 13 Décembre 2024 > LIVRE III > TITRE II : DES ATTEINTES A LA PERSONNE HUMAINE ET AU GENRE > Chapitre 1 : Des atteintes à l'intégrité physique ou psychique de la personne > Section 3

Article 321-59

Blackmail is the act of obtaining, by threatening to reveal or impute facts likely to harm the honour or reputation, either a signature, a commitment or a waiver, or the disclosure of a secret, or the handing over of funds, securities or any property.

Blackmail is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 100,000 francs.

Article 321-60

Blackmail committed on the basis of gender is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 500,000 francs.

The following constitute aggravating circumstances:

1°) where the victim is in a state of particular vulnerability due to their age, illness, physical or mental disability or pregnancy;

2°) where the perpetrator is an ascendant, close relative, spouse, intimate partner or person having authority over the victim;

3°) where the perpetrator abuses the authority conferred by their functions;

4°) where the blackmail is committed by several persons;

5°) where the blackmail is committed with the use or threat of a weapon.

Whoever is guilty of blackmail with one of the aforementioned aggravating circumstances is punished by seven years' imprisonment.

Section 6: Moral harassment

Article 321-61

The act of harassing another person through repeated words or behaviour having the object or effect of degrading working conditions likely to harm their rights and dignity, impair their physical or mental health or compromise their professional future, is punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 1,000,000 francs.

Article 321-62

The act of harassing a person through repeated words or behaviour having the object or effect of degrading their living conditions resulting in impairment of their physical or mental health is punished by imprisonment of one year and a fine of 500,000 francs.

The offence is also constituted:

a) when such words or behaviour are imposed on the same victim by several persons, in a concerted manner or at the instigation of one of them, even though each of these persons has not acted repeatedly;

b) when such words or behaviour are imposed on the same victim, successively, by several persons who, even in the absence of concertation, know that such words or behaviour constitute repetition.

The following acts also constitute moral harassment, when committed in the following cases:

1°) when they have caused total incapacity for work exceeding twenty-one days;

2°) when they have been committed against a minor under fifteen years of age;

3°) when they have been committed against a person whose particular vulnerability, due to their age, illness, infirmity, physical or mental deficiency or pregnancy, is apparent or known to the perpetrator;

4°) when they have been committed through the use of an online public communication service or by means of digital or electronic media;

5°) when a minor was present and witnessed them.

The acts mentioned in the first to third paragraphs are punished by imprisonment of three years and a fine of 2,000,000 francs when they are committed in two of the circumstances mentioned in points 1 to 5.

Article 321-63

The penalties are increased to imprisonment of five years and the fine to 1,000,000 francs when the harassment has led the victim to commit suicide or attempt suicide.

Section 7: Incitement to suicide

Article 321-64

The act of provoking or assisting the suicide of another person is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 1,000,000 francs, when the provocation has been followed by suicide or attempted suicide.

Article 321-65

The penalty of imprisonment is five years and the fine 3,000,000 francs when the victim of the offence defined in the preceding article is a minor of fifteen years or less.

Article 321-66

Natural or legal persons guilty of the offence provided for in this article also incur the additional penalty of prohibition from carrying on the activity of vocational training provider within the meaning of the Labour Code for a period not exceeding five years.

Article 321-67

Propaganda or advertising, whatever the mode, in favour of products, objects or methods recommended as means of causing one's own death is punished by imprisonment of three years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.

Section 8: False accusation

Article 321-68

Whoever makes verbally or in writing to a public authority a false accusation against one or more individuals is punished by imprisonment of three years and a fine of 300,000 francs.

An accusation is false when it is an intentionally mendacious denunciation of a false fact, likely to expose the person who is the subject of it to an administrative sanction or judicial proceedings.

Section 9: Witchcraft, magic, charlatanism

Article 321-69

Whoever engages in practices of witchcraft, magic or charlatanism, likely to disturb public order or harm persons or their property, is punished by imprisonment of two years without prejudice, where applicable, to the penalties for fraud.

Article 321-70

Whoever is guilty of moral or psychological violence related to allegations of witchcraft, magic or charlatanism is punished by imprisonment of three years and a fine of 500,000 francs.

Chapter 2

Violations of personal privacy Section 1: Violation of domicile

Article 322-1

Whoever enters without right and by means of threats or violence into the domicile of a citizen is punished by imprisonment of three months.

If the offender is a public official within the meaning of this Code acting outside the cases provided for by law, the penalty is one year's imprisonment.

The Judges may, in addition, impose a fine of 120,000 francs.

Violence is not necessary if the perpetrator of the violation enters another person's home with the intention of provoking them.

Section 2: Disclosure of secrets

Article 322-2

All those who, being depositaries by status or profession or by reason of a function or temporary mission of secrets entrusted to them, except in cases where the law requires them to act as informers, disclose such secrets, are punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 150,000 francs.

The same penalties apply, in particular, to members of all courts guilty of having violated the secrecy of deliberations.

Section 3: Suppression of letters or opening of letters

Article 322-3

The total or partial suppression or opening of letters, cards, telegrams or packages entrusted to the postal service, except in cases provided for by law, is punished by imprisonment of one year and a fine of 240,000 francs.

If the offender is a public official or agent of the administration, they are punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 240,000 francs. The offender is further prohibited from any public function or employment for ten years.

Outside the cases provided for above, any suppression, any opening of correspondence addressed to third parties, done in bad faith, is punished by the same penalties.

The attempt of this offence is punished as the offence itself.

Section 4: Other violations of personal privacy

Paragraph 1: Violations of freedom of expression and image

Article 322-4

Any person who, outside the authorization of the law or the judge, willfully violates the privacy of a citizen's private life by listening to, recording or transmitting by means of any device words spoken in a private place by a person without that person's consent, is punished by imprisonment of three years and a fine of 300,000 francs.

Article 322-5

Any person who violates personal privacy by capturing, transmitting by means of any device, the image of a person in a private place without that person's consent, is punished by the same penalties.

When the acts denounced in the preceding paragraph have been accomplished during a meeting in the sight and knowledge of its participants, their consent is presumed.

Article 322-6

Any person who knowingly retains, willfully brings or allows to be brought to the knowledge of the public or a third party any of the acts provided for in the same article, is punished by the penalties provided for in Article 322-4 above.

In case of publication, prosecutions are brought against the persons listed by the law governing the press regime and press offenses under the conditions set by that article, if the offense is committed through the press and against the persons responsible for the broadcast or, failing that, the heads of establishment, directors or managers of the enterprises having proceeded with the dissemination or having profited from it, if the offense is committed by any other means without prejudice to the application of the provisions of Articles 121-6 and 121-7 of this Code on complicity.

The offense is constituted as soon as the publication is made, received or perceived in Mali.

Article 322-7

Any person who knowingly publishes by any means whatsoever, a montage made with the words or images of a person without that person's consent, if it does not appear evident that it is a montage or if it is not expressly mentioned, is punished by the penalties provided for in Article 322-4 above.

Article 322-8

For all the offenses provided for in Articles 322-4, 322-5, 322-6 and 322-7 above, the attempt of the offense is punished as the offense itself.

In the cases provided for in Articles 322-4 and 322-6 above, the Court may order the confiscation of the equipment used to commit the offense.

In the cases referred to in Articles 322-4, 322-5 and 322-7 above, it may also order the confiscation of any recording, document, or medium of the montage obtained by means of the acts provided for in Articles 322-4 and 322-5 above.

In the cases referred to in Article 322-7 above, it may order the confiscation of the medium of the montage.

Paragraph 2

Specific violations of personal rights with regard to the processing of personal data

[Law No. 2013-015 of May 21, 2013 on the protection of personal data in the Republic of Mali].

Article 322-9: The following are understood as

1°) personal data or personal information: information existing in various forms and allowing direct or indirect identification of a person, by reference to a registration number or to one or more elements specific to their physical, physiological, biometric, genetic, mental, cultural, social or economic identity.

Personal data may be universal identifiers allowing the connection of several files constituting databases, or the carrying out of their interconnection;

2°) processing of personal data: any operation or set of operations carried out using automated or non-automated processes and applied to data, such as collection, exploitation, recording, organization, storage, adaptation, modification, extraction, backup, copying, consultation, use, communication by transmission, dissemination or any other form of making available, comparison or interconnection, as well as locking, encryption, erasure or destruction of personal data.

Article 322-10

Any person who, even through negligence, carries out or causes to be carried out processing of personal data without having complied with the formalities prior to their implementation provided for by the law on personal data, is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.

Article 322-11

Any person who, even through negligence, carries out or causes to be carried out processing that has been subject to an injunction to stop processing or withdrawal of authorization provided for by the provisions of the law on the protection of personal data, is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.

Article 322-12

The failure to comply, including through negligence, with the simplified or exemption standards established for this purpose by the Personal Data Protection Authority, in the context of processing of personal data, which has been subject either to a simplified standard or to an exemption standard, is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.

Article 322-13

Any person who, outside the cases where the processing has been authorized under the conditions provided for by legislative and regulatory texts, carries out or causes to be carried out processing of personal data including, among the data on which it relates, the National Identification Number of natural persons, is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.

Article 322-14

Any person who carries out or causes to be carried out processing of personal data without implementing the security and confidentiality measures prescribed by the provisions of the law on the protection of personal data, is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.

Article 322-15

The failure by a provider of electronic communications services or by a data controller to notify a personal data breach to the Personal Data Protection Authority or to the data subject, in disregard of the standards set by legislative and regulatory texts, is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.

The failure by a processor to notify this breach to the data controller is punished by the same penalties.

Article 322-16

Any person who collects and processes personal data by fraudulent, unfair or illicit means is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.

Article 322-17

Any person who carries out or causes to be carried out processing of personal data concerning a natural person despite the opposition of that person in accordance with the provisions of the law on personal data, when this processing is for prospecting purposes, in particular commercial, or when this opposition is based on legitimate grounds, is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.

Article 322-18

Anyone who, except in cases provided for by law, stores or keeps on computer media or memory, without the express consent of the person concerned, personal data which, directly or indirectly, reveal racial or ethnic origin, political, philosophical or religious opinions, or trade union membership, or which relate to the health or sexual life of that person, as well as data relating to offences, convictions or security measures concerning him or her, is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.

Article 322-19

In the case of processing of personal data for the purpose of research in the field of health, anyone who carries out processing is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs:

1°) without having previously informed the persons concerned individually of their right of access, rectification and opposition, of the nature of the data transmitted and the recipients thereof, as well as the provisions made for their processing, storage and protection;

2°) despite the opposition of the person concerned or, where provided for by law, in the absence of the informed and express consent of the person, or if it concerns a deceased person, despite the refusal expressed by that person during his or her lifetime.

Article 322-20

The act of keeping personal data beyond the period provided for by law or regulation, by the request for authorization or opinion, or by the prior declaration sent to the Personal Data Protection Authority, is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs, unless such storage is carried out for historical, statistical or scientific purposes under the conditions provided for by law.

The act of processing, except in cases provided for by law, for purposes other than historical, statistical or scientific, personal data kept beyond the period mentioned in the first paragraph is punished by the same penalties.

Article 322-21

Anyone who, holding personal data on the occasion of their recording, classification, transmission or any form of processing, diverts such information from their purpose as defined by the legislative provision, the regulatory act or the decision of the Personal Data Protection Authority authorizing the processing, or by the prior declarations for the implementation of such processing, is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.

Article 322-22

Anyone who collects, on the occasion of their recording, classification, transmission or another form of processing, personal data whose disclosure would have the effect of damaging the reputation of the person concerned or the privacy of his or her private life, brings, without authorization from the person concerned, such data to the knowledge of a third party who is not qualified to receive them, is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.

Where the disclosure provided for in the preceding paragraph of this article was committed through carelessness or negligence, the person responsible is punished by imprisonment of three years and a fine of 2,000,000 francs.

In the cases provided for in the two paragraphs of this article, prosecution may only be brought upon complaint by the victim, his or her legal representative or his or her successors in title.

Article 322-23

The act of carrying out or having carried out processing of personal data subject to or intended to be subject to transfer to a State not appearing on the list of States having an adequate level of personal data protection, drawn up by the Personal Data Protection Authority, is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.

Article 322-24

Anyone who obstructs the action of the Personal Data Protection Authority is punished by imprisonment of three months and a fine of 120,000 francs:

1°) either by opposing the exercise of the missions entrusted to its members or to authorized agents pursuant to the law on personal data;

2°) either by refusing to communicate to its members or to authorized agents pursuant to the law on personal data, information and documents useful to their mission, or by concealing said documents or information, or by causing them to disappear;

3°) either by communicating information which does not conform to the content of the records as it was at the time the request was made or which does not present such content in a directly accessible form.

Article 322-25

In the cases provided for in articles 322-10 to 322-23 above, the erasure of all or part of the personal data subject to the processing which gave rise to the offence may be ordered. The commissioners and agents of the Personal Data Protection Authority are authorized to verify the erasure of such data.

Article 322-26

Where such information is required by law, the following acts by the person responsible for automated processing of personal data are punished by imprisonment of three months and a fine of 120,000 francs:

1°) failing to inform the person from whom personal data concerning him or her are collected:

a)	of the identity of the person responsible for the processing and, where applicable, that of his or her representative;

b)	of the purpose pursued by the processing for which the data are intended;

c)	of the mandatory or optional nature of the responses;

d)	of the possible consequences for him or her of a failure to respond;

e)	of the recipients or categories of recipients of the data;

f)	of his or her rights of opposition, inquiry, access and rectification;

g)	where applicable, of the transfers of personal data envisaged to a State not appearing on the list of countries having an adequate level of personal data protection;

2°) where the data are collected by means of a questionnaire, failing to include on the questionnaire the information relating to:

a)	the identity of the person responsible for the processing and, where applicable, that of his or her representative;

b)	the purpose pursued by the processing for which the data are intended;

c)	the mandatory or optional nature of the responses;

d)	the rights of opposition, inquiry, access and rectification of the persons from whom the data are collected;

3°) failing to inform clearly and precisely any person using electronic communications networks:

a)	of the purpose of any action tending to access, by electronic transmission, information stored in his or her terminal connection equipment or to record, by the same means, information in his or her terminal connection equipment;

b)	of the means at his or her disposal to oppose it;

4°) failing to provide to the person concerned, where the personal data have not been collected from him or her, the information listed in 1° and 2° from the time of recording the data or, if communication of the data to third parties is envisaged, at the latest at the time of the first communication of the data.

Article 322-27

Is punished by imprisonment of three months and a fine of 120,000 francs, the act by the person responsible for automated processing of personal data, of failing to respond to requests from a natural person proving their identity which have as their object:

1°) confirmation that personal data concerning them is or is not the subject of this processing;

2°) information relating to the purposes of the processing, the categories of personal data processed and the recipients or categories of recipients to whom the data are communicated;

3°) where applicable, information relating to transfers of personal data envisaged to a third country;

4°) the communication, in an accessible form, of the personal data concerning them as well as any available information as to the origin thereof;

5°) information making it possible to know and contest the logic underlying the automated processing in the event of a decision taken on the basis thereof and producing legal effects with regard to the person concerned.

Is punished by the same penalty the act of refusing to deliver, at the request of the person concerned, a copy of the personal data concerning them, where applicable, against payment of a sum which may not exceed the cost of reproduction.

The offences provided for by this article are not however constituted if the refusal to respond is authorised by law either in order not to infringe copyright, or because they are manifestly abusive requests, in particular by their number, their repetitive or systematic nature, or because the personal data are kept in a form manifestly excluding any risk of infringement of the privacy of the persons concerned and for a period not exceeding that necessary for the sole purposes of establishing statistics or scientific or historical research.

Article 322-28

Is punished by imprisonment of three months and a fine of 120,000 francs, the act, by the person responsible for automated processing of personal data, of failing to carry out, free of charge for the applicant, the operations requested by a natural person proving their identity and who requires that personal data concerning them or concerning the deceased person of whom they are the heir be rectified, completed, updated, locked or erased, when such data are inaccurate, incomplete, ambiguous, out of date, or when their collection, use, communication or storage is prohibited.

Article 322-29

Legal persons declared criminally responsible for the offences defined in this paragraph incur the penalties provided for by Article 431-8 of this Code.

The prohibition mentioned in 2° of Article 431-8 relates to the activity in the exercise or on the occasion of the exercise of which the offence was committed.

Article 322-30

The Public Prosecutor notifies the President of the Personal Data Protection Authority of all prosecutions relating to offences concerning personal data contained in this Code and the action taken thereon. He informs them of the date and subject of the trial hearing by registered letter sent at least fifteen days before that date. The investigating or trial court may call upon the President of the Authority or their representative to submit their observations or to develop them orally at the hearing.

Chapter 3

Endangering the person Section 1: Risks caused to others

Article 323-1

The act of directly exposing another person to an immediate risk of death or injury of a nature to cause mutilation or permanent disability by the manifestly deliberate violation of a particular obligation of prudence or safety imposed by law or regulation is punished by imprisonment of one year and a fine of 1,000,000 francs.

Section 2: Abandonment of a vulnerable person

Article 323-2

Whoever voluntarily abandons, in conditions such that their safety depends on chance, a child or a person incapable of protecting themselves, or who voluntarily interrupts the provision of food or care owed to them, is, if it results in mutilation, disability or permanent illness, punished by imprisonment of ten years.

When the abandonment causes death, the action is considered as murder and punished as such.

If the abandonment results in an illness or incapacity of more than twenty days, the penalty is imprisonment of five years.

In other cases, the penalty is imprisonment of three years.

Section 3: Failure to assist a person in danger

Article 323-3

Is punished by imprisonment of three years and a fine of 1,000,000 francs, whoever voluntarily refrains from providing assistance to a person in danger which, without risk to themselves or to third parties, they can provide either by their personal action or by calling for help.

Article 323-4

When the offence of failure to assist a person in danger as specified in the preceding article is the result of a serious violation of the obligations imposed by the function, profession or occupation of the perpetrator, the penalties of the preceding article may be doubled.

In any event, the penalty imposed may not be less than imprisonment of one month.

Chapter 4: Infringements of personal freedoms

Section 1

Unlawful arrests and confinement of persons, hostage-taking

Paragraph 1: Unlawful arrests and confinement of persons

Article 324-1

Are punished by imprisonment of twenty years and prohibition of residence for ten years:

1°) those who, without order from the public authorities and outside the cases where the law orders the seizure of suspects, accused or defendants, in particular cases of crime or flagrant offence, arrest, detain or confine any person;

2°) those who, knowingly, provide a place to carry out the detention or confinement.

The guilty parties incur the death penalty, if the persons arrested, detained or confined are subjected to bodily torture.

If the confinement is accompanied either by violence not having the character of bodily torture, or by death threats, the penalty is that of life imprisonment.

Article 324-2

Whoever is guilty of confinement, within the meaning of Article 324-1 above, of a person by reason of gender is punished by criminal imprisonment of twenty years and prohibition of residence for ten years.

If the confined person is in a state of particular vulnerability by reason of their age, an illness, a physical or psychological disability, their state of pregnancy, the judge may in no case impose a penalty below twenty years and prohibition of residence for ten years.

Paragraph 2: Hostage-taking

Article 324-3

Is punished by life imprisonment whoever seizes a person or detains them and threatens to kill them, injure them or continue to detain them in order to compel a third party, namely a State, an international organisation, a population, a natural or legal person or a group of persons, to perform any act or to refrain from doing so as an explicit or implicit condition for the release of the hostage.

If the above facts result in injuries or illnesses, the penalty is imprisonment of thirty years.

If death has resulted therefrom, the death penalty is imposed.

Article 324-4

If the person arrested, detained or sequestered is held as a hostage either to prepare or facilitate the commission of a crime or an offence, or to favour the flight or ensure the impunity of the perpetrators or accomplices of a crime or an offence, or in a secret place, to respond to the execution of an order or a condition, the guilty parties are punished by life imprisonment.

However, the penalty is twenty years' imprisonment if the person arrested, detained, or sequestered as a hostage to respond to the execution of an order or a condition is released voluntarily before the fifth day completed from that of the arrest, detention or sequestration without the order or condition having been executed.

The penalty of life imprisonment is pronounced:

a)	if the arrest is carried out with a false costume, under a false name or on a false order of the public authority;

b)	if the individual arrested, detained or sequestered is threatened with death.

The death penalty is pronounced if the person arrested, detained or sequestered is subjected to bodily torture.

Article 324-5

In the cases referred to in articles 324-1 and 324-2 above, those who, knowingly, have provided a place to carry out the detention or sequestration are punished with the same penalties as the perpetrators of that detention or sequestration.

Section 2 : De l'enlèvement de personnes

Article 324-6

Whoever by fraud, violence or threats, abducts an individual from the place where he has been placed by those to whose authority he is subject or entrusted, is punished by twenty years' imprisonment and a ten-year residence ban.

Article 324-7

When the abduction of persons, referred to in the preceding article, is committed without fraud, violence or threats, or if it is committed with a view to marrying a woman without her consent, the guilty party is punished by five years' imprisonment and a twenty-year residence ban.

When the abduction referred to in the preceding article is committed without fraud, violence or threats, on the person of a child under fifteen years of age, the penalty is ten years' imprisonment and a ten-year residence ban.

Section 3 : Des conventions portant atteinte à la liberté

Article 324-8

Whoever concludes an agreement having as its object the alienation, either gratuitously or for consideration, of the freedom of a third person, is punished by ten years' imprisonment.

Money, goods and other objects of value received in execution of the agreement or as a deposit for an agreement to be concluded, are confiscated.

Article 324-9

The act of introducing into the Republic of Mali individuals intended to be the subject of the aforementioned agreement, or of removing or attempting to remove individuals from the Republic, with a view to the said agreement to be concluded abroad, is punished by the same penalty.

However, the penalty of imprisonment may be increased to twenty years if the person who was the subject thereof, either inside or outside Mali, is a child under fifteen years of age.

Article 324-10

In the cases provided for in article 324-9 above, the Court may in addition pronounce the deprivation of the rights provided for in article 131-6 of this Code.

A ten-year residence ban may also be pronounced.

Section 4 : De l'esclavage et des pratiques assimilées

Article 324-11 : L'esclavage et les pratiques assimilées sont interdits

Paragraphe 1 : De l'esclavage

Article 324-12

Slavery is the status or condition of an individual over whom are exercised attributes comparable to those of the right of ownership, or some of them.

The slave is the person who is not free and finds himself under the absolute dependence of a master in the conditions defined in the preceding paragraph.

The slave trade includes any act of capture, acquisition or transfer of an individual with a view to reducing him to slavery, any act of acquiring a slave with a view to selling or exchanging him, any act of transfer by sale or exchange of a slave acquired with a view to being sold or exchanged, as well as any act of trade or transport of slaves.

Article 324-13

Slavery and the slave trade are punished by ten years' imprisonment and a fine of 10,000,000 francs.

Paragraphe 2 : Du délit d'esclavage sexuel

Article 324-14

The offence of sexual slavery is constituted by the act of compelling a person, over whom the perpetrator exercises a power associated with the right of ownership either by having purchased, lent or bartered that person, and imposes on that person acts of a sexual nature.
Whoever is guilty of sexual slavery is punished by twenty years' imprisonment, a fine of 5,000,000 francs and a ten-year residence ban.

A state of particular vulnerability of the victim constitutes an aggravating circumstance related to:

1°) his or her age;

2°) an illness;

3°) a physical or mental disability; 4°) his or her state of pregnancy.
In the case of one of the aforementioned aggravating circumstances, the penalty incurred is twenty years' imprisonment, a fine of 10,000,000 francs and a fifteen-year residence ban.

Paragraphe 3 : Des pratiques assimilées à l'esclavage

Article 324-15

Practices similar to slavery are placement, serfdom and debt bondage.

Article 324-16 : Le placement est une pratique en vertu de laquelle

1°) a woman is, without her having the right to refuse, promised or given in marriage in return for a consideration in cash or in kind paid to her parents, guardian, family or to any other person or group of persons;

2°) the husband of a woman or his family transfers her for consideration to a third party or otherwise;

3°) a woman is transmitted by succession, upon the death of her husband, to another person;

4°) a child is handed over, either by his parents or by one of them, or by his guardian, to a third party, with or without payment, with a view to exploiting him or subjecting him to labour.

Placement, whatever its form, is punished by ten years' imprisonment and a fine of 1,000,000 francs.

Article 324-17

Serfdom is the condition of anyone who is required by law, custom or agreement, to live and work on land belonging to another person and to provide to that person, for remuneration or free of charge, certain specified services, without being able to change his condition.

Serfdom is punished by ten years' imprisonment and a fine of 1,000,000 francs.

Article 324-18

Debt bondage is the status or condition resulting from the fact that a debtor has undertaken to provide, as security for a debt, his personal services or those of someone over whom he has authority, if the equitable value of those services is not applied to the liquidation of the debt or if the duration of those services is not limited nor their nature defined.

Debt bondage is punished by ten years' imprisonment and a fine of 1,000,000 francs.

Article 324-19

The act of marrying, having married, or preventing from marrying, a woman reduced to a state of slavery, against her will, is punished by five years' imprisonment and a fine of 1,000,000 francs.

Any sexual intercourse committed during a marriage that occurred in the conditions described in paragraph 1 is deemed to be rape; and the provisions of paragraph 4 of article 325-3 of this Code are applicable.

Article 324-20

The deprivation of a widow of the enjoyment of her period of widowhood, or its reduction, on grounds of slavery, is punished by five years' imprisonment and a fine of 1,000,000 francs.
