Article 324-21

Depriving a child of access to education, because of the slave status of his or her parents or of himself or herself, is punishable by five years' imprisonment and a fine of 1,000,000 francs.

Article 324-22

Depriving an entitled person of his or her right to inheritance because of a state of slavery is punishable by five years' imprisonment and a fine of 1,000,000 francs.

The property of which the victim has been deprived shall be restored to him or her.

Article 324-23

Any discrimination based on slave status is punishable by five years' imprisonment and a fine of 250,000 francs.

Article 324-24

Any private or public statement or writing, any private or public act, likely to approve, encourage, or facilitate slavery, is punishable by five years' imprisonment and a fine of 250,000 francs.

The penalty is doubled when the perpetrator of the offence is a public official.

The destruction of the media used to commit the offence shall be ordered.

Article 324-25

Public insults uttered against a person in a state of slavery and referring to that state are punishable by five years' imprisonment.

Article 324-26

Any public official informed, in the course of his or her duties, of practices likely to be classified as slavery or related practices has the obligation to refer the matter to the territorially competent public prosecutor.

Any public official who, having witnessed it, refrained from intervening to prevent its perpetration or who, having become aware of it, refrained from reporting the perpetrators or accomplices is guilty of passive complicity in the crime of slavery.

Article 324-27

The attempt and complicity in slavery and related practices are punished in the same manner as these offences.

Article 324-28

The temporary deprivation of civil rights is, in addition, pronounced against persons convicted of the offences of slavery and related practices.

Section 5

Human trafficking Paragraph 1: The offence

Article 324-29

The offence of human trafficking is constituted by the act of recruiting, transporting, transferring, harbouring or receiving persons, by means of the threat or use of force or other forms of coercion, by abduction, fraud, deception, abuse of authority or of a position of vulnerability or by the giving or receiving of payments or benefits to achieve the consent of a person having control over another person for the purpose of exploitation.

Exploitation includes, at a minimum, the exploitation of the prostitution of others or other forms of sexual exploitation, the exploitation of the begging of others, the exploitation of forced labour or services, including enlistment in an armed conflict, slavery or practices similar to slavery, servitude and the removal of organs.

The recruitment, transportation, transfer, harbouring or receipt of a minor for the purpose of exploitation is considered to be human trafficking even if it does not involve any of the means set forth in the first paragraph.

Whoever commits the offence of human trafficking is punishable by ten years' criminal imprisonment and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.

A ten-year residence ban may, in addition, be pronounced.

Paragraph 2: Absence of effect of consent

Article 324-30

When the constituent elements of the offences referred to in this section are met, the perpetrator of the acts may in no case invoke the consent of the victim to evade prosecution.

Similarly, the perpetrator of the acts may not invoke the consent of the parents or any other person having legal authority over a minor to evade prosecution.

Paragraph 3: Proof of the victim's age

Article 324-31

In the case where no reliable official document can determine the age of the victim, proof of the victim's age must be provided by medical examination or any other legal means.

Paragraph 4: Inadmissibility of the victim's prior sexual conduct

Article 324-32

In the context of prosecutions of perpetrators of human trafficking offences or any other offence referred to in this chapter, the victim's prior sexual conduct is irrelevant in the gathering of evidence and in the search for the manifestation of truth.

Paragraph 5: Adoption for the purpose of exploitation

Article 324-33

Whoever, in violation of national and international laws on adoption, vitiates the consent of the parents or any other person having legal authority over a child, with a view to having the child adopted for the commission of the offences referred to in this section is punishable by ten years' imprisonment and a fine of 500,000 francs.

Paragraph 6: Offences relating to travel or identity documents

Article 324-34

Whoever, for the purpose of exploitation, makes, obtains, procures, conceals, retains, removes, falsifies or destroys any travel document of a person or any document that may establish or is purported to establish the identity or immigrant status of a person, or any other official identification document whether authentic or not, national or foreign, is punishable by ten years' criminal imprisonment and a ten-year residence ban.

Paragraph 7

Obligations and sanctions of carriers in case of failure to comply with their obligations

Article 324-35

Without prejudice to international conventions on the matter to which Mali is a party, transport companies and any owner or operator of a means of transport are required to ensure that passengers possess the documents, whatever they may be, required to enter Mali, transit through it or leave it.

This obligation applies to companies and their employees who sell, issue, collect, verify travel tickets, boarding passes or any other document authorizing transport.

The carrier who intentionally fails to comply with the obligation imposed upon him or her commits an offence punishable by a fine of 500,000 francs.

In case of recidivism, the perpetrator is punishable by three months' imprisonment and a fine of 2,000,000 francs; the suspension or withdrawal of the carrier's licence may be pronounced.

In addition, the costs relating to the detention, escort and repatriation of the passenger not having the required documents are borne by the carrier.

Paragraph 8: Exemption from criminal liability of carriers

Article 324-36: The carrier is not criminally liable in the case where

1°) the person was in possession of the required documents at the time of boarding to enter Mali;

2°) entry into Mali occurred only in circumstances beyond the will and control of the carrier or in a case of force majeure.

Paragraph 9: Money laundering

Article 324-37

The laundering of proceeds from the offences referred to in this section is punishable in accordance with the provisions relating to the fight against money laundering and the financing of terrorism.

Paragraph 10: Corruption

Article 324-38

Corruption with a view to committing the offences referred to in this section is provided for and punished in accordance with the provisions of this Code.

Paragraph 11: Obstruction of the functioning of investigation and enforcement services

Article 324-39

Any obstruction of the exercise of the activities of investigation and enforcement services, or any refusal to submit to an investigation or a legal obligation, is punishable by one year's imprisonment and a fine of 200,000 francs.

Paragraphe 12 : Confidentiality of information relating to victims and witnesses

Article 324-40

Anyone who knowingly discloses, directly or indirectly, information deemed confidential, relating in particular to the location of a victim or witness and their identity, commits an offence punishable by two years' imprisonment and a fine of 200,000 francs without prejudice to the provisions set out in Article 243-92 of this Code.

Paragraphe 13 : Aggravating circumstances

Article 324-41

Where one of the offences referred to in this section is committed with one of the circumstances listed below, the penalties incurred are:

1°) imprisonment for fifteen years in the case of intentional assault and battery;

2°) double the maximum penalty incurred:

a)	if the perpetrator has absconded from justice;

b)	if the perpetrator is in a state of recidivism;

c)	if the perpetrator has participated in other offences definitively judged to have facilitated the offence of trafficking;

d)	if there is a concurrence of offences referred to in this section;

e)	if the perpetrator exercised public authority functions and the offence was committed in the exercise of those functions;

3°) imprisonment for twenty years:

a)	if the offence is committed against a minor;

b)	in the case of a relationship of trust between the victim and the perpetrator, particularly where the perpetrator has abused their hierarchical position;

c)	if the perpetrator is the spouse of the victim;

d)	if the perpetrator is vested with moral authority over the victim, particularly their legal representative, a social worker responsible for the victim;

4°) life imprisonment:

a)	in the case of sexual abuse or rape;

b)	in the case of intentional assault and battery resulting in amputation, mutilation or deprivation of the use of a limb, blindness, loss of an eye or other permanent infirmities;

c)	if the victim is particularly vulnerable;

d)	if the offence was committed by an organized group as part of systematic criminal activity or over a long period or on a large scale, involving in particular multiple victims;

e)	if weapons or prohibited drugs were used.

5°) life imprisonment in the case of death of the victim.

In all cases, the provisions relating to mitigating circumstances and suspended sentences may not be applied.

Paragraphe 14 : Criminal liability of legal persons

Article 324-42

The criminal liability of legal persons is engaged for the offences provided for in this section.

Where one of the offences referred to in this section is committed by a legal person, on its behalf, by its organs or representatives, with the exception of the State, it is punishable by a fine of 10,000,000 francs.

The criminal liability of legal persons does not exclude that of natural persons who are perpetrators or accomplices of the same acts.

The competent court may also seize the assets and any property of a legal person and order their confiscation for the benefit of the State.

The above provisions are applicable in matters of illicit trafficking in migrants.

Paragraphe 15 : Criminal immunity of victims

Article 324-43

Victims of the offences referred to in this section may not be prosecuted or convicted for the offences:

1°) of illegal entry into Mali;

2°) of residence in an illegal situation in Mali;

3°) of possession of illegal travel or identity documents obtained or received with a view to illegal entry into Mali.

Furthermore, the victim is not held criminally or administratively liable for having committed offences when they were reduced to doing so by their condition as a victim of trafficking.

The provisions of this article do not apply where the offence committed is a serious crime.

Section 6

Illicit trafficking in migrants Paragraphe 1: The offence

Article 324-44

Any person who, intentionally and in order to obtain, directly or indirectly, a financial advantage or other material benefit, procures the illegal entry into a State Party of a person who is neither a national nor a permanent resident of that State, commits an offence punishable by imprisonment for twenty years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.

Paragraphe 2 : Fraudulent travel or identity documents

Article 324-45

Any person who, intentionally and in order to obtain, directly or indirectly, a financial advantage or other material benefit, produces, procures, provides or possesses a fraudulent travel or identity document in order to enable the illicit trafficking in migrants, commits an offence punishable by imprisonment for ten years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.

Paragraphe 3 : Facilitation of illegal residence

Article 324-46

Any person who, intentionally and in order to obtain, directly or indirectly, a financial advantage or other material benefit, uses illegal means to enable a person who is neither a national nor a permanent resident to remain in the State, without satisfying the conditions necessary for legal residence in the said State, commits an offence punishable by imprisonment for ten years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.

Paragraphe 4 : Aggravating circumstances

Article 324-47

Where one of the offences referred to in this section has been committed with one of the circumstances listed below, the penalties incurred are:

1°) imprisonment for twenty years if:

a)	the offence endangers or is likely to endanger the life or safety of the smuggled migrant;

b)	the offence entails inhuman or degrading treatment of the smuggled migrant;

c)	the offence entails the exploitation of the smuggled migrant;

d)	the perpetrator of the offence uses or threatens to use any form of violence against the smuggled migrant or their family;

e)	the offence is committed against a minor;

f)	the smuggled migrant is a pregnant woman;

g)	the smuggled migrant has an intellectual or physical disability;

h)	the perpetrator of the offence takes advantage of the particular vulnerability or dependence of the smuggled migrant in order to obtain a financial advantage or other material benefit;

i)	the perpetrator of the offence uses a child as an accomplice or participant in the criminal act;

j)	the perpetrator of the offence uses drugs or psychotropic substances, medicines, weapons to facilitate the commission of the offence;

k)	the perpetrator exercised public authority functions and the offence was committed, including by omission, in the exercise of those functions;

l)	the perpetrator of the offence confiscates, destroys or attempts to destroy the travel or identity documents of the smuggled migrant;

m)	the perpetrator absconds from justice;

n)	the perpetrator is in a state of recidivism;

o)	the perpetrator has participated in other offences that have facilitated the offence of illicit trafficking;

p)	the offence is committed within the framework of the activities of an organized criminal group.

2°) life imprisonment:

a)	in the case of intentional assault and battery resulting in amputation, mutilation or deprivation of the use of a limb, blindness, loss of an eye or other permanent infirmities;

b)	in the case of sexual abuse or rape;

c)	if the offence results in the death of the smuggled migrant or a third party, including death by suicide.

Paragraph 5: Complementary penalties

Article 324-48

When a person has been found guilty of an offence under this section, the competent court may, in addition to any sanction imposed under this section and without limitation of its other powers, order the following measures:

a) confiscation of assets, proceeds of crime and instruments of the offence;

b) publication of the judicial decision;

c) prohibition from exercising, directly or indirectly, one or more social or professional activities, permanently or for a minimum period of five years;

d) temporary or permanent closure of any business or establishment that was used to commit the offence;

e) exclusion from the right to public benefits or assistance;

f) temporary or permanent prohibition from participating in public procurement;

g) temporary or permanent prohibition from engaging in other commercial activities and/or from creating another legal person;

h) any other appropriate non-custodial measure provided for by law.

Paragraph 6: Criminal non-liability of migrants who are the object of trafficking

Article 324-49

Without prejudice to the applicability of other laws establishing criminal offences, migrants are not liable to criminal prosecution under this section by reason of having been the object of the acts set out in this chapter.

Paragraph 7

Obligations of commercial carriers and offences committed by them

Article 324-50

On pain of the sanctions provided for below, commercial carriers are subject to the following obligations:

1°) Any commercial carrier who fails to verify that each passenger is in possession of the identity and/or travel documents required for entry into the State of destination and into any transit State, commits an offence punishable by three months' imprisonment and a fine of 500,000 francs.

In case of recidivism, the offender is punished by one year's imprisonment and a fine of 2,000,000 francs.

In addition, the carrier's licence may be suspended or withdrawn.

2°) Any commercial carrier who fails to notify the competent authorities that a person has attempted to travel or has travelled using its services without the identity and travel documents required for entry into the State of destination or into any transit State, when it has knowledge of the fact that that person was a trafficked migrant or when it demonstrates culpable negligence in that regard, commits an offence and is liable to three months' imprisonment and a fine of 500,000 francs.

The provisions of this article apply equally to the driver, the escort and the lessee.

3°) A commercial carrier does not commit an offence under this article if:

a) there were reasonable grounds to believe that the documents the passenger had in his or her possession were the documents required to enter Mali legally;

b) the passenger was in possession of regular travel documents when he or she boarded or last boarded the means of transport bound for or departing from Mali;

c) entry into or exit from Mali took place only by reason of circumstances beyond the control of the commercial carrier;

4°) A commercial carrier is not liable under this article when the persons it was transporting were granted protection against refoulement and/or access to the asylum system by the competent authorities.

Paragraph 8: Attempt

Article 324-51

Any attempt to commit the offences provided for by this section and by the preceding section, manifested by a commencement of execution and suspended or having failed to have its effect only by reason of circumstances independent of the will of its perpetrator, is considered as the offence itself.

Section 7: Child trafficking

Article 324-52

Child trafficking is the entire process by which a child is moved, within or outside a country under conditions that transform him or her into a commodity for at least one of the persons involved, and whatever the purpose of the child's movement:

1°) any act involving the recruitment, transport, harbouring or sale of a child;

2°) any act that results in the movement of the child within or outside a country.

Any person convicted of child trafficking is punished by twenty years' criminal imprisonment.

Section 8: Enforced disappearance

Article 324-53

Constitutes an enforced disappearance, the arrest, detention, abduction or any other form of deprivation of liberty of a person, under conditions removing him or her from the protection of the law, by one or more agents of the State or by a person or group of persons acting with the authorization, support or acquiescence of the State authorities, when such acts are followed by his or her disappearance and accompanied either by denial of recognition of the deprivation of liberty, or by concealment of the fate reserved for him or her or of the place where he or she is located.

Article 324-54: Enforced disappearance is punished by life criminal imprisonment

The Court may, in addition, impose one or more of the complementary penalties mentioned in Article 131-6 of this Code.

Article 324-55: Without prejudice to the provisions of Articles 121

6 and 121-7 of this Code, is considered as an accomplice to the crime of enforced disappearance mentioned in the preceding article committed by subordinates placed under his or her authority and effective control, the superior who knew, or deliberately disregarded information which clearly indicated that his or her subordinates were committing or were going to commit a crime of enforced disappearance and who did not take all necessary and reasonable measures within his or her power to prevent or repress its execution or to refer the matter to the competent authorities for the purposes of investigation and prosecution, when that crime was related to activities falling within his or her responsibility and effective control.

Chapter 5

Offences against public morals Section 1: Public indecency

Article 325-1

Any act performed publicly, offending decency and the moral sentiment of individuals who are involuntary witnesses thereto and likely to disturb public order and cause manifest social harm, is public indecency.

Also constitutes public indecency, any sexual act against nature performed publicly with an individual of the same sex.

Indecency, committed publicly and intentionally is punished by two years' imprisonment and a fine of 200,000 francs.

Section 2: Indecent assault

Article 325-2

Any act of a sexual nature contrary to morals intentionally and directly committed against a person constitutes an indecent assault.

Also constitutes an indecent assault, any act of an unnatural sexual nature committed with an individual of the same sex.

Any statement, image, public or private writing, any public or private act, likely to approve, encourage, promote or facilitate the indecent assault provided for in the preceding paragraph and in Article 325-1 above is punishable by seven years' imprisonment and a fine of 500,000 francs.

Any indecent assault, consummated or attempted without violence against a child of either sex, under the age of fifteen years, is punishable by ten years' imprisonment and a twenty-year residence ban.

Is punishable by the same penalties, indecent assault, consummated or attempted with violence, against individuals of either sex, over the age of fifteen years or committed against adults in a state of slavery.

If the crime provided for in the 4th paragraph of this article is committed against a child under fifteen years of age, the offender is punishable by twenty years' imprisonment and a twenty-year residence ban.

If the assault is committed with the help of a third party or several persons, or against minors in a state of slavery, the penalty is twenty years' imprisonment with the possibility of applying a residence ban for the same duration in the cases provided for in paragraphs 2 and 4 of this article, and life imprisonment, in the cases provided for in paragraph 4 above.

Those guilty of assault committed without violence against a minor over fifteen years and under eighteen years of age, if they are ascendants of the person against whom the assault was committed, if they are among those who have authority over that person, or if they are responsible for that person's education, supervision or if they are employees of the persons designated above, are punishable by the penalties provided for in Article 325-1 of this Code.

Section 3: Rape

Article 325-3

Constitutes rape, any act of vaginal, anal, oral penetration or of any nature whatsoever for sexual purposes, imposed on another person without their consent by using a part of the human body or an object, by violence, threat, coercion or surprise.

Also constitutes rape, any act of vaginal, anal, oral penetration or of any nature whatsoever for sexual purposes committed against a minor of fifteen years even with their consent.

Rape is punishable by twenty years' imprisonment and a five-year residence ban.

If the rape is committed with the help of several persons or against a child under sixteen years of age, or against a person reduced to slavery, the offender is punishable by twenty years' imprisonment, a ten-year residence ban and the Judges may not reduce the sentence below five years' imprisonment.

Also constitute aggravating circumstances, the fact that the victim is pregnant at the time of the acts, the fact that the perpetrator is an ascendant or close relative of the victim, the fact that the acts result in a pregnancy of the raped person, or an injury or lesion, the fact that the perpetrator abuses the authority conferred by their functions, the fact that the perpetrator commits the rape with use or threat of a weapon or a psychotropic or alcoholic substance.

If the rape has resulted in mutilation or permanent disability or the death of the victim or was committed with two of the aggravating circumstances provided for in the preceding paragraph, the penalty is life imprisonment.

If the offenders are ascendants of the person against whom the rape is committed, if they are among those who have authority over that person, or if they are responsible for that person's education, supervision or if they are employees of the persons designated above, no suspension of the execution of the sentence may be pronounced.

Section 4: Customary coitus

Article 325-4

The individual who performs or attempts to perform the sexual act customarily authorized on a girl under the age of fifteen years, is punishable by five years' imprisonment without prejudice to the penalties incurred for crimes or offences committed on the occasion of the performance of this act.

Are punished as accomplices the persons, including parents who, knowingly provoke the acts referred to in this article, or, with knowledge, aid or assist the perpetrator in the acts that prepare or facilitate them.

Section 5: Paedophilia

Article 325-5

Constitutes the crime of paedophilia and punishable by twenty years' imprisonment and a fine of 1,000,000 francs, any act of sexual penetration or sexual touching of any nature whatsoever, committed against a minor under thirteen years of age, or any exposure or exploitation for commercial or tourist purposes of photographs, films or drawings of a pornographic nature featuring one or more minors under thirteen years of age.

If the offenders are ascendants of the person against whom the crime was committed, if they are among those who have authority over that person, or if they are responsible for that person's education, supervision or if they are employees of the persons designated above, or if the crime is committed with the help of several persons, the penalty is life imprisonment and a fine of 10,000,000 francs. However, the above crime is not constituted if the age difference between the perpetrator and the victim does not exceed five years.

Section 6

Incitement to debauchery-Forced prostitution-Procuring-Soliciting

Paragraphe 1: Incitement to debauchery

Article 325-6

Whoever, either habitually incites, favours or facilitates the debauchery or corruption of youth of either sex, or, to satisfy the passions of another, leads or diverts, even with their consent, a person with a view to debauchery, or detains against their will a person in a house of debauchery, or compels them to engage in prostitution, is punishable by three years' imprisonment, a fine of 1,000,000 francs and a ten-year residence ban.

Paragraphe 2: Forced prostitution

Article 325-7

Constitutes forced prostitution, the act of causing a person to provide sexual services, for profit, by any form of coercion, threat, deception or false promises.

Prostitution involving a child is assimilated to forced prostitution.

Whoever forces a person to engage in prostitution is punishable by ten years' imprisonment, a fine of 5,000,000 francs and a ten-year residence ban.

When the victim is in a state of particular vulnerability due to their age, illness, physical or mental disability, their state of pregnancy, the applicable penalty is fifteen years' imprisonment, a fine of 10,000,000 francs and a ten-year residence ban.

Paragraph 3: Procuring

Article 325-8

The offense of procuring is constituted by the act, by any person, in any manner whatsoever:

1°) of aiding, assisting or protecting the prostitution of another person;

2°) of profiting from the prostitution of another person, or sharing the proceeds thereof;

3°) of recruiting, enticing or diverting a person for the purpose of prostitution or exerting pressure on that person to engage in prostitution.

Any person guilty of procuring is punished by five years' imprisonment, a fine of 1,000,000 francs and a residence ban of ten years.

Where the person is in a state of particular vulnerability by reason of age, illness, physical or mental disability, or pregnancy, the applicable penalty is twenty years' imprisonment, a fine of 5,000,000 francs and a residence ban of ten years.

Paragraph 4: Soliciting

Article 325-9

The act, by any means, including by an attitude even if passive, of publicly soliciting another person with a view to inciting that person to sexual relations in exchange for remuneration or a promise of remuneration is punished by two months' imprisonment and a fine of 100,000 francs.

Section 7: Incest

Article 325-10

The offense of incest is constituted and punished by five years' imprisonment and a fine of 5,000,000 francs, by the act of having sexual relations with one's ascendants or descendants without limitation of degree or with a full, consanguineous or uterine brother or sister.

Incest committed between persons of the same sex constitutes an aggravating circumstance and is punished by seven years' imprisonment and a fine of 10,000,000 francs.

Except in cases of notorious cohabitation or incestuous marriage, prosecution may only be initiated upon complaint by a relative and only against the person or persons designated in the complaint.

Chapter 6

Offenses against the family Section 1: Bigamy

Article 326-1

Any woman who, being bound by the ties of marriage, contracts another marriage before the dissolution of the previous one is punished by three years' imprisonment and a fine of 1,200,000 francs.

The same applies to a monogamous man who contracts a second marriage or to one who, having four lawful wives, contracts a fifth union.

The public officer who knowingly lends his ministry to such marriages is punished by the same penalties.

Section 2: Adultery

Article 326-2

Adultery is the act of having consensual sexual intercourse with a person other than one's spouse.

Article 326-3

Adultery by a wife may only be reported by the husband. Adultery by a husband may only be reported by the lawful wife or wives.

Article 326-4

A spouse convicted of adultery is punished by two years' imprisonment and a fine of 200,000 francs. The accomplice is punished as the adulterous spouse.

Section 3

Abandonment of the marital home - Abandonment of the household or child and failure to produce a child

Article 326-5

A wife who abandons the marital home without serious cause or a spouse who abandons his or her spouse or child and refuses to provide for their maintenance is punished by three months' imprisonment and a fine of 120,000 francs.

Article 326-6

The act of wrongfully refusing to produce a minor child to the person who has the right to claim that child is punished by one year's imprisonment and a fine of 100,000 francs.

Article 326-7

Any person who refuses to comply with a child custody order is punished by three years' imprisonment and a fine of 1,000,000 francs.

Section 4: Repudiation

Article 326-8

Repudiation is the expressed and unequivocal will of a spouse to unilaterally break the conjugal bond.

Any spouse convicted of repudiation is punished by the penalties set out in Article 326-5 above.

Article 326-9

In the cases provided for in Articles 326-4, 326-5 and 326-8 above, prosecution may only be brought upon complaint by the husband, the wife or the person responsible for the custody of the child. They remain free to discontinue proceedings or to discontinue the effect of the conviction. Such withdrawal benefits the accomplice.

Section 5: Overbidding of the dowry - Exchange of women

Article 326-10

Any person who, by overbidding the dowry, promises, gifts, any means of persuasion or corruption, obtains or attempts to obtain in marriage a woman or girl already promised to another man, is punished by two years' imprisonment and a fine of 400,000 francs. Confiscation of the means of corruption is ordered.

Persons, including relatives, who have knowingly incited, aided or assisted the perpetrator in accomplishing the above-mentioned acts are punished by the same penalties.

These penalties are also applicable to individuals who have been guilty of exchanging women as well as to their accomplices.

Chapter 7

Offenses against gender Section 1: Gender

Article 327-1

Gender means the set of socially and culturally constructed relationships that determine the roles, behaviors, attitudes, rights and duties associated with men, women, boys and girls respectively.

Section 2: Sexual assault and other gender-based violence

Article 327-2

Sexual assault is constituted by any sexual violation committed with violence, threat or surprise upon a person without that person's consent.

Any person guilty of sexual assault is punished by two years' imprisonment, a fine of 500,000 francs and a residence ban of ten years.

Aggravating circumstances are constituted by the fact that the assault results in injury or lesion, the fact that the person sexually assaulted is a child, the fact that the perpetrator is an ascendant or a person having authority over the person sexually assaulted, the fact that the perpetrator abuses the authority conferred by his or her functions, the fact that the perpetrator commits the sexual assault with other persons, whether perpetrators or accomplices, the fact that the perpetrator commits the sexual assault with use or threat of a weapon, administration of psychotropic or alcoholic substances.

Any person guilty of sexual assault in one or more of the aforementioned aggravating circumstances is punished by ten years' imprisonment, a fine of 1,000,000 francs and a residence ban of ten years.

Section 3: Castration

Article 327-3

Castration is the voluntary ablation or amputation of an organ necessary for reproduction.

Outside of any medical prescription, any person guilty of the crime of castration is punished by thirty years' imprisonment.

If death has resulted therefrom, the perpetrator is punished by life imprisonment.

Section 4: Sexual harassment

Article 327-4

Sexual harassment is constituted by the act of giving orders, using words, gestures, writings, messages and this, repeatedly, uttering threats, imposing constraints, exerting pressure or using any other means for the purpose of obtaining from a person in a situation of vulnerability or subordination, favors of a sexual nature for one's own benefit or for the benefit of a third party against the will of the harassed person.

The situation of vulnerability of the victim may result from age, social and economic status as well as physical or mental condition or any other related situation left to the judge's discretion.

Article 327-5

Any person guilty of sexual harassment is punished by three years' imprisonment.

Article 327-6 : The following constitute aggravating circumstances:

1°) the harassed person being a minor child;

2°) the perpetrator being an ascendant or a person having authority over the sexually harassed person;

3°) the perpetrator abusing the authority conferred by their functions;

4°) the perpetrator committing the act of sexual harassment with other persons, whether as perpetrators or accomplices;

5°) the perpetrator committing the act of sexual harassment with use or threat of a weapon.

Whoever is guilty of sexual harassment in one or more of the aforementioned aggravating circumstances is punished by imprisonment of five years.

If it has resulted, for the sexually harassed person, in an illness, an incapacity for personal work for more than twenty days, the loss of employment or the abandonment of studies, the applicable penalty is ten years' imprisonment.

Section 5 : Forced pornography

Article 327-7 : The following constitutes the offence of forced pornography

1°) the act of producing, being in possession of or disseminating any photographic, filmed, video, sound recording or other representation, featuring either a person engaging in explicit sexual activity without their consent, or the sexual organs or anal region of the person without their consent;

2°) the act of producing, being in possession of or disseminating any writing, photographic, filmed, video or other representation or any sound recording that encourages sexual activity with a person without their consent;

3°) the act of producing, being in possession of or disseminating any writing whose dominant characteristic is the description, for a sexual purpose, of sexual activity with a person without their consent.

Pornography involving a child is assimilated to forced pornography.

Whoever is guilty of forced pornography is punished by imprisonment of ten years, a fine of 5,000,000 francs and a residence ban of ten years.

The following constitutes an aggravating circumstance of the offence: the act of producing, being in possession of or disseminating pornography featuring a child or a person with a disability incapable of formulating consent.

Whoever is guilty of forced pornography in one of the aggravating circumstances provided for in the preceding paragraph is punished by imprisonment of twenty years, a fine of 10,000,000 francs and a residence ban of ten years.

Section 6 : Sex tourism involving children

Article 327-8

The following constitutes the offence of sex tourism involving children: any travel whose essential purpose is the intention to facilitate or to have sexual relations with a child, the sale of children for this same purpose, the prostitution of children or any other illegal sexual practice involving a child.

Whoever is guilty of sex tourism involving children is punished by imprisonment of ten years, a fine of 5,000,000 francs and a residence ban of ten years.

Section 7 : The offence of forced sterilisation

Article 327-9

Forced sterilisation constitutes any act, of a nature to deprive of the biological capacity of reproduction, committed on a person without their consent and not justified by a therapeutic necessity.

Whoever is guilty of forced sterilisation is punished by imprisonment of twenty years, a fine of 1,000,000 francs and a residence ban of ten years.

Article 327-10

The offences provided for by articles 327-8, 327-9 and 327-11 of this Code are excluded from the scope of mediation or any amicable settlement.

Section 8 : Forced marriage

Article 327-11

Forced marriage constitutes the union between a man and a woman, celebrated by a civil registrar or a minister of religion, of which at least one has not given their free and full consent to the marriage.

Whoever celebrates, forces, facilitates or is an accomplice to a forced marriage is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 1,000,000 francs.

Any agreement by which the hand of a person is granted without their consent is assimilated to forced marriage.

Section 9 : Child marriage

Article 327-12

Child marriage constitutes the union between a man and a woman, celebrated by a civil registrar or a minister of religion, of which at least one is under sixteen years of age.

Any unofficial union involving at least one person under sixteen years of age is assimilated to child marriage.

Whoever celebrates, forces or facilitates a child marriage is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 1,000,000 francs.

Any agreement by which the hand of a person under sixteen years of age is granted is assimilated to child marriage.

Section 10 : Forced levirate

Article 327-13

Forced levirate constitutes the forced union of a widow with a relative of her deceased husband.

Whoever by coercion, threat or blackmail celebrates or is an accomplice to a forced levirate is punished by imprisonment of three years and a fine of 1,000,000 francs.

Section 11 : Forced sororate

Article 327-14

Forced sororate constitutes the forced union of a widower with a relative of his deceased wife.

Whoever by coercion, threat or blackmail celebrates or is an accomplice to a forced sororate is punished by imprisonment of three years and a fine of 1,000,000 francs.

Section 12 : Gender-based insult

Article 327-15

Gender-based insult constitutes any invective, vulgar or contemptuous expression, not preceded by provocation, said or written by one person to another in private or in public, which imputes no specific fact to the insulted person and which is uttered against a person by reason of gender. Whoever is guilty of an insult against a person by reason of gender is punished by imprisonment of one year and a fine of 250,000 francs.

Section 13 : Economic discrimination in the workplace

Article 327-16

Economic discrimination in the workplace constitutes any discrimination related to gender and having in particular the purpose of hindering the normal exercise of any economic activity by a person, of refusing to hire a person, of not giving them equal pay or of not allowing them access to training and promotion opportunities, of sanctioning them in their workplace or of dismissing them.

Whoever is guilty of economic discrimination in the workplace against a person by reason of gender is punished by imprisonment of three years and a fine of 1,000,000 francs.

Section 14 : Refusal to assume a duty associated with parental authority

Article 327-17

Any refusal to assume a duty of parental authority for reasons of gender is prohibited, in particular to protect the child in their safety, health, full development or morality, to ensure their custody, supervision or education, or to ensure the costs of maintenance or education of the child.

Whoever is guilty of a refusal to assume a duty associated with parental authority is punished by imprisonment of three years and a fine of 1,000,000 francs.

Section 15

Denial of resources, services or opportunities with regard to a person

Article 327-18

Denial of resources, services or opportunities means any prevention of legitimate access to resources, economic assets, services or opportunities with regard to a person, and in particular:
1°) preventing a person from receiving their share of inheritance on the basis of gender;
2°) preventing a person from having access to land on the basis of gender;
3°) a person having their income confiscated, in particular by a member of their family, on the basis of gender;

4°) preventing a person from accessing basic social services, in particular education and health, on the basis of gender;

5°) preventing a person from engaging in a professional activity, on the basis of gender.

Whoever commits a denial of resources, services or opportunities is punished by three years' imprisonment and a fine of 1,000,000 francs.

Section 16 : Du refus d'exercer ses obligations suite à une rupture

Article 327-19

It constitutes an offence for a person not to execute a judicial decision or an approved judicial agreement imposing obligations upon them for the benefit of their former spouse and their children. These obligations may be imposed on the former spouse, whether the marriage was celebrated before the civil registrar or before a minister of religion.

Proof of a marriage celebrated before a minister of religion may be provided by any means.

Whoever refuses to perform their obligations following a separation is punished by three years' imprisonment and a fine of 1,000,000 francs.

Section 17 : De l'imposition d'un interdit alimentaire

Article 327-20

Whoever imposes a dietary prohibition negatively affecting the health of the mother, foetus or infant in the event of pregnancy or childbirth is punished by three years' imprisonment and a fine of 300,000 francs.

Section 18 : Du gavage

Article 327-21

Force-feeding means any act aimed at forcing a person to ingest an excessive quantity of food in relation to their needs.

Whoever is guilty of force-feeding is punished by three years' imprisonment and a fine of 300,000 francs.

LIVRE IV

DES CRIMES ET DELITS CONTRE LA PROPRIETE TITRE I: DES APPROPRIATIONS FRAUDULEUSES

Chapitre 1

Des atteintes à la propriété intellectuelle-brevets d'invention- modèles d'utilité-marques de produits ou de services-dessins ou modèles industriels-œuvres littéraires et artistiques

[Loi n°2017-012 du 1er juin 2017 fixant le régime de la propriété littéraire et artistique]

Article 411-1

Any infringement of the rights of a patent holder, holder of a utility model or creator of an industrial design or model, whether by manufacture of a product or by use of means that are the subject of the patent, public utility model, industrial design or model constitutes the offence of counterfeiting punished by five years' imprisonment and a fine of 15,000,000 francs.

Article 411-2

Receivers and those who sell or introduce into the national territory one or more counterfeit objects are punished by the same penalties as counterfeiters.

In the event of a repeat offence, in addition to the fine referred to in the preceding article, a term of imprisonment of six months may be imposed.

Article 411-3

Any edition of a writing, musical composition, drawing, painting or any other production printed or engraved in whole or in part in disregard of the laws and regulations relating to the property of authors, constitutes the offence of counterfeiting punished by five years' imprisonment and a fine of 15,000,000 francs.

Article 411-4

Counterfeiting of a trademark, use of a counterfeit trademark and fraudulent affixing of a trademark belonging to another person, constitute infringements of the rights of the owner of the trademark and are punished by three years' imprisonment and a fine of 20,000,000 francs.

The Court may also order the confiscation, repackaging or destruction of the seized counterfeit products.

Chapitre 2 : Des vols

Article 412-1

Whoever fraudulently misappropriates a thing that does not belong to them is guilty of theft.

Section 1 : Des vols qualifiés

Article 412-2

Any individual guilty of theft committed in a gang or with a weapon is punished by death.

The same penalty applies in the event of theft committed with the use of violence, with or without carrying a weapon, or with the use of intoxicating or anaesthetic substances.

Article 412-3

Any individual guilty of theft committed at night, with one of the following circumstances, is punished by life imprisonment:

1°) in an inhabited dwelling;

2°) with the use of breaking and entering, scaling or false keys within the meaning of articles 132-46 and 132-47 of this Code;

3°) by two or more persons.

Article 412-4

Any individual guilty of theft to the detriment of the army committed in a barracks, quarters, premises, depot, on board a ship, aircraft or any vehicle or in any other military establishment is punished by the penalty provided for in the preceding article.

Article 412-5

Any individual guilty of theft committed at night is punished by ten years' imprisonment and a ten-year residence ban.

The same penalties apply in the event of theft committed during the day, with one of the following circumstances:

1°) with the use of breaking and entering, scaling or false keys within the meaning of articles 132-46 and 132-47 of this Code;

2°) by two or more persons;

3°) if the theft relates to livestock;

4°) if the thief is a domestic servant or employee, even when they commit the theft against persons they do not serve, but who are either in the house of their employer, or in the house to which they accompany them, or if they are a worker or apprentice, in the house, workshop, shop or agricultural operation of their employer, or an individual working in the dwelling where they stole.

5°) if the theft is committed by the employer to the detriment of their domestic servant, hired servant, worker or apprentice.

Article 412-6

For the purposes of this Code, an inhabited dwelling means any building, lodging, hut, cabin even if mobile, tent which, without being currently inhabited, is intended for habitation.

Section 2 : Des vols simples-des grivèleries

Article 412-7

All other thefts not specified in this section, petty thefts and pilfering, as well as attempts at these same offences, are punished by five years' imprisonment and a fine of 1,800,000 francs.

The guilty parties may, in addition, be deprived of the rights mentioned in article 131-6 of this Code for ten years, from the day they have served their sentence.

They may also be subject to a residence ban for ten years.

Article 412-8

Whoever, knowing that they are in absolute impossibility of paying, has drinks or food served to them or consumed by a third party in establishments intended for that purpose, has themselves or a third party lodged or transported, is punished by six months' imprisonment and a fine of 100,000 francs.

Article 412-9

Whoever uses a vehicle against the will or without the consent of its owner is punished by the penalties set out in article 412-7 above.

Chapitre 3 : Des fraudes

Section 1 : Des fraudes et autres pratiques illicites

Article 413-1

Any act performed with the intention of evading a provision of any nature relating to the examination system in schools, institutes and faculties constitutes the offence of examination fraud.

Any act performed with the intention of evading a provision of any nature relating to any recruitment process aimed at the designation, by a panel following an appropriate test, of the candidate or candidates, constitutes the offence of competitive examination fraud.

Article 413-2

The following are notably considered as fraud in an examination or competitive examination, all practices tending to:

1°) transmit, communicate, disseminate or sell examination papers, their answer keys or their solutions;

2°) substitute said examination papers, results or lists of candidates;

3°) modify by addition or removal of marks or names of candidates from lists relating to said examinations or competitive examinations;

4°) communicate a code or any sign to certain candidates, with a view to identifying them and attributing to them an undeserved mark;

5°) corrupt an examiner, an invigilator, a supervisor, a jury president, a candidate or any person participating in the organisation of the competitive examination or examination;

6°) carry out any blackmail against an examiner, an invigilator, a supervisor, a jury president, a candidate or any person participating in the organisation of the competitive examination or examination;

7°) fraudulently use any means of communication.

Article 413-3

Is punished by imprisonment of three years and a fine of 3,000,000 francs, anyone who, by any means and in any form whatsoever, commits fraud in or on the occasion of an examination or public competitive examination having as its object entry into a public administration or the obtaining of an official diploma.

Article 413-4

Is punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 2,000,000 francs, anyone who through carelessness, negligence or non-observance of regulations facilitates fraud in an examination or competitive examination.

Article 413-5

The imprisonment is three years and the fine 1,000,000 francs, when the fraud is committed on the occasion of an examination or competitive examination other than public.

Article 413-6

Anyone who commits an offence of this nature, notably by delivering to a third party or by knowingly communicating, before or during the examination or competitive examination, to any of the interested parties the text or subject of the test or by making use of false documents, such as diplomas, certificates, birth extracts or others, or by substituting a third person for the true candidates or by carrying out one of the acts provided for in article 413-2 above is punished by the penalties provided for in article 413-3 above.

Section 2: Unlawful retention

Article 413-7

Is punished by the penalties provided for in article 413-3 above, anyone who voluntarily retains in their possession information, documents, copies of tests, examinations or competitive examinations, tools or any other documents, whatever their form or medium, belonging to another.

Article 413-8

The attempt of the offences provided for in this chapter is punished as the offences themselves.

Article 413-9: The same penalties are pronounced against accomplices to the offences

Article 413-10

Public prosecution does not preclude disciplinary action in all cases where the law has provided for the latter.

Chapter 4

Extortion-Fraudulent dispossession-Disposal of another's property

Section 1: Extortion-fraudulent dispossession

Article 414-1

Anyone who extorts by force, violence or constraint, the signature or delivery of a writing, an act, a title, any document whatsoever, containing or effecting an obligation, disposition or discharge, is punished by criminal imprisonment of twenty years and prohibition of residence of twenty years.

Anyone who, by means of written or verbal threats, revelations or defamatory imputations, extorts or attempts to extort, either the delivery of funds or securities, or the signature or delivery of the writings enumerated in the preceding paragraph, is punished by imprisonment of five years.

Prohibition of residence of ten years and incapacity to ever hold public employment may be pronounced.

The person subject to seizure who destroys, misappropriates or attempts to destroy or misappropriate objects seized from them, is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 300,000 francs.

These penalties are equally applicable to any debtor, borrower or third party pledgor, who destroys, misappropriates or attempts to destroy or misappropriate objects given by them as pledges.

Article 414-2

Anyone who, by force or by fraudulent means, dispossesses another of immovable property, is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 300,000 francs without prejudice, where applicable, to the penalties incurred for armed assembly, violence and assault, threats, fraud and other offences.

The attempt is punished as the offence itself.

Article 414-3

The provisions of article 414-2 above apply to provisional titles capable of being registered.

Section 2: Disposal of another's property

Article 414-4

The sale or pledging of another's property consented to in bad faith is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 1,800,000 francs.

Chapter 5

Fraud and other related offences Section 1: Fraud

Article 415-1

Anyone who, either by using false names, false titles, or false qualities, or by abuse of a true quality, or by employing fraudulent manoeuvres, characterised lies, to persuade of the existence of false enterprises, of an imaginary power or credit, or to create hope or fear of a success or an accident or any other chimerical event, causes or attempts to cause the delivery to them of funds, titles, objects or movable effects and, by one of these means, defrauds or attempts to defraud all or part of another's fortune, obtains or attempts to obtain services, is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 1,200,000 francs.

These penalties are applicable to any person who gives or attempts to give in marriage a girl already married or promised or a girl over whom custom confers no right upon them and who receives or attempts to receive all or part of the dowry.

Section 2: Stellionate

Article 415-2

Constitutes the offence of stellionate, any manoeuvre consisting in selling property which one knows one does not own, selling the same property to several persons, presenting as unencumbered property that is mortgaged or understating the mortgages that burden property.

Article 415-3: Is deemed a stellionataire

1°) anyone who causes a real right to be registered on a title which they know does not belong to them and anyone who knowingly accepts a certificate of registration thus established;

2°) anyone who, knowingly, transfers a title of ownership which they know does not belong to them and anyone who knowingly accepts this transfer, in this case, the penalty is doubled, without prejudice to damages;

3°) anyone who, obliged to register a legal mortgage on property subject to registration or a forced mortgage on registered property, grants a conventional mortgage on the property that should have been charged;

4°) anyone who, in bad faith, has caused to be registered by the State and transferred to their benefit an immovable.

Ministerial officers and public officials or agents within the meaning of article 241-14 of this Code having participated in the drafting of acts tainted with stellionate are prosecuted as accomplices.

Stellionate is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 4,000,000 francs.

Article 415-4

Is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs whoever:

1°) converts a provisional title into a definitive title, as manager of the land ownership system without observing all the required procedures incumbent upon them;

2°) as manager of the land ownership system, abusively cancels a clause encumbering a property;

3°) in the capacity of granting authority, allocates to the same person, by subdivision of surface area, lands from the private immovable domain of the State on the same site;

4°) makes false declarations in the context of the adversarial boundary demarcation procedure or the determination of customary land rights;

5°) refuses to comply with the summons of the manager of the land ownership system;

6°) alienates a portion of the public domain without prior declassification;

7°) violates the rules relating to the policing, conservation and use of the public domain, traffic policing, as well as the exercise of military easements and public utility easements;

8°) issues several sale agreements, or relocation notifications to different persons on the same parcel;

9°) signs, as mayor, neighborhood or village chief, any agreement relating to an immovable property that has not been the subject of confirmation of land rights;

10°) in bad faith illegally occupies or develops an immovable property without the prior consent of the owner or holder of the land right by a natural or legal person;

11°) carries out an urbanization operation on a domain that has not been the subject of allocation;

12°) by coercion, threat or any other pressure, compels an official of the administration of the Land Registry and Cadastre services to create, to transfer a land title, or to register real rights on a land title, to abusively cancel a resolutory clause, or to act in violation of the provisions of the Land and Property Code;

13°) acts in land matters in a domain where the law denies them any competence.

Article 415-5: Complicity in the above acts is punished as the offense itself

Article 415-6

Constitutes the offense of forgery in land and property matters and punished by imprisonment of ten years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs:

1°) the fraudulent alteration of land registers as well as the intentional use of said documents;

2°) the total or partial destruction of land registers;

3°) physical alterations intentionally caused with the aim of making the use or exploitation of land registers impossible.

Article 415-7

The offense of forgery indicated above is covered by the ten-year limitation period which begins to run from the commission of the acts.

Article 415-8

Constitutes the offense of criminal breach of trust and punished by the penalties of article 416-1 in fine of this Code, the act by a representative of the public authority of disposing without allocation or without granted competence, of lands from the private immovable domain of the State.

Article 415-9

The discovery of an act or transfer subject to the provisions of this Code not presented to the land registry office for publication within the prescribed time limits is sanctioned by the application of a double fee in addition without this fee being less than double the fixed fees relating thereto.

Article 415-10

The voluntary presentation, outside the time limits, of acts subject to the formality of publication is liable to an additional fee, without this fee being less than the amount of the fixed fees relating thereto.

Article 415-11

The person or parties who should have the formality of publication carried out bear the penalties.

Section 3

Offenses in matters of checks, bank cards and other electronic payment instruments and processes

[Law No. 10-020 of 31 May 2010 establishing uniform law relating to offenses in matters of checks, bank cards and other electronic payment instruments and processes]

Paragraph 1: Repression of offenses related to the use of checks

Article 415-12

Is punished by imprisonment of three years and a fine of 2,000,000 francs:

1°) the drawer or agent who, knowingly, issues a check domiciled on a closed account;

2°) the drawer who, after issuing a check, withdraws all or part of the provision, by transfer, wire transfer or any means whatsoever, with the intention of infringing the rights of others;

3°) the drawer who issues one or more checks in defiance of the injunction addressed to them pursuant to article 115 of the Regulation relating to payment systems in the Member States of the West African Economic and Monetary Union;

4°) the agent who, knowingly, issues one or more checks whose issuance is prohibited to their principal, pursuant to article 115 of the Regulation referred to above;

5°) any person who prohibits the drawee from paying outside the cases provided for by article 84 paragraph 3 of the Regulation referred to above;

6°) any person who agrees to receive or endorse, knowingly, a check without provision;

7°) any person who, knowingly, makes use of a stolen check.

The attempt of the offenses listed above is punished as the completed offense.
The fine referred to in paragraph 1 may be increased to 5,000,000 francs if the drawer is a merchant or repeat offender.

Article 415-13

Is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs:
1°) any person who counterfeits, falsifies or attempts to counterfeit or falsify a check;
2°) any person who knowingly, makes use or attempts to make use of a counterfeited or falsified check;
3°) any person who, knowingly, agrees to receive a counterfeited or falsified check.

Article 415-14

Is punished by imprisonment of seven years and a fine of 10,000,000 francs whoever knowingly, manufactures, acquires, possesses, transfers, offers or makes available materials, machines, apparatus, equipment, instruments, computer programs or any data designed or specially adapted to commit the offenses provided for in point 1 of article 415-13 above.
The attempt of the offenses listed above is punished as the completed offense.

Article 415-15

The confiscation, for purposes of destruction, of counterfeited or falsified checks, is mandatory in the cases provided for in articles 415-12 to 415-14 above.
Also mandatory is the confiscation of materials, machines, apparatus, equipment, instruments, computer programs or any data that serve or are intended to serve in the manufacture of said checks, except when they are used without the knowledge of the owner.

Article 415-16

In all cases provided for in Articles 415-12 and 415-13 above, the competent court pursuant to Article 113 of the Regulation referred to in Article 415-12 above, shall prohibit the convicted person, for a period of five years, from issuing cheques other than those which exclusively permit the withdrawal of funds by the drawer from the drawee or those which are certified.

This prohibition may be declared provisionally enforceable. It is accompanied by an injunction addressed to the convicted person to return to the bankers who had issued them the cheque forms in his possession and in that of his agents.

The competent court may order, at the expense of the convicted person, the publication of the extract of the decision imposing the prohibition in the newspapers it designates and according to the procedures it determines.

Any banker informed of the prohibition by the Central Bank in accordance with Articles 127 and 129 of the Regulation referred to in Article 415-12 above, must refrain from issuing to the convicted person and his agents cheque forms other than those mentioned in paragraph 2 of this article.

When the conviction is pronounced following a payment incident noted on a joint account with or without joint and several liability, the prohibition provided for in paragraph 2 of this article is, by operation of law, applicable to the other holders with regard to said account.

Article 415-17

Is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 2,000,000 francs, pursuant to Article 415-16 paragraph 1 above:

1°) the drawer who issues one or more cheques in violation of the prohibition imposed;

2°) the agent who, knowingly, issues one or more cheques the issuance of which is prohibited to his principal.

Article 415-18

The acts referred to in Articles 415-12 and 415-13 above, are considered, for the application of provisions concerning recidivism, as constituting an offence of the same nature. In case of recidivism, double the fine provided for in the aforementioned articles is imposed.

Article 415-19

On the occasion of criminal proceedings brought against the drawer, the holder who has joined as a civil party is entitled to claim before the competent criminal court a sum equal to the amount of the cheque, without prejudice, where applicable, to all damages. He may, nevertheless, if he prefers, bring an action for payment of his claim before the civil or commercial court.

Article 415-20

Is punished by a fine of 3,000,000 francs the drawee who, outside the cases mentioned in Article 84 paragraph 3 of the Regulation referred to in Article 415-12 above, refuses payment of a cheque on the ground that the drawer has placed a stop payment on it.

Article 415-21 : Is punished by a fine of 3,000,000 francs

1°) the drawee who indicates a provision lower than the existing and available provision;

2°) the drawee who rejects a cheque for insufficiency or unavailability of the provision without indicating, when such is the case, that the cheque was issued in defiance of an injunction addressed pursuant to Article 115 of the Regulation referred to in Article 415-12 above or in violation of a prohibition imposed pursuant to Article 6 paragraph 1 of Law No. 10-020 of 31 May 2010 enacting the uniform law on offences relating to cheques, bank cards and other electronic payment instruments and processes;

3°) the drawee who has not declared, under the conditions provided for in Article 127 of the Regulation referred to above, payment incidents as well as the offences provided for by Articles 415-12 (a) to (g), 415-13 and 415-17 above;

4°) the drawee who contravenes the provisions of Articles 113, 115, 123 of the Regulation referred to above and of Article 415-16 paragraph 2 above;

5°) the drawee who contravenes the provisions of Articles 43 and 45 of the Regulation referred to in Article 415-12 above.

In the cases referred to in points 1, 2, 3, 4 and 5, the drawee legal person may be brought by the victim before the court seized of the public action for unpaid cheque, for compensation for the damage related to the faults indicated above.

Article 415-22

Is punished by imprisonment of three years and a fine of 10,000,000 francs, anyone who uses for purposes other than those provided for by the provisions of the Regulation, the information centralized by the Central Bank pursuant to Articles 127 to 130 of said Regulation referred to in Article 415-12 above.

Article 415-23

Is punished by imprisonment of three years and a fine of 10,000,000 francs, anyone who ensures, in place of the Central Bank, without express authorization from the BCEAO, the centralization of information provided for by Articles 127 to 130 of the Regulation referred to in Article 415-12 above.

Article 415-24

Is punished by imprisonment of three years and a fine of 10,000,000 francs anyone who disseminates without the prior authorization of the Central Bank, information obtained pursuant to Article 129 paragraph 7 of the Regulation referred to in Article 415-12 above.

Paragraphe 2

Prosecution of offences relating to bank cards and other electronic payment instruments and processes

Article 415-25 : Is punished by a fine of 2,000,000 francs

1°) any issuer who issues a payment card in violation of Article 139 paragraphs 1 and 2 of the Regulation referred to in Article 415-12 above. The competent court orders the withdrawal of the card;

2°) any issuer who fails to inform the Central Bank within the required time limits of the existence of an abuse noted in the use of the payment card or who does not comply with the provisions of Article 140 of the Regulation referred to above.

Article 415-26

Are punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 10,000,000 francs:
1°) those who counterfeit, falsify or attempt to counterfeit or falsify a bank card or any other electronic payment instrument;
2°) those who, knowingly, make use or attempt to make use of a bank card or any other electronic payment instrument counterfeited, falsified or fraudulently obtained;
3°) those who, knowingly, agree to receive a payment by means of a bank card or any other electronic payment instrument counterfeited, falsified or fraudulently obtained;

4°) those who possess, knowingly, a bank card or any other electronic payment instrument counterfeited, falsified or fraudulently obtained.

Article 415-27

Is punished by imprisonment of seven years and a fine of 10,000,000 francs, anyone who, knowingly, manufactures, acquires, possesses, transfers, offers or makes available equipment, instruments, computer programs or any data designed or specially adapted to commit the offences provided for in Article 415-26 point 1 of this section.

The attempt of the offences listed above is punished as the completed offence.

Article 415-28

Are punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 10,000,000 francs those who:

a) use without authorization and, knowingly, identification data for the initiation or processing of an electronic payment transaction;

b) use, knowingly, fictitious identification data for the initiation or processing of an electronic payment transaction;

c) manipulate data or information relating to accounts or other identification data, with a view to the initiation or processing of an electronic payment transaction;

d) transmit without authorization identification data with a view to the initiation or processing of an electronic payment transaction;

e) manufacture, handle, possess or use without authorization specific equipment, with a view to:
the manufacture or alteration of a bank card, an electronic purse or part thereof; the initiation or processing of an electronic payment transaction; the modification or alteration of any information or data relating to any electronic payment instrument or transaction.
f) possess without authorization and, knowingly, an element or part of a bank card or any other electronic payment instrument.

The attempt of the offences listed above is punished as the completed offence.

The same penalties are applied to any person involved, as an accomplice by aid or instigation, with the perpetrators of the above-mentioned offences and involving criminal intent or who obtains, knowingly, values or pecuniary advantages arising from said offences.

Article 415-29

Are punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 10,000,000 francs those who knowingly use a bank card or any other electronic payment instrument and process after:
the expiration of its period of validity, with fraudulent intent; opposition for loss, theft, fraudulent use or opening of collective proceedings against the beneficiary.

Are punished by the same penalties, those who, despite the injunction to return received, continue to use the bank card or any other electronic payment instrument and process irregularly held.

Article 415-30

Are punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 10,000,000 francs those who carry out, knowingly, or cause to be carried out, attempt to carry out or cause to be carried out a transfer of money or monetary value, with the aim of obtaining an illegal economic advantage or obtaining it for another person by:

a) introducing, altering, erasing or deleting computer data;

b) disrupting the operation of software or a computer system.

Article 415-31

Are punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 10,000,000 francs those who, knowingly, manufacture, receive, obtain, sell, transfer, possess or attempt to manufacture, receive, obtain, sell, transfer or possess illegally:

a) equipment, instruments, articles, software or any other means specially adapted to commit the offences referred to in Article 415-30 of this section;

b) equipment, instruments, articles, software or any other means specially adapted to commit the offences referred to in point e) of Article 415-23 of this Code;

c) software intended for the commission of the offences referred to in Article 415-30 of this Code.

Article 415-32

The confiscation, for purposes of destruction of counterfeit or falsified payment or withdrawal cards, is mandatory in the cases provided for in Articles 415-26 to 415-29 and 415-31 of this section.
Also mandatory is the confiscation of materials, machines, apparatus, instruments, computer programs or any data which serve or are intended for the manufacture of said objects, except when they are used without the knowledge of the owner.

Article 415-33: The offences provided for in this section constitute offences

The decisions pronounced on civil interests are enforceable provisionally, on minute and before registration.
The decisions rendered in application of this section are notified without delay to the Central Bank at the diligence of the Public Prosecutor.
The Central Bank is required to disseminate to issuing institutions all judicial decisions, according to procedures it defines.

Section 4: Abuse of blank signature

Article 415-34

Whoever, abusing a blank signature which has been entrusted to him, fraudulently writes thereon an obligation or discharge or any other act which may compromise the person or fortune of the signatory, is punished by the penalties set forth in Article 415-12 of this Code.
In the case where the blank signature has not been entrusted to him, he is prosecuted as a forger and punished as such.

Section 5: Illegal speculation

Article 415-35

Those who, either in order to obtain a gain which would not be the result of free competition in trade or the natural play of the law of supply and demand, or with any other immoral intention or contrary to the general interest by any means whatsoever, directly or through an intermediary, operate or attempt to operate the artificial increase or decrease of the price of foodstuffs or goods or public or private securities, are punished by imprisonment of three years and a fine of 100,000,000 francs.

The fine provided for in the preceding paragraph may be increased to 10% of the turnover achieved during the last financial year closed at the date of the decision by each of the enterprises having participated in the offence.

Section 6: Misleading advertising

Article 415-36

Any merchant, industrialist, artisan or service provider who carries out misleading advertising is punished by imprisonment of three years and a fine of 10,000,000 francs.

In addition the Court may order the publication of a corrective notice at the expense of the convicted person. In all cases, the department in charge of Trade, Consumption and Competition may, as a precautionary measure, order the cessation of the advertising.

Is misleading, any advertising containing:

1°) in any form whatsoever, allegations, indications, presentations that are false or likely to mislead when these relate to one or more of the following elements of the good or product:
the existence, nature, quality, species, origin, composition, method and date of manufacture, substantial qualities, prices and conditions of sale, conditions of use;
the results expected from the use of the product, the reason or method of sale;
compliance with safety standards when the product is subject thereto, the identity, qualities or aptitudes of the manufacturer, reseller, service providers, promoters and the quality of commitments made by the latter.

2°) the indication of price reductions or any advantages whatsoever which are not actually granted to any purchaser under the conditions stated by the advertising.

3°) indications to the consumer concerning articles which are not available for sale or services which cannot be provided during the period to which this advertising relates.

Article 415-37: In the cases referred to in Articles 415-35 and 415

36 above, without prejudice to the right of prosecution of the administration of Trade, Consumption and Competition, any consumer or any approved consumer association who is a victim may refer the matter to the competent authority.

Chapter 6: Breach of trust and other related offences

Section 1: Breach of trust

Article 416-1

Breach of trust is the fraudulent misappropriation, committed to the detriment of the owner or holder of a sum of money, a document or any movable object whatsoever, which would have been entrusted for whatever reason by said owner or holder to the perpetrator of the misappropriation, on the condition that the latter return it or produce it.

Any person guilty of breach of trust is punished by three years' imprisonment and a fine of 1,220,000 francs.

If the breach of trust provided for above is committed by a domestic servant, pupil, clerk, employee, worker or apprentice, to the detriment of his master, the penalty is five years' imprisonment.

If the breach of trust is committed by a ministerial officer, a public official within the meaning of Article 241-14 of this Code, acting in the exercise or on the occasion of the exercise of his duties, the penalty is ten years' imprisonment and a fine which may not exceed 6,000,000 francs.

Section 2: Usury

Article 416-2

Any loan or any agreement concealing a loan of money granted, in any matter, at an effective overall rate exceeding, on the date of stipulation, the usury rate constitutes a usurious loan.

The usury rate is determined by the Council of Ministers of the West African Monetary Union. It is published in the Official Journal or in a legal notices newspaper on the initiative of the Minister responsible for Finance.

Article 416-3

The effective overall rate is freely negotiated between the borrower and the lender subject to compliance with the ceiling set in Article 416-2 above.

Article 416-4

The effective overall rate of conventional interest is the interest rate calculated taking into account the amortization of the debt and to which are added fees, remuneration of any kind, including those paid to intermediaries involved in any manner whatsoever in the granting of the loan.

However, taxes and duties paid on the occasion of the conclusion or performance of the contract are not included in the calculation of the effective overall interest rate.

Article 416-5

The ceiling rate as defined in Article 416-2 above and beyond which the offence of usury is constituted, may be increased, for certain categories of transactions which, by reason of their nature, involve high fixed costs, by flat-rate charges the amount of which is fixed by the Council of Ministers of the West African Monetary Union, on the proposal of the Central Bank of West African States.

Article 416-6

Credits granted on the occasion of instalment sales are, for the application of these provisions, assimilated to conventional loans and as such, subject to the requirements of Article 416-2 above.

Article 416-7
