In the case of loans on foodstuffs or other movable things and in credit sale or barter transactions, the value of the things delivered or the price paid by the debtor, in principal and accessories, may not exceed the value of the things received by an amount greater than that corresponding to the maximum interest rate set in Article 416-2 above.

Article 416-8

Any person who grants a usurious loan to another or knowingly provides, in whatever capacity and in whatever manner, directly or indirectly, his assistance in obtaining or granting a usurious loan is punished by two years' imprisonment and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.

In the event of a repeat offence, the penalty may be increased to five years' imprisonment and the fine to 15,000,000 francs.

Article 416-9: In addition to the penalties set in the preceding article, the Court may order

1°) the publication of its decision at the expense of the convicted person in the newspapers it designates, as well as in any other form it deems appropriate;

2°) the provisional or permanent closure of the enterprise which has engaged or whose managers have engaged in usurious transactions, together with the appointment of an administrator or liquidator.

In the event of provisional closure, the offender or the enterprise must continue to pay its staff the salaries and allowances of any kind to which they are entitled. This period may not exceed three months.

In the event of a repeat offence, permanent closure is ordered.

Article 416-10: Those who, responsible in any capacity for the management or administration of an enterprise, company, association, cooperative, or other legal person, knowingly allow any person subject to their authority or control to contravene the provisions of this section are punished by the penalties provided for in Article 416

8 above and possibly by the measures set in Article 416-
9 above.

Article 416-11

When a loan is usurious, the excessive charges are automatically applied to the interest calculated under the conditions set in Article 416-4 above, then due and for the surplus, if applicable, to the capital of the debt.

If the debt is extinguished in capital and interest, the sums unduly received are returned with legal interest from the day they were paid.

Article 416-12

The limitation period for the offence of usury runs from the day of the last receipt, either of interest or of capital, or of the last delivery of a thing relating to the usurious transaction.

Article 416-13

The legal interest rate is, in all matters, fixed for the duration of the calendar year. It is, for the year in question, equal to the weighted average of the discount rate applied by the Central Bank of West African States during the preceding calendar year.

It is published in the Official Journal, on the initiative of the Minister responsible for Finance.

Article 416-14

In the event of conviction to pay interest at the legal interest rate, the latter is increased by half upon expiry of a period of two months from the day on which the court decision became enforceable, even provisionally.

Article 416-15

The provisions of Articles 416-2 to 416-14 above are not applicable to current contracts having a certain date.

Section 3: Removal of procedural documents

Article 416-16

Any person who, after having produced in a legal dispute any title, document or memorandum, removes it in any manner whatsoever, or refuses to produce it, is punished by three months' imprisonment and a fine of 200,000 francs.

Section 4: Obstruction of freedom of bidding

Article 416-17

Those who, in auctions for the ownership, usufruct or lease of movable or immovable property, an enterprise, a supply, an operation or any service, obstruct or disturb the freedom of bids or offers, by acts of violence, violence, threats or disturbances, either before or during the bids or offers, are punished by three months' imprisonment and a fine of 200,000 francs.

The same penalty is imposed on those who, by gifts, promises or any fraudulent manoeuvres, drive away bidders.

Section 5: Clandestine transport of passengers

Article 416-18

Any person who, piloting or ensuring the custody of a vehicle, not specially intended for the transport of passengers, without the express authorization of his employer, transports or attempts to transport one or more persons free of charge or for remuneration, is punished by six months' imprisonment and a fine of 100,000 francs.

TITLE II

OFFENCES PROVIDED FOR BY THE UNIFORM ACTS OF THE ORGANISATION FOR THE HARMONISATION OF BUSINESS LAW IN AFRICA (OHADA)

Chapter 1

Offences provided for in the Uniform Act of 15 December 2010 on general commercial law

Article 421-1

In application of articles 69 and 140 of the Uniform Act on general commercial law, any person required to complete one of the formalities prescribed in that uniform act who fails to do so, or who carries out a formality by fraud, is punished by three years' imprisonment and a fine of 1,000,000 francs.
If necessary, the competent court pronouncing the conviction orders the registration or rectification of inaccurate entries.

Chapter 2

Offences contained in the Uniform Act of 15 December 2010 on the organization of security interests

Article 422-1

In application of article 65 of the Uniform Act on the organization of security interests, any person who registers a movable security interest either by fraud or by entering inaccurate information or data in bad faith is punished by three years' imprisonment and a fine of 1,000,000 francs.

If necessary, the competent court pronouncing the conviction orders the registration or rectification of inaccurate entries.

Article 422-2

In application of article 184 of the Uniform Act on the organization of security interests, the lessee or any person who, by fraudulent maneuvers, deprives the lessor of his privilege totally or partially is punished by five years' imprisonment and a fine of 1,000,000 francs.

Chapter 3

Offences contained in the Uniform Act of 30 January 2014 on the law of commercial companies and economic interest groupings

Section 1: Offences relating to the formation of companies

Article 423-1

In application of article 886 of the Uniform Act on the law of commercial companies and economic interest groupings, the founders, the chairman and chief executive officer, the chief executive officer, the general manager or the deputy general manager of a public limited company who issue shares before registration or at any time when registration is obtained by fraud or the company is irregularly formed are punished by three years' imprisonment and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.

Article 423-2

In application of article 887 of the Uniform Act on the law of commercial companies and economic interest groupings, those who are punished by three years' imprisonment and a fine of 5,000,000 francs:

a)	knowingly affirm as true and accurate subscriptions which they know to be fictitious or declare that funds which have not been placed entirely at the disposal of the company have been actually paid;

b)	deliver to the notary or depositary a list of shareholders or subscription and payment forms mentioning fictitious subscriptions or payments of funds which have not been placed definitively at the disposal of the company;

c)	knowingly, by simulating subscription or payment or by publishing payment which does not exist or any other false facts, obtain or attempt to obtain subscriptions or payments;

d)	knowingly, to induce subscriptions or payments, publish the names of persons designated contrary to the truth as being or to be connected with the company in any capacity whatsoever;

e)	fraudulently, cause a contribution in kind to be assigned a valuation higher than its actual value.

Article 423-3

In application of article 888 of the Uniform Act on the law of commercial companies and economic interest groupings, those who knowingly negotiate are punished by three years' imprisonment and a fine of 5,000,000 francs:

a)	shares not fully paid up;

b)	cash shares for which payment of one quarter of the nominal value has not been made.

Section 2

Offences relating to the management, administration and direction of companies

Article 423-4

In application of article 889 of the Uniform Act on the law of commercial companies and economic interest groupings, company directors who, in the absence of an inventory or by means of fraudulent inventories, knowingly distribute fictitious dividends among the shareholders or members are punished by five years' imprisonment and a fine of 10,000,000 francs.

Article 423-5

In application of article 890 of the Uniform Act on the law of commercial companies and economic interest groupings, company directors who knowingly, even in the absence of any distribution of dividends, publish or present to shareholders or members, with a view to concealing the true situation of the company, summary financial statements not giving, for each financial year, a true picture of the operations of the financial year, the financial situation and that of the company's assets at the end of that period are punished by five years' imprisonment and a fine of 10,000,000 francs.

Incur the same penalties, in application of article 890-1 of the Uniform Act on the law of commercial companies and economic interest groupings, company directors who have not filed the summary financial statements within one month following their approval.

Article 423-6

In application of article 891 of the Uniform Act on the law of commercial companies and economic interest groupings, the manager of the limited liability company, the chairman of the simplified joint-stock company, the directors, the chairman and chief executive officer, the general manager or the deputy general manager who, in bad faith, make use of the company's assets or credit in a manner which they know to be contrary to the interests of the company, for personal purposes, material or moral, or to favor another legal entity in which they have an interest, directly or indirectly, are punished by five years' imprisonment and a fine of 20,000,000 francs.

Incur the same penalties, in application of article 891-1 of the Act on the law of commercial companies and economic interest groupings, company directors who, knowingly:
do not include the company name on acts and documents emanating from the company and intended for third parties;
do not precede or immediately follow the company name with the indication, in legible characters, of the form of the company, the amount of its share capital, the address of its registered office and the mention of its registration in the Trade and Movable Credit Register.

Incur the same penalties, in application of article 891-2 of the Uniform Act on the law of commercial companies and economic interest groupings, the company directors of a foreign company or the foreign natural person whose branch, beyond a period of two years, has been neither contributed to a pre-existing or to be created company under the law of one of the States Parties, nor struck off under the conditions laid down by article 120 of the said text.

Section 3: Offences relating to general meetings

Article 423-7

In application of article 891-3 of the Uniform Act on the law of commercial companies and economic interest groupings, those who knowingly prevent a shareholder or member from participating in a general meeting are punished by two years' imprisonment and a fine of 1,000,000 francs.

Article 423-8

In application of article 892 of the Uniform Act on the law of commercial companies and economic interest groupings, company directors who, knowingly, do not draw up the minutes of general meetings in the forms required by this Uniform Act are punished by two years' imprisonment and a fine of 1,000,000 francs.

Section 4

Offences relating to changes in the capital of public limited companies

Paragraphe 1 : De l'augmentation de capital

Article 423-9

In application of Article 893 of the Uniform Act relating to commercial companies and economic interest groups, the directors, the chairman of the board of directors, the chairman and chief executive officer, the chief executive officer, the general manager or the deputy general manager of a public limited company who, during a capital increase, issue shares or share certificates are punished by imprisonment for three years and a fine of 1,000,000 francs:

a)	before the depositary's certificate is drawn up;
b)	without the formalities prior to the capital increase being duly completed;
c)	without the previously subscribed capital of the company being fully paid up;
d)	without the new shares contributed in kind being fully paid up before the amendment registration in the Trade and Personal Property Credit Register;
e)	without the new shares being paid up by at least one quarter of their nominal value at the time of subscription;
f)	where applicable, without the entire share premium being paid up at the time of subscription.
Are punished by the same penalties, in application of Article 893-1 of the Uniform Act relating to commercial companies and economic interest groups, the managers of a limited liability company who, during a capital increase, have issued shares without these new shares having been paid up by at least half of their nominal value at the time of subscription.

Article 423-10

In application of Article 894 of the Uniform Act relating to commercial companies and economic interest groups, company directors who, during a capital increase, are punished by imprisonment for three years and a fine of 1,000,000 francs:	do not grant shareholders, in proportion to the amount of their shares, a preferential right to subscribe for cash shares when this right is not removed by the general meeting and the shareholders have not waived it;
do not reserve for shareholders a period of at least twenty days from the opening of the subscription, except when this period is closed early;
do not allocate the shares made available, for lack of a sufficient number of subscriptions on a non-reducible basis, to shareholders who have subscribed on a reducible basis for a number of shares greater than that which they could subscribe for on a non-reducible basis, in proportion to the rights they hold;
do not reserve the rights of holders of subscription warrants.

Article 423-11

In application of Article 895 of the Uniform Act relating to commercial companies and economic interest groups, company directors who knowingly give or confirm inaccurate information in the reports presented to the general meeting called to decide on the removal of the preferential subscription right are punished by imprisonment for three years and a fine of 1,000,000 francs.

Paragraphe 2 : De la réduction du capital

Article 423-12

In application of Article 896 of the Uniform Act relating to commercial companies and economic interest groups, the directors, the chairman and chief executive officer, the chief executive officer, the deputy general manager who knowingly proceed with a capital reduction are punished by imprisonment for three years and a fine of 1,000,000 francs:
without respecting the equality of shareholders;
without communicating the capital reduction project to the statutory auditors forty-five days before the holding of the general meeting called to rule on the capital reduction.

Section 5 : Des infractions relatives au contrôle des sociétés

Article 423-13

In application of Article 897 of the Uniform Act relating to commercial companies and economic interest groups, company directors who do not initiate the appointment of the company's statutory auditors or do not summon them to general meetings are punished by imprisonment for five years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.

Article 423-14

In application of Article 898 of the Uniform Act relating to commercial companies and economic interest groups, any person who, either in their own name or as a partner of a statutory auditors' firm, accepts, exercises or retains, knowingly, the functions of statutory auditor, notwithstanding legal incompatibilities, is punished by imprisonment for five years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.

Article 423-15

In application of Article 899 of the Uniform Act relating to commercial companies and economic interest groups, any statutory auditor who, either in their own name or as a partner of a statutory auditors' firm, knowingly gives or confirms false information about the situation of the company or who does not disclose to the Public Prosecutor criminal acts of which they have knowledge is punished by imprisonment for five years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.

Article 423- 16

In application of Article 900 of the Uniform Act relating to commercial companies and economic interest groups, company directors or any persons in the service of the company who knowingly obstruct the verifications or control of the statutory auditors or who refuse the communication, on site, of all documents useful for the exercise of their mission and in particular of all contracts, books, accounting documents and minutes registers are punished by imprisonment for five years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.

Section 6 : Des infractions relatives à la dissolution des sociétés

Article 423-17

In application of Article 901 of the Uniform Act relating to commercial companies and economic interest groups, company directors who knowingly, when the company's equity becomes less than half of the share capital due to losses recorded in the summary financial statements, are punished by imprisonment for five years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs:
do not convene, within four months following the approval of the summary financial statements that revealed these losses, the extraordinary general meeting for the purpose of deciding, if necessary, on the early dissolution of the company;
do not file at the registry of the Court responsible for commercial matters, do not have registered in the Trade and Personal Property Credit Register and do not publish, in a newspaper authorized to receive legal notices, the early dissolution of the company.

Section 7: Offences relating to the liquidation of companies

Article 423-18

In application of Article 902 of the Uniform Act on the law of commercial companies and economic interest groups, a liquidator of a company who knowingly:
within one month from his appointment, does not publish in a journal authorized to receive legal notices of the place of the registered office, the act appointing him liquidator and deposit with the Trade and Personal Property Credit Register the decisions pronouncing the dissolution;	does not convene the members, at the end of the liquidation, to decide on the final account of the liquidation, on the discharge of his management and the release of his mandate and to record the closure of the liquidation;	in the case provided for by Article 219 of the Uniform Act, does not deposit his final accounts at the registry of the Court responsible for commercial matters of the place of the registered office, nor request judicial approval thereof.

shall be punished by five years' imprisonment and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.

Article 423-19

In application of Article 903 of the Uniform Act on the law of commercial companies and economic interest groups, when the liquidation occurs by judicial decision, a liquidator who knowingly:
within six months of the closure of his appointment, does not present a report on the assets and liabilities of the company in liquidation and on the continuation of liquidation operations, nor request the necessary authorizations to complete them;
within three months of the closure of each financial year, does not prepare the summary financial statements in view of the inventory and a written report in which he accounts for the liquidation operations during the past financial year;	does not allow the members to exercise, during the liquidation period, their right to access company documents under the same conditions as previously;	does not convene the members, at least once a year, to report to them on the summary financial statements in case of continuation of the company's operations;	does not deposit in an account opened in a bank in the name of the company in liquidation, within fifteen days from the distribution decision, the sums allocated to distributions among the members and creditors;	does not deposit, in a consignment account opened in the books of the Public Treasury, within one year from the closure of the liquidation, the sums allocated to creditors or members and not claimed by them.

shall be punished by five years' imprisonment and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.

Article 423-20

In application of Article 904 of the Uniform Act on the law of commercial companies and economic interest groups, a liquidator who, in bad faith:
makes use of the assets or credit of the company in liquidation in a manner he knows to be contrary to the interest of the company, for personal purposes or to favor another legal entity in which he has an interest, directly or indirectly;
transfers all or part of the assets of the company in liquidation to a person having in the company the capacity of general partner, limited partner, manager, member of the board of directors, general administrator or auditor, without having obtained the unanimous consent of the members or, failing that, the authorization of the competent court.

shall be punished by five years' imprisonment and a fine of 20,000,000 francs.

Section 8

Offences relating to the failure to disclose information on the beneficial owners of legal entities and other legal arrangements

Article 423-21

In application of legislative and regulatory provisions on the obligation to disclose the beneficial owners of legal entities, shareholders and designated directors, managers and administrators of fiducies, trusts and other similar legal arrangements, as well as their directors, shareholders and designated directors, managers and administrators of fiducies, trusts and other similar legal arrangements who, knowingly:
fail to maintain and preserve adequate, accurate and up-to-date information on their own beneficial owners;
do not file with the competent authorities the declaration of their beneficial owners or fail to update said declaration;
do not make available to the competent authorities and reporting entities, without hindrance, in a timely manner, the above information;
do not keep for ten years the registers of beneficial owners;
do not disclose their status as designated director or candidate for such status;
perform belatedly or provide false declarations on beneficial owners.
Shall be punished by the penalties provided for by the uniform law on the fight against money laundering and the financing of terrorism with regard to the identification of beneficial owners.

Article 423-22

The above criminal sanction applies without prejudice to the specific fines provided for by the texts establishing this obligation.

Chapter 4

Offences contained in the Uniform Act of 10 September 2015 on the organization of collective proceedings for the settlement of liabilities

Section 1: Bankruptcy and related offences

Article 424-1

In application of Article 227 of the Uniform Act on the organization of collective proceedings for the settlement of liabilities, the provisions of this section apply to traders, natural persons, and to members of commercial companies who have the capacity of trader.

Paragraph 1: Bankruptcy

Article 424-2

In application of Article 228 of the Uniform Act on the organization of collective proceedings for the settlement of liabilities, any trader, natural person, in a state of cessation of payments, who:
contracts without receiving values in exchange, commitments deemed too large in view of his situation when he contracts them with the intention of delaying the finding of the cessation of his payments, makes purchases with a view to resale below the market price or if, with the same intention, employs ruinous means to procure funds;
without legitimate excuse, does not make at the registry of the competent court, the declaration of his state of cessation of payments within thirty days;
keeps incomplete or irregular accounts or does not keep them in accordance with accounting rules and recognized practices in the profession having regard to the size of the enterprise.

shall be declared guilty of simple bankruptcy and punished by two years' imprisonment.

The trader, natural person, is also guilty of bankruptcy and punished by the penalties provided for in paragraph 1 above when, after having been declared three times in a state of cessation of payments within five years, these proceedings have been closed for insufficiency of assets.

Article 424-3

In application of Article 229 of the Uniform Act on collective proceedings for clearing liabilities, any natural person who, in the event of cessation of payments, is declared guilty of fraudulent bankruptcy and punished by ten years' imprisonment:
conceals their accounting records;
misappropriates or dissipates all or part of their assets;
fraudulently acknowledges being a debtor of sums they did not owe either in their records, or by public deeds or private written commitments, or in their balance sheet;
exercises the commercial profession in violation of a prohibition provided for by the Uniform Acts or by law;
after cessation of payments, pays a creditor to the detriment of the estate;
stipulates with a creditor particular advantages by reason of their vote in the deliberations of the estate, or makes with a creditor a particular agreement from which results for the latter, an advantage at the expense of the debtor's assets from the day of the opening decision.

Any natural person trader who, on the occasion of a preventive settlement, judicial reorganization and liquidation of assets procedure, is also declared guilty of fraudulent bankruptcy and punished by the same penalty:
in bad faith, presents or causes to be presented an income statement, a balance sheet, a statement of receivables or debts or an active and passive statement of privileges and securities, that is inaccurate or incomplete;
without authorization from the president of the competent court, performs one of the acts prohibited by Article 11 of the Uniform Act on collective proceedings for clearing liabilities.

Paragraphe 2 : Des infractions assimilées aux banqueroutes

Article 424-4

In application of Article 230 of the Uniform Act on collective proceedings for clearing liabilities, these provisions and those of Articles 424-5 and 424-6 below are applicable to natural persons who are directors of legal persons subject to collective proceedings and to their permanent representatives.

The directors referred to in this article mean all directors de jure or de facto and in general, any person having directly or through an intermediary, administered, managed or liquidated the legal person under cover of or in place of its legal representatives.

Article 424-5

In application of Article 231 of the Uniform Act on collective proceedings for clearing liabilities, the directors referred to in Article 424-4 above who, in that capacity and in bad faith, are punished by two years' imprisonment:
consume sums belonging to the legal person by making purely speculative transactions or fictitious transactions;
make purchases with a view to resale below market price or use ruinous means to procure funds with the intention of delaying the finding of cessation of payments of the legal person;
pay or cause to be paid a creditor to the detriment of the estate after cessation of payments of the legal person;
cause the legal person to contract on behalf of others, without receiving assets in exchange, commitments deemed too significant having regard to its situation when these were contracted;
keep, cause to be kept or allow to be kept irregular or incomplete accounting records of the legal person under the conditions provided for in Article 424-2 above;
fail to make at the registry of the competent court, within thirty days, the declaration of the state of cessation of payments of the legal person.

Article 424-6

In application of Article 232 of the Uniform Act on collective proceedings for clearing liabilities, the legal or de facto representatives of legal persons comprising partners indefinitely and jointly liable for the debts thereof who, without legitimate excuse, do not make at the registry of the competent court, the declaration of the state of cessation of payments within thirty days or if this declaration does not include the list of jointly liable partners with indication of their first names, surname and domicile, are declared guilty of simple bankruptcy and punished by two years' imprisonment.

Article 424-7

In application of Article 233 of the Uniform Act on collective proceedings for clearing liabilities, the directors referred to in Article 424-6 above who fraudulently are punished by ten years' imprisonment:
misappropriate or conceal, attempt to misappropriate or conceal part of their assets or fraudulently acknowledge being debtors of sums they do not owe in order to remove all or part of their patrimony from the pursuit of the legal person in a state of cessation of payments or from those of the partners or creditors of the legal person in a state of cessation of payments;
conceal the books of the legal person;
misappropriate or conceal part of its assets;
acknowledge the legal person as debtor of sums it does not owe, either in the records, or by public deeds or private written commitments, or in the balance sheet;
exercise the profession of director contrary to a prohibition provided for by the uniform acts or by law;	stipulate with a creditor, in the name of the legal person, particular advantages by reason of their vote in the deliberations of the estate or who conclude with a creditor, a particular agreement from which results for the latter, an advantage at the expense of the assets of the legal person from the day of the decision declaring cessation of payments.
The directors referred to in Article 424-6 above, on the occasion of a collective proceeding, who are also punished by the same penalty:	in bad faith, present or cause to be presented an income statement, a balance sheet, a statement of receivables or debts or an active and passive statement, of privileges and securities, that is inaccurate or incomplete;	without authorization from the president of the competent court, perform one of the acts prohibited by Article 11 of the Uniform Act on collective proceedings for clearing liabilities.

Section 2 : Des autres infractions

Article 424-8

In application of Article 240 of the Uniform Act on collective proceedings for clearing liabilities, are punished by ten years' imprisonment:	persons convicted of having, in the interest of the debtor, removed, concealed or hidden all or part of the movable or immovable property, without prejudice to the application of criminal provisions relating to complicity;
persons convicted of having fraudulently submitted in the collective proceeding, either in their own name, or through an intermediary or impersonation, fictitious claims;
persons who, conducting business under the name of another or under a fictitious name, in bad faith, misappropriate, conceal, attempt to misappropriate or conceal part of their assets.

Article 424-9

In application of Article 241 of the Uniform Act on collective proceedings for clearing liabilities, the spouse, descendants, ascendants or collaterals of the debtor or their relatives by marriage who, without the debtor's knowledge, misappropriate, divert or conceal assets belonging to the assets of the debtor in a state of cessation of payments, are punished by three years' imprisonment and a fine of 500,000 francs.

Article 424-10

In application of Article 242 of the Uniform Act on the organization of collective proceedings for the settlement of liabilities, even if there is an acquittal in the cases provided for in Articles 424-6 and 424-7 above, the court seized shall rule on damages and on the reintegration, into the debtor's patrimony, of assets, rights or actions misappropriated.

Article 424-11

In application of Article 243 of the Uniform Act on the organization of collective proceedings for the settlement of liabilities, is punishable by imprisonment of ten years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs, any receiver of collective proceedings who:
carries on a personal activity under the cover of the debtor's enterprise concealing his actions;
disposes of the debtor's credit or assets as his own property;
dissipates the debtor's assets;
abusively and in bad faith pursues, in his personal interest, either directly or indirectly, a loss-making operation of the debtor's enterprise;
acquires for his own account, directly or indirectly, assets of the debtor in violation of Article 51 of the Uniform Act on the organization of collective proceedings for the settlement of liabilities.

Article 424-12

In application of Article 244 of the Uniform Act on the organization of collective proceedings for the settlement of liabilities, is punishable by imprisonment of three years and a fine of 1,500,000 francs, the creditor who:	stipulates with the debtor or with any person, particular advantages by reason of his vote in the deliberations of the body of creditors;	concludes a particular agreement from which results in his favor, an advantage at the expense of the debtor's assets from the day of the decision to open collective proceedings.

Article 424-13

The agreements provided for in Article 424-12 above are furthermore declared null by the criminal court with regard to all persons, including the debtor.
The judgment furthermore orders the creditor to return, to the rightful party, the sums or values that he received under the annulled agreements.

Article 424-14

In application of Article 246 of the Uniform Act on the organization of collective proceedings for the settlement of liabilities, without prejudice to the provisions relating to the criminal record, all conviction decisions rendered by virtue of the provisions of this chapter shall be, at the expense of those convicted, posted and published in a legal notices journal as well as by summary extract, in the Official Journal mentioning the number of the legal notices journal where the first insertion is published.

Chapter 5

Offences contained in the Uniform Act of 17 October 2023 on the organization of simplified recovery procedures and enforcement methods

Article 425-1

In application of the Uniform Act on the organization of simplified recovery procedures and enforcement methods, is punishable by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 1,000,000 francs, the debtor subject to seizure or the third party holder in whose hands the seizure was effected, who does not produce the seized objects when he is deemed to be the custodian thereof.

Article 425-2

In application of the Uniform Act on the organization of simplified recovery procedures and enforcement methods, is punishable by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 1,000,000 francs, the debtor or third party who, in the case of a conservatory seizure:
alienates or moves an asset, without justifying a legitimate cause making such movement or alienation necessary;
does not inform the creditor beforehand of the movement or alienation of the seized asset, except in case of absolute urgency;
does not indicate to the creditor, in case of movement, the place where the asset is located.

Article 425-3

In application of the Uniform Act on the organization of simplified recovery procedures and enforcement methods, is punishable by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 1,000,000 francs, the custodian who alienates or moves an asset subject to a seizure-sale, without justifying a legitimate cause making such movement or alienation necessary

Article 425-4

In application of the Uniform Act on the organization of simplified recovery procedures and enforcement methods, is punishable by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 1,000,000 francs:
the custodian who, in the context of an amicable sale procedure, moves, except in case of absolute urgency, an asset before the consignment of the sale price in the hands of the bailiff-commissioner of justice or the enforcement agent or at the registry, at the choice of the seizing creditor;
the debtor who, without complying with the procedure provided for the amicable sale, alienates the assets subject to the seizure-sale.

Article 425-5

In application of the Uniform Act on the organization of simplified recovery procedures and enforcement methods, is punishable by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 1,000,000 francs, the bailiff-commissioner of justice or any other auxiliary of justice responsible for the sale who receives a sum above the bid.

Article 425-6

In application of the Uniform Act on the organization of simplified recovery procedures and enforcement methods, is punishable by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 1,000,000 francs, the holder who, outside the case provided for by said uniform act, alienates or moves an asset subject to a seizure-revendication.

Article 425-7

In application of the Uniform Act on the organization of simplified recovery procedures and enforcement methods, is punishable by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 1,000,000 francs, the owner of the business goodwill subject to a seizure who, in violation of the prohibition imposed on him, from the service of the act of seizure, to alienate it or encumber it with rights or charges or to alienate or encumber the elements that compose it, transfers said goodwill or consents to a real right or charge thereon.

Article 425-8

In application of the Uniform Act on the organization of simplified recovery procedures and enforcement methods, is punishable by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 1,000,000 francs, the debtor or third party holder who, in violation of his obligation to inform the bailiff-commissioner of justice or the authority responsible for enforcement, alienates or moves an asset subject to a conservatory seizure of livestock.

Article 425-9

In application of the Uniform Act on the organization of simplified recovery procedures and enforcement methods, is punishable by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 1,000,000 francs, the debtor or custodian who alienates or moves, except for pasturing, the livestock subject to a seizure, without notifying the bailiff-commissioner of justice or the authority responsible for enforcement.

Article 425-10

In application of the Uniform Act on the organization of simplified recovery procedures and enforcement methods, is punishable by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 1,000,000 francs the bailiff-commissioner of justice or the authority responsible for enforcement who diverts from their purpose information communicated concerning the composition of the debtor's patrimony, his address, the identity and address of his employer and photographs.

Chapter 6

Offence contained in the Uniform Act relating to accounting law and financial information of 26 January 2017

Article 426-1

Are punished by imprisonment of three years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs, the directors of entities subject to the provisions of the Uniform Act on general commercial law, the Uniform Act on the law of commercial companies and economic interest groups and the Uniform Act on the law of cooperative societies as well as those of public, parapublic, and mixed economy entities who:
have not, for each financial year, prepared the inventory and established the annual, consolidated or combined financial statements as well as the management report and, where applicable, the social balance sheet;
have, knowingly, prepared and communicated financial statements that do not give a true and fair view of the assets, financial position and results of the financial year.

Chapitre 7

Offence contained in the Uniform Act of 15 December 2010 on the law of cooperative societies

Article 427-1

Is punished by imprisonment of one year and a fine of 1,000,000 francs, any person who, without being authorized in accordance with the legal and regulatory provisions applicable to this type of grouping, improperly uses the expressions cooperative societies, union of cooperative societies, federation of cooperative societies or confederations of cooperative societies, accompanied by any qualifier, as well as all designations likely to suggest that it is one of the groupings cited in this article.

Chapitre 8

Offences contained in the Uniform Act on the accounting system for non-profit entities of 22 December 2022

Article 428-1

Are punished by a fine of 5,000,000 francs, the directors of non-profit entities who:
have not, for each financial year, prepared the inventory and established the annual financial statements, as well as the activity report;
have knowingly prepared and communicated financial statements that do not give a true and fair view of the assets, financial position and results of the financial year;
have not kept and updated the register of donors.

Article 428-2

Are punished by a fine of 2,500,000 francs, the directors of non-profit entities who have not initiated the appointment of the entity's auditor or have not convened them to the general assembly or the body acting in its place ruling on the entity's accounts.

Article 428-3

Are punished by a fine of 1,000,000 francs, the directors of non-profit entities or any person in the service of the entity who, knowingly, have obstructed the auditor's verifications or control or have refused the communication, on site, of all documents useful for the exercise of their mission and in particular of all contracts, books, accounting documents and registers.

TITRE III : DES AUTRES CRIMES ET DELITS CONTRE LA PROPRIETE

Chapitre 1 : Du blanchiment de capitaux

[Loi n°008 du 17 mars 2016 relative à la lutte contre le blanchiment de capitaux et le financement du terrorisme]

Section 1 : Incrimination du blanchiment de capitaux

Article 431-1

Are considered as money laundering, the acts listed below, committed intentionally:

a)	the conversion or transfer of property, by any person who knows or should have known that such property is derived from a crime or offence or from participation in a crime or offence, for the purpose of concealing or disguising the illicit origin of said property, or of assisting any person involved in this activity to escape the legal consequences of their acts;

b)	the concealment or disguise of the nature, origin, location, disposition, movement or actual ownership of property or rights relating thereto, by any person who knows or should have known that such property is derived from a crime or offence or from participation in a crime or offence;

c)	the acquisition, possession or use of property, by one who knows or should have known, at the time of receiving it, that such property is derived from a crime or offence or from participation in a crime or offence;

d)	participation in one of the acts referred to in points a), b) and c), the act of associating to commit it, attempting to commit it, aiding or inciting someone to commit it or advising to that effect, or facilitating the execution of such an act.

There is money laundering, even if this act is committed by the perpetrator of the offence that procured the property to be laundered.

There is also money laundering, even if the activities that are the origin of the property to be laundered are carried out in the territory of another Member State of the West African Economic and Monetary Union or that of a third State.

Except in the case where the underlying offence has been the subject of an amnesty law, there is money laundering even:
if the perpetrator of the crimes or offences has been neither prosecuted nor convicted;
if a condition for legal action is missing following the commission of said crimes or offences.

Knowledge or intent, as elements of the aforementioned activities, may be inferred from objective factual circumstances.

Article 431-1

bis: No consideration of a political, philosophical, ideological, racial, ethnic, religious nature nor any other motive may be taken into account to justify the commission of the offence referred to in Article 431-1 of this Code.

Section 1

bis: Du défaut ou du retard de déclaration d'opérations suspectes (DOS) en matière de blanchiment de capitaux

Article 431-1

ter: When factual circumstances lead to the inference that sums of money or transactions involving sums of money may originate from a money laundering offence as defined in Article 7 of the uniform law on combating money laundering and the financing of terrorism, the suspicious transaction report, provided for in Article 79 of the same law, must be made immediately.
The same applies to attempted suspicious transactions.

Article 431-1

quater: Are punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine that cannot be less than 1,500,000 francs, the persons, directors and agents of natural or legal persons subject to reporting obligations in matters of combating money laundering and the financing of terrorism when the latter have intentionally failed to make the suspicious transaction report, provided for in Article 79 of said law, including in the context of attempted suspicious transactions or made a late report, when the circumstances led to the inference that the sums of money could originate from a money laundering offence as defined in Article 7 of said law.

Are also punished by a fine of 750,000 francs, the persons, directors and agents of natural or legal persons referred to in Articles 5 and 6 of the law on combating money laundering and the financing of terrorism, when the latter have unintentionally:

1°) failed to make the suspicious transaction report, provided for in Article 79 of the law on combating money laundering and the financing of terrorism, including in the context of attempted suspicious transactions or made a late report;

2°) contravened the provisions of Articles 16, 18 to 40 and 79 of the law on combating money laundering and the financing of terrorism.

Section 2: Criminal sanctions applicable to natural persons

Article 431-2

Natural persons guilty of a money laundering offence shall be punished by seven years' imprisonment and a fine equal to three times the value of the property or funds that were the subject of the laundering operations.

Attempted money laundering shall be punished by the same penalties.

Section 3

Criminal sanctions applicable to conspiracy, association, complicity with a view to money laundering

Article 431-3

Conspiracy or participation in an association with a view to committing an act constituting money laundering, association to commit said act, aiding, inciting or advising a natural or legal person with a view to executing it or facilitating its execution shall be punished by the same penalties provided for in Article 431-2 above.

Section 4: Aggravating circumstances

Article 431-4: The penalties provided for in Article 431-2 above shall be doubled

1°) where the money laundering offence is committed habitually or by using facilities provided by the exercise of a professional activity;

2°) where the perpetrator of the offence is a repeat offender. In this case, convictions pronounced abroad shall be taken into account to establish recidivism;

3°) where the money laundering offence is committed by an organised gang.

Where the crime or offence from which the property or sums of money that were the subject of the money laundering offence originates is punishable by a custodial sentence of a duration greater than that of the imprisonment incurred under Article 431-2 above, money laundering shall be punished by the penalties attached to the original offence of which its perpetrator had knowledge and, if that offence is accompanied by aggravating circumstances, by the penalties attached only to the circumstances of which he had knowledge.

Section 5: Criminal sanctions for certain acts related to money laundering

Article 431-5

Persons and directors or agents of natural or legal persons referred to in Articles 5 and 6 of the uniform law on combating money laundering and the financing of terrorism shall be punished by two years' imprisonment and a fine of 1,500,000 francs where they have intentionally:

1°) made disclosures to the owner of the sums or to the perpetrator of the operations referred to in Article 431-1 above concerning the declaration they are required to make or the action taken thereon;

2°) destroyed or removed documents or papers relating to the identification obligations referred to in Articles 26 to 31 whose retention is provided for by Article 35 of said law;
3°) carried out or attempted to carry out under a false identity one of the operations referred to in Articles 32, 33 and 39 to 45 and 53 of said law;

4°) informed by any means the person or persons covered by the investigation conducted for money laundering offences of which they have become aware by reason of their profession or functions;

5°) communicated to the judicial authorities or to the officials competent to establish the original and subsequent offences acts and documents referred to in Article 89 of said law which they know to be falsified or erroneous;

6°) communicated information or documents to persons other than those referred to in Article 36 of said law;

7°) failed to make the suspicious transaction report provided for in Article 79 of said law, when the circumstances led to the conclusion that the sums of money could originate from a money laundering offence as defined in Article 7 of said law.

Persons and directors or agents of natural or legal persons referred to in Articles 5 and 6 of said law shall be punished by a fine of 750,000 francs where they have unintentionally:
failed to make the suspicious transaction report provided for in Article 79 of said law;
contravened the provisions of Articles 16, 18 to 40 and 79 of said law.

Section 6

Optional additional criminal sanctions applicable to natural persons

Article 431-6

Natural persons guilty of the offences defined in Articles 431-1 to 431-5 above may also incur the following additional penalties:

1°) prohibition from residing in the national territory for a period of five years, pronounced against any convicted foreigner;

2°) prohibition from residing for a period of five years in one or more administrative districts;

3°) prohibition from leaving the national territory and withdrawal of passport for a period of three years;

4°) prohibition from exercising civil and political rights for a period of three years;

5°) prohibition from driving motorised land, sea and air vehicles and withdrawal of driving permits or licences for a period of six years;

6°) permanent or six-year prohibition from exercising the profession or activity in connection with which the offence was committed and prohibition from exercising a public function;

7°) prohibition from issuing cheques other than those which allow the withdrawal of funds by the drawer from the drawee or those which are certified and prohibition from using payment cards for six years;

8°) prohibition from holding or carrying a weapon subject to authorisation for six years;

9°) confiscation of all or part of the convicted person's property of lawful origin;

10°) confiscation of the property or thing which was used or intended to commit the offence or of the thing which is the product thereof, with the exception of objects capable of restitution.

Section 7: Exclusion of benefit of suspended sentence

Article 431-7

No criminal sanction pronounced for a money laundering offence may be accompanied by a suspended sentence.

Section 8: Criminal sanctions applicable to legal persons

Article 431-8

Legal persons other than the State, on whose account or for whose benefit a money laundering offence or one of the related offences has been committed by one of their organs or representatives, are punishable by a fine at a rate equal to five times that incurred by natural persons, without prejudice to the conviction of the latter as perpetrators or accomplices of the same acts.

Legal persons, other than the State, may, in addition, be sentenced to one or more of the following penalties:

1°) exclusion from public procurement, permanently or for a period of five years;

2°) confiscation of the property that was used or intended to be used to commit the offence or of the property that is the proceeds thereof;

3°) placement under judicial supervision for a period of five years;

4°) prohibition, permanently or for a period of five years, from directly or indirectly engaging in one or more professional or social activities in connection with which the offence was committed;

5°) permanent closure or closure for a period of five years, of the establishments or one of the establishments of the enterprise that was used to commit the incriminated acts;

6°) dissolution, when they were created to commit the incriminated acts;

7°) posting of the decision rendered or dissemination thereof by the written press or by any audiovisual means of communication, at the expense of the convicted legal person.

The sanctions provided for in points 3, 4, 5, 6 and 7 of the second paragraph of this article are not applicable to financial institutions subject to a supervisory authority with disciplinary powers.

The competent supervisory authority, referred to by the Public Prosecutor of any prosecution initiated against a financial institution, may take appropriate sanctions, in accordance with the specific legislative and regulatory texts in force.

Section 9: Grounds for exemption from criminal sanctions

Article 431-9

Any person guilty, on the one hand, of participation in an association or agreement with a view to committing one of the offences provided for in this chapter and, on the other hand, of aiding, inciting or advising a natural or legal person with a view to executing them or facilitating their execution, is exempted from criminal sanctions if, having disclosed the existence of this agreement, association, aid or advice to the judicial authority, they thereby enable, on the one hand, the identification of the other persons involved and, on the other hand, the prevention of the commission of money laundering offences.

Section 10: Grounds for mitigation of criminal sanctions

Article 431-10

The penalties incurred by any person, perpetrator or accomplice of one of the offences provided for in this chapter who, before any prosecution, enables or facilitates the identification of the other guilty parties or after the initiation of prosecution, enables or facilitates the arrest of the latter, are reduced by half. In addition, said person is exempted from the fine and, where applicable, from accessory measures and optional complementary penalties.

In matters of terrorist financing, when the penalty incurred is life imprisonment, it is reduced to twenty years.

Section 11: Mandatory complementary penalties

Article 431-11

In all cases of conviction for a money laundering offence or attempt, the courts order the confiscation for the benefit of the State, of property that was used or intended to be used to commit the offence, of the proceeds of the offence, of movable or immovable property into which these proceeds are transformed or converted and, up to their value, of legitimately acquired property with which said proceeds are mixed as well as income and other benefits derived from these proceeds, property into which they are transformed or invested or property with which they are mixed to whomever these proceeds and property belong, unless their owner establishes that they are unaware of their fraudulent origin.

The confiscation of illicit income and property is pronounced even in the absence of a criminal conviction due to the extinction of the public prosecution or any other cause blocking the public prosecution.

Chapter 2: Offences against the transparency of the WAEMU financial market

[Decision No. CM/07 709/2021 adopting the uniform law on stock market offences on the regional financial market]

Section 1

General provisions Subsection 1: Purpose

Article 432-1: The following provisions have as their purpose

1°) to define the rules and practices likely to guarantee the transparency and security of transactions on the regional financial market;

2°) to incriminate fraudulent actions and practices that compromise the transparency and security of transactions on the regional financial market;

3°) to set the penalties applicable to perpetrators and accomplices of the offences thus defined;

4°) to deal with cooperation between the Financial Markets Authority of UMOA and the judicial authorities within the framework of criminal procedure with a view to the repression of stock market offences.

Subsection 2: Scope of application

Article 432-2: The following provisions apply

1°) to transactions that take place on the regional financial market regardless of their place of trading;

2°) to conduct relating to reference indices. However, they do not apply to:
a)	transactions in own shares carried out within the framework of "buyback" programmes;

b)	stabilisation measures for a financial instrument;

c)	transactions, orders or conduct that occur within the framework of the implementation of monetary, exchange rate or public debt management policy and emanating from a public administration of a UMOA member State, an agency or an ad hoc entity of one or more member States or a person acting on behalf and in the interest of the latter.

Subsection 3: Attempt, association and agreement

Article 432-3

In the cases of offences provided for in this chapter, the attempt is punishable as the completed offence. The same applies to the agreement or association formed with a view to committing one of these offences.

Subsection 4: Complicity

Article 432-4

Complicity by providing, knowingly, means, advice or by assistance or aid for the commission of one of the offences provided for in this chapter, is punishable by the same penalties as the commission of the offence.

Subsection 5: Suspicious transactions

Paragraph 1: Declaration of suspicious transactions

Article 432-5

Any market participant or market management structure approved or authorised in accordance with the Annex is required to declare without delay to the Financial Markets Authority of UMOA, in writing and in the forms provided for by the regulations, any transaction carried out on its own account or on behalf of a third party, in an asset or product traded on a financial market, which it has reason to suspect could constitute an offence within the meaning of this Code.

Paragraphe 2 : Means of detecting suspicious transactions

Article 432-6

Any person subject to the declaration provided for in Article 432-5 above shall establish internal procedures the purpose of which is, in particular, to establish and update a typology of transactions in order to detect those that must be declared.

It shall also establish, for the benefit of its personnel, a training and awareness program regarding the detection and declaration of suspicious transactions.

Sous-section 6 : Insider information

Paragraphe 1 : Declaration of insider information

Article 432-7

Any entity making a public offering shall be required, without delay, to bring to the attention of the Financial Markets Authority and the public any insider information which directly concerns it.

Paragraphe 2 : Conditions for deferring the declaration of insider information

Article 432-8

An entity making a public offering may defer the publication of insider information in order not to prejudice its legitimate interests, provided that the failure to publish does not mislead the public and that the entity is in a position to ensure its confidentiality by controlling access, in particular by:

1°) establishing effective internal procedures preventing access to such information to persons other than those who need it in the course of their duties within the entity making a public offering;
2°) taking the necessary measures to ensure that any person having access to such information is aware of the obligations related to such access and is warned of the penalties provided for in the event of improper use or dissemination of such information;
3°) establishing arrangements allowing for immediate publication of the insider information in the event that it is unable to ensure its confidentiality.

Paragraphe 3 : Legitimate interests

Article 432-9

The legitimate interests mentioned in Article 432-8 above may in particular concern the following situations:
1°) where making the insider information public would be likely to affect the outcome or normal course of negotiations in progress, in particular in the event of serious and imminent danger threatening the financial viability of the entity making a public offering;

2°) where the information relates to a decision taken or a contract entered into by the management body of the entity making a public offering, requiring, where appropriate, the approval of another body of that entity to become effective, if the publication of the information, combined with the simultaneous announcement that such approval has not yet been given, is likely to distort their correct assessment by the public.

Paragraphe 4 : Dissemination of insider information

Article 432-10

Where an entity making a public offering or a person acting on behalf and for the account of that entity, communicates insider information to a third party in the normal exercise of its activities, profession or duties, it shall ensure dissemination thereof in accordance with the procedures set out in Article 432-7 above, either simultaneously in the case of intentional communication, or as soon as possible in the case of unintentional communication.

The provisions of the preceding paragraph shall not apply where the person receiving the information is bound by an obligation of confidentiality, whether the basis thereof is legislative, regulatory, statutory or contractual.

Paragraphe 5 : Integrity of insider information

Article 432-11

An entity making a public offering shall refrain from combining, in a manner likely to mislead the public, the provision of insider information and advertising or commercial elements relating to its activities.

Paragraphe 6 : Disclosure of changes relating to insider information

Article 432-12

Any significant change concerning insider information already made public shall be disclosed in the same manner as that used during its initial dissemination.

Sous-section 7 : Communication of stock exchange transactions

Paragraphe 1 : Obligation to declare stock exchange transactions

Article 432-13

All information relating to any acquisition, disposal, subscription or exchange of assets or products issued by an entity making a public offering, traded on a financial market, shall be communicated to the Regional Council within five trading days following the completion of the transaction concerned, where such transaction is carried out by:

1°) the chairman and chief executive officer, chairman of the board of directors, chief executive officer, general manager, director, deputy chief executive officer or any other legal representative;

2°) any other person who has, on the one hand, within the entity mentioned in this article, the power to make management decisions concerning the development and strategy thereof and, on the other hand, regular access to insider information directly or indirectly concerning that entity;

3°) any person having, with any person mentioned in points 1 and 2, close personal ties of spouse not legally separated, kinship or affinity resulting in a common residence for at least one year on the date of the transaction concerned;

4°) any legal person or entity whose direction, administration or management is provided by one of the persons mentioned in points 1 and 2 or by one of the persons mentioned in point 3 acting in the interest of one or more persons mentioned in points 1 and 2;

5°) any legal person or entity controlled, directly or indirectly, by one of the persons mentioned in points 1, 2 and 3;

6°) any legal person or entity which is established for the benefit of one of the persons mentioned in points 1, 2 and 3;

7°) any legal person or entity for which one of the persons mentioned in points 1, 2 or 3 receives at least the majority of the economic benefits.

Paragraphe 2 : Obligation to provide a copy of the communication

Article 432-14

The declaration obligation provided for in Article 432-13 above rests on the persons mentioned in points 1 to 6 of the said article who are required, when communicating to the Financial Markets Authority, to provide to the entity making a public offering, referred to in the same article, a copy of such communication.

Paragraphe 3 : Persons having access to inside information

Article 432-15

Any entity making a public offering which has issued assets or products traded on a financial market, or for which an application for admission to trading on such a market has been submitted, is required to establish, update, and communicate to the Financial Markets Authority, upon its request, and without delay, a list of persons working within it and having access to inside information directly concerning that entity.

The entity shall establish, update and communicate to the Financial Markets Authority, under the same conditions, the list of third parties acting in its name or on its behalf having access to inside information in the context of their professional relations with it.

Under the same conditions, any third party acting in the name and on behalf of any entity making a public offering is required to establish, update and, upon its request, communicate to the Financial Markets Authority a list of persons working within it and having access to inside information directly or indirectly concerning the entity making a public offering mentioned in the first paragraph, as well as a list of persons acting in its name or on its behalf, having access to the same information in the context of their professional relations with it.

Paragraphe 4 : Content of the list

Article 432-16: The lists mentioned in Article 432-15 above include

1°) the name or corporate name of the persons to appear therein;
2°) the reason for registration of the persons to appear therein;
3°) the legal relationship between these persons and the entity making the declaration.

Paragraphe 5 : Information of persons appearing on the list

Article 432-17

Any person or entity required to establish the list provided for in Article 432-15 above shall inform, in writing and as soon as possible, the persons appearing on it. This information shall include mention of their registration on the list, a reminder of the rules applicable to the holding, communication and exploitation of inside information as well as the sanctions attached to their violation.

Section 2 : Repression of offences against market transparency

Sous-section 1

Repression of stock exchange offences Paragraphe 1: Violation of the disclosure obligation

Article 432-18

Any person who knowingly contravenes any disclosure obligation incumbent upon them under the terms of the preceding subsection, of whatever nature, shall be punished by a fine of 20,000,000 francs.

Paragraphe 2 : Failure to declare stock exchange transactions

Article 432-19

Any person referred to in Article 432-13 above who knowingly contravenes the disclosure obligation incumbent upon them under the terms of said article shall be punished by a fine of 20,000,000 francs.

Paragraphe 3 : Failure to publish inside information

Article 432-20

Any entity making a public offering which knowingly contravenes the disclosure obligation incumbent upon it under the terms of Article 8 of the uniform law relating to stock exchange offences on the regional financial market shall be punished by a fine of 20,000,000 francs.

Paragraphe 4 : Failure to declare threshold crossing

Article 432-21

Any natural or legal person who fails to fulfill the information obligations to which they are subject, pursuant to the provisions of the General Regulations relating to declarations of threshold crossing, shall be punished by a fine of 20,000,000 francs.

Paragraphe 5 : Insider trading offence

Article 432-22

Any insider holding inside information on the situation of an issuer or the prospects for evolution of the securities of the issuer and who, knowingly, carries out or enables to be carried out, directly or indirectly, one or more transactions on a financial market before the information is accessible to the public, shall be punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 50,000,000 francs, this amount being able to be increased up to ten times the amount of the advantage derived from the offence, without the fine being less than that advantage.

In case of recidivism, the maximum fine shall be imposed, whether or not the perpetrator has derived a profit from the offence.

Legal persons declared criminally liable for the insider trading offence shall be punished by a fine of 250,000,000 francs, this amount being able to be increased up to ten times the amount of the advantage derived from the offence, without the fine being less than that advantage.

By way of derogation, the Financial Markets Authority may, on an ad hoc and specific basis, authorize the following practices, at the request of the entity making a public offering which has issued the assets or products traded on a financial market to which the inside information relates:

1°) purchases and sales of securities in the context of the operation of a liquidity contract;

2°) purchases and sales carried out in the context of a program with a precise schedule.

Paragraphe 6 : Incitement or recommendation of inside information

Article 432-23

Any insider holding inside information who, intentionally, recommends to a third party to acquire or dispose of, or to have acquired or disposed of by another person, the asset or assets or products traded on a financial market to which this information relates, or incites the carrying out of such transactions on the basis of this inside information, shall be punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 50,000,000 francs, this amount being able to be increased up to ten times the amount of the advantage derived from the offence, without the fine being less than that advantage.

Legal persons declared criminally liable for the offence of incitement or recommendation to a third party of inside information shall be punished by a fine of 250,000,000 francs, this amount being able to be increased up to ten times the amount of the advantage derived from the offence, without the fine being less than that advantage.

In case of recidivism, the maximum fine shall be imposed, whether or not the perpetrator has derived a profit from the offence.

Paragraphe 7 : Communication of inside information

Article 432-24

Any insider who knowingly communicates inside information to a third party outside the normal course of their profession or functions shall be punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 50,000,000 francs, this amount being able to be increased up to ten times the amount of the advantage derived from the offence, without the fine being less than that advantage.

Legal persons declared criminally liable for the offence of communication of inside information shall be punished by a fine of 250,000,000 francs, this amount being able to be increased up to ten times the amount of the advantage derived from the offence, without the fine being less than that advantage.

In case of recidivism, the maximum fine shall be imposed, whether or not the perpetrator has derived a profit from the offence.

Paragraphe 8 : Dissemination of false or misleading information

Article 432-25

Any person who knowingly disseminates to the public by any means false or misleading information concerning the prospects or situation of an entity making a public offering or concerning the prospects for the evolution of an asset or product traded on a financial market, likely to affect prices, is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 50,000,000 francs, this amount being able to be increased up to ten times the amount of the benefit derived from the offence, without the fine being able to be less than that benefit.

Legal persons declared criminally liable for the offence of dissemination of false or misleading information are punished by a fine of 250,000,000 francs, this amount being able to be increased up to ten times the amount of the benefit derived from the offence, without the fine being able to be less than that benefit. In case of recidivism, the maximum fine is imposed, depending on whether or not the perpetrator derived a profit from the offence.

Paragraphe 9 : Market manipulation

Article 432-26

Any person who knowingly, acting alone or in concert with one or more other persons, exercises directly or through an intermediary, a manoeuvre having the object of hindering the regular functioning of a financial market by misleading others, in the sense that it is likely to induce one or more third parties to acquire or dispose of an asset or product traded on a financial market, is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 50,000,000 francs, this amount being able to be increased up to ten times the amount of the benefit derived from the offence, without the fine being able to be less than that benefit.

Legal persons declared criminally liable for the offence of market manipulation are punished by a fine of 250,000,000 francs, this amount being able to be increased up to ten times the amount of the benefit derived from the offence, without the fine being able to be less than that benefit.

In case of recidivism, the maximum fine is imposed, whether or not the perpetrator derived a profit from the offence.

Paragraphe 10 : Manipulation of benchmark

Article 432-27

Any person who knowingly, acting alone or in concert with one or more other persons, is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 50,000,000 francs, this amount being able to be increased up to ten times the amount of the benefit derived from the offence, without the fine being able to be less than that benefit:

1°) provides or transmits false or misleading information used to calculate a benchmark or information likely to distort the price of a financial instrument or asset to which such a benchmark is linked;

2°) adopts conduct leading to the manipulation of the calculation of such a benchmark.

Legal persons declared criminally liable for the offence of manipulation of benchmark are punished by a fine of 250,000,000 francs, this amount being able to be increased up to ten times the amount of the benefit derived from the offence, without the fine being able to be less than that benefit.

In case of recidivism, the maximum fine is imposed, depending on whether or not the perpetrator derived a profit from the offence.

Sous-section 2 : Sanction for illegal exercise of a regulated activity

Paragraphe 1 : Illegal exercise of a regulated activity

Article 432-28

Any person who knowingly is punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 20,000,000 francs, this amount being able to be increased to five times the benefit derived from the offence:

1°) exercises a regulated activity on the regional financial market without prior authorization from the Regional Council;

2°) does not comply with the restriction, suspension, or prohibition of professional activity notified to him by the Financial Markets Authority.

Paragraphe 2 : Fraudulent public offering made by directors

Article 432-29

Any director of an entity who knowingly makes a public offering without being authorized to do so is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 20,000,000 francs, this amount being able to be increased to five times the benefit derived from the offence.

Paragraphe 3 : Fraudulent public offering made by an entity

Article 432-30

Any entity that knowingly makes a public offering without prior authorization from the Financial Markets Authority, under the conditions of its General Regulations, is punished by a fine of 20,000,000 francs, without prejudice to prosecution against natural persons.

Paragraphe 4 : Extension of the sanction to foreign entities

Article 432-31

Article 432-30 above also applies to non-resident entities making a public offering on the regional market in violation of the provisions of the General Regulations.

Paragraphe 5

Failure to communicate truthful and accurate information or a written contract

Article 432-32

Any authorized intermediary is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 20,000,000 francs:

1°) having not communicated to its clients truthful and accurate information on the contemplated transactions;

2°) executing a transaction on behalf of a client without having, beforehand, concluded a written contract with the latter.

If it is a legal person, only the fine is applied.

Paragraphe 6 : Violation of the monopoly of trading on the Stock Exchange

Article 432-33

Any person who violates the monopolies of trading on the stock exchange and securities account keeping vested in management and intermediation companies is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 20,000,000 francs. If it is a legal person, only the fine is applied.

With regard to securities account keeping, the provisions of the preceding paragraph do not apply to banks authorized by the Financial Markets Authority to exercise the functions of securities account keeping and custody.

Paragraphe 7

Violation of public information formalities upon issuance of securities

Article 432-34

Any chairman, director, general manager of a company who has issued securities offered to the public without is punished by imprisonment of twelve months and a fine of 5,000,000 francs:

1°) a notice being inserted in a newspaper authorized to receive legal notices, prior to any publicity measure;

2°) the prospectuses and circulars reproducing the statements of the notice provided for in paragraph 1 of this article, and containing the mention of the insertion of this notice in the newspaper authorized to receive legal notices with reference to the issue in which it was published;

3°) the posters and announcements in newspapers reproducing the same statements, or at least an extract of these statements with reference to the said notice, and indications of the issue number of the newspaper authorized to receive legal notices in which it was published;

4°) the posters, prospectuses and circulars mentioning the signature of the person or representative of the company from which the offer emanates and specifying whether the securities offered are listed or not and, if so, on which stock exchange.

The same penalties are applicable to persons who have served as intermediaries on the occasion of the transfer of securities in violation of the requirements of this article.

Subsection 3: Sanctions for Breaches of Collective Management

Paragraph 1: Fraudulent Subscription of Shares

Article 432-35
