Any director of an open-ended investment company, of a management company of a Mutual Fund, or of any other collective investment undertaking, who carries out subscription and issuance activities of units or shares of Undertakings for Collective Investment in Transferable Securities, without prior approval from the Financial Markets Authority, or without obtaining the visa of the Financial Markets Authority on the information note or the distribution thereof to the public, shall be punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 20,000,000 francs, which, in case of recidivism, may be increased to 50,000,000 francs.

Paragraph 2: Violation of Irregular Borrowing Operations

Article 432-36

Any director of an open-ended investment company (SICAV), of a management company of a Mutual Fund or of any other collective investment undertaking who:

1°) borrows sums of money on behalf of an Undertaking for Collective Investment in Transferable Securities, under conditions other than those provided for in the General Regulations of the Financial Markets Authority, of UMOA;

2°) in the name of the SICAV or the Mutual Fund, engages in operations other than those provided for in the General Regulations of the Financial Markets Authority, of UMOA.

shall be punished by a fine of 20,000,000 francs.

Paragraph 3: Collection of Commission Beyond the Threshold

Article 432-37

Any director of a SICAV, of a management company of a Mutual Fund, or of any other collective investment undertaking who has, knowingly, allowed the collection of commissions or management fees exceeding the levels indicated in the information note approved by the Financial Markets Authority, shall be punished by a fine of 20,000,000 francs.

Paragraph 4: Failure to Appoint an Auditor

Article 432-38

Any director of a SICAV or of a management company of a Mutual Fund or of any other form of collective investment undertaking who, knowingly, has not proceeded with the appointment of an auditor approved by the Financial Markets Authority, or has not convened him to the general meetings of the SICAV or of the management company of a Mutual Fund, shall be punished by a fine of 20,000,000 francs.

Paragraph 5: Obstruction of the Auditor's Operations

Article 432-39

Any director of an open-ended investment company or of a management company of a Mutual Fund or of any other collective investment undertaking and of the depositary institution of a Mutual Fund of a SICAV or any other market participant as well as all persons under his authority, who has knowingly obstructed the verifications or controls of the auditor, or who has refused him on-site access to all documents useful for the exercise of his mission, shall be punished by a fine of 20,000,000 francs.

Subsection 4: Sanctions for Breaches of Discretionary Management

Paragraph 1: Abuse Against Clients

Article 432-40

Any member of the administrative, management or executive bodies or of the staff of a Management and Intermediation Company or of an Asset Management Company who, knowingly, carries out operations to the detriment of the clients' interest, shall be punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 20,000,000 francs.

Paragraph 2: Execution of Related Operations

Article 432-41

Any member of the administrative, management or executive bodies or of the staff of an Asset Management Company or of a Management and Intermediation Company who, knowingly, carries out or causes to be carried out off-market operations between managed clients and companies with which that person maintains direct or indirect legal ties, shall be punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 20,000,000 francs.

Paragraph 3

Obstruction of Investigation or Control Missions of the Financial Markets Authority, of UMOA

Article 432-42

Any person who has knowingly prevented a control or investigation mission of the Financial Markets Authority, or who has communicated inaccurate information to it, shall be punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 20,000,000 francs.

Section 3: Procedure

Paragraph 1: Territorial Jurisdiction

Article 432-43

For the purposes of this Code, the public prosecutor of the place where the offences were committed has jurisdiction to prosecute the offences.

The public prosecutor is seized upon report from the Financial Markets Authority, upon complaint from a market participant, or upon denunciation.

He may also seize himself when he becomes aware of the commission of one of the offences provided for by this Code.

In all cases of seizure other than upon report from the Financial Markets Authority, including the case of self-seizure, the prosecutor seizes the Financial Markets Authority, for the purposes of investigation.

The Financial Markets Authority prepares a report to which are annexed all information, minutes and related documents, which it transmits to the Prosecutor, who initiates public prosecution, if appropriate.

Paragraph 2

Seizure of the Public Prosecutor's Office Upon Investigation Report from the Financial Markets Authority

Article 432-44

When, in the course of its duties, the Financial Markets Authority becomes aware of facts likely to constitute an offence provided for by this Code, it conducts an investigation and prepares a report which it transmits to the competent public prosecutor's office for the purposes of judicial prosecution. All information, minutes and related documents are annexed to the report.
When seized by the Financial Markets Authority, the public prosecutor, without delay, initiates public prosecution.

Paragraph 3: Initiation of Public Prosecution by the Victim

Article 432-45

Any market participant who considers himself injured by an act falling within the offences provided for by this Code, may directly initiate public prosecution, according to the procedures provided for by the law of the State where the offence was committed.

Paragraph 4

Constitution as Civil Party by the Financial Markets Authority

Article 432-46

When criminal proceedings are initiated on the basis of this Code, the Financial Markets Authority may exercise the rights of the civil party.

Paragraph 5: Seizure of the Financial Markets Authority, for Opinion

Article 432-47

The judicial authorities seized on the basis of this Code may, at any stage of the proceedings, request the Financial Markets Authority, for a simple opinion, on any question falling within its field of competence.

Paragraph 6: Powers of the Financial Markets Authority

Article 432-48

In all cases, the Financial Markets Authority retains the power to implement the administrative and disciplinary sanctions provided for by the WAEMU Treaty as amended on 12 July 2019.

At any time, the Financial Markets Authority may take any interim measures it deems appropriate, when the facts subject to prosecution present a real threat to the integrity of the system, the protection of investors or the stability of the market, until a final decision is made on the public action or on the proceedings it has initiated on the basis of the Annex.

Paragraph 7: Limitation of public action

Article 432-49

Public action for the prosecution of offences in stock exchange matters is time-barred after three full years if, during that period, no act of prosecution or investigation has been carried out.

If any such act has been carried out during that period, it is only time-barred after three full years from the last act. This applies even to persons who are not involved in that act of investigation or prosecution.

The limitation period is suspended by any legal or factual obstacle preventing the exercise of public action.

It is also suspended by the notification of charges by the Financial Markets Authority.

The limitation period provided for in paragraph 1 runs from the day the offence was discovered.

Paragraph 8: Settlement

Article 432-50

Public action is also extinguished by a settlement reached between the Regional Council and the person prosecuted and, where applicable, with the victim of the offence.

The victim who has not reached a settlement with the person prosecuted retains the right to compensation before the civil courts.

In the event of a settlement with a person prosecuted, the Financial Markets Authority informs the Public Prosecutor.

Chapter 3: Illicit enrichment

[Law No. 2014-015 of 27 May 2014 on the prevention and punishment of illicit enrichment]

Section 1: General provisions

Article 433-1

Illicit enrichment is constituted either by the substantial increase in the assets of any person referred to in Article 433-2 below that such person cannot justify in relation to their legitimate income, or by a lifestyle led by that person that is inconsistent with their legitimate income.

Article 433-2

Subject to the provisions of this chapter are any natural person, civilian or military, holder of public authority, responsible for public service even occasionally, or vested with an elective mandate; any agent or employee of the State, public authorities, State Companies and Enterprises, Public Establishments, cooperative organisations, unions, associations or federations of such organisations, Associations recognised as being of Public Utility, Professional Orders, organisations of an industrial or commercial nature in which the State or a public authority holds a fraction of the share capital, and in general, any person acting in the name of or on behalf of the public authority and/or with the means or resources thereof.

The provisions of this chapter also apply to any legal person that has participated in the commission of the offence.

Article 433-3

Any person who has intentionally permitted or facilitated acts of illicit enrichment is an accomplice to the commission of the offence.

The same applies to any person who, knowing that the property in their possession comes from illicit enrichment, nevertheless agrees to keep it or conceal it by assisting the perpetrator of the offence.

However, a person who, before the opening of an investigation for illicit enrichment, has disclosed to the judicial authorities the facts constituting such an offence shall not be prosecuted as an accomplice.

For the establishment of complicity, personal knowledge, intention or motivation may be inferred from objective factual circumstances.

Article 433-4

Illicit enrichment is a continuing offence. The limitation period runs only from the day of discovery of the constituent elements of the offence. Illicit enrichment is deemed to be consummated by the mere continuation of its effects.

Section 2: Penalties

Paragraph 1: Common provisions

Article 433-5

When the offence of illicit enrichment is committed through a third party who is a natural person, that person is prosecuted for complicity in illicit enrichment without prejudice to the sanctions specifically provided for in the event of refusal or false declaration.

When the offence is committed through a third party who is a legal person, criminal liability lies with the natural person who committed the offence.

However, the legal person in question is sentenced, jointly and severally with the physical perpetrator(s), to pay all or part of the fines, costs and expenses to the State, as well as civil damages.

Paragraph 2: Penalties applicable to natural persons

Article 433-6

When the value of the property deemed illicit is less than or equal to 50,000,000 francs, the penalty is three years' imprisonment and a fine equal to that value.

When the value of the property deemed illicit exceeds 50,000,000 francs, the penalty is five years' imprisonment and a fine equal to twice the value of such property.

Accomplices incur the same penalties as the principal perpetrator.

In all cases, a suspended sentence may only be imposed when the amount illicitly acquired has been fully repaid.

Article 433-7

A natural person convicted of illicit enrichment also incurs the following additional penalties:
1°) confiscation of all or part of their property;
2°) permanent or six-year prohibition from exercising the profession or activity in connection with which the offence was committed and prohibition from exercising any public function;
3°) temporary deprivation of civil and political rights not exceeding ten years.

Paragraph 3: Penalties and sanctions applicable to legal persons

Article 433-8

Any legal person, other than the State and its subdivisions, on whose account or for whose benefit the offence of illicit enrichment was committed by one of its organs or representatives, is punishable by a fine at a rate equal to five times that incurred by natural persons, whether or not such persons have been convicted as perpetrators or accomplices of the offence.
The liability of the legal person does not exclude that of the natural person.

Article 433-9

A convicted legal person, other than the State or a company with public financial participation, also incurs one of the following sanctions:
1°) exclusion from public procurement, permanently or for a period of at least five years;
2°) prohibition, permanently or for a period of at least five years, from exercising directly or indirectly one or more commercial, professional or social activities in connection with which the offence was committed;
3°) permanent or at least five-year closure of one or more of the company's establishment(s) used to commit the acts;
4°) posting of the decision handed down at the Court and/or the Town Hall or its publication in the print and/or audiovisual press, at its expense;
5°) dissolution.

Paragraph 4: Confiscation

Article 433-10

In the event of a conviction for illicit enrichment, the competent Court shall issue a decision for the confiscation of funds and property that constitute the proceeds of criminal activity, including property mixed with such proceeds or derived from such proceeds or exchanged for such proceeds, or property whose value corresponds to that of such proceeds:
those that constitute the object of the offence;
those that constitute the income and other benefits derived from such funds or property;
those that have been transferred to a party.

Chapter 4: Receiving stolen goods

Article 434-1

Receiving stolen goods is the act of possessing, concealing or transmitting a thing, or acting as an intermediary in order to transmit it, knowing that this thing comes from a crime or an offence.

Also constitutes receiving stolen goods, the act of knowingly benefiting by any means from the proceeds of a crime or an offence.

Article 434-2

Receiving stolen goods is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 1,000,000 francs.

Article 434-3

In the case where a criminal penalty is applicable to the act that procured the things received, the receiver is punished by the penalty attached by law to the crime and to the circumstances of the crime of which he had knowledge at the time of receiving.

Chapter 5: Arson-destruction-damage-injury

Section 1: Wilful arson

Article 435-1

Whoever wilfully sets fire to buildings, ships, boats, warehouses, yards when they are inhabited or used for habitation, and generally to an inhabited place or used for habitation, whether they belong or do not belong to the perpetrator of the crime, is punished by death.
Is punished by the same penalty whoever wilfully sets fire, either to carriages or wagons containing persons, or to carriages or wagons not containing persons, but forming part of a convoy that contains them.
Whoever wilfully sets fire to buildings, ships, boats, warehouses, yards, when they are neither inhabited nor used for habitation or forests, woods, coppices or standing crops, when these objects do not belong to him is punished by life imprisonment.
He who, by setting or causing to be set fire to one of the objects listed in the preceding paragraph and belonging to himself, has wilfully caused any prejudice to another, is punished by twenty years' imprisonment. Is punished by the same penalty he who sets fire on the order of the owner.
Whoever wilfully sets fire, either to straw or crops in stacks or in ricks, or to wood arranged in piles or in steres, or to carriages or wagons loaded or not loaded with goods, or other movable objects not forming part of a convoy containing persons, if these objects do not belong to him, is punished by imprisonment of twenty years.

He who, by setting or causing to be set fire to one of the objects listed in the preceding paragraph and belonging to himself, wilfully causes any prejudice to another, is punished by imprisonment of ten years. Is punished by the same penalty he who sets fire on the order of the owner.

He who communicates the fire to one of the objects listed in the preceding paragraphs, by wilfully setting fire to any objects whatsoever belonging either to himself or to another and placed in such a way as to communicate said fire, is punished by the same penalty as if he had directly set fire to one of said objects.

In all cases, if the fire causes the death of one or more persons, the penalty is death.

However, in the case of wilful arson of forests, woods or coppices, the death penalty or that of imprisonment is applicable according to the cases only if the fire was lit with criminal intent.

If the fire is wilfully lit in a personal interest of cultivation or otherwise, the guilty party is punished by imprisonment of three years and a fine of 200,000 francs.

If the wilful fire causes loss of human life, the penalty of imprisonment may be increased up to five years and a fine of 1,000,000 francs.

Section 2: Involuntary arson

Article 435-2

Without prejudice to damages, is punished by imprisonment of three years and a fine of 1,000,000 francs he who, through clumsiness, imprudence, inattention, negligence or non-observance of regulations, causes a fire on the movable or immovable property of another.

The above penalty of imprisonment may be increased to five years and the fine doubled when the above-specified offence is committed in an enterprise, a plant, a factory, a sales or storage warehouse and generally in all places where public or private goods are likely to be kept and when it has resulted in material damage exceeding 1,000,000 francs.

Whoever, through imprudence, inattention, negligence or non-observance of regulations, outside areas protected by forestry legislation, involuntarily causes a fire or a bush fire in violation of texts drawn up for this purpose, is, without prejudice to damages, punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 300,000 francs.

Section 3: Destruction of buildings, depositing of explosives

Article 435-3

Whoever wilfully and otherwise than by explosion or fire destroys, in whole or in part, buildings, dwellings, dikes, causeways, ships, boats, aircraft, vehicles of any kind, warehouses or yards or their outbuildings, bridges, public or private roads, wells, hydraulic installations and all other works of public utility, is punished by imprisonment of twenty years and prohibition of residence for twenty years, without prejudice to the penalties for homicide, if the destruction or attempted destruction has caused death.

If the crime provided for in the preceding paragraph is committed by means of an explosive device, the penalty is death.

The depositing with criminal intent, on a public or private road, of an explosive device is assimilated to attempted murder.

Section 4

Offences against the safety of civil aviation, river navigation and railways

Article 435-4

Any person on board an aircraft in flight or stationary, who by violence or threat of violence, seizes that aircraft or exercises control over it, is punished by imprisonment of ten years.

If these acts have resulted in injuries or illnesses, the penalty is imprisonment of twenty years.

If they have resulted in the death of one or more persons, the death penalty is applicable.

Article 435-5

Any person who, by communicating information that he knows to be false compromises the safety of an aircraft in flight, the services of an airport, a railway station or a river quay, is punished by imprisonment of ten years.

Article 435-6

Is punished by imprisonment of twenty years, any person who, knowingly by means of a device, a substance or a weapon, commits against one or more persons in an airport, an aerodrome, a railway station or a river quay an act of violence that causes or is likely to cause serious violence or death.

Article 435-7

Is punished by the death penalty, any person who, knowingly by means of an explosive or by fire destroys or seriously damages rails, aids to air or river navigation, the installations of an airport or aerodrome or aircraft in service or not, located at that airport, aerodrome, station or quay.

Article 435-8

Any attempt to sabotage an aircraft, train or vessel by means of an explosive or substance capable of destroying said aircraft, train or vessel, or causing damage rendering it unfit for flight, circulation or navigation is punished by twenty years' imprisonment.

Section 5: Damage to immovable property

Article 435-9

Whoever, outside the cases provided for in the first paragraph of Article 435-3 above, voluntarily and otherwise than by explosive or fire, causes or attempts to cause damage to the immovable property of another, is punished by five years' imprisonment and a ten-year residence ban.

Whoever, outside the cases provided for in paragraph 2 of Article 435-3 above, voluntarily, by means of an explosive device causes or attempts to cause damage to the movable or immovable property of another is punished by twenty years' imprisonment and a twenty-year residence ban, without prejudice to the penalties for homicide if the damage or attempted damage results in death.

Section 6: Pillage, poisoning of drinking water

Article 435-10

Any pillage, any damage to deposits of foodstuffs or goods, effects, movable property, committed in assembly or in a gang, by open force, is punished by life imprisonment.

The same penalty applies to those guilty of poisoning wells, cisterns, springs and drinking water.

However, the death penalty is incurred when it has resulted in the poisoning of one or more persons.

Section 7: Threat of destruction

Article 435-11

The written or verbal threat to set fire to or destroy the objects listed in Articles 435-1 first paragraph and 435-3 above, is punished by three years' imprisonment. If the threat is made with an order to deposit a sum of money or under any other condition, the penalty is five years' imprisonment.

Section 8

Damage to crops, domestic animals, forests and water points

Article 435-12

Whoever voluntarily, outside the cases provided for in the preceding articles of this chapter devastates crops or plants, fells one or more trees, destroys agricultural instruments, breaks fences, removes or moves boundary markers and without necessity, poisons fish in ponds, fish ponds or reservoirs, or kills a domestic animal, is punished by three months' imprisonment and a fine of 100,000 francs.

Whoever cuts, uproots, mutilates or damages in any way trees or natural plants of protected species, or plants or trees of essence or value, is punished by five years' imprisonment and a fine of 300,000 francs.

The attempt is punished as the offence itself.

Section 9: Straying of animals

Article 435-13

Whoever releases into nature or into a built-up area domestic animals without effective control, predisposing them by this fact alone to cause damage to others or to obstruct traffic on public roads, is punished by six months' imprisonment and a fine of 1,000,000 francs.

When stray animals cause damage to others or to public property, the penalties provided for in Article 435-12 above apply.

Section 10: Voluntary damage to movable property

Article 435-14

Any other voluntary damage to the movable property of another is punished by three months' imprisonment and a fine of 100,000 francs.

The attempt is punished as the offence itself.

LIVRE V

OTHER CRIMES AND OFFENCES TITLE I: CYBERCRIME
[Law No. 2019-056 of 05 December 2019 on the repression of cybercrime]

Chapitre 1

General provisions Article 511-1: This title applies to: any offence committed by means of information and communication technologies on all or part of the territory of the Republic of Mali;
any offence committed in cyberspace and whose effects occur on national territory.

Article 511-2 : The following terms mean

1°) covert access: the mechanism allowing access to data or an information system to be concealed without the authorization of the legitimate user;

2°) fraudulent access: any irregular mode of penetration of an automated data processing system;

3°) electronic communication: any emission, transmission or reception of signs, signals, writings, images or sounds, by electromagnetic means; any making available to the public or a category of the public by an electronic or magnetic communication process, of signs, signals, writings, images, sounds or messages of any nature;

4°) cybercrime: all criminal offences committed by means of electronic communications networks and information systems or against said networks and systems;

5°) cryptography: application of mathematics allowing information to be written in such a way as to make it unintelligible to those not possessing the capacity to decipher it;

6°) computer data: any representation of facts, information or concepts in a form suitable for computer processing, including a program of a nature to cause a computer system to perform a function;

7°) subscriber data: any information, in the form of computer data or in any other form, held by a service provider and relating to subscribers to its services, other than traffic or content data, and allowing the establishment of:

a)	the type of communication service used, the technical arrangements taken in this regard and the period of service;

b)	the identity, electronic, postal or geographical address and telephone number of the subscriber, and any other access number, data concerning billing and payment, available on the basis of a contract or service arrangement;

c)	any other information relating to the location of communication equipment, available on the basis of a contract or service arrangement;

8°) traffic data: all data relating to a communication passing through an information system, produced by the latter as an element of the communication chain, indicating the origin, destination, route, time, date, size and duration of the communication or the type of underlying service;

9°) fraudulent remaining in a computer system: any irregular and continuous presence in an automated data processing system:

10°) racist and xenophobic material: any writing, any image or any other representation of ideas or theories which advocates or encourages hatred, discrimination or violence against a person or group of persons, by reason of race, colour, ancestry, affiliation or national or ethnic origin or religion, insofar as the latter serves as a pretext for one or other of these elements or which incites such acts;

11°) minor: any person under the age of eighteen within the meaning of the United Nations Convention on the Rights of the Child;

12°) child pornography: any data of whatever nature or form or medium representing:

a)	a minor engaging in sexually explicit behaviour;

b)	a person who appears as a minor engaging in sexually explicit behaviour;

c)	realistic images representing a minor engaging in sexually explicit behaviour.

13°) computer program: sequence of instructions which specifies step by step the operations to be performed by a computer or computer component to obtain a result;

14°) direct marketing: any sending of a message intended to promote, directly or indirectly, goods, services or the image of a person selling goods or providing services;

15°) information system: organized set of resources (hardware, software, personnel, data and procedures) which allows the collection, grouping, classification, processing and dissemination of information;

16°) computer system: any device whether isolated or not, any set of interconnected or related devices, which ensures or one or more elements of which ensure automated data processing by executing a program;

17°) information and communication technologies: technologies used to collect, store, use and send information as well as those which involve the use of computers or any communication system including telecommunications;

18°) networks: systems for sharing information between several machines.

Chapitre 2

Crimes and offences related to information and communication technologies

Section 1 : Attacks on the confidentiality of information systems

Paragraphe 1 : Fraudulent access to a computer system

Article 512-1

Whoever accesses or attempts to access fraudulently, all or part of an information system, is punished by imprisonment of one year and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.

Whoever obtains or attempts to obtain fraudulently, for himself or for another, any advantage whatsoever by entering an information system is punished by the same penalties.

Where this has resulted in either the deletion or modification of data contained in the system, or an alteration of the functioning of this system, the imprisonment is two years and the fine is 10,000,000 francs.

Paragraphe 2 : Fraudulent remaining in a computer system

Article 512-2

Whoever remains or attempts to remain fraudulently in all or part of an information system, is punished by imprisonment of one year and a fine of 5,000,000 CFA francs.

Where this has resulted in either the deletion or modification of data contained in the system, or an alteration of the functioning of this system, the imprisonment is two years and the fine is 10,000,000 francs.

Section 2

Attacks on the integrity and availability of information systems

Paragraphe 1 : Obstruction of the functioning of an information system

Article 512-3

Whoever intentionally obstructs or attempts to obstruct the functioning of an information system is punished by imprisonment of three years and a fine of 10,000,000 francs.

Paragraphe 2 : Fraudulent introduction of data into an information system

Article 512-4

Whoever introduces or attempts to introduce fraudulently data into an information system is punished by imprisonment of three years and a fine of 50,000,000 francs.

Section 3 : Attacks on the integrity of data in an information system

Paragraphe 1 : Fraudulent interception of computerized data

Article 512-5

Whoever intercepts or attempts to intercept fraudulently computerized data during their non-public transmission to, from or within an information system, is punished by imprisonment of three years and a fine of 50,000,000 francs.

Paragraphe 2 : Fraudulent modification of computerized data

Article 512-6

Whoever intentionally modifies or attempts to modify, damages or attempts to damage, deletes or attempts to delete, erases or attempts to erase, alters or attempts to alter the data of an information system or their modes of processing or transmission is punished by imprisonment of three years and a fine of 50,000,000 francs.

Paragraphe 3 : Falsification of computerised data

Article 512-7

Whoever produces or manufactures a set of digitised data by the fraudulent introduction, erasure or deletion of computerised data stored, processed or transmitted by an information system, generating counterfeit data, with the intention that they be taken into account or used for legal purposes as if they were original, is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 60,000,000 francs.

Paragraphe 4 : Use of falsified data

Article 512-8

Whoever, knowingly, makes use or attempts to make use of data obtained under the conditions provided for in Article 512-7 above, is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 50,000,000 francs.

Section 4 : Fraudulent obtaining of advantage

Article 512-9

Whoever fraudulently obtains, for himself or for another, any advantage whatsoever, by the introduction, alteration, erasure or deletion of computerised data, is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 50,000,000 francs.

Section 5 : Possession of equipment to commit offences

Article 512-10

Whoever produces, sells, imports, possesses, distributes, offers, transfers or makes available, without right or without authorisation, equipment, a computer program, any device or data designed or specially adapted to commit one or more of the offences provided for in this Code or a password, an access code or similar computerised data allowing access to all or part of an information system, is punished by imprisonment of ten years and a fine of 200,000,000 francs.

Section 6

Association formed or agreement with a view to committing computer offences

Article 512-11

Is punished by imprisonment of ten years and a fine of 200,000,000 francs, whoever participates in an association formed or an agreement established with a view to the preparation, or the commission concretised by one or more material acts, of one or more of the offences provided for in this title.

Section 7 : Child pornography

Paragraphe 1

Production of images or representations of a child pornographic nature

Article 512-12

Whoever produces, records, offers, makes available, distributes, transmits an image or a representation presenting a character of child pornography by means of an information system is punished by imprisonment of ten years and a fine of 15,000,000 francs.

Paragraphe 2

Import or export of images or representations of a child pornographic nature

Article 512-13

Whoever procures or procures for another, imports or causes to be imported, exports or causes to be exported an image or a representation presenting a character of child pornography by means of an information system, is punished by imprisonment of ten years and a fine of 15,000,000 francs.

Paragraphe 3

Possession of images or representations of a child pornographic nature

Article 512-14

Whoever possesses an image or a representation presenting a character of child pornography in an information system or in any means whatsoever of storing computerised data, is punished by imprisonment of ten years and a fine of 15,000,000 francs.

Is punished by the same penalties, whoever facilitates access to images, documents, sound or a representation presenting a character of child pornography.

Article 512-15

Any adult person who intentionally proposes, by means of information and communication technologies, a meeting with a minor child, with the aim of committing against him or her one of the offences provided for in Articles 512-12, 512-13 and 512-14 above, when this proposal has been followed by material acts leading to the said meeting, is punished by imprisonment of ten years and a fine of 15,000,000 francs.

Section 8

Racist, xenophobic acts, threats and insults by means of an information system

Paragraphe 1

Provision of writings or images of a racist or xenophobic nature by means of an information system

Article 512-16

Whoever creates, downloads, distributes or makes available, in any form whatsoever, racist and xenophobic material, by means of an information system is punished by imprisonment of ten years and a fine of 10,000,000 francs.

Paragraphe 2 : Threats by means of an information system

Article 512-17

Whoever utters a threat by means of an information system, to commit a criminal offence, towards a person is punished by imprisonment of ten years and a fine of 10,000,000 francs.

Paragraphe 3 : Insults committed by means of an information system

Article 512-18

Whoever utters an insult by means of an information system towards a person is punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 10,000,000 francs.

Paragraphe 4 : Negationism

Article 512-19

Whoever distributes or makes available, by means of an information system, material which denies, grossly minimises, approves or justifies acts constituting genocide or crimes against humanity as defined by national and international legislation is punished by imprisonment of ten years and a fine of 50,000,000 francs.

Section 9

Offences related to the activities of providers of communication services to the public by electronic means

Article 512-20

Whoever presents content or an activity as being unlawful with the aim of obtaining its removal or of causing its distribution by a provider of communication services to the public by electronic means to cease, when he knows this information to be inaccurate, is punished by imprisonment of one year and a fine of 2,000,000 francs.

Article 512-21

Any provider of communication services to the public by electronic means who does not satisfy the obligation to put in place an easily accessible and visible device allowing any person to bring to notice unlawful data constituting facts of apology for crimes against humanity, incitement to racial hatred and child pornography is punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 2,000,000 francs.

Article 512-22

Any provider of communication services to the public by electronic means who does not satisfy the obligation to inform promptly the competent public authorities of all unlawful activities which are reported to him and which are carried out by the recipients of their services, is punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 2,000,000 francs.

Article 512-23

Any provider of communication services to the public by electronic means who does not satisfy the obligation to retain data allowing the identification of whoever has contributed to the creation of the content or of one of the contents of the services of which he is the provider, is punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 2,000,000 francs.

The penalties provided for in the first paragraph of this article apply when the provider of communication services by electronic means does not comply with the request of a judicial authority to obtain communication of the data referred to in the same paragraph.

Article 512-24

Is punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 2,000,000 francs, any publisher of professional online public communication services who does not make available to the public in an open standard, information on:

1°) if it is a natural person, their surname, first name, domicile and telephone number and, if they are subject to the formalities of registration in the Trade and Movable Credit Register, in the Register of Trades, their registration number;

2°) if it is a legal person, its name or corporate name, its registered office, its telephone number and if it is a company subject to the formalities of registration in the Trade and Movable Credit Register or in the National Register of Enterprises and Associations, its registration number, its share capital, the address of its registered office;

3°) the name of the director or co-director of the publication of the electronic public communication service and, where applicable, that of the person responsible for editing.

Is punished by the same penalties, any person publishing on a non-professional basis an online public communication service who does not make available to the public, their name, their name or corporate name and their address in addition to the communication of their personal identification elements provided for by this title.

Article 512-25

The provider of electronic public communication services who does not provide to publishers of online public communication services the technical means enabling them to satisfy the identification conditions provided for in Article 512-36 below is punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 2,000,000 francs.

Article 512-26

Is punished by a fine of 20,000,000 francs, without prejudice to any other penalties provided for by the legislation in force, any professional or non-professional publisher of a public communication service using information and communication technologies, who, within 24 hours of receiving the request, does not publish the response arising from the exercise of the right of reply.

Article 512-27

Is punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 500,000 francs, anyone carrying out an activity in the field of electronic commerce who does not ensure by means of an open standard to those to whom the supply of goods or the provision of services is intended, easy, direct and permanent access to the following information:

1°) if it is a natural person, their surname and first name and, if it is a legal person, its corporate name;

2°) the complete address of the place where it is established, its electronic mail address, as well as its telephone number;

3°) if it is subject to the formalities of registration in the trade register, the number of its registration in the Trade and Movable Credit Register (RCCM), in the Register of Trades, its share capital and the address of its registered office;

4°) if its activity is subject to an authorization regime, the name and address of the authority having issued it;

5°) if it is a member of a regulated profession, the reference to the applicable professional rules, its professional title, the State in which it was granted as well as the name of the order or professional body with which it is registered;

6°) the Code of Conduct to which it is possibly subject as well as information relating to the way in which these codes can be consulted electronically;

7°) if it is a company subject to value added tax, its Identification Number.

Is punished by the same penalties, any person carrying out an activity in the field of electronic commerce, even in the absence of a contract offer, when they mention a price and do not indicate in a clear manner, in particular whether taxes and delivery charges are included.

Article 512-28

Any electronic supplier of goods or services who refuses to reimburse the amounts received from a consumer who exercises their right of withdrawal, is punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 2,000,000 francs.

Article 512-29

Anyone who deceives or attempts to deceive, by fraudulent maneuvers, the purchaser as to the identity, nature or origin of the good sold, by delivering a good other than that ordered and purchased by the consumer, is punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.

Section 10: Offences relating to direct marketing

Article 512-30

Is punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 500,000 francs, anyone who sends one or more messages by means of an automatic calling machine, a fax machine or an electronic mail using, in any form whatsoever, the contact details of a natural person who has not expressed their prior consent to receive direct marketing by this means.

Article 512-31

Is punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 500,000 francs, anyone who sends, in authorized cases, for the purposes of direct marketing, messages by means of automatic calling machines, fax machines and electronic mail, without indicating valid contact details to which the recipient can usefully transmit a request to obtain that these communications cease.

Is punished by the same penalties provided for in the first paragraph of this article anyone who conceals or attempts to conceal the identity of the person on whose behalf the communication is sent and to mention a subject unrelated to the service or service offered.

Article 512-32

Is punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 500,000 francs, any provider who does not satisfy the request of a recipient to cease sending messages, for the purposes of direct marketing, by means of automatic calling machines, fax machines or electronic mail.

Section 11: Offences relating to advertising by electronic means

Article 512-33

Is punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 500,000 francs:
anyone who sends advertising in any form whatsoever, accessible by an electronic communication service, not clearly identified as such;
anyone who does not clearly identify the natural or legal person on whose behalf the advertising is made;
anyone who does not clearly identify as such, competitions or promotional games and their conditions of participation in an easily accessible, precise and unequivocal manner, including, where applicable, the provider's authorization number.

Section 12: Offences relating to cryptology

Article 512-34

Is punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 2,000,000 francs, any provider of cryptology services who does not satisfy the obligation to communicate to the Authority in charge of the regulation of cryptology, the description of the technical characteristics of the means of cryptology as well as the source code of the software used.

Article 512-35

Is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs, anyone who supplies or imports a means of cryptology not exclusively ensuring authentication or integrity control functions without satisfying the obligation of prior declaration to the competent Authority.

Article 512-36

Is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 20,000,000 francs anyone who exports a means of cryptology not exclusively ensuring authentication or integrity control functions without having previously obtained the authorization of the Authority in charge of the regulation of cryptology.

Article 512-37

Is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 20,000,000 francs, anyone who provides cryptology services without having previously obtained approval from the Authority in charge of cryptology regulation.

Article 512-38

Is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 2,000,000 francs, anyone who makes available to others a means of cryptology that has been subject to a prohibition on use and circulation, even free of charge.

Article 512-39

Is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 20,000,000 francs, anyone who obstructs the exercise of the control mission of the Authority in charge of cryptology regulation.

Article 512-40

Is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 30,000,000 francs anyone who establishes backdoor access to data or to an information system without the authorization of the legitimate user.

Section 13

Offences committed by means of information and communication technologies

Paragraphe 1

Offences against property by means of information and communication technologies

Article 512-41

Is punished by imprisonment of five years, and a fine of 5,000,000 francs, theft committed by means of information and communication technologies.

Article 512-42

Is punished by imprisonment of five years, and a fine of 10,000,000 francs extortion, whether of funds, securities, signature, writing, deed, title or any document containing or creating an obligation, disposition or discharge committed by means of information and communication technologies.

Article 512-43

Is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs, breach of trust committed by means of information and communication technologies.

When the same means are used by a clerk or an employee to commit the offence referred to in paragraph 1, to the detriment of his master, the same imprisonment penalty is applicable but the fine is increased to 10,000,000 francs.

When the breach of trust is committed by an approved electronic service provider acting in the exercise or on the occasion of the exercise of his functions, the imprisonment penalty is that referred to in paragraph 1 but the fine penalty may be increased to 15,000,000 francs.

Article 512-44

Is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs, fraud committed by means of information and communication technologies.

Article 512-45

Is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs, anyone who receives, in whole or in part, things removed, misappropriated or obtained by means of a crime or an offence, by means of information and communication technologies.

Article 512-46

Is punished by imprisonment of ten years and a fine equal to at least three times and at most five times the value of the property or funds on which the money laundering operations related any person who commits money laundering by means of information and communication technologies.

Article 512-47: The penalties provided for in Article 512-45 above are doubled

when the offence of money laundering is committed habitually or by using the facilities provided by the exercise of a professional activity;
when the perpetrator of the offence is in a state of recidivism. In this case, convictions pronounced abroad are taken into account to establish recidivism;
when the offence of money laundering is committed by an organized gang;
when the crime or offence from which the property or sums of money on which the offence of money laundering related originates is punished by a custodial sentence of a duration greater than that of the imprisonment incurred under Article 512-45, money laundering is punished by the penalties attached to the original offence of which its perpetrator had knowledge and, if this offence is accompanied by aggravating circumstances, by the penalties attached only to the circumstances of which he had knowledge.

Article 512-48

Is punished by the penalties provided for in Article 512-45 above, any person who, within the framework of an agreement or participation in an association, with a view to committing money laundering, assists, incites or advises a natural or legal person with a view to the execution or facilitation of the execution of money laundering.

Article 512-49

Is punished by imprisonment of twenty years and a fine equal to at least three times and at most five times the value of the property or funds on which the terrorist financing operations related, when the offence of terrorist financing is committed by means of information and communication technologies.

Article 512-50

Is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs any person who commits theft, fraud, receiving, breach of trust, extortion, blackmail relating to computer data.

Paragraphe 2

Press offences committed by means of information and communication technologies

Article 512-51

Press offences, provided for by the law on the Press Regime and Press Offences, committed by means of information and communication technologies, with the exception of those committed by the press on the Internet, are punished by common law penalties.

Paragraphe 3: Offences committed by any means of public dissemination

Article 512-52

Are considered as means of public dissemination: radio broadcasting, television, cinema, the press, posting, exhibition, distribution of writings or images of all kinds, speeches, songs, cries or threats, uttered in public places or meetings, any technical process intended to reach the public and generally any means of digital communication by electronic means.

Article 512-53

Is punished by imprisonment of seven years and a fine of 10,000,000 francs anyone who:

1°) manufactures or possesses with a view to trading, distributing, renting, posting or exhibiting;

2°) imports or causes to be imported, exports or causes to be exported, transports or causes to be transported knowingly for the same purposes;

3°) posts, exhibits or projects to public view;

4°) sells, rents, offers for sale or rental, even non-publicly;

5°) offers, even free of charge, even non-publicly in any form whatsoever, directly or by indirect means;

6°) distributes or delivers with a view to their distribution by any means whatsoever, all printed matter, all writings, drawings, posters, engravings, paintings, photographs, films or plates, matrices or photographic reproductions, emblems, all objects or images contrary to good morals.

Article 512-54

When the acts referred to in Article 512-52 above are of a pornographic nature, the maximum penalty is pronounced.

The convicted person may, in addition, be subject, for a period not exceeding six months, to a prohibition from exercising, directly or through an intermediary, in law or in fact, management functions of any printing, publishing or grouping and distribution company of newspapers and periodical publications.

Anyone who contravenes the prohibition referred to above is punished by the penalties provided for in this article.

Paragraphe 4 : Digital identity theft

Article 512-55

Any person who usurps the digital identity of a third party or one or more data allowing their identification, with a view to disturbing their peace or that of others or to harm their honour, reputation or interests, is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 10,000,000 francs.

Paragraphe 5 : Infringements of copyright and related rights

Article 512-56

Any person who deliberately commits, by means of information and communication technologies, an infringement of literary and artistic property is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 15,000,000 francs.

Article 512-57

Any person who commits an infringement of the patrimonial right or the moral right of the author of a computer creation, namely a computer program or a database, is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 15,000,000 francs.

Article 512-58

Any electronic service provider who, intentionally, without legitimate excuse or justification, discloses information relating to a criminal investigation, when they have received, in the context of that investigation, an explicit injunction relating to confidentiality or when such confidentiality results from the law, is punished by imprisonment of three years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.

Paragraphe 6 : Offences against defence and security

Article 512-59

Any person who is guilty of treason and punished by criminal imprisonment for life:

1°) delivers to a foreign power or its agents, in any form or by any means whatsoever, information, an object, document, process, digitised data or computerised file which must be kept secret in the interest of national defence and security;

2°) secures, by any means whatsoever, possession of such information, object, document, process, digitised data or computerised file with a view to delivering it to a foreign power or its agents;

3°) destroys or allows to be destroyed information, an object, document, process, digitised data or computerised file with a view to favouring a foreign power.

4°) discloses by means of information and communication technologies rumours or false information on the state or actions of armed forces engaged at the front with the intention of undermining troop morale or sowing panic and doubt among the population.

Article 512-60

Any person who, with the intention of delivering them to a third country, gathers information, objects, documents, processes, data or computerised files whose assembly and exploitation are likely to harm national defence and security is guilty of treason and punished by criminal imprisonment for life.

Article 512-61

Any person who, without intention of treason or espionage, is punished by imprisonment of ten years:

1°) secures, being without authority, possession of information, an object, document, process, digitised data or computerised file, which must be kept secret in the interest of national defence and security or whose knowledge could lead to the discovery of a secret of national defence and security;

2°) destroys, removes, allows to be destroyed or removed, reproduces or allows to be reproduced information, an object, document, process, digitised data or computerised files;

3°) brings or allows to be brought to the knowledge of an unauthorised person or the public information, an object, document, process, digitised data or computerised file, or extends its disclosure.

Section 14 : Complementary penalties

Article 512-62

In the event of conviction for an offence committed by means of information and communication technologies, the court may impose as complementary penalties: the prohibition from sending digital communication messages; the provisional or permanent prohibition of access to the site used to commit the offence; or the injunction to cut off access by all available technical means or even to prohibit its hosting.

The judge may issue an injunction to any person legally responsible for the site used to commit the offence or to any qualified person to implement technical means likely to guarantee the prohibition of access, hosting or the cutting off of access to the incriminated site.

Article 512-63

Any person who violates the prohibitions pronounced by the judge, pursuant to Article 131-15 of this Code, is punished by imprisonment of three years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.

Article 512-64

In the event of conviction for an offence committed by means of electronic communication media, the judge may, as a complementary measure, order the dissemination at the expense of the convicted person, by extract, of the decision on that same media.

When it is ordered, the publication provided for in the first paragraph of this article is executed within fifteen days following the day on which the conviction became final, on pain of imprisonment of three years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.

Article 512-65

Without prejudice to the provisions of Articles 512-33 to 512-39 of this Code, the following complementary penalties may be imposed for offences related to cryptology:

1°) confiscation of the tools which were used to commit the offence or which are the product thereof;

2°) the prohibition from exercising a public function or a professional activity related to cryptology for a period of five years;

3°) the closure of one or more establishments of the enterprise used to commit the incriminated acts for a period of five years;

4°) exclusion from public procurement for a period of five years.

The complementary penalties apply to any natural or legal person guilty of one of the offences referred to in this article.

TITRE II : OFFENCES AGAINST CULTURAL HERITAGE

[Law No. 2022-034 of 28 July 2022 establishing the regime for the protection and promotion of national cultural heritage]

Chapitre 1 : General provisions

Article 521-1

For the purposes of this Code, cultural heritage means all tangible and intangible cultural property which, for religious or secular purposes, is of importance to the State, local authorities, communities, groups and individuals for history, art, thought, science and technology.

Article 521-2

This Code applies to cultural property located on national territory, whatever its nature and origin and having exceptional universal value from the aesthetic, scientific or conservation point of view.

Article 521-3

Tangible cultural heritage consists of movable and immovable cultural property.

Intangible cultural heritage consists of all practices, traditions and oral expressions, representations, knowledge, know-how and cultural spaces that the State, local authorities, communities, groups and individuals recognise as part of their cultural heritage.

Article 521-4

Cultural property is considered to be, regardless of its owners, movable or immovable property that is of great importance to the State, Territorial Collectivities and communities.

Movable cultural property is capable of being moved. It includes: archaeological property, products of regular, fortuitous or clandestine archaeological excavations and discoveries;
ethnographic property;
works of art, paintings, drawings, statuary and sculptures, engravings, prints and lithographs, tapestries, weavings, assemblages, installations, shipwrecks and underwater objects;
digital works of art as well as digital libraries based on the interactivity of creative genius and software intelligence;
elements originating from an artistic or historical monument;
rare collections and specimens of zoology, anatomy, botany, mineralogy; objects of paleontological interest; ancient, rare manuscripts, incunabula, books, documents and publications of historical, artistic, scientific, literary interest, isolated or in collections, more than seventy years old; objects and items of antiquity such as inscriptions, coins and engraved seals; archives, iconographic, photographic and audiovisual documents.
Immovable cultural property is property which, either by nature or by destination, cannot be moved without damage to itself and to its environment.
It includes: archaeological sites, buildings, monuments of architecture, art or history, major museums, major libraries, major archives repositories; architectural ensembles or elements of traditional habitats grouped or isolated; roads and cultural routes illustrating the interaction of the movement of exchanges and dialogue between several regions of the country in space and time, ruins and cities buried under the seas or any other aquatic spaces; natural monuments, formations or groups of physical and biological formations having aesthetic or scientific value;
geological formations, areas or zones constituting the habitat of animal and plant species of national importance;
dead cities of archaeological, historical and scientific interest;
living historic cities evolving with medinas or ancient fabrics of historical, scientific and cultural interest;
museum institutions, archives, libraries and centers of ancient manuscripts having exceptional value for history, science, religion.

Article 521-5

A cultural property is under national protection when it is registered in the inventory or classified in the national cultural heritage.

Article 521-6

Any cultural property inscribed on the World Heritage List or in the International Register of Cultural Property under Special Protection, the International List of Cultural Property under Enhanced Protection and on the Lists of other UNESCO cultural Conventions is under international protection.

Article 521-7

Taking into account the specific nature of certain major development works of perimeters and sites, construction of large dams, highways, execution of mining and petroleum contracts, environmental, cultural and social impact studies, the archaeological component must be included in the feasibility study costs of said works.

Chapitre 2 : Des infractions

Article 522-1

Commits the crime of destruction of cultural property, whoever intentionally directs an attack against cultural property registered in the inventory, classified in the national cultural heritage or inscribed on a World Heritage List.

Also commits the crime of destruction of cultural property, whoever, in time of armed conflict, intentionally accomplishes in violation of laws and conventions, in particular the 1954 Hague Convention for the Protection of Cultural Property in the Event of Armed Conflict and its Second Protocol of 1999, one of the following acts:
the act of directing an attack against cultural property under enhanced protection;
the act of using cultural property under enhanced protection or its immediate surroundings in support of military action;
the act of destroying or appropriating on a large scale cultural property protected by laws and conventions, in particular the 1954 Hague Convention for the Protection of Cultural Property in the Event of Armed Conflict and its Second Protocol of 1999;
the act of committing an attack on cultural property covered by the 1954 Hague Convention for the Protection of Cultural Property in the Event of Armed Conflict and its Second Protocol of 1999;
the theft, pillage or misappropriation of cultural property protected by the Convention, and acts of vandalism directed against cultural property protected by these international legal instruments.

Article 522-2

Any person guilty of the crime of destruction of cultural property defined in Article 522-1 above is punished by ten years' imprisonment and a fine of 2,000,000 francs, if the property is classified in the national cultural heritage.

Article 522-3

If the destruction of such property was committed in a gang, with weapons, by means of threats or in a concerted action, the penalty is fifteen years' imprisonment and a fine of 3,000,000 francs.

Article 522-4

If the property subject to the attack is under international protection, the offender is punished by twenty years' imprisonment and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.

Article 522-5

The attempt and complicity in the offenses referred to in this chapter are punished by the same penalties as those provided against the principal perpetrator.

Article 522-6

Is punished by two years' imprisonment and a fine of 500,000 francs whoever has willfully desecrated buildings, places of worship or religious sites or mutilated, destroyed cultural objects.

The penalty is five years' imprisonment and a fine of 1,000,000 francs, if such property is registered in the inventory.

Article 522-7

The removal of items from public collections, the removal of fragments of classified property, the destruction of registers of registration of cultural property, of public collections are punished by one year's imprisonment and a fine of 1,500,000 francs.
If the offense is the work of a depositary of the competent authority, he is punished by five years' imprisonment.

Article 522-8

Whoever is guilty of pillaging an archaeological site, theft of archaeological property or illicit archaeological excavation is punished by five years' imprisonment and a fine of 1,000,000 francs.
When the acts of pillage or illicit excavation are the work of a person who is a depositary of the competent authority, the manager of the site, museums, or any person involved in the management, surveillance, of the site or establishment, the penalty is five years' imprisonment and a fine of 2,000,000 francs.

Article 522-9

Whoever engages in the export of classified property is punished by five years' imprisonment and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.
Any person who issues an export authorization for the purpose of selling classified property is treated as an accomplice of the exporter and punished by the same penalty.

Article 522-10

Whoever, knowing that a property is proposed for classification, exports it, or attempts to export it, is punished by three years' imprisonment and a fine of 1,000,000 francs, without prejudice to the action for damages that may be brought by the competent authority.
In addition, the Court may order the confiscation of the property subject to illicit export.

Article 522-11

Anyone who voluntarily fails to include the archaeological component in the feasibility studies for major works referred to in Article 521-7 above is punished by a fine of 20,000,000 francs, without prejudice to the suspension of works and the assumption of necessary studies.

Article 522-12

The alienation of classified property belonging to the State, the modification, transformation, reproduction, exploitation for profit of classified property, are punished by imprisonment of six months and a fine of 500,000 francs.

Article 522-13

The commercialization, exportation of cultural property without authorization from the competent authority are punished by a fine of 5,000,000 francs, without prejudice to the confiscation of property.

TITRE III : DES CRIMES ET DELITS CONTRE L'ENVIRONNEMENT

[Loi n°2021-032/du 24 mai 2021 relative aux pollutions et aux nuisances ;

Loi n°2014-024/du 03 juillet 2014 portant interdiction de la production, de l'importation et de la commercialisation des sachets plastiques non biodégradables en République du Mali ;

Loi n°10-028 du 12 juillet 2010 déterminant les principes de gestion des ressources du domaine forestier national ;

Loi n°2018-036 du 27 juin 2018 déterminant les principes de gestion de la faune et de son habitat ;

Loi n°02-017 du 03 juin 2002 régissant la détention, le commerce, l'exportation, la réexportation, l'importation, le transport et le transit de spécimen d'espèces de faune et de flore sauvages].

Chapitre 1

General Provisions Section 1: Definitions

Article 531-1 : For the purposes of this Code, the following are understood as

1°) environment: a whole perceived as an entity, a space and at a given time, of physical, chemical, biological and social factors likely to have a direct or indirect effect, immediate or in the long term, on the human species and its activities and on animal and plant species;

2°) sanitation: any action aimed at improving all conditions which, in the physical environment of human life, influence or are likely to have an unfavorable influence on physical, mental or social well-being;

3°) precautionary principle: the principle according to which the absence of certainty, taking into account the scientific and technical knowledge of the moment, must not delay the adoption of effective and proportionate measures aimed at preventing a risk of serious and irreversible damage to the environment;
4°) polluter-pays principle: the principle according to which the costs resulting from measures to prevent, reduce pollution and combat it must be borne by the polluter;

5°) environmental information: any data that is available in written form, images or on any other information medium relating to:
the state of water, air, soil, fauna and flora;
activities causing nuisances and pollution;
activities or measures aimed at protecting the environment.

6°) nuisance: any aggression against the natural or artificial environment surrounding man and causing inconvenience or damage to the latter;

7°) waste: any solid, liquid, gaseous substance or residue from a production, transformation or use process of any other substances eliminated or intended to be eliminated;

8°) domestic waste: any waste resulting from household activities, including human excreta;

10°) industrial waste: any waste resulting from industrial, artisanal or commercial activities not assimilated to domestic waste;

11°) agricultural waste: any container having contained chemical products or any packaging having been used for the use of these products in agricultural, horticultural, fish farming and livestock activities;

12°) biomedical waste: any waste from care, pharmacy and biomedical analysis activities;

13°) hazardous waste: unused and unusable products and by-products, residues and waste resulting from an industrial, commercial, artisanal, agricultural, scientific or any other activity which may present a danger to health and the environment, either by themselves or when they come into contact with other products due to their chemical reactivity or their toxic properties;

14°) pollutant: any solid, liquid or gaseous discharge, any waste, odor, sound, vibration, radiation or any combination thereof likely to cause pollution;

15°) pollution: any contamination or direct or indirect modification of the environment caused by an act likely to negatively influence the environment, to cause a situation detrimental to the health, safety, well-being of man, fauna, flora or collective and individual property;

16°) obsolete product: product whose use is prohibited due to its depressive nature for health or environmental protection reasons;

17°) environmental impact study: the identification, description and evaluation of the effects of projects on man, fauna and flora, soil, water, air, climate and landscape, including interactions between these factors, cultural heritage and other material property;

18°) environmental impact study report: any document containing the results of the environmental impact study required to obtain administrative authorization for the realization of any project;

19°) environmental audit: the internal evaluation and management tool carried out by companies and public administration services to ensure that political, regulatory and nominative requirements for environmental protection are respected;

20°) competent administration: National Directorate of Sanitation and Control of Pollution and Nuisances or any other structure authorized by the State.

21°) plastic bag: bag of large or small format made of synthetic material (polyethylene), serving as a container for foodstuffs or any other product;

22°) biodegradable plastic bag: any plastic bag capable of decomposing within a period of at most eighteen months, under the action of microorganisms present in nature.

Section 2 : Provisions relating to pollution and nuisances

[Loi n°2021-032 du 24 mai 2021 relative aux pollutions et aux nuisances]

Paragraphe 1 : Environmental assessments

Article 531-2

Activities likely to harm the environment and the quality of the living environment are subject to a study or to the environmental and social impact notice.

A decree taken in the Council of Ministers sets the list of projects subject to the environmental and social impact study and notice and specifies the content of the procedures.

Article 531-3

All work, all development and all industrial, agricultural, mining, artisanal, commercial or transport works whose activity may be a source of pollution, nuisance or environmental degradation are compulsorily subject to environmental audit.

A decree taken in the Council of Ministers determines the conditions for carrying out the audit.

Paragraphe 2 : Environmental information

Article 531-4 : Every person has the right to free access to environmental information

Access to environmental information, the publication of which affects international relations, national defense, confidentiality or which may cause serious danger to security, is subject to authorization.

Article 531-5

A request for environmental information must be refused if it refers to the transmission of files that are not yet closed or data whose processing is not yet completed or internal administrative communications.

Paragraphe 3 : Des déchets

Sous paragraphe I: Des déchets domestiques solides

Article 531-6

It is prohibited to hold or abandon solid domestic waste under conditions favoring the development of harmful organisms, insects and disease vectors likely to cause damage to persons and the environment.

Article 531-7

Any person who produces or holds solid domestic waste under conditions likely to harm public health and safety or the environment in general, is required to ensure its disposal or recycling.

Article 531-8

It is prohibited to burn solid domestic waste in the open air inside any built-up area.

Article 531-9

It is prohibited to store or bury solid domestic waste in places other than those designated by the competent authority.

Sous paragraphe II: Des déchets domestiques liquides

Article 531-10

It is prohibited to discharge into watercourses, gutters and collectors or other public or private places, liquid domestic waste that does not comply with discharge standards.

Article 531-11

Any person who produces liquid domestic waste is required to ensure that it cannot harm human health and the environment.

Any person who damages or destroys a public, collective or semi-collective sanitation facility must restore it to its original condition or bear the costs of restoration.

Sous paragraphe III: Des déchets agricoles

Article 531-12

It is prohibited to hold or abandon agricultural waste under conditions that favor the development of harmful organisms, insects and other disease vectors likely to cause damage to persons and the environment.

Article 531-13

Any person who produces or holds agricultural waste under conditions likely to harm public health and safety or the environment in general, is required to ensure its disposal and recycling.

Sous paragraphe IV: Des déchets biomédicaux et industriels

Article 531-14

It is prohibited to discharge biomedical and industrial, craft or commercial waste into watercourses, gutters and collectors or other public or private places.

Article 531-15

It is prohibited to deposit biomedical waste and solid industrial waste in a transit depot or in a landfill other than that reserved for them.

Article 531-16

It is prohibited to burn biomedical waste and solid industrial waste in the open air inside any built-up area.

Disposal operations by incineration may only take place in facilities authorized by the ministry in charge of the Environment.

Article 531-17

It is prohibited to bury biomedical waste and solid industrial waste and to deposit them in places other than the landfills reserved for them and authorized storage centers.

Sous paragraphe V: Des déchets dangereux

Article 531-18

Any waste originating from abroad is presumed to be hazardous within the meaning of this Code.

Article 531-19

Prohibited throughout the entire territory of the Republic of Mali are the offer or sale, acquisition and transfer for consideration or free of charge, possession, transformation, destruction, neutralization and disposal of hazardous waste from other countries.

Article 531-20

Prohibited are all acts relating to the purchase, sale, import, export, transit, transport, treatment, deposit and storage of hazardous waste without prior authorization from the minister in charge of the Environment.

Article 531-21

Any producer of hazardous waste is required to send annually to the minister in charge of the Environment, the nature, quantity, date and disposal procedures of the waste produced.

Article 531-22

Exports of hazardous waste may only be authorized to a country when that country has adequate facilities to dispose of them using ecologically sound methods.

Hazardous waste intended for export must be packaged, labeled and transported in accordance with accepted and recognized international standards and rules on packaging, labeling and transport.

Article 531-23

When hazardous waste is the subject of illicit trafficking or mismanagement, the costs of return, the costs of restoring contaminated sites and the repair of damage caused are borne by the offender, without prejudice to the penalties provided for by the regulations in force.

Article 531-24

Prohibited are all forms of use and management of hazardous waste without prior authorization from the minister in charge of the Environment, who sets technical requirements and special rules to be observed.

The procedures for managing hazardous waste are established by order of the Minister in charge of the Environment.

Sous paragraphe VI: Des déchets spéciaux

Article 531-25

Special waste includes biomedical waste, plastic waste, used oils, obsolete pesticides, polychlorinated biphenyls (PCBs) and polychlorinated terphenyls (PCTs), used batteries and accumulators, radioactive waste, electronic, electrical and household appliance waste, waste containing asbestos, waste from military activities, used drums, barrels and packaging, chemical waste related to mining operations, used solvents.

Article 531-26

Any producer of special waste is required to send annually to the minister in charge of the Environment, the nature, quantity, date and disposal procedures of the waste produced.

Article 531-27

Special waste requires specific treatment different from that of household and similar waste. It must be disposed of in specialized facilities.

Special waste intended for export for disposal must be packaged, labeled and transported in accordance with accepted and recognized international standards and rules on packaging, labeling and transport.

Paragraphe 4 : De la pollution atmosphérique et des odeurs incommodantes

Article 531-28

Air, sea, river, road and rail transport, buildings, industrial, craft and agricultural establishments, mines and quarries, treatment plants, generators, mills, or other immovable or movable objects must be constructed, operated or used in such a way as to avoid air pollution or odors that inconvenience populations and compromise public health or safety.

Article 531-29
