It is prohibited to operate an industrial, mining or craft unit emitting polluting substances into the air in the form of smoke, dust, gas or liquid without complying with emission standards.

Paragraphe 5 : Des bruits et des nuisances

Article 531-30

Prohibited is any noise likely to disturb rest, tranquility, health, public safety or to harm the environment.

Article 531-31

The establishment and operation of any human, industrial, mining or artisanal facility housing sound or light sources or other sources likely to constitute a threat to rest, tranquility, health, public safety or to harm the environment must be carried out in compliance with sensitivity zones and standards set by regulations in force.

Paragraphe 6 : Protection of green spaces, cemeteries and landfills

Article 531-32

The protection of green spaces against all causes of degradation is of general interest.

When they have acquired ownership of green spaces, wooded or not, the State, public establishments or Local Authorities undertake to preserve, develop and maintain them.

It is prohibited to carry out any activity that degrades green spaces.

Article 531-33

The decommissioning of green spaces, cemeteries, landfills and wastewater and sewage sludge treatment plants is subject to prior authorization from the minister responsible for the Environment, after favorable opinion from the ministers responsible for Urban Planning, Health, Culture and Local Authorities.

Paragraphe 7 : Chemical substances

Article 531-34

Harmful and dangerous chemical substances which, by reason of their toxicity, radioactivity, destructive power in the environment or concentration in biological chains, present or are likely to present a danger to humans, the natural environment or their environment when they are produced, imported into the national territory or discharged into the environment, are subject to control and monitoring by competent State services in this matter.

Article 531-35

Industrial establishments likely to hold and/or dispose of persistent organic pollutants are subject to an audit of their facilities.

Article 531-36

The importation, use, possession, distribution, repackaging and storage of chemical substances that have not been subject to approval or authorization are prohibited.

However, exemptions may be granted by joint order of the ministers responsible for the Environment, Health and Scientific Research to specialized institutions for research and experimentation needs.

Article 531-37

Any person involved in the importation, production and distribution of chemical substances must obtain an authorization issued by the ministers responsible for the Environment, Health, Agriculture and Industries.

Article 531-38

Any holder of a chemical substance must prove the quality of their product by presenting an analysis certificate issued by an approved laboratory.
In case of doubt about the quality of the product, the competent Administration carries out counter-expertise analyses. The analysis costs are borne by the holder.

Article 531-39

Any obsolete or expired chemical substance must be declared to the competent administration.

The holder is required to eliminate their stock or have it eliminated at their expense by a company approved by the minister responsible for the Environment.

In case of voluntary or accidental discharge of chemical substances into the environment, the costs of restoring contaminated sites and repairing the damage caused are borne by the offender, without prejudice to the sanctions provided for by regulations in force.

Paragraphe 8 : Prosecution of offenses

Article 531-40

The sworn agents of the National Directorate of Sanitation and Control of Pollution and Nuisances and Judicial Police Officers investigate and record by official reports offenses against the provisions of this Code and exercise the powers related thereto.

Article 531-41

The agents referred to in the article above may be assisted when necessary by agents of the Police, Gendarmerie, Customs, Health and Water and Forests.

Section 3

Provisions relating to the production, importation and marketing of non-biodegradable plastic bags in the Republic of Mali

[Loi n°2014-024 du 03 juillet 2014 portant interdiction de la production, de l'importation et de la commercialisation des sachets plastiques non biodégradables en République du Mali]

Article 531-42

This Code prohibits the production, importation and marketing of non-biodegradable plastic bags in the Republic of Mali.

Chapitre 2 : Incriminations

Section 1 : Incriminations relating to pollution and nuisances

[Loi n°2021-032 du 24 mai 2021 relative aux pollutions et aux nuisances]

Article 532-1

Is punished by life imprisonment and a fine of 10,000,000 francs, any person violating the provisions of Article 531-18 of this Code. The offender is sentenced to re-export without delay and at their expense the waste introduced.

Article 532-2

In the cases provided for in Articles 531-19 and 531-20 above, a residence ban for a period of ten years may be pronounced.

Article 532-3

When the offense is committed within the framework of the activity of a legal person, criminal liability falls on any natural person who by virtue of their functions has responsibility for the management, supervision or control of this activity.

Any legal person in question is held primarily liable for payment of fines, civil reparations, costs and expenses.

Article 532-4

Are punished by a fine of 20,000 francs the perpetrators of offenses against the provisions of Articles 531-6, 531-8, 531-10 and 531-12 of this Code.

Article 532-5

Is punished by the penalties provided for in Article 242-40 of this Code anyone who opposes the reporting agents referred to in Articles 531-40 and 531-41 above.

In case of recidivism, the fine and penalty are doubled.

Article 532-6

Is punished by a fine of 5,000,000 francs anyone who executes a project likely to harm the environment without carrying out an environmental impact study approved by the minister responsible for the Environment.

In case of recidivism, they are punished by six months' imprisonment and the fine may be doubled.

Article 532-7

Is punished by a fine of 3,000,000 francs anyone who executes a project for five years, likely to harm the environment without carrying out an environmental and social audit approved by the minister responsible for the Environment.

Article 532-8

Are punished by three months' imprisonment and a fine of 3,000,000 francs the perpetrators of offenses against the provisions of Articles 531-14 and 531-36 of this Code.

Article 532-9

Are punished by two years' imprisonment and a fine of 1,000,000 francs the perpetrators of offenses against the provisions of Articles 531-17, 531-20 and 531-21 of this Code.

In case of recidivism the fines and penalties are doubled.

Article 532-10

Are punished by two years' imprisonment and a fine of 1,000,000 francs, the perpetrators of offenses against the provisions of Articles 531-24, and 531-30 of this Code.

In case of recidivism, these penalties may be doubled.

Article 532-11

Is punished by two years' imprisonment and a fine of 10,000,000 francs the perpetrators of offenses against the provisions of paragraph 2 of Article 531-22 of this Code.

In case of recidivism, these penalties may be doubled.

Section 2

Penalties incurred in case of violation of the prohibition on the production, importation and marketing of non-biodegradable plastic bags in the Republic of Mali

[Law No. 2014-024 of July 3, 2014 prohibiting the production, importation and marketing of non-biodegradable plastic bags in the Republic of Mali]

Article 532-12

Anyone who produces, imports or markets non-biodegradable plastic bags is liable to pay 100 francs per small format bag and 200 francs per large format bag. In addition, these plastic bags are seized and confiscated.

Section 3: Settlements

Article 532-13

In case of violations of the provisions of this Code, the competent administration has full power to settle.

The settlement procedure is exercised before or after judgment. In the latter case, the settlement only concerns pecuniary penalties.

The settlement procedure is excluded in case of recidivism.

TITRE IV: CRIMES AND OFFENSES AGAINST FORESTS AND WILDLIFE

Chapitre 1: Definitions

Section 1: Definitions relating to forests

[Law No. 10-028 of July 12, 2010 determining the principles of management of the resources of the national forest domain]

Article 541-1: For the purposes of this Code, the following are understood as

1°) conservation areas: areas delimited, classified, protected and managed for the purposes of conservation and sustainable use of natural resources;

2°) management: set of rules and techniques implemented in a forest formation or conservation area, with a view to achieving sustainable management;

3°) conservation: development of forest resources with a view to achieving both protection and use objectives;

4°) CITES: the Convention on International Trade in Endangered Species of Wild Fauna and Flora, concluded in Washington,
D.C, on March 3, 1973;

5°) clearing: any voluntary operation during which all or part of the natural vegetation is cut down for the purpose of establishing a human dwelling, agricultural, industrial or forestry production or on the occasion of carrying out major works in the forest domain;

6°) forest species: indigenous or exotic non-agricultural plant species whose evolutionary process has not been influenced by man to meet his needs;

7°) fully protected forest species: indigenous, non-agricultural, non-cultivated plant species, threatened or presenting particular interest from an ecological, botanical, cultural, economic, scientific or medicinal point of view;

8°) partially protected forest species: indigenous non-agricultural, non-cultivated plant species, protected because of the quality of its wood and whose felling is subject to obtaining a title issued after prior payment of a fee per tree and whose minimum diameter is fixed by the texts in force;

9°) forest species of economic value: indigenous or exotic non-agricultural plant species, protected because of the economic value of its wood, not listed on the list of protected forest species but whose exploitation is prohibited for the production of fuelwood;

10°) forest exploitation: exploitation of the natural resources of the forest, in particular the cutting or collection of forest products;

11°) forest: plant formation whose exclusive or principal products are timber, service wood or fuelwood and which, incidentally, may produce resins, latex, gum, flowers, fruits, bark, roots, leaves, bamboos, raphias, vines, grasses, mushrooms and all other non-agricultural plant products;

12°) classified forest: natural or artificial forest having been the subject of a classification act following a consultation procedure with the populations in accordance with the provisions of the texts in force;
13°) protected forest: natural or artificial forest subject to the provisions of this Code and not having been the subject of a classification act;
14°) exploitation title: document issued for the cutting, harvesting or collection of a determined quantity of woody or non-woody forest products;
15°) transport title: document issued for the transport or circulation of woody or non-woody forest products.

Section 2: Definitions relating to wildlife

[Law No. 2018-036 of June 27, 2018 determining the principles of management of wildlife and its habitat]

Article 541-2: For the purposes of this Code, the following terms mean

1°) core area: central part of the biosphere reserve managed primarily for the purpose of integral protection of ecosystems and having the legal status of a national park;
2°) conservation areas: any area, protected, delimited, specially reserved and managed primarily or entirely for one of the following purposes: protection for scientific purposes or protection of wild resources; protection of ecosystems and for recreational purposes; conservation of specific natural elements; conservation with management interventions; conservation of terrestrial or aquatic landscapes and for recreational purposes as well as:
other areas or zones designated and/or managed primarily for the purposes of conservation and sustainable use of natural resources, as well as those for which criteria are adopted in accordance with the provisions of this Code and its implementing texts or international conventions, treaties or agreements signed and/or ratified by Mali;

3°) protected area: terrestrial or aquatic conservation space, geographically delimited, having been the subject of a legal text of classification following a consultation procedure with populations, particularly those living nearby, and benefiting from special measures for the protection and management of fauna and/or preservation of biological diversity;

4°) migration corridor: one or more environments functionally connecting different vital habitats for a species, a population, a metapopulation or a group of species allowing its dispersal and migration;

5°) biological diversity: variability of living organisms from all sources including, among others, terrestrial, aquatic ecosystems and the ecological complexes of which they are part; this variability includes genetic diversity, diversity within species and between species as well as that of ecosystems;

6°) fully protected species: species removed from all taking except for scientific reasons or for the protection of persons and/or their property;

7°) partially protected species: species for which the hunting or capture regime is strictly limited and whose hunting or capture permit is accompanied by culling latitude;

8°) non-protected species: all animals not listed on the list of fully or partially protected species;

9°) national park: protected area managed primarily for the purpose of protecting ecosystems and for recreational purposes;

10°) strict nature reserve: protected area classified in the name of the State, managed primarily for scientific purposes. It consists of a terrestrial and/or aquatic space comprising ecosystems, geological or physiological characteristics and/or remarkable or representative species, managed primarily for the purposes of scientific research and/or continuous environmental monitoring;

11°) Biosphere Reserve: protected area covering an ecosystem or a combination of terrestrial and/or aquatic ecosystems, aimed at promoting a balanced relationship between human beings and the biosphere and providing a demonstration thereof and recognized at the international level within the framework of the UNESCO programme on Man and the Biosphere (MAB).

It is designated by the International Coordinating Council of the MAB Programme, at the request of the State;

12°) partial reserve or sanctuary: area set aside for the protection of characteristic communities of animals and/or the protection of particularly threatened animal or plant species as well as habitats essential to their survival, in which any other interest or activity is subordinated to the achievement of this objective;

13°) specimen: any wild animal, living or dead, belonging to the species covered by this Code and its implementing texts, their remains and trophies, any part or any product obtained from them, whether or not incorporated in goods as well as any other goods in the case where it appears from a supporting document, packaging or a mark, or label or any other element that these are parts or products of these species;

14°) hunting interest zone: classified area, geographically delimited in the protected forest domain, having been the subject of a classification act, and developed for the conservation and sustainable exploitation of fauna and its habitats for hunting, tourism, recreational, economic and/or scientific purposes.

Section 3

Definitions relating to the possession, trade, export, re-export, import, transport and transit of specimens of wild fauna and flora species

[Law No. 02-017 of 03 June 2002 governing the possession, trade, export, re-export, import, transport and transit of specimens of wild fauna and flora species]

Article 541-3: For the purposes of this Code, the following terms mean

1°) wild fauna: all animal species living freely in their natural environment;

2°) wild flora: all plant species growing in the natural environment;

3°) export: the operation by which a specimen, originating from the country, part or product, belonging to one of the species covered by this Code is sent outside the national jurisdiction;

4°) import: the operation by which a specimen, part or product belonging to one of the species covered by this Code is introduced into the national jurisdiction from a foreign country;

5°) re-export: the export of any specimen that has been previously imported;

6°) hunting products: captured animals, meat, eggs and trophies;

7°) permit or certificate: the official document issued by the Management Authority in order to authorize the import, export, re-export or introduction from the sea of specimens of species covered by this Code;

8°) specimen: any animal or plant, either living or dead belonging to the species covered by this Code, or any part or any product obtained from them, whether or not incorporated in other goods as well as any other goods in the case where it appears from a supporting document, packaging or a mark, or label or any other element that these are parts or products of animals or plants of these species.

Chapter 2: Crimes and offences against forests

[Law No. 10-028 of 12 July 2010 determining the principles of management of resources of the national forest domain]

Section 1: Provisions relating to the forest

Paragraph 1: Mining operations in classified forest

Article 542-1

Any natural or legal person who, without authorization, digs in the soil, extracts or removes sand, peat, turf, stones, earth or in general organizes the search and/or mining operations in a classified forest with or without occupation of the premises, is punished by a fine calculated at the rate of 500 francs per square meter of damaged and/or occupied surface, without prejudice to confiscations, restitutions, restoration of the premises and damages.

In addition, the offender incurs the following sanctions:
prohibition from continuing operations or activities in the classified domain;
restoration of the premises;
demolition of installations, dwellings or other equipment and expulsion of occupants from the classified perimeter.

However, compensatory reforestation must be carried out with seedlings of indigenous forest species adapted to the deforested area.

Paragraphe 2

Damage to and disappearance of boundary markers, pollution and degradation of the classified forest domain

Article 542-2

Without prejudice to confiscations, restitutions, restoration of premises and damages, the following are punishable by two years' imprisonment and a fine of 5,000,000 francs:
anyone who destroys, moves or causes to disappear all or part of the boundary markers, beacons, signs, marks or fences used to delimit the classified forest domain;
anyone who, without authorization, deposits rubble or refuse of any kind in the classified forest domain.

In cases of pollution using hazardous waste, the provisions of Title III of Book Five of this Code apply.

Paragraphe 3

Clearing, cultivation, occupation of the classified forest domain

Article 542-3

Anyone who, in violation of the provisions of this Code, occupies the classified forest domain, clears and/or cultivates it with or without occupation of the premises, is punishable by a fine calculated at the rate of 250 francs per square meter of cleared area, without prejudice to confiscations, restitutions, restoration of premises and damages.

The offender is also liable to the following sanctions:
prohibition from continuing the operations or activities for which or during which the clearing was carried out;
restoration of the premises, consisting of planting or sowing forest species and other work necessary to ensure the functions that characterized the cleared forest;
demolition of installations, dwellings or other equipment and expulsion of occupants from the classified perimeter;
pure and simple cancellation of the clearing authorization or the title authorizing occupation or exploitation of the land if it exists.

Paragraphe 4 : Clearing without authorization in the protected forest domain

Article 542-4

Anyone who clears without authorization in the protected forest domain is punishable by two years' imprisonment and a fine of 25,000 francs per hectare.

If there is incineration of trees, the fine is doubled.

Article 542-5

The owner of wooded land or private forest who clears his land or forest in violation of the provisions of this Code is punishable by a fine of 25,000 francs per hectare without prejudice to restoration of the premises, consisting of planting or sowing forest species and other work necessary to ensure the functions that characterized the cleared forest.

Paragraphe 5 : Grazing of domestic animals in the classified forest domain

Article 542-6

Anyone who, in violation of the provisions of this Code, grazes or moves one or more domestic animals or camps in the classified forest domain is punishable by two years' imprisonment and a fine of 250,000 francs and without prejudice to the confiscation of any tool or means of cutting vegetation and damages.

In addition, he is liable to the following additional sanctions:

a)	demolition of installations, dwellings or other equipment and expulsion of the shepherd(s) and other occupants from the classified perimeter;

b)	payment of a fine of:
1,000 francs per bovine, equine, asinine and camelid;
2,000 francs per ovine, caprine and porcine.

c)	when the offense does not result from purely fortuitous circumstances, imprisonment of two months may be pronounced against the shepherd and his accomplices.

Animals found in the classified perimeter are impounded and confiscated.

Article 542-7

Possession in the classified perimeter of a firearm and/or any tool or means of cutting prohibited vegetation is assimilated to the incriminated act and punished as such. In cases of possession of a firearm, the provisions of the texts governing the possession of weapons and the management of wild fauna and its habitat apply.

Section 2

Arson or intentional bush fire in the protected or classified domain

Article 542-8

In case of fire or bush fire caused intentionally in either the protected or classified domain, the applicable penalties are those of articles 435-1, paragraph 9 and 435-2, paragraph 3 of this Code.

Section 3

Cutting, mutilation, unauthorized exploitation of protected species or species of economic value

Article 542-9

Anyone who, without authorization, cuts, barks, tops, prunes, branches, fells, uproots, mutilates or damages in any way one or more planted trees or natural plants of species listed in the category of fully protected forest species in accordance with the provisions of this Code, is punishable by five years' imprisonment and a fine of 500,000 francs.

The attempt is punishable as the offense itself.

Article 542-10

Anyone who tops, cuts, uproots, prunes, pollards, branches, mutilates or damages in any way one or more planted trees or natural plants of species listed in the category of partially protected forest species or species of economic value, in accordance with the provisions of this Code, is punishable by three years' imprisonment and a fine of 300,000 francs.

The attempt is punishable as the offense itself.

Article 542-11

Anyone who barks, tops, prunes or fells one or more planted trees or natural plants of unprotected forest species, for the purpose of feeding domestic animals, is punishable by six months' imprisonment and a fine of 50,000 francs.

Article 542-12

Any owner of an artificial forest formation who exploits and/or transports, without authorization, species listed among those partially or fully protected by the State or Local Authorities, in violation of the provisions of this Code, is punishable by a fine of 150,000 francs.

Section 4 : Forest exploitation without a management plan

Article 542-13

Any commercial exploitation in the forest domain of the State or Local Authorities without a management plan previously approved or adopted by the competent authority of the forest domain concerned, is punishable by a fine of:
500,000 francs for natural persons;
5,000,000 francs for legal persons;

In addition, the exploitation is stopped by the competent authority with jurisdiction over the area.

Section 5 : Import and export of forest products

Article 542-14

Without prejudice to the provisions in force relating to the illegal exercise of trade, anyone who imports or exports a specimen of a product or an object from a forest species in violation of the provisions of this Code, is punishable:
for a fully protected forest species, by one year's imprisonment and a fine of 250,000 francs;
for a partially protected forest species, by six months' imprisonment and a fine of 500,000 francs;
for a forest species of economic value, by three months' imprisonment and a fine of 250,000 francs;
for an unprotected forest species, by three months' imprisonment and a fine of 100,000 francs.

In addition, the specimen or product is confiscated or returned to the country of origin at his expense in cases of importation.

Article 542-15

Anyone who, without authorization, outside the cases provided for in the exercise of usage rights, cuts or harvests non-timber forest products for commercial purposes, is punishable by eleven days' imprisonment and a fine of 100,000 francs without prejudice to the confiscation of the products.

Section 6

False indication, falsification of writings and reproduction of public seals

Article 542-16

Is punished by the penalties provided for in Article 243-44 of this Code, anyone who:
gives false information with a view to concealing the nature of forest products during the issuance of exploitation or circulation or export permits;
falsifies writings or fraudulently reproduces public seals.

Article 542-17

Without prejudice to the sanctions provided for by this Code, the withdrawal of permits and the prohibition for a period of five years from obtaining new permits are pronounced against any forest operator who is guilty of false information, falsification of writings and/or reproduction of public seals.

In case of recidivism, the prohibition and withdrawal of the permit for a period of five years are mandatory.

Section 7: Opposition to the authority of Water and Forests agents

Article 542-18: Is punished by the penalties provided for in Article 242-40 of this Code

anyone who opposes by acts, words, gestures, any manoeuvres whatsoever the exercise of the functions of Water and Forests agents of the State or of Local Authorities and thereby, undermines or attempts to hinder the proper functioning of the service responsible for forests as well as any incitement to such opposition;
anyone who, without legitimate excuse, does not respond to regular summons from Water and Forests agents of the State or of Local Authorities;
anyone who, by voluntary abstention hinders or attempts to hinder the exercise of the missions of Water and Forests agents.

When the offence defined above is committed by several persons acting in concert, the penalties provided for are doubled.

Chapter 3: Crimes and offences against fauna

[Law No. 2018-036 of 27 June 2018 determining the principles of management of fauna and its habitat]

Section 1: Provisions relating to fauna

Article 543-1

Anyone who hunts or captures one or more wild animals or organises these activities in a strict nature reserve, a core area of a biosphere reserve or in a national park, is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs without prejudice to the confiscation of hunting or capture products, material means, vehicles, devices and equipment used to commit the offence.

Are punished by the same penalties:
those who, in any place are found in possession of an elephant tusk or other specimen of a fully protected species whose origin they cannot justify;
those who unlawfully appropriate one or more trophies or remains of fully protected animals found dead or killed in the exercise of legitimate defence or the protection of persons and property;
those who are convicted of having accomplished acts of hunting or capturing fully protected animals without the required permit;
those who, without authorisation, introduce one or more exotic animal species into nature.

In cases of illegal introduction of one or more exotic animal and/or plant species into a protected area, the penalties are doubled.

Section 2

Provisions relating to international trade in specimens of wild animals

Article 543-2

Without prejudice to the application of the sanctions provided for by the Commercial Code and by the Customs Code, anyone who transits, imports, exports or re-exports one or more specimens of a species subject to the provisions of this Code and its implementing texts is punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.

In addition, the specimen is confiscated or returned to the country of origin or provenance at their expense in cases of attempted illegal importation or re-exportation.

Section 3

Mining operations in protected areas or in a migration corridor

Article 543-3

Any natural or legal person who digs in the soil, extracts or removes sand, peat, turf, stones, earth or in general organises prospecting, research and/or mining operations or earthworks or constructions, or tending to modify the appearance of the land and/or vegetation in a protected area, a buffer zone of a protected area or in a fauna migration corridor with or without occupation of the premises is punished:
for the natural person by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs, without prejudice to the restoration of the premises, the confiscation of mineral substances extracted in the area concerned as well as all material means, vehicles, devices and equipment used to commit the offence within the classified perimeter;
for the legal person by a fine of 20,000,000 francs without prejudice to the restoration of the premises, the confiscation of mineral substances extracted in the area concerned as well as all material means, vehicles, devices and equipment used to commit the offence within the classified perimeter.

In addition, they incur the following complementary penalties:
the payment of a penalty calculated at the rate of 1000 francs per square metre of damaged and/or occupied surface;
the definitive cessation of works and the prohibition to continue operations or activities in the protected area;
the pure and simple cancellation of the authorisation or mining title authorising the occupation or exploitation of the land if it exists;
the demolition of installations, dwellings and the confiscation of equipment and the expulsion of occupants outside the limits of the area concerned.

However, when the offence is committed in a strict nature reserve, a core area of a biosphere reserve or a national park, the fines are doubled.

Section 4: Degradation and pollution of protected areas

Article 543-4

Is punished by imprisonment of three years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs, without prejudice to confiscations, restitutions, restoration of premises, anyone who commits the following acts:
the deposit of rubble, waste or refuse of any nature in a protected area;
any pollution of waters and in general, any act likely to harm or cause disturbances to the fauna and/or aquatic flora of a protected area.

In cases of pollution using hazardous waste, the provisions of Title III of this Code apply.

Section 5

Destruction, displacement or disappearance of boundary markers or beacons of a protected area or a fauna migration corridor

Article 543-5

Anyone who destroys, displaces or causes to disappear all or part of the boundary markers, beacons, signs, marks or fencing used to mark the limits of a protected area or a migration corridor, without prejudice to confiscations, restitutions, restoration of premises, is punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 6,000,000 francs.
When the offence is committed in a strict nature reserve, a core area of a biosphere reserve or a national park, the fine is doubled.

Section 6: Agricultural and forestry exploitation in a protected area

Article 543-6

Any natural or legal person under private or public law, except the State and local authorities, in violation of the provisions of this Code and its implementing texts, who engages in agricultural and/or forestry activity in a protected area, with or without occupation of the premises, is punished:
for natural persons, by imprisonment of six months and a fine of 2,000,000 francs, without prejudice to confiscations, restitutions and restoration of the premises;
for legal persons, by a fine of 10,000,000 francs, without prejudice to confiscations, restitutions and restoration of the premises.

The accused also incurs the following sanctions:
payment of a penalty calculated at the rate of 500 francs per square meter of cleared and/or occupied surface area;
prohibition from continuing the operations or activities for which or during which the clearing was carried out;
restoration of the premises, consisting of planting or sowing native forest species adapted to the deforested area and other work necessary to ensure the functions that characterized the cleared forest;
demolition of installations and dwellings as well as confiscation of equipment and expulsion of occupants from the boundaries of the conservation area;
pure and simple cancellation of the clearing authorization or the title authorizing occupation or exploitation of the land, if it exists.

When the offense is committed in a strict nature reserve, a core area of a biosphere reserve or a national park, or when the incriminated activities are organized by a legal person or with its complicity, the fine is doubled.

Section 7: Grazing of domestic animals in protected areas

Article 543-7

Anyone who, in violation of the provisions of this Code and its implementing texts, grazes or moves one or more domestic animals or camps or possesses weapons and ammunition and/or cutting tools or means in a special reserve is punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 1,000,000 francs, without prejudice to the confiscation of weapons and ammunition as well as tools or means of cutting plants held by the offender and damages.

In addition, he incurs the following complementary sanctions:

1°) demolition of installations and dwellings and confiscation of equipment found, as well as expulsion of the herder(s) and other occupants from the boundaries of the protected area;

2°) payment of a penalty of:
2,000 francs per bovine, equine, asinine and camelid;
4,000 francs per ovine, caprine and porcine.

Article 543-8

When the offenses incriminated in the above article do not result from purely fortuitous circumstances, imprisonment of six months is pronounced against the perpetrators and their accomplices.

Animals found in the protected area are impounded and confiscated.

Article 543-9

Domestic animals found grazing and/or wandering in a strict nature reserve, in a national park or in a core area of a biosphere reserve are slaughtered by agents of the services responsible for surveillance and protection of these protected areas when they cannot capture them alive.

Animals captured in the protected area are impounded and confiscated.

Chapter 4

Crimes and offenses relating to possession, trade, export, re-export, import, transport and transit of specimens of wild fauna and flora species

[Law No. 02-017 of June 3, 2002 governing the possession, trade, export, re-export, import, transport and transit of specimens of wild fauna and flora species]

Article 544-1

Anyone who contravenes the provisions of the CITES law, with the exception of the purchase, sale and offering for sale of fish or animal meat, is punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 2,000,000 francs.

Article 544-2

Anyone who contravenes the provisions of the CITES law is punished by imprisonment of three months and a fine of 1,000,000 francs, without prejudice to the confiscation of specimens.

Article 544-3

Anyone who transits, exhibits to the public for commercial purposes, uses for profit without authorization a specimen of a species subject to the provisions of the CITES law, is punished by imprisonment of six months and a fine of 1,000,000 francs without prejudice to the confiscation of said specimen.

Article 544-4

Anyone who introduces an animal or plant species of wild origin in violation of the provisions of the CITES law is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.

TITLE V: OFFENSES IN MATTERS OF TRAFFIC

Chapter 1: Offenses in matters of road traffic

[Law No. 2023-045 of August 31, 2023 governing road traffic]

Section 1: Offenses against the rules of vehicle operation

Article 551-1

The driver of a vehicle is criminally liable for offenses committed by him in the operation of said vehicle. However, when the driver acts in the capacity of an employee, the Court may, taking into account the factual circumstances and working conditions, decide that the legal costs shall be wholly or partly borne by the employer.

Article 551-2

Is punished by imprisonment of three months and a fine of 200,000 francs:

1°) any driver of a vehicle who, knowing that this vehicle has just caused or occasioned an accident, did not stop and thus attempted to escape the civil or criminal liability he may incur;

2°) any person who drives or attempts to drive a vehicle while in a state of intoxication or under the influence of an alcoholic state, or under the effect of a narcotic;

3°) any person who drives a vehicle without having obtained the permit or authorization to drive valid for the category of vehicle concerned or whose permit or authorization is the subject of a duly justified measure of suspension, withdrawal or cancellation;

4°) any person who, being the owner or having the use or custody of a vehicle, has it driven or allows it to be driven by a third party whom he knows to be without the required permit.

If there is occasion to apply articles 321-5 and 321-18 of this Code relating to involuntary homicide and injury, the penalties are doubled.

Article 551-3

Is punished by the penalties provided for in the provisions of article 242-40 of this Code, any driver of a vehicle who obstructs the immobilization of the vehicle by an agent authorized for this purpose.

Article 551-4

In case of recidivism of one of the offenses provided for in articles 551-2 and 551-3 above, the Court may pronounce, as a complementary measure, the confiscation for the benefit of the State, of the vehicle which the accused used to commit the offense, if he is the owner.

Article 551-5

Are punished by the penalties provided for in the provisions of article 414-1 of this Code, those who destroy or divert or attempt to destroy or divert any vehicle confiscated in application of the provisions of the preceding article.

Section 2

Offenses against the rules of use of roads open to public traffic

Article 551-6

Is punished by imprisonment of six months and a fine of 500,000 francs anyone who:

1°) with a view to obstructing or hindering traffic and without lawful authorization, creates an obstacle by any means to the passage of vehicles;

2°) knowingly violates legal or regulatory provisions intended to ensure the conservation of public roads open to traffic as well as bridges, ferries and other structures constituting their extension or incorporated therein.

Article : 551-7

Are punished by imprisonment of six months and a fine of 1,200,000 francs those who organize motor vehicle races without authorization from the competent administrative authority.

Section 3

Offenses against the rules for the use of vehicles and their equipment

Article 551-8

Is punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 250,000 francs anyone who:

1°) knowingly puts into circulation a motor vehicle or trailer without holding the authorizations or administrative documents required for the circulation of that vehicle;

2°) voluntarily makes use of a license plate bearing false or supposed false indications or of authorizations and administrative documents required for the circulation of vehicles which he knows to be false, expired or cancelled;

3°) operates a motor vehicle or trailer without the vehicle being equipped with the plates or inscriptions required by regulations and who in addition knowingly declares a false or supposed number, name, or domicile.

Article 551-9

Is punished by imprisonment of six months and a fine of 200,000 francs anyone who:

1°) puts or maintains in circulation a motor vehicle intended for public passenger transport whose general condition presents a manifest danger to users and passengers and which has not been submitted to technical inspection within the regulatory time limits;

2°) violates the rules specially adopted by decree, with a view to ensuring the safety of transported persons;

3°) transports or causes to be transported in a public transport vehicle a number of persons greater than that which the vehicle used was authorized to carry on board.

In the cases provided for above, the Court may also order the confiscation of the vehicle.

Article 551-10

Shall be immobilized any moped or motorcycle that circulates without the driver and passenger wearing helmets or equipped with mandatory equipment intended to guarantee their own safety. If, within seventy-two hours, the driver or passenger of the vehicle has not justified the cessation of the offense, the immobilization is converted into impoundment.

Any person violating the provisions of the first paragraph is punished by a fine of 50,000 francs.

Section 4 : Driving license

Article 551-11 : Is punished by the following penalties

1. any person who, by a false declaration, obtains or attempts to obtain a driving license, is punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 300,000 francs;

2. any person who, having been notified of a decision pronouncing against him the suspension or cancellation of the driving license, refuses to return the suspended or cancelled license to the agent responsible for executing that decision.

3. The courts and tribunals may order the cancellation of the driving license, in case of conviction, either for one of the offenses provided for in Article 551-2 paragraphs 1, 2, 3, or for the offenses provided for in this Code when involuntary homicide or injuries were committed on the occasion of driving a vehicle.

They may also order cancellation, in case of conviction, in the following cases:

1°) driving a vehicle when a decision of suspension or retention of the license has been notified;

2°) refusal to return one's driving license to the competent authority when a decision of suspension or retention has been notified.

4. The driving license is cancelled, by operation of law, as a consequence of conviction:

1°) in case of recidivism of one of the offenses provided for in Article 551-2 paragraphs 1, 2 and 3 above;

2°) when there is simultaneous application of Article 551-2 paragraphs 1, 2 and 3 and the provisions of this Code relating to involuntary homicide and injuries.

5. In case of cancellation of the driving license pursuant to paragraphs 3 and 4 above, the person concerned may not apply for a new license before the expiration of a period fixed by the judge within a maximum limit of three years and provided that he is recognized as fit, after a medical examination carried out at his expense.

6. In case of recidivism of offenses giving rise to the simultaneous application of Article 551-2 paragraphs 1, 2 and 3 above and the provisions of Articles 321-5 and 321-18 of this Code, the person concerned may not apply for a new license before the expiration of a period of ten years provided that he is recognized as fit, after a medical examination carried out at his expense.

Section 5

Provisions relating to the teaching of driving motor vehicles

Article 551-12

No one may, without having been previously authorized under the conditions fixed by decree, teach the driving of motor vehicles.

Is punished by a fine of 500,000 francs anyone who violates the prohibition stated above or the provisions relating to the teaching of driving motor vehicles.

The confiscation of the vehicle or vehicles used for the illegal practice of teaching may also be ordered.

Section 6

Provisions concerning the recording and communication of information relating to the documentation required for driving and the circulation of vehicles

Article 551-13

There shall be carried out, in the State services, under the authority and control of the minister responsible for Transport, the recording of:

1°) all information relating to driving licenses for which issuance is requested or which are issued pursuant to this title, as well as driving licenses issued by foreign authorities and recognized as valid on the national territory;

2°) all information concerning the administrative documents required for the circulation of vehicles or affecting their availability;

3°) all duly notified administrative decisions imposing restriction of validity, suspension, cancellation and restriction of issuance of a driving license;

4°) all measures of withdrawal of the right to use a driving license taken by a foreign authority and communicated to the Malian authorities in accordance with international agreements in force;

5°) official reports of the offenses mentioned in Articles 551-2, 551-3, 551-6, 551-8, 551-9 and 551-11 of this chapter;

6°) all final judicial decisions relating to offenses in matters of road traffic.

Article 551-14

The information mentioned in Article 551-13 above is subject to automated processing.

Article 551-15

Without prejudice to the application of amnesty laws, information relating to judicial convictions and administrative measures affecting the driving licence must be erased in accordance with the statutory limitation period without a judicial decision or a measure mentioned in paragraph 3 of Article 551-13 above having occurred again.

The period provided for in the preceding paragraph runs:

1°) for judicial convictions, from the day on which the last conviction became final;

2°) for administrative measures, from the day of the last decision.

In the case where an administrative measure is annulled, the erasure of information relating to that measure is carried out on the day of the judicial or administrative decision pronouncing that annulment.

The period is extended to ten years from the day on which the conviction became final when paragraph 3 of Article 551-11 above is applied.

The period is reduced to two years from the day of registration for information relating to driving licences for which issuance is requested.

Article 551-16

The holder of the driving licence has the right to communication of the complete record of entries concerning him or her. He or she may not obtain a copy thereof.

Article 551-17

The complete record of entries relating to licences applicable to the same person is issued at their request:

1°) to the judicial authorities;

2°) to Judicial Police Officers responsible for executing a jurisdictional order or acting within the framework of a flagrante delicto investigation.

Article 551-18

Information relating to the existence, category and validity of the driving licence is communicated at their request:

1°) to the holder of the licence, his or her lawyer or his or her representative;

2°) to the competent foreign authorities, for the purposes of authentication of the driving licence in accordance with international agreements in force;

3°) to Judicial Police Officers acting within the framework of a preliminary investigation;

4°) to Gendarmes and Military Personnel of the National Police authorised to carry out road checks in application of regulations relating to road traffic;

5°) to civil and military administrative authorities for persons employed or likely to be employed as drivers of motor land vehicles;

6°) to insurance companies for persons whose liability incurred as a result of damage caused by motor land vehicles they guarantee or are called upon to guarantee.

Article 551-19

Information, other than that mentioned in Article 551-19 below relating to administrative documents required for the circulation of vehicles, is communicated at their request:
1°) to the natural or legal person holding the administrative documents, his or her lawyer or his or her representative;
2°) to the judicial authorities;
3°) to Judicial Police Officers in the exercise of their mission defined in the Code of Criminal Procedure;
4°) to Gendarmes and Military Personnel of the National Police authorised to carry out road checks in application of regulations relating to road traffic;

5°) to officials authorised to record offences against traffic police regulations for the sole purpose of identifying the perpetrators of such offences;

6°) to the authorities of Territorial Collectivities for the exercise of their competence in matters of vehicle circulation;

7°) to the services of the Ministry in charge of Industry for the exercise of their competences;

8°) to insurance companies guaranteeing damage suffered by third parties resulting from harm to persons or property in the occurrence of which a motor land vehicle, as well as its trailers or semi-trailers are involved and to organisations assimilated to these companies provided that such information has the sole purpose of identifying the property or persons involved in a traffic accident on condition that at least one of the vehicles is insured by the applicant or that the latter is responsible for compensating one of the victims.

Insurance companies must provide in support of their request all useful elements making it possible to verify the reality of the claim.

Article 551-20

Information relating, on the one hand to pledges constituted on motor land vehicles and, on the other hand to oppositions to the transfer of the registration certificate, is communicated at their request:

1°) to the natural or legal person holding the administrative documents, his or her lawyer or his or her representative;

2°) to the judicial authorities;

3°) to Judicial Police Officers in the exercise of their mission defined in the Code of Criminal Procedure;

4°) to the authorities of Territorial Collectivities for the exercise of their powers in matters of vehicle use and circulation on public roads.

The absence of declaration of pledge or opposition made to the transfer of certificate of a vehicle defined by its registration number alone may, to the exclusion of any other information, be brought to the attention of any other person who requests it.

Article 551-21

Information relating to the civil status of the holder of the registration certificate, the registration number and the characteristics of the vehicle, as well as to pledges constituted and oppositions are, to the exclusion of any other information, communicated at their request for their mission:

1°) to agents responsible for executing an enforceable instrument;

2°) to judicial administrators and liquidators or to trustees appointed within the framework of a judicial reorganisation or liquidation of assets procedure provided for by the Commercial Code.

Article 551-22

No nominative information may be disclosed outside the cases provided for in Articles 551-15 and 551-21 above.

Article 551-23: Is punished by the penalties provided for by this Code, anyone who

1°) takes the name of a person in circumstances which have determined or which could have determined the registration in the name of that person of a judicial conviction or an administrative decision;

2°) has communicated to himself or herself, by taking a false name or a false capacity, the record of entries registered concerning a third party;

3°) obtains, either directly or indirectly, communication of nominative information the disclosure of which is not expressly provided for by the law relating to road traffic.

[Law No. 029 of 29 June 2006, as amended, relating to protection of public roads]

Article 551-24

Is punished by imprisonment of six months and a fine of 200,000 francs, after a formal notice without follow-up from the competent authority, any person who contravenes the provisions of Article 5 of the law relating to the protection of public roads.

Article 551-25

Is punished by imprisonment of thirty days and a fine of 200,000 francs, any person who erects or orders the erection without authorisation from the competent authorities of a speed bump on the public road.

Chapter 2: Offences in matters of circulation within the framework of civil aviation

Section 1

Circulation of persons and vehicles in restricted areas of airports of Mali

Paragraph 1: Movement of Persons

Article 552-1

The movement of persons in a restricted zone is regulated and subject to holding a security access pass or a valid badge.

No one may be present in a security restricted access zone without complying with the measures and procedures applicable to access control to said zone.

Any natural person who accesses a zone of the airport perimeter for which they have neither a pass nor authorization is punished by three months' imprisonment and a fine of 150,000 francs.

Suspension may, in addition, be imposed for a period of six days.

The following are punished by the same penalties:
wearing the access pass in a non-visible manner;
usurpation or fraudulent use of access passes;
use of the access pass outside its zone of validity;
refusal to present documents required by the regulations;
failure to return an access pass three days after cessation of work at the airport;
access to a security restricted access zone without complying with the measures and procedures applicable to access control to said zone.

Complicity is punished as the offense itself.

Article 552-2

A legal person with authority who does not take measures to ensure the accompaniment of a person holding a visitor pass, or who does not communicate within a maximum period of five days their cessation of activity in the restricted zone, or who does not return the access card of an employee upon cessation of activity within the same period, incurs a fine of 500,000 francs.

Article 552-3

A natural person who is subject to a conviction for a correctional penalty, who contravenes the regulations in force or who lends their access pass to another person is deprived of said pass.

Article 552-4

Intrusion of any nature whatsoever into the airport perimeter is strictly prohibited.

Any person who contravenes this measure is punished by five years' imprisonment and a fine which may not be less than 1,000,000 francs.

These penalties are doubled if the acts are committed during the night or if the perpetrator is aeronautical personnel.

The perpetrator may not benefit from mitigating circumstances in case of recidivism.

Article 552-5

When circumstances require, restrictions are placed on the free movement of natural and legal persons in the public zone of an airport. These restrictions are immediately brought to the attention of the services responsible for the Police, the Gendarmerie, users, operators and the competent authority for Civil Aviation Security in Mali.

Anyone who contravenes these measures is punished by three months' imprisonment and a fine of 150,000 francs.

The fine is 500,000 francs for a legal person.

Confiscation may be applied as an additional penalty.

Paragraph 2: Movement of Vehicles

Article 552-6

Access of any vehicle to the security restricted access zone of the airport is conditional upon presentation of a circulation pass or sticker corresponding to the activity zone of said vehicle with the exception of:
airport vehicles;	maintenance vehicles;	ambulances;	bank vans;	vehicles and equipment operated by airlines.

Any natural person who contravenes this measure is punished by three months' imprisonment and a fine of 125,000 francs.

The fine is 500,000 francs for a legal person.

The following offenses are punished by the same penalties:
wearing the access pass or sticker in a non-visible manner;
usurpation or fraudulent use of access passes;
use of the access pass outside its zone of validity;
refusal to present documents required by the regulations;
failure to return one's access pass after cessation of work at the airport within three days;
access to a security restricted access zone without complying with the measures and procedures applicable to access control to said zone.

Section 2

Obstruction of the Exercise of Profession of Security and Safety Control Agents

Article 552-7

Inspectors, Judicial Police Officers, Gendarmerie Officers and any person representing legitimate authority must not be subject to any hindrance in the exercise of their functions. Any opposition to legitimate authority in the exercise of its function or the act of obstructing the proper conduct thereof constitutes the offense provided for and punished by the penalties provided for in Article 242-40 of this Code.

Section 3: Human Safety

Article 552-8

Any person who smokes in open or closed zones of airport terminals such as the boarding lounge, the check-in hall and warehouses intended for air freight is punished by three months' imprisonment and a fine of 100,000 francs, except when it is a zone reserved for this purpose.

Article 552-9

Any natural person who dumps household waste in the airport domain, practices tall crops along the roads of said domain endangers human safety is punished by three months' imprisonment and a fine of 450,000 francs.

Any violation of other hygiene and sanitation rules in the airport domain is punished by the same penalties.

The fine is 1,000,000 francs for a legal person.

Article 552-10

Any natural person who erects a pylon of more than sixty meters in the airport domain or proceeds to degrade said domain endangers human safety is punished by three months' imprisonment and a fine of 450,000 francs.

The fine is 1,000,000 francs for a legal person.

Section 4: Movement of Rolling Equipment in the Airport Domain

Article 552-11

Any equipment operator who contravenes airport regulations regarding:
traffic;
parking outside a reserved area;
drop-off zone.

is punished by three months' imprisonment and a fine of 150,000 francs.

For any person who abandons their equipment in the airport domain, including in reserved areas beyond forty-eight hours, the fine is tripled.

Impoundment of the equipment may be ordered.

Section 5: Failure to Declare a Firearm and Ammunition at Airports

Article 552-12

Airport users or passengers holding a firearm and ammunition carrying authorization are required to declare in advance to the Air and Border Police their firearms and ammunition before accessing the restricted zones of the airport. To this end, forms are made available to them at the Air and Border Police.

Any person who fails to make this declaration is punished by a fine which may not be less than 300,000 francs.

Section 6: Safety of passengers, airport personnel and baggage

Article 552-13: No one shall

enter the restricted access zone, nor board an aircraft unless they have been subjected to screening or inspection in accordance with applicable measures and procedures. This provision applies to agents holding credentials giving them access to said zone;
obstruct, assault, threaten or intimidate an authorized agent when they are carrying out screening operations;
bring baggage or cargo into the reserved zone nor load it aboard an aircraft unless it has been subjected to screening or inspection in accordance with applicable measures and procedures.

Any individual holding a document issued by the aeronautical authority or by an organization approved by the latter, including a crew member certificate, a medical certificate, an authorization or a license must present said document for inspection.

Any person violating these measures is punished by three months' imprisonment and a fine of 200,000 francs.

Any article or package left unattended at any location in the airport, or in its vicinity, is systematically removed and possibly destroyed.

Article 552-14

Any aircraft operator, natural person, having transported passengers who do not meet the conditions for entry and stay on Malian territory is punished by three months' imprisonment and a fine of 3,000,000 francs per passenger transported.

The fine is 5,000,000 francs per passenger transported when the aircraft operator or airline is a legal person.

In addition, all costs of returning the passengers concerned to their port of origin and their medical care, if applicable, are the entire responsibility of the aircraft operator or airline concerned.

Section 7: Documentary fraud

Article 552-15

Any person responsible for the following is punished by three months' imprisonment and a fine of 150,000 francs:
a fraudulent declaration in an application form for their identification, for obtaining an airport circulation permit or for approval of a security program;
a fraudulent entry in a report or record that must be kept or used to prove compliance with regulations in force;
reproduction, falsification or modification for fraudulent purposes of a report, record, program, means of access or means of identification.

Section 8: Attempt to commit offenses against civil aviation

Article 552-16

The attempt to commit the offenses provided for in this chapter is punished as the offenses themselves.

Article 552-17

The provisions of this chapter apply without prejudice to regional and international legal instruments relating to civil aviation.

TITLE VI: OFFENSES RELATING TO BIOLOGICAL SAFETY AND SECURITY

Chapter I: Offenses

Article 561-1

The following constitute offenses relating to biological safety and security:
the development, acquisition, manufacture, possession, storage, transport, stockpiling, release, abandonment, destruction, use or handling in a confined environment of controlled pathogens or toxins without a permit, license, authorization or prior agreement granted by the organization in charge of biological safety and security or in violation of the conditions of any permit, license, authorization or prior agreement granted by said authority;
the import, export, re-export, or transshipment of pathogens and/or toxins, dual-use goods or technologies controlled within the national territory without a permit, license, authorization, prior agreement granted by the organization in charge of biological safety and security or without an end-use certificate;
the transport of controlled pathogens and/or toxins either domestically or internationally through carriers not authorized for this purpose;
the construction, acquisition or possession of a facility designed or intended to manufacture or to conduct research on controlled pathogens and/or toxins, except those authorized by the provisions of this Code;
the damaging of any facility, packaging or contents of a containment enclosure containing controlled pathogens and/or toxins in order to release them;
the diversion of controlled pathogens and/or toxins from a facility or vehicle authorized for their transport, or the use or taking control of an authorized vehicle containing controlled pathogens and/or toxins to release the controlled pathogens and/or toxins;
the development, manufacture, acquisition by other means, stockpiling, possession, transport, retention of biological and/or toxin weapons or the transfer, directly or indirectly, of a biological or toxin weapon for any purpose whatsoever;
the use of biological and/or toxin weapons;
engagement in preparations for the use of biological and/or toxin weapons;
the construction, acquisition or possession of any facility intended for the production of biological weapons;
the transformation of any controlled pathogen and/or toxin into a biological weapon;
the intentional release of pathogens and/or toxins for the purpose of causing injury or killing human beings, animals or plants/vegetation and degrading the environment and living environment with the aim of intimidating or coercing a government or civilian population.

Chapter 2: Penalties

Article 562-1

Anyone who commits an offense relating to biological safety and security is punished by ten years' imprisonment and a fine of 10,000,000 francs.

In case of death, the penalty incurred is life imprisonment.

When the offense relating to biological safety and security is committed through a legal person, criminal liability falls on the natural person who committed the offense.

However, the legal person in question is jointly and severally condemned with the physical perpetrator(s) to payment of all or part of the fines, costs and legal fees as well as civil damages.

In case of conviction, the competent court orders the confiscation of the proceeds of the offense or the means that were used or intended to commit it.

Article 562-2

Complicity in the offense relating to biological safety and security is punished as the offense itself.

Article 562-3

Any public agent or any private sector agent found guilty of complicity in the offense specified above or of negligence in the exercise of their duties with regard to the law relating to biological safety and security incurs disciplinary sanctions.

Article 562-4

In case of violations of the provisions of this title, the organization in charge of biological safety and security may negotiate with the persons involved as long as a decision ruling on the merits has not become final.

When the transaction results in an agreement, the file is transmitted to the competent court which finds that the public action is extinguished.

In case of recidivism, no transaction is permitted.

TITLE VII: OFFENCES RELATING TO PUBLIC HYGIENE AND SANITATION

Single Chapter: Offences and penalties

Article 571-1

Any person who contravenes the provisions of the law relating to public hygiene and sanitation concerning housing hygiene and food hygiene is punished by three months' imprisonment and a fine of 30,000 francs.

Article 571-2

Any person who contravenes the provisions of the law relating to public hygiene and sanitation concerning water hygiene and collective catering hygiene is punished by three months' imprisonment and a fine of 50,000 francs.

Article 571-3

Any person who contravenes the provisions of the law relating to public hygiene and sanitation concerning hospital hygiene and hygiene of classified establishments is punished by three months' imprisonment and a fine of 50,000 francs.

Article 571-4

Any person who contravenes the provisions of the law relating to public hygiene and sanitation concerning mortuary hygiene is punished by three months' imprisonment and a fine of 50,000 francs.

Article 571-5

In the event of a repeat offence in the cases referred to in the above articles, the penalties are doubled.

In addition, the competent court shall impose one of the following additional penalties:
temporary closure for eight days with respect to industrial, artisanal, commercial and leisure facilities;
suspension of the administrative authorization or licence.

During this period, the owner or head of the establishment is required to take all useful measures to comply with the regulations in force, repair the damage caused, which are necessary conditions for the reopening of the establishment.

If upon reopening, the same offences are found, the competent court shall order the permanent closure of the establishment.

Article 571-6

The person responsible for hygiene officers may reach a settlement with the persons concerned as long as a decision on the merits has not become final.

When the settlement results in an agreement, the file is transmitted to the competent court which finds that the public prosecution is extinguished.

In the event of a repeat offence, no settlement is permitted.

TITLE VIII: OFFENCES RELATING TO ARCHIVES

[Law No. 002-052 of 22 July 2002 relating to archives]

Single Chapter: Offences and penalties

Article 581-1

Any person who, upon ceasing their functions, misappropriates, even without fraudulent intent, public archives of which they are the holder by reason of their functions, is punished by one year's imprisonment and a fine of 100,000 francs.

Article 581-2

Any person who deliberately alters in any manner whatsoever, destroys or neglects public or private archive documents entrusted on deposit otherwise than under the conditions provided for by the texts in force is punished by one year's imprisonment and a fine of 100,000 francs.

Article 581-3

Any natural person who deliberately misappropriates or removes said documents from the national Territory is punished by a penalty of two years' imprisonment and a fine of 800,000 francs.

The fine is 2,000,000 francs for a legal person without prejudice to the other penalties provided for in Article 131-15 of this Code.

TITLE IX: OFFENCES RELATING TO BLOOD TRANSFUSION

[Law No. 2022-003/of 20 May 2022 relating to blood transfusion]

Single Chapter: Offences and penalties

Article 591-1

Any person who traffics in their own blood is punished by three months' imprisonment and a fine of 500,000 francs.

Article 591-2

Any person who traffics in the blood of others or in blood products of human origin is punished by one year's imprisonment and a fine of 1,000,000 francs.

Article 591-3

Any person who carries out a collection of blood of human origin outside the National Blood Transfusion Centre, its branches and the Armed Forces Health Services or uses it outside authorized healthcare establishments is punished by three years' imprisonment and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.

Article 591-4: Any person is punished by the penalties provided for in Article 591-3 who

1°) imports, exports or facilitates the import or export of blood products of human origin outside authorized establishments;

2°) establishes or operates without authorization a blood bank or blood depot;

3°) disregards closure orders for their establishment. Altered or non-compliant products are confiscated.
Repeat offences are judged in accordance with the provisions of Articles 132-8 and following of this Code.

If these acts are committed by a health worker, a ban on practising the profession for a period not exceeding one year may be imposed by the competent court as an additional penalty.

TITLE X: OFFENCES RELATING TO FISHING AND AQUACULTURE

Chapter 1: Offences and penalties relating to fishing

Section 1

Mining operations in a protected area established as a fish reserve or sanctuary

Article 510-1

Any natural or legal person who undertakes prospecting, construction or excavation work in the ground, extracts or removes sand, peat, turf, stones, earth or in general organizes the search for and/or mining operations in an aquatic reserve or in an aquatic sanctuary with or without occupation of the premises, is punished by a fine calculated at the rate of 500 francs per square metre of damaged and/or occupied surface without prejudice to confiscations, restitutions, restoration of the premises and damages.

In addition, the offender is liable to the following penalties:

1°) prohibition from continuing operations or activities in the classified area;

2°) restoration of the premises;

3°) demolition of installations, dwellings or other equipment and expulsion of occupants from the classified perimeter.

Section 2

Destruction and removal of boundary markers and beacons delimiting the classified fishing area

Article 510-2

Without prejudice to confiscations, restitutions, restoration of the premises and damages, any person who destroys, moves or removes all or part of the boundary markers, beacons, signs, marks or fences used to delimit the classified fishing area is punished by two years' imprisonment and a fine of 5,000,000 francs.

Section 3: Occupation of the classified fishing area

Article 510-3

Any person who, in violation of the provisions of this title, occupies the classified fishing area, is punished by a fine of 10,000,000 francs without prejudice to confiscations, restitutions, restoration of the premises and damages.

The offender is liable, in addition, to the following penalties:

1°) prohibition from continuing the operations or activities for which or during which the clearing was carried out;

2°) restoration of the premises;

3°) demolition of installations, dwellings or other equipment and expulsion of occupants from the classified perimeter;

4°) outright cancellation of the authorization or title authorizing the occupation or exploitation of the land if it exists.

Section 4: Illegal practice of the fishing profession

Article 510-4

Any person who, in violation of the provisions of this chapter, practises the profession of operator without holding a fishing licence is punished by one month's imprisonment and a fine of 100,000 francs without prejudice to damages.

Section 5

False indication, falsification of writings and reproduction of public seals

Article 510-5

Is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 800,000 francs, without prejudice to damages and the provisions of this Code relating to forgery in writings, anyone who gives false information during the issuance of fishing licenses.

The penalty is imprisonment of twenty years and a fine of 800,000 francs without prejudice to damages and the provisions of this Code relating to forgery in writings and counterfeiting of State seals, for anyone who falsifies writings and/or fraudulently reproduces public seals.

Article 510-6

Without prejudice to the sanctions provided for by this Code, the withdrawal of licenses and the prohibition for a period of one to five years from obtaining new licenses are pronounced against any holder of a fishing license who is guilty of false information, falsification of writings and/or reproduction of public seals.

In case of recidivism, the prohibition and withdrawal of the license for five years are mandatory.

Section 6 : Des moyens, méthodes de pêche et fermeture de la pêche

Article 510-7

Anyone who, in violation of the provisions of this chapter, bars or encloses for fishing purposes the beds of rivers, streams or their direct tributaries and to prevent the free passage of fish, is punished by imprisonment of one month and a fine of 5,000,000 francs without prejudice to damages.

Section 7 : De l'opposition à l'autorité des agents chargés de la police de pêche

Article 510-8

Opposition to the authority of agents responsible for fishing police is provided for and punished in accordance with the provisions of Article 242-40 of this Code.

Section 8 : Des espèces protégées et des eaux

Article 510-9

Is punished by imprisonment of one month and a fine of 500,000 francs without prejudice to damages, anyone who in violation of the provisions of this chapter: fishes juveniles of specimens of species that have not reached the minimum capture size;
proceeds with the capture, detention, sale and offering for sale, trade and export of protected species;
discharges, releases or deposits directly or indirectly into waters materials likely to harm public health as well as aquatic fauna and flora.

Chapitre 2 : Des infractions et des peines en matière d'aquaculture

Section 1 : De l'exploitation illégale en matière d'aquaculture

Article 510-10

Is punished by a fine of 2,000,000 francs, any natural or legal person who operates a commercial or scientific aquaculture establishment without holding an aquaculture operator license.

Article 510-11

Is punished by a fine of 500,000 francs, anyone who operates an aquaculture establishment for subsistence purposes in the public or private domain of the State or Territorial Authorities without prior authorization from the administration responsible for aquaculture.

Article 510-12

In the case of offenses provided for by the above articles, the Court may in addition order the closure of the aquaculture establishment and the confiscation of objects and products.

Section 2 : De la gestion d'établissements aquacoles

Article 510-13

Is punished by imprisonment of five years and a fine of 10,000,000 francs, anyone who commits one or more of the following offenses:

1°) the use of prohibited products in aquaculture establishments;

2°) the modification, extension and conversion of a subsistence aquaculture establishment for commercial or scientific purposes without prior authorization;

3°) the failure to declare to the competent services within the imposed time limit, the change of operator or any modification occurring in the company;

4°) the transplantation from one site to another of different eco-geographical nature of a species or variety of species without authorization;

5°) the release of organisms from aquaculture into the natural environment and for restocking purposes without authorization;

6°) the absence of an aquaculture establishment register and failure to update;

7°) the failure to report to the competent services, within the imposed time limit, the appearance of a contagious disease among the exploited species;

8°) the failure to comply with prescribed measures in order to prevent, halt the development or promote the extinction of diseases affecting the exploited species;

9°) the failure to observe, within the time limit imposed on a commercial aquaculture establishment, the obligation of annual declaration of production, marketing and the status of ongoing cultures as well as employed personnel;

10°) the failure to communicate, by an aquaculture establishment to the competent services within the imposed time limit, the annual report on research conducted;

11°) the failure to comply with one of the clauses of the specifications;

12°) the failure to comply with sanitary standards relating to the hygiene of aquaculture products and the quality of breeding waters;

13°) the failure to comply with breeding standards and food standards;

14°) fishing and placing for consumption or sale in an area or establishment declared infected.

Article 510-14

In the case of a subsistence aquaculture establishment, the offenses provided for in Article 510-13 above are punished by imprisonment of three months and a fine of 500,000 francs.

Article 510-15

Without prejudice to the provisions of texts governing the control of international trade, anyone who imports, exports, re-exports or introduces a specimen of an animal or plant species into an aquaculture establishment in violation of the provisions of this chapter and texts adopted for its application, is punished by imprisonment of two years and a fine of 500,000 francs without prejudice to damages.

In addition, the specimen is confiscated or returned to the country of origin at the expense of the offender in cases of importation.

When the imported species specimen is a genetically modified organism or a product derived from a genetically modified organism, the provisions of texts relating to biosafety in force in Mali apply.

Article 510-16

In case of failure to report, to the competent services, within the fixed time limit, the appearance of a contagious disease among the species being farmed, the costs arising from measures taken by the competent authorities to halt the development of the disease are borne by the operator.

Article 510-17
